# OmniPotent — Full Feature Audit

_Generated 2026-08-24. Method: 20 parallel domain auditors + 20 independent adversarial verifiers + cross-cutting synthesis. Every verdict is backed by executed commands (FastAPI `TestClient` probes, targeted pytest runs, import-closure analysis), not by reading docs or module names. 13 verdicts were overturned on verification._

**Scope audited:** ~239k LOC Python (636 modules, ~145 top-level packages), 292 live HTTP routes, 19 React pages, 2,600 tests.

## Verdict tally

| Verdict | Count | % | Meaning |
|---|---:|---:|---|
| **WORKS** | 41 | 11% | Executed end-to-end: real logic, real persistence, correct output |
| **PARTIAL** | 111 | 31% | Core path works, but a meaningful part is faked, missing, or needs config |
| **STUB** | 51 | 14% | Wired and returns 200, but the logic is hardcoded / in-memory only |
| **BROKEN** | 84 | 23% | Reachable but errors, returns wrong data, or cannot succeed |
| **DEAD_CODE** | 71 | 20% | No entrypoint — unreachable from any router, CLI, or task |
| **UNVERIFIABLE** | 3 | 1% | Requires a live external service/credential unavailable here |
| | **361** | | |

# OmniPotent — Cross-Cutting Audit Synthesis

*Lead auditor synthesis over 20 domain audits + 20 adversarial verifications, 361 assessed features, with independent re-verification of the load-bearing contradictions.*

---

## 1. The honest bottom line

**Roughly 11% of the assessed product surface works (41 of 361 features "WORKS"), 45% of the backend source can even be executed (I independently recomputed the import closure: 286 of 636 modules, 107,920 of 238,756 LOC reachable from any shipped entrypoint — 75 of ~145 top-level packages have zero reachable module), and the parts that work are almost entirely one layer deep.** There is a genuinely real product buried in here — a working LangGraph agent that plans, calls ~140 real MCP tools across 22 stdio subprocesses, enforces a real human-in-the-loop approval interrupt, checkpoints to SQLite, and returns an honest error when it has no API key — plus a real multi-tenant identity/metering/quota system with hashed API keys and Fernet-encrypted per-tenant secrets, plus correct Stripe/PagerDuty/Slack webhook HMAC verification.

**But a paying customer could not use this today for AI-assisted incident response**, for four independent reasons, each of which alone is fatal: (a) the shipped React UI cannot authenticate at all — `frontend/src/api.ts` sends a *build-time* `VITE_API_TOKEN` that neither the Dockerfile nor `render.yaml` ever sets, and the login flow that would supply a real token is broken at step one (`/auth/{provider}/login` returns JSON, so clicking "Sign in with GitHub" renders raw JSON in the browser); (b) the only conversational transport the UI uses, `POST /chat/stream`, **permanently bricks the thread on turn 2** with `anyio.ClosedResourceError` and the thread then 409s on every transport for the life of the process; (c) the one WebSocket route in the app crashes on every connect — I reproduced `TypeError: require_auth() missing 1 required positional argument: 'request'` — and (d) in the multi-tenant mode that makes this a SaaS, **there is no tenant isolation on any of the 13 feature routers**: org A reads, patches and deletes org B's incidents, runbooks, knowledge base and audit trail. Everything the package names promise — RCA, postmortems, dedup, similar-incident search, SSO/SAML, RBAC, compliance, cost optimization, predictive detection — is unreachable code. What ships is a well-produced demo: I confirmed `app/incidents/store.py` line 1 literally reads `"""In-memory incident store for demo purposes."""` and re-seeds three fake incidents with fresh UUIDs on every process start.

---

## 2. Systemic patterns

I ranked these by blast radius, with the number of the 20 audited domains in which each appears.

### P1. The `*_advanced` twin-package pattern — 14 of 20 domains, ~112,000 dead LOC
The single dominant structural failure. Nearly every feature exists **two or three times**: `rca` + `rca_advanced`; `postmortem` + `postmortem_advanced`; `sso` + `sso_advanced`; `predictive` + `predictive_advanced` + `predictive_scaling`; `cost_tracking` + `cost_optimization` + `cost_optimizer`; `analytics` + `analytics_advanced`; `router` + `model_router` + `llm_advanced`; `runbook` + `runbooks` + `runbook_builder` + `playbook_marketplace`; `security` + `security_audit` + `security_scanner`; `session.py` + `session_store.py` + `redis_sessions.py`; `secrets_vault` + `secrets_advanced` + `secretsmgr`; `ratelimit` + `ratelimit_advanced`; four separate Slack bots (`slack_bot`, `slack_advanced`, `chatops`, `integrations/slack_bot`, plus a fifth via MCP). **In every single pair, the wired one is the smaller/simpler one and the "advanced" one is dead.** Consequence: the repo advertises ~110 feature packages and ships ~60; every capability claim in a package name is a 404. Worse, several dead twins are *better built* than the live ones — `app/slack_bot/api.py` has real signature verification and a real `views.open` client; `app/session_store.py` is a working distributed session store; `app/integrations_advanced/oauth.py` has correct S256 PKCE. The good code is the dead code.

### P2. Module-level Python dicts as the persistence layer — 15 of 20 domains
Incidents, status-page incidents (a *second*, disjoint incident store), runbooks, runbook executions, knowledge base documents, knowledge graph nodes/edges, SLOs and SLI history, billing subscriptions/invoices/usage, customers/trials/MRR, waitlist entries, OAuth accounts and sessions, war rooms, feature store, verification cache, planning graphs, coordinator executions, model-router budgets. Consequence, in order of severity: **(i)** every customer artifact is destroyed on deploy; **(ii)** with >1 uvicorn worker the product is non-deterministic — a runbook created on worker 1 404s on worker 2, a user's plan flips per request; **(iii)** there is no `org_id` column anywhere in these stores, which is the *mechanical cause* of the cross-tenant leak in P4. Only two live surfaces are genuinely persistent: the tenancy SQLite store (orgs/keys/usage/secrets) and the LangGraph checkpointer.

### P3. Route registered ≠ code wired — 20 of 20 domains
Three distinct sub-shapes, all present: **(a)** router written but never `include_router`'d — the entire `app/api/v1` namespace, 9 modules and 70 routes, is dead (policy engine, digital twin, the *only* real health-check subsystem); **(b)** router registered but its own service layer is not imported by it — `app/runbooks/router.py` never imports `app/runbooks/executor.py`, so `POST /api/runbooks/{id}/execute` returns `steps: []` and executes nothing; **(c)** module registered but the mount is a no-op — `include_graphql_router()` is called *after* the parent router was mounted, which FastAPI silently ignores. Consequence: 18 routers, ~291 routes, and I confirmed **44 of them return HTTP 500 right now** on a well-formed request.

### P4. Auth at the perimeter, nothing behind it — 12 of 20 domains
The app-level `Depends(require_auth)` genuinely covers all 291 routes and uses `hmac.compare_digest`. That is the *only* control. `require_perm` RBAC is attached to 18 routes (~6%). Zero of the 13 feature routers read `tenant_context`. Consequence, verified by the security domain with two live tenants: org A's API key reads/patches/deletes org B's incidents, runbooks and audit events. `app/slo/api_router.py` is worse than "no scoping" — `org_id` is a **client-supplied query parameter**, so `?org_id=<victim>` is the entire exploit. And the perimeter itself has two holes: `/api/slack/*` (10 state-mutating routes) has its HMAC check commented out at `router.py:349-353`, and `app/teams/router.py` parses JWTs **without verifying the signature**, so a forged `header.payload.FAKESIG` deletes another user's team.

### P5. Canned data presented as AI output — 9 of 20 domains
`@omnipotent why is checkout slow?` in Slack → `asyncio.sleep(3)` then a hardcoded "Database connection pool exhaustion, 87% confidence." The onboarding wizard's "run a real investigation" posts to `POST /api/chat`, which does not exist (405), and falls through to the same fabricated RCA with the same 87%. `Postmortem.tsx` labels hardcoded template prose `aiGenerated: true` and its Regenerate button is `setTimeout(2000)`. `/oncall` in Slack returns "Alice Smith / Bob Jones". `/status` returns four fake service latencies — during a real outage it will state checkout is "Degraded, 230ms" whether checkout is fine or on fire. `GET /api/incidents/{id}/investigation` serves hardcoded `safety_gates: [{confidence, passed, 0.87}]` that has zero connection to `app/safety_gates/`. **This is the most reputationally dangerous pattern in the repo**: it is not "unfinished," it is output that is indistinguishable from a working product and wrong at the worst possible moment.

### P6. Silent success on failure (fail-open everywhere) — 11 of 20 domains
`app/spend.py` swallows every Redis exception and returns 0, so a Redis outage **disables the fleet spend cap**. GitHub/GitLab webhook signature checks are skipped when no secret is configured. `_run_triggered_investigation` ends in `except Exception: log.exception(...)`, defeating the job queue's entire retry/dead-letter design (`process_one` only retries when the handler raises). `app/autonomy.py:61` calls `is_page_worthy()` on an error dict, so remediation reports **`status: succeeded` when it cannot observe the target at all**. The coordinator marks investigations `completed` with empty evidence when no telemetry backend is configured. `PolicyEngine` with zero backends returns `allow` while advertising `deny_on_error=True`. `neo4j_backend.connect()` catches all exceptions and silently falls back to empty in-memory results, so blast-radius returns a confident zero.

### P7. Green tests that cannot fail — 20 of 20 domains
~2,600 backend tests, 0 failures, and it means nothing. Only ~4% of test functions issue an HTTP request; only ~11% of live routes are referenced by any test; **all 18 registered routers have zero HTTP-level tests**. 22-25 test files (~500-570 tests) target packages with no entrypoint. Concrete instances: `tests/test_auth_endpoints.py` has 66 passing tests over the OAuth code and catches none of the open redirect, the `dev-secret-key` forgery, or the hardcoded `role="owner"`. `tests/integration/test_slack_integration.py` POSTs to paths that don't exist and passes on an `in [200, 404]` assertion. The one error-budget test asserts `total_budget` and never `consumed`, which is the broken field. The connector tests **skip when a real credential is configured** — configuring the integration disables its test. `evals/run_golden.py` exits 0 when the cassette is missing. `evals/run_redteam.py` scores 13/13 against a corpus written to match the regex list it tests. The CI migration-drift gate greps `alembic/versions/`, a directory that does not exist. The frontend coverage gate reads `coverage-summary.json`, which the configured reporters never emit.

### P8. Frontend/backend contract drift — 6 of 20 domains
6 UI calls hit paths that don't exist; ~230 of 291 live routes have no frontend caller. `Team.tsx` calls `/api/team/members` while the real route is `/api/teams`. `ServiceMap.tsx` calls a nonexistent `/api/kubernetes/services` and then **synthesizes its entire topology client-side** while the live `/api/knowledge-graph/dependencies/{id}` sits unused. Ack/Resolve on incidents always 422 (missing required `user` param) and the error is swallowed by `if (res.ok)`. `RunbookBuilder`'s `isNew` check is `id.startsWith("rb-") && !id.includes("-")` — a logical contradiction, always false, so Save is always PUT, and there is no PUT route (405). CORS allows only `GET, POST, OPTIONS` with no `allow_credentials` (I confirmed this at `app/api/main.py:688`), so every DELETE/PUT the UI issues is blocked by the browser in the documented dev setup.

### P9. Config that is declared and never read — 5 of 20 domains
~74 env vars read via raw `os.environ` are absent from `.env.example`; ~61 `Settings` fields are undocumented; several documented fields are read by nothing (`copilot_retention_days`, `COPILOT_LOG_*` in the k8s ConfigMap). Conversely, `Settings` has **no `prometheus_url`/`loki_url`/`elasticsearch_url` field at all**, which is why the verification agent's `EvidenceCollector` always has zero evidence sources, and why the coordinator's handlers can never be configured. `migrations/env.py` reads `COPILOT_DATABASE_URL` while docker-compose, the k8s Job, the Helm chart and the migration CI workflow all set `DATABASE_URL` — so every scripted `alembic upgrade head` silently targets the SQLite default and dies on `CompileError: JSONB`.

---

## 3. The critical path — shortest ordered list from demo to usable

Ordered so each step unblocks the next. Steps 0–5 are days of work and convert a demo into something a design partner could actually run. **Do not touch anything else until step 9.**

**Phase 0 — Stop shipping money and data leaks (hours, do today)**
0. **Delete the demo checkout branch** (`app/billing/api.py:254-272`). I verified live: unauthenticated `POST /billing/checkout {"plan_name":"scale"}` → 200, and `GET /billing/subscription` then reports the $2,499/mo plan. Return 503 when Stripe is unconfigured. In the same commit remove `PATCH /admin/plan` from the public app (signup → self-grant enterprise is a one-call bypass of the *working* quota system).
1. **Delete `or "dev-secret-key"`** in `app/auth/oauth.py:273` and `:292` and fail closed; extend the production validator to require `COPILOT_SECRET_KEY` whenever any OAuth var is set, and add it to `render.yaml` with `generateValue: true`. The same constant is `app/secrets_vault.py`'s tenant encryption key.
2. **Validate `redirect_uri`** against `settings.allowed_origins` in `oauth_login` and `oauth_callback`, and stop putting the session token in a query string.
3. **Fix the Teams JWT** — replace `_get_user_from_header`'s signature-less parse with `app.tenancy.supabase_auth.verify_jwt`. **Uncomment the Slack HMAC check** (`app/slack_bot/router.py:349-353`), hoist it to a router-level dependency, fail closed when the secret is unset, and guard the `int(timestamp)` against a missing header.

**Phase 1 — Make the core loop survive turn 2 (days)**
4. **Move MCP session ownership into the FastAPI lifespan.** One app-scoped `AsyncExitStack` + shared tool list, built once at startup. This is the single highest-leverage change in the repo: it fixes `/chat/stream` turn-2 `ClosedResourceError`, fixes `/approve/stream`, and eliminates the ~29 seconds of SSE dead air (which is 22 subprocess boots, not model latency). Short-term mitigation if you need it same-day: evict the `CopilotSession` on failure regardless of `existed` at `app/api/main.py:1798`, and never add a thread to `_AWAITING` on error.
5. **Make `require_auth` websocket-safe** — accept `HTTPConnection` (the shared base of `Request` and `WebSocket`), or register the WS router on a sub-app. Verified crash: `TypeError: require_auth() missing 1 required positional argument: 'request'` on every connect, and this is the only WS route in the app.

**Phase 2 — Let a logged-in human reach the product (days)**
6. **Fix login end to end, in this order:** make `GET /auth/{provider}/login` return a 302 instead of a JSON body (the browser currently renders raw JSON — the user never reaches GitHub); read `?token=` first in `AuthCallback.tsx`; replace the module-const `TOKEN` in `api.ts` / `billing.ts` / `useWebSocket.ts` with a getter over `localStorage.session_token` → Supabase `access_token` → `VITE_API_TOKEN`; and route the 13 pages that use bare `fetch` through the shared `authHeaders` helper. Also standardize `API_BASE` — the committed `frontend/dist` bundle hardcodes `http://localhost:8000` in 6 places while every page module uses `""`.
7. **Fix CORS**: `allow_methods=["GET","POST","PUT","PATCH","DELETE","OPTIONS"]`, `allow_credentials=True` with the existing non-wildcard origin list. Add a Vite dev proxy so dev is same-origin.
8. **Add the SPA fallback.** `StaticFiles(html=True)` only serves index.html for *directory* paths — `/dashboard`, `/login` and `/settings/billing` return 404 JSON. Stripe's default `success_url` is `{frontend_url}/settings/billing?success=true`, so a user completing checkout lands on a hard 404. Add react-router at the same time (every page is currently unaddressable and refresh loses state).

**Phase 3 — Make it a multi-tenant SaaS rather than a shared demo (1–2 weeks)**
9. **Add `org_id` to every store and a shared `Depends(tenant_scope)`.** Filter/assert on every read, update and delete in incidents, runbooks, knowledge, SLOs, status, planning, coordinator, knowledge-graph, on-call, audit. Delete the `org_id: str = Query("default")` parameter in `app/slo/api_router.py` — a client-chosen tenant id is not a tenant id.
10. **Replace the module dicts with tables**, in this priority order: incidents → runbooks + executions → billing subscriptions → knowledge base → SLOs → status page. Add the Alembic revisions in the same commits.
11. **Fix the DB layer that already exists**: add a plain `async def get_db_session()` generator alongside the `@asynccontextmanager` `get_session` (fixes ~40 on-call routes, verified `TypeError` at `fastapi/dependencies/utils.py:586`); add `import app.oncall.models` to `migrations/env.py` and generate the 17 missing `oncall_*` tables; make `migrations/env.py` read `DATABASE_URL` as a fallback; use `JSON().with_variant(JSONB, 'postgresql')` so SQLite compiles.
12. **Fix `/api/investigations`** — move `_investigations` out of the strawberry-importing module (verified 500: `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'strawberry'` through `app/api/main.py:2118`) and back it with the `investigations` table, writing a row when an investigation completes.

**Phase 4 — Delete, then test (1 week, and it is the cheapest week here)**
13. **Delete the 75 zero-reachability top-level packages (~112,000 LOC) and their ~500-570 tests.** Keep only: `app/security/headers.py` (register it — the SPA currently ships with no CSP/HSTS/X-Frame-Options), `app/session_store.py` (wire it for OAuth state + a logout denylist), `app/integrations_advanced/oauth.py`'s PKCE, and `app/api/v1/health.py` (register it — but note `setup_health_checks()` and `mark_startup_complete()` are called only from tests, so registering the router alone yields `components: {}` and `started: false`).
14. **Add the 20-line route smoke test**: enumerate `app.routes`, assert no route returns ≥500 for a well-formed request. That one test fails on 44 routes today. Then one authenticated happy-path test per route per registered router (~290 tests) — worth more than the ~1,500 unit tests of internal helpers they'd replace. Raise `--cov-fail-under` to the measured value and remove `continue-on-error` from the frontend and e2e steps (the frontend suite exits 1 today at 0.46% line coverage against a configured 70% threshold).

**Deferred but do not forget:** the four correctness bugs that produce *plausible wrong numbers* — error budget `consumed = max(0, target - SLI)` instead of `1 - SLI` (every healthy SLO reports 100% budget remaining); burn rate inflated by `slo_window/window` (360x, pages on anything); `_score_to_level`'s `stakes > 0.5` when p0 contributes exactly 0.5 (a declared P0 routes to the *balanced* model and a declared P1 to the *fast* one); and the model-router pricing table returning **$0.00 for every model the product actually calls**.

---

## 4. Contradictions and resolutions

**C1 — Is `app/connectors` dead?** *Reachability census said dead (11,950 LOC cluster); its verifier said import-reachable.* **Resolution: the verifier is right on imports, the auditor is right on the verdict.** `app/coordinator/__init__.py:26` → `decision_integration` → `app.digital_twin` → `infrastructure_clone` → `app.connectors`, and the coordinator router *is* registered; `app/policy_engine/criteria.py:705` also imports `app.connectors.prometheus`. But the only instantiators of `InfrastructureCloner`/`ChaosEngine` live in the unrouted `app/api/v1/digital_twin.py`, so the code is imported and never called. **This distinction matters for the census methodology**: import-reachability over-counts. My own closure (286/636 modules, 45.2% LOC) sits between the two estimates; all three agree the dead fraction is 53–55%, and the disagreement is ±3 points of accounting, not of substance.

**C2 — Does `/metrics/slo` work?** *SLO domain said WORKS; its verifier overturned to PARTIAL.* **Resolution: verifier confirmed, and it's worse than PARTIAL.** I grepped it myself: `_record_slo` has exactly **one** call site, `app/api/main.py:1613`, inside non-streaming `POST /chat`, guarded by `status == "completed"`. So `/chat/stream` (the only path the UI uses), `/approve`, and the PagerDuty-triggered path record nothing, and `copilot_investigations_failed_total` is **structurally always zero** — the most important agent-reliability signal is a hardcoded 0.

**C3 — Is the incident API unauthenticated?** *Incidents domain: "ZERO AUTH on the entire incident surface." Knowledge domain verifier: "the no-auth evidence is wrong — `app.router.dependencies` is `[Depends(require_auth)]`."* **Resolution: the knowledge verifier is right about the mechanism, the incidents auditor is right about the outcome.** The app-level dependency covers all 291 routes but is a **no-op when `COPILOT_API_TOKEN` is unset**, which is the documented local-dev default and (per the security audit) the effective state of the deployed SPA, which can't send a token anyway. The correct framing: not "no auth," but "one shared gate token, no per-user identity, and no tenant scoping behind it" — which for a SaaS is the worse problem, because it means *authenticated* tenants read each other's data.

**C4 — Is `frontend/node_modules` installed?** *Orchestrator env facts said NO; the frontend-build verifier said YES and ran the suite.* **Resolution: the verifier is right — I confirmed 420 entries present.** This matters because it makes the frontend test failure a *live, reproducible* fact rather than an inference: `npm test` exits 1 (0.46% lines vs a 70% threshold) and CI hides it with `continue-on-error: true`.

**C5 — Is `app/runbooks/executor.py` CLI-only?** *Automation auditor said CLI-only; its verifier said it's in the live agent path.* **Resolution: verifier confirmed and this is the more alarming reading.** `app/mcp/client.py:22-27` includes `"runbooks"` in `_SERVERS`, so `execute_runbook` is an agent tool reachable from live `POST /chat`. Combined with the security verifier's finding that `_exec_command` returns `f"Command executed: {step.action}"` and never calls subprocess: **the agent is told remediations succeeded when nothing happened.** Two mitigations point the same way — that executor must either become real behind a sandbox, or return `"simulated": true` so the model and UI cannot mistake it.

**C6 — Is the auto-investigation path BROKEN or STUB?** *Slack auditor said BROKEN (500s); verifier said STUB (the 500 was a sandbox network artifact).* **Resolution: verifier confirmed — STUB.** With httpx patched it returns 200 and emits 5 real outbound Slack calls. The defect is the canned RCA, not a crash. Important because it changes the fix from "handle errors" to "connect it to the real orchestrator."

**C7 — Is `SafetyGateOrchestrator` evidence real?** The verification domain's verifier showed the auditor's quoted call **raises TypeError** on the real signature, and the correct call returns something *worse* than reported: `AUTO_APPROVED, requires_human_approval=False` for a production rollback of `payments`, with a fabricated `"✓ Tests: 5/5 passed"`. **Resolution: verdict survives, evidence replaced.** Flagging it because at least one auditor quoted output it did not produce — treat unreplicated quotes in these reports as unverified.

---

## 5. What is genuinely good

I want to be fair: there is real engineering here, and it is concentrated in a small number of files that a rewrite should preserve verbatim.

- **The agent core (`app/graph/`, `app/session.py`, `app/mcp/`).** A real LangGraph state machine (plan → agent → tools → reflect → report → verify) binding ~140 tools from 22 real MCP stdio subprocesses, executing genuine multi-step tool-calling loops, persisting to a real checkpointer, and enforcing a real `interrupt()`-based human approval gate. It returns an honest error with no API key — **there is no canned fake response anywhere in the agent loop**, which is more than can be said for the Slack and onboarding surfaces layered on top of it. Independently verified by a scripted-LLM harness driving the real graph, real subprocesses and the real checkpointer.
- **`app/policy.py` + `app/guardrails.py`.** The safety layer that is actually on the hot path: a graduated approve/notify/allow tool gate and an evidence-confidence gate wired at `app/graph/edges.py:29`, plus prompt-injection provenance boxing wired into every `ToolMessage` at `app/graph/builder.py:91`. Pure, deterministic, unit-tested, and genuinely invoked. Includes a real Unicode-confusables normalizer. *(Caveat: the allowlist is incomplete — 27 mutating MCP tools, including `execute_runbook`, classify as `allow`.)*
- **`app/tenancy/` — the only real identity system.** Verified end to end by two independent auditors: signup writes rows, `dcp_` keys are SHA-256-hashed with expiry and revocation, a viewer key correctly 403s on `/admin/api-keys` and `/chat`, Supabase RS256 JWTs are verified against a real JWKS with alg-confusion and expiry correctly rejected, and SCIM writes real user + membership rows. Keep this and delete `app/auth/`, `app/sso/`, `app/sso_advanced/`, `app/rbac/`.
- **`app/metering.py` + `quota_gate`.** Genuinely persistent, genuinely idempotent (duplicate `event_key` verified to not double-count), and genuinely enforcing — reproduced: 60 investigations on a free plan → `POST /chat` returns **402 "Monthly investigation quota reached."** This is the one billing-adjacent thing in the repo that actually works.
- **Webhook signature verification in `app/api/main.py`.** Stripe, PagerDuty and Slack are all correct: constant-time comparison, replay windows, and — verified with *valid* signatures, not just bad ones — a valid Stripe event actually persists the plan change to SQLite. The PagerDuty path is the one end-to-end flow in the product that genuinely works: signed webhook → dedupe → real agent investigation.
- **`app/secrets_vault.py`.** Real Fernet envelope encryption per tenant; the SQLite row contains only `gAAAAAB...` ciphertext, roundtrip verified, and tenant B cannot read tenant A's integration secrets.
- **The DAG orchestration primitives** (`app/planning/execution_engine.py`, `app/coordinator/dag_executor.py`). Topological ordering, parallel fan-out, output propagation to dependents, cycle rejection, retry, and an approval gate that genuinely blocks on an `asyncio.Future` until resolved — all verified by direct execution. The orchestration is real; only the handlers behind it are empty.
- **`app/incident_memory.py`** — working BM25 ranking over a real corpus, actually consumed by the live agent at `app/graph/nodes.py:107`. This is the one "similar incidents" implementation of four that works and is wired.
- **The frontend toolchain and backend static analysis.** `npm ci` clean, `tsc -b` zero errors, `eslint` zero errors, `vite build` in 5.6s, `ruff check app tests evals` passes, **`mypy app` succeeds on 636 files**, `uv.lock` in sync, single alembic head rendering valid PostgreSQL DDL. The scaffolding is professional; it is pointed at the wrong things.
- **Cold-start behavior.** The app boots with zero environment variables and serves `/healthz` and `/readyz` 200, and the production `model_validator` genuinely refuses to boot an unauthenticated API. Good instincts, correctly implemented.

---

## 6. Risk register

Ranked by expected loss. **R1–R4 are live today in the default configuration.**

| # | Risk | Class | Evidence | Impact |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **R1** | **Free provisioning of the $2,499/mo plan by an unauthenticated POST.** | Revenue | I reproduced it: `POST /billing/checkout {"plan_name":"scale"}` with no auth header → 200, `session_id: demo_session_00000000-...-0000_scale`, and `GET /billing/subscription` then reports the scale plan. The all-zero UUID means **one caller's free upgrade upgrades every user** of a single-tenant deploy. Enshrined by a passing test. | 100% revenue loss on the upgrade path |
| **R2** | **Cross-tenant data breach across all 13 feature routers.** | Security | Two live tenants: org A's key reads, PATCHes and DELETEs org B's incidents and runbooks, and reads org B's audit trail (including key ids, actor ids, org ids). `/api/slos` takes the tenant as a **query parameter**. No store has an `org_id` column. | Total loss of tenant confidentiality; unshippable as SaaS |
| **R3** | **Forgeable owner sessions via `"dev-secret-key"`.** | Security | `app/auth/oauth.py:273/292` — confirmed present. `render.yaml` (the production deploy) never sets `COPILOT_SECRET_KEY`, and the guard only fires when multi-tenant AND production, so a production single-tenant deploy signs with the constant. A forged `{'sub':'attacker','role':'owner'}` returned `/auth/me → 200 role=owner`. Same value is the tenant secret encryption key. | Full account takeover + secrets vault compromise |
| **R4** | **Signature-less Slack and Teams endpoints.** | Security | HMAC commented out at `app/slack_bot/router.py:349-353`; 10 state-mutating routes. `app/teams/router.py` parses JWTs without verifying signatures — a forged `header.payload.FAKESIG` created, read and DELETED another user's team. | Anyone with the URL forges incident acks, escalations, war rooms; team data destruction |
| **R5** | **Total data loss on every deploy.** | Data loss | 15 domains store customer data in module-level dicts; `app/incidents/store.py` line 1 says "for demo purposes" and re-seeds fake incidents with new UUIDs (I got a different UUID than every prior auditor). No `incidents`, `runbooks`, `knowledge_documents`, `slo`, `status_*`, `subscriptions` or `waitlist` table exists. | Every incident, runbook, article, SLO, subscription and lead lost on restart |
| **R6** | **Migrations have never run correctly in any automated path.** | Data loss | `migrations/env.py` reads `COPILOT_DATABASE_URL`; docker-compose, the k8s Job, the Helm chart and `database-migrations.yml` all set `DATABASE_URL` → silent fallback to SQLite → `CompileError: JSONB`. 17 `oncall_*` tables have no migration and alembic cannot see them. The drift-detection CI step greps a directory that does not exist. | Schema-less production DB; drift is undetectable by design |
| **R7** | **Fabricated safety/remediation success.** | Operational | `app/autonomy.py:61` reports `status: succeeded` when the target is unobservable; `app/runbooks/executor.py` returns `f"Command executed: {action}"` and is in the live agent tool path; `SafetyGateOrchestrator` AUTO_APPROVES a production `payments` rollback with a fabricated "5/5 tests passed"; `GET /api/incidents/{id}/investigation` serves hardcoded `passed` safety gates. | An operator trusts a remediation that never happened, during an incident |
| **R8** | **Fabricated telemetry during an incident.** | Operational | Slack `/status` returns four fake latencies; `/oncall` returns "Alice Smith / Bob Jones"; `uptime_30d` is a literal `100.0` (`record_uptime()` has zero callers); SLOTracker renders `budget_total: 43.2` and a 0.5 burn rate from nowhere; burn-rate alerting fires "critical" at 0.5x true burn; error budget always reports 100% remaining. | Wrong paging decisions, wrong severity calls, invalidated SLA reporting |
| **R9** | **The core product loop is single-turn.** | Revenue (churn) | `/chat/stream` turn 2 → `ClosedResourceError`, thread permanently 409s on every transport until process restart; `/ws/chat/*` 500s on every connect (reproduced). Zero test coverage on all three transports (`grep` for `chat/stream`, `approve/stream`, `websocket_connect` in `tests/` returns nothing). | A paying user gets exactly one streamed investigation per thread, then a generic error forever |
| **R10** | **Escalation never reaches a human.** | Operational | `EscalationService` writes DB rows marked `"sent"` with no delivery provider and no timer to advance past step 0; `_send_alert_notification` is unwired. DST handling leaves a literal 1-hour coverage hole twice a year; accepting a shift swap never reassigns the shift. All moot today since 40 of 45 `/oncall/*` routes 500. | A paging product that does not page |
| **R11** | **Metered usage never reaches Stripe.** | Revenue | `sync_usage_to_stripe` exists twice and is called by nothing outside tests; a test run left 62 unsynced usage rows. `stripe` appears **zero times** in `pyproject.toml` and zero times in `uv.lock` — not even transitively resolvable. `POST /billing/track` and `GET /billing/usage` read a meter nothing writes, while real usage goes to a separate ledger. | Metered billing invoices $0 |
| **R12** | **Compliance artifacts that cannot be trusted.** | Legal/compliance | `/audit/verify` reports "tampered" on any healthy deployment past 2,000 events or any restart (`prev_hash` seeds from `""`); the audit ring buffer is volatile, per-worker and cross-tenant readable; `app/governance.py` (GDPR erasure, retention) has zero call sites; `copilot_retention_days` is read by nothing; the entire `app/compliance/` package (3,257 LOC) is unreachable. | SOC2/GDPR claims with no implementation and a self-refuting integrity check |
| **R13** | **SSRF from any authenticated user, incl. viewers.** | Security | `POST /api/knowledge-graph/sync/configure` + `/sync/once` fetch attacker-chosen URLs from inside the deployment network — the security verifier stood up a listener and **confirmed the server actually fetched it**. No scheme/IP-range validation. | Internal network reconnaissance, cloud metadata access |
| **R14** | **No security response headers.** | Security | `app/security/headers.py` exists, is written, and is registered nowhere. The SPA is served same-origin from `frontend/dist` with no CSP, HSTS, X-Frame-Options or X-Content-Type-Options. Compounded by `GithubConnect.tsx` storing a long-lived GitHub PAT in JS-readable storage. | Clickjacking, XSS token theft, PAT exfiltration |

---

### Closing judgment

This is not a product with bugs; it is a **demo with a production-shaped shell around it**, plus about 112,000 lines of code that was written, unit-tested, and never connected to anything. The good news is unusually good: the hardest part — a real agent that orchestrates real tools with a real approval gate — exists and works, and the identity/metering/webhook substrate underneath it is correctly built. The path forward is mostly **deletion and connection, not invention**: delete the 75 dead packages and their ~550 tests, connect the ~10 things that are one wire away (MCP lifespan, the session dependency, the SPA token getter, `include_router` on v1 health), and add `org_id` plus real tables to the 15 stores that are currently Python dicts. Phases 0–2 above are roughly two weeks and convert "unusable" into "a design partner could run this." Phase 3 is the difference between a single-tenant tool and a SaaS. Everything else can wait — and most of it should be deleted rather than waited on.

---

# Appendix — Every assessed feature, by domain


## Auth, OAuth, SSO, SCIM, sessions

**Summary.** There are two entirely separate identity systems in this repo and only one of them is real. The multi-tenant system (app/tenancy/: aiosqlite store, `dcp_` API keys, Supabase JWT verification, a ranked role matrix enforced by `require_perm`) is genuinely implemented and I verified it end-to-end — signup writes rows, keys resolve, a viewer key gets 403 on `/admin/api-keys` and `/chat`, SCIM writes real user+membership rows, and Supabase RS256 JWTs are verified against a JWKS with alg-confusion and expiry correctly rejected. The OAuth system (app/auth/) is a parallel, self-contained toy: it completes a real OAuth round trip but persists identity in module-level dicts, hands every user `role="owner"`, invents org IDs by string-mangling the email domain, and issues an HS256 JWT that is rejected by `require_auth` — so "Sign in with GitHub" logs you in to `/auth/me` and nothing else. The single biggest problem is that the user-facing login story is broken end to end: the SPA's `/auth/callback` page only handles Supabase and silently discards the `?token=` the backend redirects with, and `frontend/src/api.ts` only ever sends a build-time `VITE_API_TOKEN`, never the logged-in user's token. On top of that I confirmed three exploitable defects: an unvalidated `redirect_uri` that exfiltrates the session token to any attacker domain, a `"dev-secret-key"` signing fallback that let me forge an owner session, and `/ws/chat/{thread_id}` crashing on every connect with `TypeError: require_auth() missing 1 required positional argument: 'request'`. Roughly 4,500 lines under app/sso/, app/sso_advanced/, app/rbac/, app/session_store.py, app/redis_sessions.py and app/security/ have no entrypoint at all.

**Wiring.** LIVE: `app/auth/endpoints.py` router is registered at app/api/main.py:709-710 (/auth/providers, /auth/{provider}/login, /auth/{provider}/callback, /auth/me, /auth/accounts, /auth/{provider}/link, /auth/logout). `/auth/callback` is a separate handler in app/api/main.py:776 that just serves the SPA index.html. `/me` (main.py:1073), `/signup` (main.py:2732), `/scim/v2/Users` (main.py:2658) and `/scim/v2/Users/{email}` (main.py:2693) are defined inline in main.py. The real auth gate is `require_auth` at main.py:417, attached app-wide via `dependencies=[Depends(require_auth)]` at main.py:671; it delegates to app/tenancy/auth.py (`resolve` for `dcp_` keys, `resolve_jwt` -> app/tenancy/supabase_auth.py for JWTs) backed by app/tenancy/store.py (aiosqlite). RBAC is `require_perm` (main.py:474) over `app/tenancy/models.py:_PERMISSIONS`. NOT WIRED (no import from any live path, confirmed by `grep -rn "app.sso|app.rbac|app.security|session_store" --include="*.py" app/`): all of app/sso/ (saml.py, abac.py, multi_idp.py, emergency_bypass.py, session_intelligence.py — imported only by tests/test_enterprise_features.py); all of app/sso_advanced/ (scim.py, group_mapping.py, audit.py, session_management.py — imported by NOTHING, not even tests); all of app/rbac/ (imported only by tests/test_advanced_features.py); app/session_store.py and app/redis_sessions.py (imported only by tests/test_scaling.py and by app/security/session_security.py, which is itself dead); all of app/security/ including websocket_auth.py. app/session.py IS live (main.py:54) but is the agent/LLM session, not an auth session.


### `BROKEN` — Sign in with Google/GitHub (end-to-end, in the product UI)

- **Surface:** frontend/src/pages/Login.tsx -> GET /auth/{provider}/login -> GET /auth/{provider}/callback -> SPA /auth/callback
- **Severity:** critical · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** The backend redirects the browser to `{redirect_uri}?token={session_token}` (app/auth/endpoints.py:204-207). Login.tsx:102-103 sets `redirect_uri = window.location.origin + '/auth/callback'`. frontend/src/App.tsx:37 routes that path to `AuthCallback`, NOT to `Login`. AuthCallback.tsx:16-50 only calls `checkSupabaseConfigured()` then `supabase.auth.getSession()` — it never reads `params.get('token')`. The only code that reads `?token=` is Login.tsx:78-86, which is never mounted at /auth/callback. So the user lands on 'Supabase is not configured' or 'Authentication failed. Please try again.' and the token is dropped. Even if the token were stored, frontend/src/api.ts:7 sets `const TOKEN = import.meta.env.VITE_API_TOKEN ?? ""` and authHeaders() (api.ts:9-11) sends only that build-time value; `grep -rn "session_token" frontend/src` shows it is written at Login.tsx:82 and read at Login.tsx:95 and nowhere else — it is never attached to any API request. I also confirmed on the backend that the OAuth token is useless anyway: with COPILOT_MULTI_TENANT=true, `create_session_token(...)` then `GET /me` -> 401 and `POST /chat` -> 401, while `GET /auth/me` -> 200.
- **Blocker:** The SPA callback route handles only Supabase sessions and discards the backend's ?token=; and the API client never sends a user session token, so even a stored token authenticates nothing.
- **Fix:** In AuthCallback.tsx, read `?token=` first and persist it before falling through to Supabase; then replace the module-const TOKEN in api.ts/billing.ts/useWebSocket.ts with a getter that returns localStorage `session_token` or the Supabase `session.access_token`, falling back to VITE_API_TOKEN.

### `BROKEN` — WebSocket chat session (/ws/chat/{thread_id})

- **Surface:** WS /ws/chat/{thread_id} (app/api/websocket.py:208)
- **Severity:** critical · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** Every connection attempt raises before the handler runs. Ran: `c.websocket_connect('/ws/chat/t1', subprotocols=['copilot-v1'])` against a default-env TestClient -> `TypeError: require_auth() missing 1 required positional argument: 'request'`, raised in fastapi/dependencies/utils.py solve_dependencies. Cause: app/api/main.py:671 attaches `dependencies=[Depends(require_auth)]` at the FastAPI() level, which FastAPI also applies to websocket routes, but `require_auth(request: Request)` (main.py:417) cannot be resolved in a websocket scope. The comment at main.py:696-697 asserts 'WebSocket connections bypass the auth dependency (handled in the WS endpoint)' — that is false. Reproduced identically with COPILOT_MULTI_TENANT=true and with defaults. No test covers it: `grep -rln websocket_connect tests/` hits only test_scaling.py and test_observability_advanced.py, neither of which exercises this route.
- **Blocker:** App-level require_auth dependency is unresolvable for websocket scopes, so the route 500s/closes on connect 100% of the time.
- **Fix:** Change the signature to `async def require_auth(request: Request = None, websocket: WebSocket = None)` and no-op for websocket scopes, or register the ws router on a sub-application without the global dependency. Then fill in app/api/websocket.py:245 (`TODO: Validate token against tenant auth if multi-tenant`) — today presence of any non-empty subprotocol token is accepted.

### `BROKEN` — OAuth session token issuance and /auth/me

- **Surface:** GET /auth/me, GET /auth/accounts (app/auth/endpoints.py:238, :266)
- **Severity:** critical · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** app/auth/oauth.py:272-273 — `secret = s.copilot_secret_key or "dev-secret-key"`. app/config.py:169 defaults `copilot_secret_key: str = ""`, and the startup validator (config.py:488-497) only requires it when multi_tenant AND production. I forged a token with the literal fallback secret against a default-config app: `jwt.encode({'sub':'attacker','org_id':'org_corp_com','email':'ceo@corp.com','role':'owner','type':'session',...}, 'dev-secret-key', 'HS256')` then `GET /auth/me` -> 200 `{'id': 'attacker', 'email': 'ceo@corp.com', 'org_id': 'org_corp_com', 'role': 'owner'}`. Separately, app/auth/endpoints.py:200 hardcodes `role="owner"` for every OAuth login regardless of who logs in, and _get_or_create_org_for_user (endpoints.py:222-225) fabricates `org_id` as `f"org_{domain.replace('.','_')}"` from the email domain — it never touches the tenant store, so `org_acme_com` is not a real org row.
- **Blocker:** Session JWTs are signed with a publicly-known constant when COPILOT_SECRET_KEY is unset, and every OAuth user is granted role=owner in a fabricated org.
- **Fix:** Remove the `or "dev-secret-key"` fallback and fail closed; extend the config validator to require COPILOT_SECRET_KEY whenever any OAuth provider env var is set. Derive role and org_id from a real membership lookup (`store.get_membership_by_email`) instead of hardcoding owner and string-mangling the domain.
- **Verifier:** Confirmed. app/auth/oauth.py:273 and :292 both read `s.copilot_secret_key or "dev-secret-key"`; config.py:169 defaults it to "" and the validator at config.py:488-497 only requires it under multi_tenant AND production — so a production SINGLE-tenant deploy also uses the constant (auditor understated this). I forged jwt.encode({'sub':'attacker','org_id':'org_corp_com','email':'ceo@corp.com','role':'owner','type':'session','exp':2000000000},'dev-secret-key','HS256') and GET /auth/me returned 200 {'id':'attacker','email':'ceo@corp.com','role':'owner'}. Live GitHub login produced role='owner' and org_id='org_corp_com' string-mangled from the email domain (endpoints.py:200, :222-225) with zero tenant-store involvement. Same key is also the secrets_vault encryption key (app/secrets_vault.py:31).

### `BROKEN` — OAuth redirect_uri handling (open redirect / token exfiltration)

- **Surface:** GET /auth/{provider}/login?redirect_uri=..., GET /auth/{provider}/callback
- **Severity:** critical · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** `redirect_uri` is taken raw from the query string (app/auth/endpoints.py:105, :140) and echoed into `RedirectResponse(f"{redirect_uri}?token={session_token}")` at endpoints.py:204-205 with no allowlist anywhere in the file. Executed with a mocked GitHub transport: `GET /auth/github/login?redirect_uri=https://evil.example.com/steal` then the callback with the returned state produced `Location: https://evil.example.com/steal?token=eyJhbGciOiJIUzI1NiJ9...` containing a valid 24h owner session JWT for victim@corp.com. Secondary issue: the same value is spliced into the provider redirect_uri (`.../auth/github/callback?redirect_uri=...`), which Google rejects as redirect_uri_mismatch since Google requires exact registered-URI matching — so the Google path is likely unusable in production even after the security fix.
- **Blocker:** Any attacker-supplied absolute URL receives the freshly minted session token in a query parameter.
- **Fix:** Validate redirect_uri against `settings.allowed_origins` (or a dedicated allowlist) in both oauth_login and oauth_callback; reject absolute URLs to unknown hosts. Carry the post-login destination in server-side state keyed by the OAuth `state` value instead of appending it to the provider redirect_uri.
- **Verifier:** Reproduced end-to-end with an httpx.MockTransport IdP. GET /auth/github/login?redirect_uri=https://evil.example.com/steal then the callback with the returned state gave: 307, Location: https://evil.example.com/steal?token=eyJ... which decodes with 'dev-secret-key' to {'sub':'user_e450760722ace29e','org_id':'org_corp_com','email':'victim@corp.com','role':'owner','exp':<+24h>,'type':'session'}. No allowlist exists anywhere in app/auth/endpoints.py (values flow raw from :105 and :140 into the RedirectResponse at :204-205). The same raw value is also spliced into the provider redirect_uri, which I saw in the generated authorize URL.

### `BROKEN` — Link an additional OAuth provider to an existing account

- **Surface:** POST /auth/{provider}/link (app/auth/endpoints.py:291)
- **Severity:** medium · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** The link endpoint builds a callback URL with `?link=true` (endpoints.py:313), but `oauth_callback` (endpoints.py:133-141) has no `link` parameter and never reads it; its only join key is provider_id, then email (endpoints.py:181-190). Executed: logged in via GitHub as work@corp.com -> user_0fac76194d0a3a54, providers ['github']; called `POST /auth/google/link` with that bearer -> 200 with a Google authorize URL; completed the Google callback returning personal@gmail.com -> new identity user_0ce6c1c175e0cd71, providers ['google']. Same user? False. The caller's authenticated user_id is discarded entirely.
- **Blocker:** The link callback ignores the authenticated user and creates a second, unrelated account whenever the second provider returns a different email.
- **Fix:** Persist the initiating user_id alongside the OAuth state in generate_oauth_state, and in oauth_callback, when the state carries a link intent, call link_oauth_account(existing_user_id, oauth_user) instead of minting a new user id.
- **Verifier:** Reproduced. Logged in via GitHub as work@corp.com -> id user_7612216d6c6a846e, providers ['github']. POST /auth/google/link with that bearer -> 200 with redirect_uri=.../auth/google/callback?link=true. Completing the Google callback (personal@gmail.com) produced id user_b65f95c3287ef380, providers ['google'], org_gmail_com. same user? False. Confirmed by reading oauth_callback (endpoints.py:133-141): its signature has provider/request/code/state/error/redirect_uri — no `link` parameter at all, so ?link=true is inert and the authenticated caller's user_id is never consulted. Extra defect found: because _extract_token reads the cookie before the header, GET /auth/accounts with user A's bearer returned user B's Google account.

### `STUB` — OAuth account persistence / linked identities

- **Surface:** app/auth/oauth.py link_oauth_account / find_account_by_provider / get_user_accounts; surfaced via /auth/me and /auth/accounts
- **Severity:** high · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** app/auth/oauth.py:322-323 — `_OAUTH_ACCOUNTS: dict[str, OAuthAccount] = {}` and `_USER_ACCOUNTS: dict[str, list[str]] = {}`. link_oauth_account (oauth.py:326-352) writes only to those dicts; there is no SQLAlchemy model, no aiosqlite table, and no migration for oauth accounts (`grep -n 'CREATE TABLE' app/tenancy/store.py` shows orgs/users/memberships/api_keys/integration_secrets/usage only — no oauth_accounts). Consequences I observed live: after a successful GitHub login, `/auth/me` returned providers ['github'] and an avatar_url; on process restart that record is gone, so the next login mints a brand-new `user_{secrets.token_hex(8)}` id (endpoints.py:190). Nothing is ever written to the real tenant DB by the OAuth path.
- **Blocker:** 'Persistence' is two module-level dicts: user IDs are not stable across restarts and are not shared across workers, and no OAuth user ever becomes a row in the tenant store.
- **Fix:** Add an oauth_accounts table to app/tenancy/store.py (user_id, provider, provider_id, email, avatar, timestamps) and have the callback create/lookup a real `users` row + membership via `tenant_auth.get_store()`, replacing the dicts.
- **Verifier:** Read link_oauth_account (oauth.py:326-352) to its return: it writes only _OAUTH_ACCOUNTS[account_id] and appends to _USER_ACCOUNTS[user_id], both module-level dicts declared at oauth.py:322-323. find_account_by_provider/_by_email/get_user_accounts (oauth.py:355-372) read only those dicts. No SQLAlchemy model, no migration; sqlite inspection of the live tenant DB shows tables ['orgs','users','memberships','api_keys','integration_secrets','usage'] — no oauth_accounts. In each fresh process my GitHub login minted a brand-new user_<token_hex(8)> id (user_e450760722ace29e, user_7612216d6c6a846e, user_0af1278371beb3e6 across three runs with identical provider_id=1), proving IDs are not stable.

### `DEAD_CODE` — SAML 2.0 SSO

- **Surface:** app/sso/saml.py (SAMLService, SAMLProvider) — no route, no import from live code
- **Severity:** high · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** `grep -rn "app.sso" --include="*.py" .` outside app/sso/ matches only tests/test_enterprise_features.py:10-28. There is no APIRouter in the package, no ACS endpoint in the 292 live routes (no /saml, no /sso path exists), and no include_router. Worse, the code is unsafe if it were ever wired: app/sso/saml.py:367-371 `_verify_signature` is `if not config.want_response_signed and not config.want_assertions_signed: return True` followed by `return True` — every branch returns True, with a docstring admitting 'placeholder - requires xmlsec library in production'. IdP config and pending AuthnRequests are process dicts (saml.py:192-193).
- **Blocker:** No entrypoint at all; and the assertion signature check is an unconditional `return True`, so wiring it as-is would accept forged SAML assertions.
- **Fix:** Either delete the package or, before exposing it, implement real signature verification with python3-saml/xmlsec, persist IdP config, and register an ACS route behind require_auth.

### `DEAD_CODE` — Enterprise SSO suite: SCIM provider, group mapping, session management, SSO audit

- **Surface:** app/sso_advanced/ (scim.py, group_mapping.py, audit.py, session_management.py) — 2,137 lines
- **Severity:** medium · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** `grep -rn "sso_advanced" --include="*.py" .` returns matches ONLY inside app/sso_advanced/ itself (its own __init__.py re-exports and its own loggers). Nothing in app/ imports it, and unlike app/sso/ and app/rbac/ not even a test file touches it. It also duplicates the live SCIM feature with a fully in-memory backend: app/sso_advanced/scim.py:177-181 `self._users: dict[str, dict[str, SCIMUser]] = {}`, `self._groups`, `self._events`, `self._bearer_tokens` — plus a module singleton `get_scim_provider()` at scim.py:631.
- **Blocker:** Zero entrypoints and zero test coverage; a second, in-memory SCIM implementation competing with the real one in main.py.
- **Fix:** Delete it, or fold the genuinely missing pieces (Groups, filtering, provisioning events) into the live main.py SCIM handlers backed by app/tenancy/store.py.

### `DEAD_CODE` — Distributed / Redis session store

- **Surface:** app/session_store.py (DistributedSessionStore), app/redis_sessions.py (RedisSessionStore), app/security/session_security.py (SecureSessionStore)
- **Severity:** medium · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** The Redis code itself is genuine (app/session_store.py:83-110 does real `setex`/`sadd`/`expire` against app/redis_pool), but nothing calls it. `grep -rn "session_store|redis_sessions" --include="*.py" app/` outside those files hits only app/security/session_security.py:145 and app/security/__init__.py — and `grep -rln "app.security" --include="*.py" .` outside app/security/ hits only app/security_audit, app/security_scanner and test files, none of which are reachable from app.api.main. Meanwhile the auth paths that actually run keep state in process dicts: `_OAUTH_STATES` (app/auth/oauth.py:109), `_OAUTH_ACCOUNTS`/`_USER_ACCOUNTS` (oauth.py:322-323), and the signup rate limiter `_RL: dict[str, tuple[float,int]]` (app/api/main.py:237, whose own comment says 'single-instance only').
- **Blocker:** The working Redis session infrastructure has no entrypoint, so all live session/CSRF/rate-limit state is per-process and breaks under horizontal scaling.
- **Fix:** Wire DistributedSessionStore (or app/redis_sessions.py) into app/auth/oauth.py for OAuth state and into the logout denylist, and back the signup rate limiter with the same Redis pool.
- **Verifier:** Confirmed. grep -rn 'session_store|redis_sessions' --include=*.py excluding the two files themselves hits only app/security/__init__.py:97,179 and app/security/session_security.py:145 — and app/security is itself unreachable from app.api.main (18 include_router calls, none of them security). Meanwhile the state that actually runs is per-process: _OAUTH_STATES (oauth.py:109), _OAUTH_ACCOUNTS/_USER_ACCOUNTS (oauth.py:322-323), and _RL at main.py:237 whose own comment says 'single-instance only'.

### `DEAD_CODE` — RBAC library (app/rbac/)

- **Surface:** app/rbac/permissions.py, manager.py, middleware.py (require_permission) — 944 lines
- **Severity:** low · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** `grep -rln "app.rbac" --include="*.py" .` -> app/rbac/__init__.py, app/rbac/manager.py, app/rbac/middleware.py, tests/test_advanced_features.py. No live module imports it. The actual enforcement in production is the unrelated `require_perm` (app/api/main.py:474) over the flat matrix in app/tenancy/models.py:17-27. So there are two independent permission models, and the fine-grained ResourceType/Action one (with `require_permission` at middleware.py:104) is never called by any route.
- **Blocker:** Never imported by live code; only tests keep it green, which makes it look covered.
- **Fix:** Delete it, or migrate require_perm to it if resource-scoped permissions are actually wanted — but do not leave two matrices in the tree.
- **Verifier:** Confirmed. grep for app.rbac outside app/rbac/ hits only tests/test_advanced_features.py (several lazy imports inside test bodies). grep for require_permission across app/ excluding app/rbac|app/sso|app/sso_advanced returns nothing. The live enforcement path is the unrelated require_perm at main.py:474 over app/tenancy/models.py, which I verified working separately — so there really are two disjoint permission models and the fine-grained one is never invoked by any route.

### `PARTIAL` — OAuth authorization-code round trip (state/CSRF, token exchange, userinfo)

- **Surface:** GET /auth/{provider}/login, GET /auth/{provider}/callback (app/auth/endpoints.py:101, :133)
- **Severity:** high · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** I ran the full flow against a MockTransport IdP (real handler code, only the network faked): `/auth/providers` -> both providers after setting GITHUB_OAUTH_CLIENT_ID/SECRET; `/auth/github/login` -> 200 with a real GitHub authorize URL incl. a 43-char state; callback with that state -> 307 to `/?token=<JWT>` plus `Set-Cookie: session=...`; `/auth/me` -> 200 with the right email/avatar; `/auth/accounts` -> 200 with the linked account. Replaying the same state -> 400 'Invalid or expired state parameter' (state is popped, oauth.py:130). So the mechanics are real: real httpx POST to token_url (oauth.py:180-194), real userinfo parse incl. the GitHub /user/emails fallback (oauth.py:232-247). Gaps: (a) NO PKCE anywhere — grep for code_challenge/code_verifier in app/auth returns nothing; (b) state lives in a process-local dict `_OAUTH_STATES` (oauth.py:109), so with >1 uvicorn worker the callback frequently lands on a worker that never issued the state -> 400; (c) the state is not bound to the browser (no cookie/nonce), only to the provider name.
- **Blocker:** No PKCE; CSRF state is a process-local dict that breaks under multi-worker/multi-pod deployment and is not bound to the user's browser.
- **Fix:** Add PKCE (S256) for both providers, and move _OAUTH_STATES into Redis or a signed, httpOnly state cookie so it survives across workers and is bound to the client.
- **Verifier:** Confirmed the mechanics are real: with GITHUB_OAUTH_CLIENT_ID/SECRET set, /auth/providers returned the descriptor, /auth/github/login produced a real authorize URL with a 43-char state, the callback did a real httpx POST to token_url and a real GET to api.github.com/user, and /auth/me + /auth/accounts returned correct data. State replay -> 400 'Invalid or expired state parameter' (popped at oauth.py:130). Gaps confirmed: grep for code_challenge|code_verifier|pkce across app/ hits ONLY app/integrations_advanced/oauth.py:245-269 (a different, unwired module) — app/auth has none. _OAUTH_STATES is a process-local dict at oauth.py:109 and carries only (provider, timestamp), so it is not bound to the browser and cannot survive multi-worker.

### `PARTIAL` — Logout / session revocation

- **Surface:** POST /auth/logout (app/auth/endpoints.py:228)
- **Severity:** medium · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** The handler is two lines: `response.delete_cookie("session"); return {"status": "logged_out"}` (endpoints.py:234-235) — the docstring claims it 'invalidates the token' but there is no denylist, no jti, no server-side session record. Executed: after `POST /auth/logout` -> 200, `GET /auth/me` with the same bearer token still returned 200 with full user info. The token remains valid for its full 24h (`expires_hours=24`, endpoints.py:201). A token already leaked via the redirect_uri issue therefore cannot be revoked at all.
- **Blocker:** Logout only clears a cookie; the bearer JWT stays valid for 24 hours and there is no revocation mechanism.
- **Fix:** Add a `jti` claim and a Redis/DB denylist checked in verify_session_token, or move to opaque server-side sessions (the already-written app/session_store.py would serve if it were wired up).
- **Verifier:** Confirmed. Handler body is exactly `response.delete_cookie("session"); return {"status":"logged_out"}` (endpoints.py:234-235). Executed: POST /auth/logout -> 200 {'status':'logged_out'}, then GET /auth/me with the SAME bearer -> 200 with full user info. verify_session_token (oauth.py:289-304) only calls jwt.decode and checks type=='session' — no jti, no denylist, no server-side record. expires_hours=24 at endpoints.py:201.

### `PARTIAL` — SCIM 2.0 user provisioning / deprovisioning

- **Surface:** POST /scim/v2/Users (app/api/main.py:2658), PATCH|DELETE /scim/v2/Users/{email} (main.py:2693)
- **Severity:** medium · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** This is real persistence, not a stub. With COPILOT_SCIM_TOKEN=scimtok and COPILOT_SCIM_ORG=org_fixed: `POST /scim/v2/Users {'userName':'alice@acme.com'}` -> 201 with a proper SCIM resource; direct sqlite inspection showed `users = [('69291efac5...', 'alice@acme.com', '2026-08-24T00:23:41Z')]` and `memberships = [('org_fixed','69291efac5...','responder')]`. Re-POST -> 201 and users count stayed 1 (idempotent via get_user_by_email, main.py:2685). `PATCH` with active=false -> `{"deprovisioned":true}` and memberships became `[]`; a second DELETE -> `{"deprovisioned":false}`. Bad token -> 401, missing userName -> 400, SCIM unconfigured (empty COPILOT_SCIM_ORG) -> 403 (_scim_guard, main.py:2644). Audit events fire. Gaps: (1) only POST/PATCH/DELETE exist — there is no `GET /scim/v2/Users?filter=userName eq ...`, no `GET /Users/{id}`, no `/Groups`, and no `/ServiceProviderConfig`, all of which Okta and Azure AD require for reconciliation and group push; (2) `orgs` table was EMPTY in my run — the handler adds a membership to whatever COPILOT_SCIM_ORG says without checking the org exists, and resolve_jwt (app/tenancy/auth.py:95-97) returns None when get_org() misses, so a typo'd org id silently provisions users who can then never authenticate.
- **Blocker:** Read/GET, filtering, Groups and ServiceProviderConfig are unimplemented, so real IdP SCIM connectors cannot reconcile; and COPILOT_SCIM_ORG is never validated against the orgs table.
- **Fix:** Add GET /scim/v2/Users with `filter=userName eq "..."`, GET /scim/v2/Users/{id}, and /ServiceProviderConfig; validate COPILOT_SCIM_ORG against store.get_org() at startup (or in _scim_guard) and 500/refuse to boot on a missing org.
- **Verifier:** Reproduced exactly. With COPILOT_SCIM_TOKEN=scimtok / COPILOT_SCIM_ORG=org_fixed: POST /scim/v2/Users -> 201 with a proper SCIM resource; sqlite3 shows users=[('cf7fe7c9...','alice@acme.com',...)] and a real membership row. Re-POST -> 201, still 1 user (idempotent). PATCH active=false -> {'deprovisioned':True}; memberships table is then empty; second DELETE -> {'deprovisioned':False}. Bad token -> 401, missing userName -> 400. Gaps confirmed by probing rather than grepping: GET /scim/v2/Users -> 404, GET /scim/v2/ServiceProviderConfig -> 404, POST /scim/v2/Groups -> 405. And the orgs table was EMPTY after provisioning — COPILOT_SCIM_ORG is never validated against orgs, so provisioned users can never authenticate (resolve_jwt returns None at auth.py:95-97 when get_org misses).

### `PARTIAL` — Auth-path exemption in the global auth gate

- **Surface:** require_auth (app/api/main.py:424-441), _AUTH_PATH_PREFIXES (main.py:232)
- **Severity:** medium · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** main.py:427 exempts every path starting with `/auth/` from the global bearer gate, which is necessary for /login and /callback but also exempts /auth/me, /auth/accounts and /auth/{provider}/link. Those three do enforce their own token check (endpoints.py:244-250, :269-275, :300-306) so they are not open — verified 401 unauthenticated. The risk is structural: any future route added under the /auth/ prefix is silently unauthenticated by default, and the exemption is prefix-based (`startswith('/auth/')`), so it is one careless mount away from a bypass. Verified /scim/ and /signup are similarly exempted but do enforce their own credentials (401 on bad SCIM token, 403 when SCIM unconfigured, 400/403 when signup is disabled).
- **Blocker:** Blanket prefix exemption means auth on /auth/* routes is opt-in per handler rather than enforced by the framework.
- **Fix:** Narrow _AUTH_PATH_PREFIXES to the exact unauthenticated paths (/auth/providers, /auth/{provider}/login, /auth/{provider}/callback) and give /auth/me, /auth/accounts and /auth/{provider}/link a shared `Depends(current_session_user)` dependency instead of hand-rolled _extract_token calls.
- **Verifier:** Confirmed by reading main.py:424-441 and by probing. _AUTH_PATH_PREFIXES = ('/auth/',) at main.py:232 and require_auth returns early for any path with that prefix (:427). Probed unauthenticated in multi-tenant mode: /auth/me 401, /auth/accounts 401, /auth/google/link 401 (each enforces its own _extract_token+verify_session_token), /auth/providers 200, /me 401, /scim/v2/Users 403 'SCIM provisioning is not configured', POST /auth/logout 200 (no auth needed, but it is a no-op anyway). So nothing is currently open by accident, but the exemption is a startswith prefix and auth on /auth/* is opt-in per handler — the structural risk is real. /auth/callback (main.py:776) also rides this exemption to serve index.html.

### `WORKS` — Self-serve signup + API-key authentication (multi-tenant)

- **Surface:** POST /signup (app/api/main.py:2732), require_auth (main.py:417), app/tenancy/store.py
- **Severity:** low · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** Ran with COPILOT_MULTI_TENANT=true and COPILOT_TENANT_DB pointed at a tmp sqlite file: `POST /signup {'org_name':'Acme','email':'boss@acme.com'}` -> 200 with org_id + `dcp_265bcfa8_866f45c0...` owner key; `GET /me` with no auth -> 401; `GET /me` with the key -> 200 `{'multi_tenant':true,'org':'e3c971...','role':'owner','plan':'free'}`; `GET /me` with `Bearer dcp_bogus` -> 401. Keys are salted-hash stored (app/tenancy/store.py:243 resolve_api_key) in a real aiosqlite schema (store.py:55-88), and audit events fire (`audit event=org.signup`, `audit event=apikey.created` in the log output). Startup created the schema via the lifespan hook ('multi-tenant mode: tenant store ready').
- **Verifier:** Tried to break it and could not. COPILOT_MULTI_TENANT=true + COPILOT_TENANT_DB=/tmp/t2.sqlite: POST /signup -> 200 with dcp_39cf5497_... owner key. Critically, I re-tested persistence the way the instructions demand — I killed the process and started a SECOND python process against the same sqlite file: GET /me with the old key -> 200 with the same org id, and POST /chat passed RBAC (422 on a missing body field, i.e. auth+perm both passed). Direct sqlite3 dump shows real rows in orgs and api_keys (hashed; the plaintext key is not in the DB), plus an encrypted org secret blob. /me with no auth -> 401, with Bearer dcp_bogus -> 401. This is genuine durable storage, not a dict.

### `WORKS` — RBAC enforcement on API routes

- **Surface:** require_perm dependency (app/api/main.py:474) over app/tenancy/models.py:17-27; applied to /admin/*, /chat, /approve, /remediate etc.
- **Severity:** low · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** Issued a viewer key from an owner key (`POST /admin/api-keys {'role':'viewer'}` -> 200) and probed: `GET /admin/api-keys` with the viewer key -> 403 `{"detail":"Role 'viewer' is not permitted to manage api keys."}`; `POST /chat` with the viewer key -> 403 `{"detail":"Role 'viewer' is not permitted to run investigation."}`; the same calls with the owner key -> 200. The matrix is a real ranked table (viewer<responder<admin<owner) in app/tenancy/models.py:13-34 and `can()` is used by require_perm at ~14 route declarations. Caveat, by design: require_perm returns immediately when `not copilot_multi_tenant` (main.py:478-479), so in single-tenant mode there is no role enforcement at all — the shared COPILOT_API_TOKEN is all-or-nothing.
- **Verifier:** Reproduced independently. Minted a viewer key from an owner key (POST /admin/api-keys {'role':'viewer'} -> 200). Viewer: GET /admin/api-keys -> 403 "Role 'viewer' is not permitted to manage api keys."; POST /chat -> 403 "Role 'viewer' is not permitted to run investigation." Owner: same calls -> 200 / 422-on-body (so the perm gate passed). Note the 403 fires BEFORE body validation, which means it is a real dependency, not a handler-level afterthought. Read require_perm at main.py:474-486 over tn_models.can(); the documented single-tenant no-op at :478-479 is real but is a design caveat, not a defect.

### `WORKS` — SSO login via Supabase-issued OIDC/JWT

- **Surface:** require_auth JWT branch (app/api/main.py:452-456) -> app/tenancy/auth.py:resolve_jwt -> app/tenancy/supabase_auth.py
- **Severity:** low · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** Generated a real RSA keypair, published it as a JWKS, injected a fake PyJWKClient, and probed the live app: `decode_with_jwks(tok, jwks, audience='authenticated')` returned the claims; an HS256 token with the same `kid` was rejected with `InvalidAlgorithmError` (alg-confusion defence at supabase_auth.py:21 `_ALGORITHMS = ['RS256','ES256']`); `GET /me` with a valid RS256 token for a signed-up owner -> 200 with the correct org/role; a token for a non-member email -> 401 (auth.py:92 'authenticated, but not a member'); an expired token -> 401 with ExpiredSignatureError logged. Signature, expiry, audience and membership are all genuinely enforced.
- **Fix:** Requires SUPABASE_JWKS_URL to be set (config.py:249 defaults to ""), in which case verify_jwt returns None and every JWT is a 401 — that is a config requirement, not a bug.
- **Verifier:** Rebuilt the whole harness myself: generated a 2048-bit RSA key, built a JWKS from it, injected a fake PyJWKClient, signed up an org, and probed the LIVE app. Results: valid RS256 token for the member email -> GET /me 200 with correct org/role; non-member email -> 401 (auth.py:91-93); expired -> 401 with ExpiredSignatureError; wrong audience -> 401 with InvalidAudienceError; HS256 token reusing the same kid -> 401 with InvalidAlgorithmError; alg=none -> 401. decode_with_jwks also rejected the HS256 token directly. supabase_auth.py:21 pins _ALGORITHMS=['RS256','ES256']. Only nit (not enough to downgrade): no `iss` claim check, and verify_aud collapses to False if SUPABASE_JWT_AUD is blanked (it defaults to 'authenticated', config.py:250).

### `WORKS` — Identity endpoints: /me and /auth/providers

- **Surface:** GET /me (app/api/main.py:1073), GET /auth/providers (app/auth/endpoints.py:91)
- **Severity:** low · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** `GET /auth/providers` returned `[]` with no OAuth env vars and, after setting GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID/SECRET and GITHUB_OAUTH_CLIENT_ID/SECRET and clearing the OAUTH_CONFIGS cache, returned both provider descriptors — it reflects real configuration (app/auth/oauth.py:58-90), it is not hardcoded. `GET /me` returned `{'multi_tenant': false, 'authenticated': true, 'org': null, 'role': 'owner', 'label': 'Operator'}` in single-tenant mode and the real org/role/plan from the tenant store when called with a `dcp_` key or a valid Supabase JWT. `/auth/providers` is explicitly in _OPEN_PATHS (main.py:230) so the Login page can probe it before auth.
- **Verifier:** Verified both directions. GET /auth/providers -> 200 [] with no OAuth env vars; with GITHUB_OAUTH_CLIENT_ID/SECRET set in a fresh process -> 200 [{'id':'github','name':'GitHub',...}] — so it genuinely reflects _load_oauth_configs (oauth.py:58-90), not a hardcoded list. GET /me returned the correct real org/role/plan for a dcp_ key AND for a valid Supabase RS256 JWT, and 401 for no credential / bogus key in multi-tenant mode. /auth/providers is in _OPEN_PATHS (main.py:230, verified by reading). Minor caveat, not enough to downgrade: OAUTH_CONFIGS is a module cache only refreshed when falsy (oauth.py:96, :104), so adding OAuth env vars requires a restart.

**Verifier notes for this domain.** I regenerated all evidence rather than reading the auditor's. Every one of the 18 verdicts survived; I overturned none, but I found the auditor UNDERSTATED several items.

MATERIAL THINGS THE AUDITOR MISSED:

1. The "Sign in with GitHub" flow breaks one step EARLIER than reported. `GET /auth/{provider}/login` is declared with `-> LoginResponse` and returns **JSON 200** (`content-type: application/json`, body `{"redirect_url":"https://github.com/login/oauth/authorize?..."}`), NOT a 302. Login.tsx:103 does `window.location.href = ${API_BASE}/auth/${provider}/login?redirect_uri=...`, so the browser full-page-navigates and renders raw JSON. The user never even reaches GitHub. Verified live: `login status 200 / ctype application/json`. The dropped `?token=` at /auth/callback is a SECOND, downstream break.

2. `copilot_secret_key` is not just an OAuth JWT signing key — app/secrets_vault.py:31 uses the same value as the per-tenant encryption key. And the production guard (config.py:488-497) only fires when multi_tenant AND production, so a **production single-tenant** deploy boots happily with `copilot_secret_key=""` and signs OAuth session JWTs with the literal `"dev-secret-key"`. I forged `{'sub':'attacker','role':'owner',...}` with that constant and got `/auth/me -> 200 {'id':'attacker','email':'ceo@corp.com','role':'owner'}`.

3. Cross-provider account takeover via unverified email: oauth_callback falls back to `find_account_by_email(oauth_user.email)` (endpoints.py:184-186) as a join key, and the GitHub path takes `data.get("email")` from the *public profile* field, which GitHub does not guarantee is verified. Anyone who sets their public GitHub email to a victim's address inherits the victim's user_id.

4. `_extract_token` (endpoints.py:325-332) reads the **cookie first**, so an explicit `Authorization: Bearer <token>` is silently overridden by a stale `session` cookie. I hit this live: after completing a second OAuth login in the same client, `GET /auth/accounts` with user A's bearer token returned user B's linked Google account.

5. tests/test_auth_endpoints.py has **66 passing tests** over exactly this code and catches none of: the open redirect, the dev-secret-key fallback, role="owner" hardcoding, or the broken link flow. This is a "green tests = false comfort" case.

6. `/ws/chat/{thread_id}` is the ONLY websocket route in the app (confirmed by enumerating APIWebSocketRoute), so the TypeError kills 100% of websocket functionality, not just chat.

7. PKCE does exist in the repo (app/integrations_advanced/oauth.py:245-269, S256) — but that is a different, unwired module; app/auth/ has zero PKCE. So the PARTIAL gap stands and the working implementation is dead code.

COMMANDS RUN: full mocked-IdP OAuth round trip (login -> callback -> /auth/me -> /auth/accounts -> replay -> logout -> reuse -> forge); multi-tenant signup + viewer-key RBAC probes; cross-process API-key persistence against /tmp/t2.sqlite; SCIM POST/re-POST/PATCH/DELETE with direct sqlite3 inspection of orgs/users/memberships; real RSA keypair + injected PyJWKClient for the Supabase JWT path (valid / non-member / expired / wrong-aud / HS256-confusion / alg=none); websocket_connect; grep-based reachability for app/sso, app/sso_advanced, app/rbac, app/session_store.py, app/redis_sessions.py; inspection of the prebuilt frontend/dist bundle (it matches src — only two `session_token` occurrences).


## Chat / AI agent core / streaming / approvals

**Summary.** The core agent is real, not a facade: `/chat` builds a genuine LangGraph state machine (plan → agent → tools → reflect → report → verify), binds ~150 tools loaded from 22 live MCP stdio subprocesses, executes real multi-step tool-calling loops, persists every turn to a SQLite checkpointer, and enforces a real human-in-the-loop interrupt for write actions. With no API key it returns a clean, honest error ("The LLM provider rejected the API key") — there is no canned fake response anywhere in the loop. Guardrails and PII redaction are genuinely wired into the tool node and I found their provenance-boxed output inside persisted checkpoint state. The single biggest problem is that the transport the frontend actually uses is broken: `/chat/stream` dies on the second turn of any conversation that needs a tool (anyio ClosedResourceError — the MCP session is torn down when the SSE generator task ends, but the CopilotSession stays cached), and `/ws/chat/{thread_id}` returns HTTP 500 on every handshake because the app-level `Depends(require_auth)` takes a `Request` that FastAPI cannot supply in a WebSocket scope. Neither has a single test that actually connects. A paying user today can run exactly one streamed investigation per thread; after that the UI shows "Something went wrong while running the agent" forever, and they must fall back to the non-streaming `/chat`, which does work end-to-end.

**Wiring.** LIVE and reachable: POST /chat, POST /chat/stream, POST /approve, POST /approve/stream, POST /remediate, POST /reset, POST /model/configure, POST /feedback, GET /config, GET /frontend-config, WS /ws/chat/{thread_id} (route registered but handshake 500s). All are defined directly on `app` in app/api/main.py (lines 1495/1529/1629/1738/1845/2153/2899) plus app/api/websocket.py:207 included at app/api/main.py:698. Live support modules on the import graph: app/session.py, app/graph/{builder,nodes,edges,prompts,state,sandbox}.py, app/llm.py, app/replay.py, app/routing.py, app/policy.py, app/guardrails.py, app/redaction.py, app/autonomy.py, app/mcp/client.py, app/incident_memory.py. NOT wired (no import from any live module, no router registration, no CLI): app/api/v1/* (chat.py re-exports main's handlers but nothing includes the router — /api/v1/chat and /v1/chat both 404), app/prompt_registry.py (only tests/test_prompt_registry.py imports it; nodes.py imports prompts directly from app.graph.prompts), app/router/{adaptive,classifier,metrics}.py (only tests/test_router.py), app/llm_advanced/{model_router,semantic_cache}.py (only tests/test_roadmap_features.py). app/agents/* is reachable only via app/graph/builder.py:200 when COPILOT_MULTI_AGENT_ENABLED=true (off by default) and that path is broken. app/coordinator/ IS registered at /api/coordinator (different domain). Note: app/model_router/ (live, /api/model-router/*) is a different package from the dead app/router/ and app/llm_advanced/.


### `BROKEN` — Continue a streamed conversation (2nd+ turn on same thread_id)

- **Surface:** POST /chat/stream (app/api/main.py:1738) — the frontend's primary path (frontend/src/api.ts:89)
- **Severity:** critical · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** Reproduced under REAL uvicorn (not TestClient) on 127.0.0.1:8777 with a scripted model that always calls search_logs. Turn 1: `curl -N -X POST /chat/stream -d '{"thread_id":"live1","message":"q1"}'` -> full SSE stream ending in `data: {"type":"done","status":"completed",...}`. Turn 2, same thread_id -> `data: {"type":"error","thread_id":"live1","status":"error","answer":"Something went wrong while running the agent. Please try again."}`. Server log: `ERROR omnipotent.api :: chat stream failed (thread=live1)` / `anyio.ClosedResourceError`. Full traceback (captured by monkeypatching _friendly_error) goes app/api/main.py:1899 -> app/session.py:150 graph.astream -> app/graph/builder.py:80 guarded tool node -> langgraph ToolNode -> MCP stdio stream -> ClosedResourceError.
