### `PARTIAL` — iCal export + subscription URL

- **Surface:** GET /oncall/schedules/{id}/ical, GET /oncall/schedules/{id}/ical-url
- **Severity:** medium · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** Uses the real `icalendar` library (app/oncall/schedules.py:570-701), not string templating. Generated output is valid RFC 5545: BEGIN:VCALENDAR / VERSION:2.0 / PRODID:-//OmniPotent//On-Call Schedule//EN / CALSCALE / METHOD:PUBLISH / X-WR-CALNAME, VEVENT with UID, DTSTART/DTEND (UTC 'Z' form when times are aware), DTSTAMP, SUMMARY, CATEGORIES, folded DESCRIPTION, and two VALARMs (TRIGGER:-PT1H, TRIGGER:-P1D). Served with media_type text/calendar. Two real defects: (1) DTSTART/DTEND are emitted as floating local times (`DTSTART:20260302T090000`, no Z, no TZID) whenever the stored datetime is naive — calendar clients will interpret those in the viewer's own timezone; (2) ical-url returns a hardcoded relative path `/api/v1/oncall/schedules/{id}/ical` (api_router.py:~1480) which does not match the live route prefix `/oncall/...` and is not an absolute URL, so pasting it into Google/Apple Calendar cannot work. No auth token is attached either, despite CalendarExporter.get_feed_url supporting one.
- **Blocker:** The advertised subscription URL is wrong (wrong prefix, relative, no token); DTSTART lacks TZID/Z when timestamps are naive.
- **Fix:** Return an absolute URL built from settings.public_base_url + the real router prefix, include a signed feed token, and always store/emit tz-aware UTC datetimes.
- **Verifier:** Generated the feed myself. Output is genuine RFC 5545 from the icalendar library with VEVENT/UID/DTSTAMP(Z)/CATEGORIES/folded DESCRIPTION and two VALARMs (TRIGGER:-PT1H, TRIGGER:-P1D), served as text/calendar. Both defects reproduced: DTSTART:20260601T090000 and DTEND:20260608T090000 carry no Z and no TZID (floating local time) when the stored value is naive; GET /ical-url returns subscription_url "/api/v1/oncall/schedules/{id}/ical" (hardcoded at api_router.py:1506 `base_url = "/api/v1/oncall"`), and I confirmed GET /api/v1/oncall/schedules/{id}/ical -> 404 while the live prefix is /oncall. Relative, wrong prefix, no auth token. NEW minor: SUMMARY is "On-Call: Primary (Primary)" — the on-call person's name never appears in the event, so the calendar can't tell you who is on call.

### `PARTIAL` — Workload / fairness analysis

- **Surface:** GET /oncall/schedules/{id}/workload-analysis (ScheduleAnalyzer.analyze_workload)
- **Severity:** medium · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** Real computation, no hardcoding: _calculate_special_hours (app/oncall/schedules.py:953-975) walks the shift hour-by-hour classifying weekend (weekday>=5) and night (hour>=22 or <6). Executed it: for a 168h weekly shift it returned weekend_hours=48.0 and night_hours=56.0, which are arithmetically correct, plus deviation_from_average and a fairness_score. Two accuracy problems: (a) weekend/night are computed on the raw stored timestamp (UTC), not the schedule's timezone, so an Asia/Tokyo or America/Los_Angeles team gets wrong 'night' hours; (b) the SQL window uses `OncallShift.end_time <= end_date+1` (api_router.py:1523-1528), which drops shifts that merely overlap the window — in my run 2 of 6 generated shifts were excluded, producing a bogus fairness_score of 50.0 and labelling one user 'overloaded' on a perfectly even 3-way rotation.
- **Blocker:** Fairness verdicts are distorted by window-clipping and by computing night/weekend in UTC instead of schedule timezone.
- **Fix:** Change the query to overlap semantics (start_time < window_end AND end_time > window_start) and pro-rate partial shifts; convert to the schedule timezone before the weekend/night classification.
- **Verifier:** Ran GET /schedules/{id}/workload-analysis?start_date=2026-06-01&end_date=2026-06-30 -> 200 with fairness_score 63.6, total_hours 1848.0, per-user weekend_hours 240.0 / night_hours 280.0 for 5 x 168h shifts (48 weekend + 56 night per shift, arithmetically exact). So the computation is real, not hardcoded. Both accuracy defects confirmed by reading source: schedules.py:952-974 _calculate_special_hours classifies weekday()>=5 and hour>=22 or <6 on the raw stored timestamp (UTC on Postgres), never converting to the schedule timezone; api_router.py:1550-1551 filters end_time <= end_date+1 so overlapping shifts are dropped (my 2026-06-29 -> 07-06 shifts were excluded), and hours are not clipped to the window either, so a shift extending past the boundary would be counted in full. One user was labelled "overloaded" (+36.4% deviation) on what should be an even 3-way rotation.

### `PARTIAL` — AI on-call intelligence (route-alert, find-coverage, alert-fatigue, workload)

- **Surface:** POST /oncall/ai/route-alert, GET /oncall/ai/find-coverage, GET /oncall/ai/teams/{id}/alert-fatigue, GET /oncall/ai/teams/{id}/workload
- **Severity:** medium · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** Read app/oncall/ai_oncall_service.py end to end (668 lines). There is no LLM, no random(), and no canned dict — it is deterministic rule-based scoring over real SQL: _calculate_routing_score (lines 114-165) sums skills-overlap (max 40), 24h alert-count workload bucket, historical avg ack latency bucket, working-hours availability, and resolution success rate; detect_alert_fatigue (284-377) computes real ack_rate, avg response time and per-source counts and thresholds them into a 0-100 fatigue_score; find_optimal_coverage (193-278) filters on approved PTO and scores by recent on-call hours and days-since-on-call. Executed against the patched session: `/oncall/ai/route-alert -> 200 {"recommended_user_id":..., "should_reroute":false, "candidates":[{"score":72.0,"breakdown":{"skills":20.0,"workload":15.0,"response_time":20.0,"availability":7.0,"success_rate":10}}]}`; `/ai/find-coverage -> 200` with scores 85.0/70.0 derived from actual shift hours; `/ai/teams/{id}/alert-fatigue -> 200 {"fatigue_detected":false,"reason":"Insufficient data (less than 5 alerts)"}`. The 'AI' label is marketing for hand-tuned constants, and route-alert's recommendation is advisory only — nothing consumes it (create_alert never calls route_alert).
- **Blocker:** Not AI and not actuated: the routing recommendation is never applied to alert delivery, and the scoring weights are arbitrary hardcoded constants with no calibration.
- **Fix:** Either rename to heuristic scoring and have create_alert/start_escalation consult route_alert when should_reroute is true, or back the weights with measured data.
- **Verifier:** Confirmed no AI: `grep -n 'random|openai|anthropic|llm' app/oncall/ai_oncall_service.py` returns nothing; it is deterministic rule scoring over real SQL. Live behind the session fix (note the real params are alert_id and schedule_id, not the body shapes the auditor implied): POST /oncall/ai/route-alert?alert_id=... -> 200 with per-candidate breakdown {skills:20.0, workload:20.0, response_time:10.0, availability:7.0, success_rate:7.0, total:64.0}; identical repeated calls returned byte-identical output (deterministic: True). GET /ai/teams/{id}/alert-fatigue -> 200 {"fatigue_detected":false,"alert_count":1,"reason":"Insufficient data (less than 5 alerts)"}; GET /ai/teams/{id}/workload -> 200 with a real 30-day window. Not actuated: `grep -rn route_alert app` shows the only caller is its own endpoint at api_router.py:764; create_alert never consults it. NEW DEFECT the auditor got wrong: GET /oncall/ai/find-coverage returned 500 in my harness, traced to ai_oncall_service.py:662 `datetime.now(timezone.utc) - last_shift.end_time` -> TypeError: can't subtract offset-naive and offset-aware datetimes. The auditor reported this route as 200.

### `PARTIAL` — Timezone utilities (list, convert, view-in-timezone, dst-transitions) _(verdict overturned from WORKS on verification)_

- **Surface:** GET /oncall/timezones, POST /oncall/timezones/convert, GET /schedules/{id}/view-in-timezone, GET /schedules/{id}/dst-transitions
- **Severity:** low · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** TimezoneManager (app/oncall/schedules.py:166-326) uses real zoneinfo, not offset arithmetic. Live: `GET /oncall/timezones -> 200` with common list + aliases + all 500+ IANA zones. `POST /oncall/timezones/convert?time_str=2026-11-01T01:30:00&from_tz=America/New_York&to_tz=UTC -> 200 {"converted":{"datetime":"2026-11-01T05:30:00+00:00"}}` (correct EDT-side resolution of the ambiguous fall-back hour). get_dst_transitions over 2026 for America/New_York returned exactly two entries: 2026-03-08T03:00:00-04:00 spring_forward (+1.0h) and 2026-11-01T02:00:00-05:00 fall_back (-1.0h). With the session patched, `GET /schedules/{id}/dst-transitions -> 200` with those transitions plus the warning string, and `GET /schedules/{id}/view-in-timezone?timezone=Asia/Tokyo -> 200` correctly rendering 09:00 UTC as '2026-03-02 18:00 JST'. Caveat: the two schedule-scoped routes are still 500 in the shipped app because of the session dependency bug.
- **Verifier:** OVERTURNED. Conversion and listing are genuinely correct — I stress-tested 6 cases: 598 IANA zones + 16 aliases returned; NY 2026-11-01T01:30 (ambiguous) -> 05:30Z; Kolkata 12:00 -> 2026-06-14T23:30-07:00 LA (correct); Sydney 12:00 AEDT -> 01:00Z; bad zone -> 400; bad datetime -> 400. BUT get_dst_transitions is WRONG for any zone whose standard offset is UTC+0. schedules.py:315 computes offset_change only `if prev_offset and new_offset`, and timedelta(0) is falsy, so for Europe/London both 2026 transitions come back with offset_change_hours=0, from_offset/to_offset=null, and BOTH typed "fall_back" — the March 29 spring-forward is mislabeled. Reproduced through the live API: GET /oncall/schedules/{london_id}/dst-transitions?start_date=2026-01-01&end_date=2026-12-31 -> two "fall_back" entries. Affects Europe/London, Europe/Dublin, Europe/Lisbon, Atlantic/Reykjavik, Africa/Casablanca. This is the same DST-detection surface the product uses to warn about handoff risk, so it is not cosmetic. America/New_York and Australia/Sydney are correct. Also: the two schedule-scoped routes are 500 in the shipped app.

**Verifier notes for this domain.** WHAT I RE-RAN (all commands executed fresh, not read from the auditor's report):

1. Route inventory: `python3 -c "from app.api.main import app; ..."` -> 44 paths under /oncall/* plus POST /api/slack/commands/oncall = 45. DB-free routes are exactly 4 (GET /oncall/timezones, POST /oncall/timezones/convert, POST /oncall/rotation-templates, POST /oncall/rotation-templates/{id}/preview), so 40 (not 41) DB-backed. Immaterial miscount.
2. Unpatched TestClient sweep over 39 routes: Counter({500: 35, 200: 3, 422: 1}). The 422 was my own bad payload; every DB-backed route 500s with `TypeError: '_AsyncGeneratorContextManager' object is not an async iterator` raised in fastapi/dependencies/utils.py:586 `_solve_generator`. Root cause reproduced: app/oncall/api_router.py:23 imports app/database/engine.py:115 `get_session`, which is `@asynccontextmanager`.
3. Migration check: migrations/versions/ contains only 001_initial_schema.py with 10 `create_table` calls, zero `oncall`. Base.metadata registers 17 oncall_* tables of 27 total. `Base.metadata.create_all` on SQLite -> `CompileError (organizations.settings): SQLiteTypeCompiler can't render element of type JSONB`.
4. I built my own harness at /tmp/oc/harness.py (`@compiles(JSONB,"sqlite")`, `@compiles(UUID,"sqlite")`, a real async-generator session injected via `app.dependency_overrides`) and drove the full flow end to end against a real SQLite file.

MATERIAL THINGS THE AUDITOR MISSED:

A. TimezoneManager.get_dst_transitions is WRONG for every zone whose standard offset is UTC+0 (Europe/London, Europe/Dublin, Europe/Lisbon, Atlantic/Reykjavik, Africa/Casablanca...). app/oncall/schedules.py:315 guards with `if prev_offset and new_offset`, and `timedelta(0)` is falsy, so offset_change collapses to 0 and line 322 `"spring_forward" if offset_change > 0 else "fall_back"` mislabels the March transition. Verified live through the API: `GET /oncall/schedules/{london_id}/dst-transitions?start_date=2026-01-01&end_date=2026-12-31` -> `[{"datetime":"2026-03-29T02:00:00+01:00","from_offset":null,"to_offset":"1:00:00","offset_change_hours":0,"type":"fall_back"}, {"datetime":"2026-10-25T02:00:00+00:00","from_offset":"1:00:00","to_offset":null,"offset_change_hours":0,"type":"fall_back"}]`. Two fall_backs in one year, both offsets null. This is why I overturned the WORKS verdict to PARTIAL.

B. /oncall/schedules/{id}/gaps/analyze invents FALSE critical gaps even when it does run. api_router.py:1176 filters `OncallShift.end_time <= end_date+1`, so a shift that starts inside the window but ends after it is dropped. My DB contained shifts 2026-06-29T09:00 -> 2026-07-06T09:00, yet the endpoint reported `GAP 2026-06-29T09:00 -> 2026-07-01T00:00  39.0 critical`. It also emitted the same 9-hour gap twice (duplicate shifts are never merged), inflating summary to total_gaps=3 / total_gap_hours=57.0.

C. AIOncallService.find_optimal_coverage crashes at app/oncall/ai_oncall_service.py:662 `datetime.now(timezone.utc) - last_shift.end_time` -> `TypeError: can't subtract offset-naive and offset-aware datetimes`. So GET /oncall/ai/find-coverage returned 500 in my patched harness (the auditor reported it returning 200 with scores 85.0/70.0). Same aware/naive class of bug as the gaps endpoints.

D. Frontend contract mismatch, independent of the session bug. GET /oncall/teams/{id}/current-oncall returns `{primary, secondary, shadows, schedules}`, but frontend/src/pages/OnCall.tsx:600 and :615 read `currentOncall?.shifts` and `currentOncall?.participants`. Both are always undefined, so even after the dependency bug is fixed the page renders zero shifts and zero participants.

E. ZERO test coverage: `grep -rln "oncall/teams|oncall/schedules|api_router" tests/` returns nothing. Not one test exercises app/oncall/api_router.py (1772 LOC) or app/oncall/schedule_service.py. The only oncall tests target the dead in-memory modules.

F. Slack signature verification is implemented (`verify_slack_signature`, app/slack_bot/router.py:27-41) but every call site is commented out (router.py:349-353), so ALL Slack command routes are unauthenticated, not just /oncall.

G. iCal VEVENT SUMMARY is `On-Call: Primary (Primary)` — it never contains the on-call person's name or email, so a subscribed calendar cannot tell you who is on call.

H. app/oncall/api_router.py:676-680 schedules `start_escalation` via BackgroundTasks closing over the request-scoped `db`. On FastAPI 0.135.2 yield-dependency teardown runs before background tasks, so in production the escalation task would run against a torn-down session.

CONFIRMED VERBATIM REPRODUCTIONS: DST fall-back gap (`2026-10-31T09:00:00-04:00 -> 2026-11-01T09:00:00-04:00` then `2026-11-01T09:00:00-05:00`, gap=1.0h with nobody on call) and spring-forward overlap (gap=-1.0h). Swap accept leaving `user AFTER a42408df... *** UNCHANGED ***`. Escalation notifications `[('push','sent',0,...),('sms','sent',0,...)]` with `current_escalation_step` frozen at 0. `POST /oncall/rotation-templates` twice with different pattern_type both returning `template_id: template_weekly`. `/api/v1/oncall/schedules/{id}/ical` (the advertised subscription_url) -> 404.


## Billing, Stripe, subscriptions, metering, usage limits

**Summary.** There are THREE parallel, mutually disconnected billing systems in this repo, and only one of them is real. (1) `app/billing/` (the big one, 4,576 LOC, exposed at /billing/*) is an in-memory simulation: no DB table, no migration, no Stripe SDK, no price IDs, and it hands out any paid plan for free. (2) `app/tenancy/` + `app/metering.py` is genuinely real — persistent SQLite ledger, idempotent usage recording, and an actually-enforced `quota_gate` 402 on the investigation endpoints, fed by a properly HMAC-verified `POST /webhooks/stripe`. (3) `app/billing/subscriptions.py` + `app/tasks/tasks.py` are a third implementation wired to nothing. The single biggest problem is that `POST /billing/checkout` silently grants the $2,499/mo "scale" plan with zero payment (verified live: 200 + subscription becomes `scale`), and this behavior is enshrined by a passing test. Worse, even the real quota system can be defeated for free: I executed `POST /signup` → `PATCH /admin/plan {"plan":"enterprise"}` and got unlimited investigations, no payment, no owner verification. A user could "use" the billing UI today, but they would never be charged and would get every paid feature for free; nothing in this domain can collect money.

**Wiring.** LIVE and reachable: all 22 `/billing/*` routes via `app/api/billing_router.py` (registered at app/api/main.py:702), plus `POST /webhooks/stripe` (app/api/main.py:3086, a hand-written tenancy handler), `GET /usage` (app/api/main.py:3025ish, tenancy meter), `PATCH /admin/plan` (app/api/main.py:2604), and the `/customers/*` family (app/api/main.py:3544-3720). All inherit the app-level `Depends(require_auth)` (app/api/main.py:671), which is a no-op when COPILOT_API_TOKEN is unset. NOT WIRED / no entrypoint: `app/billing/subscriptions.py` (SubscriptionManager, PLANS, Plan — 492 LOC, imported only by `app/billing/__init__.py` re-export); `app/billing/webhooks.py:sync_usage_to_stripe` and `get_client`/`_LiveStripeClient` (nothing calls them — grep for `unsynced_usage`/`mark_synced` hits only the store and this function); `app/tasks/tasks.py:sync_usage_to_stripe` (dramatiq actor; nothing outside app/tasks imports app.tasks); `app/billing/stripe_integration.py:655 verify_stripe_signature` (shadowed by the `app/billing/webhooks.py` import in `__init__.py`); `frontend/src/components/billing/FeatureGate.tsx` (zero `<FeatureGate` usages); `frontend/src/api/billing.ts:trackUsage`/`getUpgradeOptions`/`checkUsageLimit` (zero .tsx callers). Critically, NOTHING outside `app/api/billing_router.py` imports `app.billing` at all — verified by grep — so `FeatureGateService`, `UsageTracker`, and `check_usage_limit` are never invoked by any product code path.


### `BROKEN` — Upgrade to a paid plan / collect payment (Stripe Checkout)

- **Surface:** POST /billing/checkout — frontend/src/pages/Billing.tsx:91 and frontend/src/components/landing/Pricing.tsx:423
- **Severity:** critical · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** app/billing/api.py:254-272 — `if not self._stripe:` writes an ACTIVE Subscription straight into the store and returns the caller-supplied success_url as `checkout_url`. Executed live: `c.post('/billing/checkout', json={'plan_name':'scale','billing_cycle':'annual'})` → `200 {"checkout_url":"/?upgrade=success","session_id":"demo_session_00000000-...-0000_scale"}`; the very next `GET /billing/subscription` returns `plan.name="scale"`, `monthly_price=2499.0`, and `GET /billing/usage` returns `ai_investigations.unlimited=true`. Frontend then does `window.location.href = result.checkout_url` (Billing.tsx:98), i.e. it bounces back to the same page with ?success=true and the UI shows a completed upgrade. This is NOT gated on a dev flag — the only condition is `self._stripe is None`, and `_get_stripe_service()` (app/api/billing_router.py:37-51) returns None whenever `import stripe` fails. `stripe` is not installed and is not declared in pyproject.toml at all (grep: "NO STRIPE DEP DECLARED"), so demo mode is the production default. Re-ran with STRIPE_API_KEY=sk_test_fake set: still `CHECKOUT_WITH_KEY: 200 {"checkout_url":"/?upgrade=success"...}` and SUB=business. tests/test_billing_api.py:379-385 asserts this exact behavior, so the test suite locks the bug in.
- **Blocker:** Any caller can obtain the highest-priced plan, permanently, with no payment and no Stripe involvement. With COPILOT_API_TOKEN unset (the documented local-dev default) it is also completely unauthenticated (verified: 200 with no Authorization header).
- **Fix:** Delete the demo branch in app/billing/api.py:254-272. When Stripe is unconfigured, return HTTP 503 "billing not configured" instead of provisioning a subscription. A subscription must only ever be created from a verified `checkout.session.completed` / `customer.subscription.created` webhook, never from an unauthenticated client POST.

### `BROKEN` — Self-grant unlimited plan via signup + admin plan change

- **Surface:** POST /signup then PATCH /admin/plan (app/api/main.py:2604-2635)
- **Severity:** critical · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** Executed end-to-end in multi-tenant mode with a fresh SQLite tenant DB:
  SIGNUP: 200 {"org_id":"5060bbcd...","plan":"free","role":"owner","api_key":"dcp_12d5c14c_..."}
  PLAN_PATCH: {"plan":"enterprise"}
  ORG_AFTER: {"plan":"enterprise"}
  USAGE: {"plan":"enterprise","investigations_quota":-1,"investigations_remaining":-1}
`/signup` is auth-exempt by design (app/api/main.py:440) and always provisions `free` — but it also hands the caller the OWNER role, and `PATCH /admin/plan` only requires `require_perm("manage_billing")` which owner satisfies (app/tenancy/models.py:24). The handler (app/api/main.py:2622-2635) calls `store.set_plan` directly with no payment/subscription check. This bypasses the only genuinely-enforced quota in the product (`quota_gate`, app/api/main.py:545-551).
- **Blocker:** Anyone who can reach /signup gets unlimited AI investigations for free in one extra HTTP call. Payment is never consulted.
- **Fix:** Remove `PATCH /admin/plan` from the public app or restrict it to an internal/service-token audience (like `_scim_guard`). Plan escalation must be driven only by the verified Stripe webhook path; keep an admin override behind a separate operator credential, and audit it.

### `BROKEN` — Real Stripe SDK integration (customers, sessions, subscriptions, invoices)

- **Surface:** app/billing/stripe_integration.py:StripeBillingService, constructed in app/api/billing_router.py:37-51
- **Severity:** critical · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** Three independent, individually fatal defects. (1) ADAPTER MISMATCH: app/api/billing_router.py:42-48 passes the bare `stripe` MODULE as `stripe_client`, but StripeBillingService calls `self._stripe.create_customer(...)`, `.create_checkout_session(...)`, `.construct_webhook_event(...)` etc. I downloaded stripe 15.5.1 and probed it: `create_customer False / create_checkout_session False / construct_webhook_event False / create_billing_portal_session False / list_invoices False / update_subscription False` — all 16 protocol methods are absent from the module namespace. Only `app/billing/store.py:FakeStripeClient` (a test double) implements this interface. (2) NO PRICE IDS: `PricingPlan.stripe_monthly_price_id`/`stripe_annual_price_id` default to "" (app/billing/models.py:221-223) and grep for "stripe" in app/billing/plans.py returns nothing — no plan definition or config setting ever populates them, so app/billing/stripe_integration.py:173-176 would always raise `ValueError("No Stripe price configured")`. (3) NO ORGANIZATION EVER EXISTS: `store.save_organization` is called from exactly one place, `create_customer` (stripe_integration.py:128), which itself needs an existing Organization — a chicken-and-egg with no bootstrap. Proven by running with the real SDK on PYTHONPATH: `CHECKOUT_REAL_STRIPE: 500 ... ValueError: Organization not found: 00000000-0000-0000-0000-000000000000`. (4) `stripe` is not in pyproject.toml dependencies or any optional-dependencies group.
- **Blocker:** There is no configuration of this repo in which a real Stripe charge can occur. Setting STRIPE_API_KEY either silently keeps demo mode (SDK missing) or produces a 500 (SDK present).
- **Fix:** Write a real adapter class mapping the Protocol onto stripe-python (`stripe.Customer.create`, `stripe.checkout.Session.create`, `stripe.Webhook.construct_event`, ...), add `stripe>=11` to pyproject.toml, add STRIPE_*_PRICE_ID settings and wire them into PRICING_PLANS, and create the billing Organization row at org-creation time.

### `BROKEN` — Second Stripe webhook route (billing router)

- **Surface:** POST /billing/webhooks/stripe (app/api/billing_router.py:286-292)
- **Severity:** high · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** Two conflicting Stripe webhook endpoints exist. Executed: `c.post('/billing/webhooks/stripe', content=b'{}')` → `500 {"error":"INTERNAL_ERROR"}`. Root cause: app/billing/api.py:464-465 `if not self._stripe: raise ValueError("Stripe not configured")`, and `_stripe` is always None because the SDK is missing (see the Stripe SDK finding). Even if it were non-None it delegates to `self._stripe.construct_webhook_event(...)` which does not exist on the stripe module. It also does not participate in the tenancy plan system at all — it only mutates the in-memory store — so if an operator points Stripe at this URL instead of /webhooks/stripe, plans in the enforced system are never updated.
- **Blocker:** Always 500s; and it is the URL an operator would naturally pick given the /billing prefix, silently diverging from the working /webhooks/stripe handler.
- **Fix:** Delete `/billing/webhooks/stripe` and document `/webhooks/stripe` as the single receiver, or make it a thin alias that delegates to the same tenancy handler.

### `BROKEN` — Change / cancel / reactivate subscription

- **Surface:** POST /billing/subscription/change, /cancel, /reactivate (app/api/billing_router.py:128-149)
- **Severity:** high · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** All three unconditionally raise when Stripe is unconfigured — app/billing/api.py:311-312 (`change_plan`), 355-356 (`cancel_subscription`), 372-373 (`reactivate_subscription`) all do `if not self._stripe: raise ValueError("Stripe not configured")`, which is not mapped to an HTTP status. Executed live (even with STRIPE_API_KEY set): `CANCEL: 500 {"error":"INTERNAL_ERROR"}`, `CHANGE: 500 {"error":"INTERNAL_ERROR"}`. The frontend surfaces these via CancelModal (frontend/src/pages/Billing.tsx:116-127) as a generic error toast. Note the asymmetry: checkout happily fakes an upgrade in demo mode, but cancel/downgrade 500s — a user can grant themselves a paid plan but cannot leave it.
- **Blocker:** Every subscription-lifecycle action in the UI returns a 500. There is no way to downgrade or cancel.
- **Fix:** Map ValueError to HTTP 503/409 in the router, and implement demo-mode equivalents (or, better, remove demo mode entirely per the checkout finding so upgrade and cancel are symmetric).
- **Verifier:** All three reproduced live: POST /billing/subscription/cancel -> 500, /change -> 500, /reactivate -> 500, all {"error":"INTERNAL_ERROR","message":"An unexpected error occurred. Please try again."}. Captured the traceback: app/billing/api.py:356 raise ValueError('Stripe not configured') propagating through billing_router.py:141 with no handler mapping ValueError to 4xx. Re-ran with STRIPE_API_KEY=sk_test_fake AND the real SDK importable: cancel still 500. Read api.py:311-312 (change_plan) and 372-373 (reactivate) -- identical unguarded raise. Confirms the asymmetry: checkout fakes success, exit paths hard-fail. Frontend surfaces it as a generic toast (Billing.tsx:119-127).

### `BROKEN` — Authorization on billing mutations

- **Surface:** app/api/billing_router.py (whole router) — POST /billing/checkout, /portal, /subscription/*, /track
- **Severity:** high · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** The router is declared as `APIRouter(prefix="/billing", tags=["Billing"])` (app/api/billing_router.py:23) with no `dependencies=`. It inherits only the app-level `Depends(require_auth)` (app/api/main.py:671). Verified both ways: with COPILOT_API_TOKEN unset (documented local-dev default) `POST /billing/checkout` returns 200 with NO Authorization header; with COPILOT_API_TOKEN=secret123 it returns `NOAUTH checkout: 401` / `AUTH checkout: 200`. Crucially there is no `require_perm("manage_billing")` on any /billing route — `grep -rn 'manage_billing' app` hits only app/tenancy/models.py:24 and the PATCH /admin/plan route. So in multi-tenant mode a `viewer`-role member can change the org's plan, cancel the subscription, and open the billing portal.
- **Blocker:** Least-privilege is violated for money operations: any authenticated member, at any role, can mutate billing. Unauthenticated in the default single-tenant config.
- **Fix:** Add `dependencies=[Depends(require_perm('manage_billing'))]` to the mutating billing routes (checkout, portal, subscription/*), keeping the read-only catalog endpoints open.
- **Verifier:** Confirmed and strengthened with a live RBAC test the auditor only reasoned about. Router declared as APIRouter(prefix='/billing', tags=['Billing']) with no dependencies= (billing_router.py:22). With COPILOT_API_TOKEN unset: POST /billing/checkout -> 200 with no Authorization header. With COPILOT_API_TOKEN=secret123: no header -> 401, correct bearer -> 200. Then in multi-tenant I minted an actual VIEWER-role key via POST /admin/api-keys and exercised it: POST /billing/checkout -> 200, POST /billing/portal -> 200, while PATCH /admin/plan -> 403. So the RBAC matrix demonstrably works elsewhere and is simply absent from every money route. grep for manage_billing hits only app/tenancy/models.py:24 and main.py:2609-2610.

### `BROKEN` — Billing database schema / migrations

- **Surface:** migrations/versions/001_initial_schema.py vs app/tenancy/store.py vs app/billing/models.py
- **Severity:** medium · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** Schema drift across three definitions of 'usage'. The single alembic migration defines `usage_records(id UUID, org_id UUID, period String(7), investigations Integer, tokens_used Integer)` with a unique index on (org_id, period) — migrations/versions/001_initial_schema.py:181-193. The code that actually runs uses a completely different SQLite table created by raw DDL in app/tenancy/store.py:77-85: `usage(id TEXT, org_id TEXT, kind TEXT, amount INTEGER, ts TEXT, meta TEXT, event_key TEXT, synced INTEGER)` — event-level rows, not a per-period rollup. And app/billing/models.py defines `UsageSummary`/`UsageRecord` dataclasses with yet another field set (ai_investigations_count, alerts_processed_count, ...) that have no table at all. There is likewise no table for Subscription, Invoice, PaymentMethod, or FeatureOverride.
- **Blocker:** Whoever runs `alembic upgrade head` gets a `usage_records` table the application never reads or writes, while the live ledger is created outside migration control.
- **Fix:** Drop `usage_records` from the migration (or make the tenancy store use it), and add real migrations for the billing entities before any of app/billing can be made persistent.
- **Verifier:** Confirmed three-way drift by reading all three definitions. migrations/versions/001_initial_schema.py:181-193 creates usage_records(id UUID, org_id UUID, period String(7), investigations Integer, tokens_used Integer, created_at, updated_at) with a UNIQUE index on (org_id, period) -- a per-period rollup. The code that actually runs creates a different table via raw DDL at app/tenancy/store.py:77-85: usage(id TEXT, org_id TEXT, kind TEXT, amount INTEGER, ts TEXT, meta TEXT, event_key TEXT, synced INTEGER) with idx_usage_org_kind_ts and the partial-unique idx_usage_event_key -- event-level rows. app/billing/models.py defines UsageSummary/UsageRecord dataclasses with a third field set (ai_investigations_count, alerts_processed_count, ...) backed by no table. I enumerated every create_table in the migration: no subscriptions, invoices, payment_methods, or feature_overrides. `alembic upgrade head` therefore produces a usage_records table the application never reads or writes, while the live ledger is created outside migration control.

### `STUB` — Persist subscription / billing state

- **Surface:** app/billing/store.py:InMemoryBillingStore, singleton at app/api/billing_router.py:24-34
- **Severity:** critical · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** app/api/billing_router.py:30-34 lazily creates a process-global `InMemoryBillingStore()`. app/billing/store.py:29-38 is nothing but eight module-instance dicts (`self._subscriptions: dict[UUID, Subscription] = {}` etc.). There is no SQLAlchemy model and no migration: `migrations/versions/` contains only `001_initial_schema.py`, which has NO subscriptions / invoices / payment_methods / feature_overrides tables (grep for those names returns only the `usage_records` table and an `orgs.plan` column). In single-tenant mode `_current_org_id()` (app/api/billing_router.py:65-72) returns a FIXED all-zero UUID, so every user in the deployment shares one global subscription object.
- **Blocker:** Every subscription, invoice, payment method, and usage counter is lost on process restart and is not shared across uvicorn workers — so with >1 worker a user's plan flips depending on which worker answers.
- **Fix:** Add real tables + an alembic migration for subscriptions/invoices/payment_methods and a SQLAlchemy-backed store implementing the same Protocol; keep InMemoryBillingStore for tests only.

### `STUB` — Usage metering for billing (/billing/track, /billing/usage)

- **Surface:** POST /billing/track, GET /billing/usage, /usage/stats, /usage/history; frontend UsageBar/UsageChart
- **Severity:** high · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** The tracking mechanism itself works in-process — `c.post('/billing/track', json={'metric_type':'ai_investigation',...})` → `200 {"tracked":true}` and `GET /billing/usage/stats` then shows `ai_investigations: 1`. But NOTHING calls it. `grep -rn 'app.billing' app --include=*.py` outside app/billing/ returns exactly three hits: app/api/billing_router.py:12, app/api/main.py:701 (the include_router), and app/api/main.py:3119 (`from app import billing` for the signature helper). No investigation, alert, postmortem, or PR-creation code path invokes `UsageTracker`. On the frontend, `trackUsage` exists at frontend/src/api/billing.ts:177 but has zero .tsx callers. So the Billing page's usage bars and charts read a counter that is only ever incremented by a manual curl.
- **Blocker:** The /billing usage numbers are permanently zero for real users, while the actual product usage is recorded in a completely separate ledger (app/metering.py → tenancy SQLite `usage` table, surfaced at GET /usage). Two meters, neither aware of the other.
- **Fix:** Call `UsageTracker.track_ai_investigation` from the same place `metering.record_investigation` is called (app/api/main.py:527), or better: delete app/billing's usage subsystem and have /billing/usage read the tenancy ledger.
- **Verifier:** Confirmed the mechanism works in-process: POST /billing/track {'metric_type':'ai_investigation'} -> 200 {"tracked":true}; GET /billing/usage/stats then shows ai_investigations:1; GET /billing/usage/history shows one 2026-08 row. And confirmed nothing calls it: grep for app.billing / 'from app import billing' across app/ outside app/billing/ returns exactly TWO hits -- app/api/billing_router.py:12 and app/api/main.py:3119 (the signature helper). No investigation/alert/postmortem/PR path touches UsageTracker. On the frontend, trackUsage/getUpgradeOptions/checkUsageLimit exist only as definitions in src/api/billing.ts (168/173/184) with zero callers; Billing.tsx calls only listPlans/getSubscription/getUsage/getUsageHistory/getInvoices/getPaymentMethods/createCheckout/createPortalSession/cancelSubscription/reactivateSubscription. The real ledger is the separate tenancy `usage` SQLite table.

### `STUB` — Invoice history and payment methods

- **Surface:** GET /billing/invoices, GET /billing/payment-methods; frontend InvoiceList.tsx / PaymentMethodCard.tsx
- **Severity:** medium · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** app/billing/api.py:384-385 and 396-397: `if not self._stripe: return []`. Since `_stripe` is permanently None (SDK not installed / not a declared dependency), both endpoints return `[]` forever. Executed: `200 /billing/invoices []` and `200 /billing/payment-methods []`. Even with a working SDK there is no invoices table in migrations/versions/001_initial_schema.py, and `FakeStripeClient.list_invoices` (app/billing/store.py:323-325) returns an empty list by construction.
- **Blocker:** Renders as an empty 'no invoices / no cards' state that is indistinguishable from a real empty account. A paying customer could never retrieve a receipt.
- **Fix:** Once the Stripe adapter exists this reads through to Stripe; until then return 503 rather than an empty list so the UI can say 'billing unavailable'.
- **Verifier:** GET /billing/invoices -> 200 [] and GET /billing/payment-methods -> 200 [] with no Stripe. Read app/billing/api.py:384-385 and 396-397 to the return: `if not self._stripe: return []`. I then re-ran with the real stripe SDK on PYTHONPATH and a key set so _stripe was non-None: GET /billing/invoices STILL returned 200 [] -- because stripe_integration.py:360-362 does `org = await self._store.get_organization(org_id); if not org or not org.stripe_customer_id: return []`, and no Organization is ever created in the store. get_payment_methods:401-403 is identical. No invoices/payment_methods table in 001_initial_schema.py, and FakeStripeClient.list_invoices (store.py:323) returns empty by construction.

### `STUB` — Customer / trial CRM (trial tracking, conversion, health score)

- **Surface:** POST /customers, GET /customers/stats, /customers/dashboard, /customers/trials/active, POST /customers/usage, POST /customers/convert (app/api/main.py:3544-3720)
- **Severity:** medium · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** Wired and auth-gated (`dependencies=[Depends(require_auth)]`), and `python3 -m pytest tests/test_customers.py -q` passes. But all state is four module-level dicts: app/customers/manager.py:193-196 `_customer_store: dict[str, Customer] = {}`, `_email_index`, `_trial_store`, `_usage_store`. No SQLAlchemy model, no table in migrations/versions/001_initial_schema.py. `_update_health_score` (manager.py:369-402) is a hand-tuned magic-number heuristic (+15 if active today, -10 if NPS<=6...), not a model. `POST /customers/convert` records `mrr_cents` and a `stripe_customer_id` string with no verification that any payment occurred. It is a fourth, independent notion of a paying customer, disconnected from both app/billing and app/tenancy.
- **Blocker:** All trial and MRR data evaporates on restart and is not shared across workers; conversion is an unverified self-report.
- **Fix:** Back it with a real table + migration, or fold it into the tenancy org model.

### `DEAD_CODE` — Feature gating / paywall enforcement

- **Surface:** GET /billing/features, /billing/features/{key}; app/billing/feature_gate.py; frontend/src/components/billing/FeatureGate.tsx
- **Severity:** high · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** The gate logic itself is real: app/billing/feature_gate.py:71-82 resolves the plan from the subscription, :90-98 honors admin FeatureOverrides, and executed `GET /billing/features/sso_saml` correctly returned `{"available":false,"reason":"Feature not available on your current plan"}` on the starter tier. But it is never consulted as a gate. `grep -rn 'require_feature|check_usage_limit|FeatureGate|feature_gate' app --include=*.py` outside app/billing/ returns ONLY app/api/billing_router.py:202-206 (the endpoint that reports the answer). The decorators `require_feature` and `check_usage_limit` (exported at app/billing/__init__.py:50-51) have zero call sites. On the frontend, `grep -rn '<FeatureGate'` across frontend/src returns nothing — the component is defined and exported but never rendered.
- **Blocker:** Paid features are advertised as gated but no server-side check ever runs. Anyone on the free tier can call every endpoint; the paywall exists only as a self-report the client is free to ignore.
- **Fix:** Attach `Depends(require_feature('...'))` to the endpoints backing each gated feature (auto_fix_pr, ai_postmortem, similar_incident_search, api_access, sso_saml...), and render <FeatureGate> around the corresponding UI.
- **Verifier:** The reporting endpoints are live and correct -- GET /billing/features/sso_saml on a fresh (starter) store returned {"available":false,"reason":"Upgrade to Business plan to access this feature","upgrade":{"current_plan":"starter","required_plan":"business",...}} -- but nothing enforces it. grep for require_feature|FeatureGateService|feature_gate across *.py outside app/billing/ hits ONLY tests/test_billing_feature_gate.py. The require_feature decorator (feature_gate.py:311) and check_usage_limit have zero product call sites. On the frontend the only FeatureGate references are its own definition (FeatureGate.tsx:7/15/21) and the barrel re-export (components/billing/index.ts:8) -- zero `<FeatureGate` render sites. I additionally confirmed Billing.tsx never even calls getFeatures/checkFeature, so the paywall is not surfaced in the UI either.

### `DEAD_CODE` — Stripe metered-usage sync (meter events)

- **Surface:** app/billing/webhooks.py:66-84 sync_usage_to_stripe; app/tasks/tasks.py:253-299 sync_usage_to_stripe (dramatiq)
- **Severity:** medium · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** Two competing implementations, neither reachable. (1) app/billing/webhooks.py:66 is the correct-looking one (reads `store.unsynced_usage`, pushes meter events, `store.mark_synced`) — `grep -rn 'unsynced_usage|mark_synced' app` outside app/tenancy/store.py hits ONLY these two lines inside the function itself, so no scheduler, CLI, or startup hook ever invokes it. (2) app/tasks/tasks.py:253 is a dramatiq actor and nothing outside app/tasks/ imports app.tasks; it is also independently broken — it posts `payload[stripe_customer_id]=org_id` (wrong ID space: the internal org id, not the `cus_...` id, which the tenancy store tracks separately via `set_stripe_customer`), sends an ISO-8601 `timestamp` where Stripe expects a unix integer, never marks rows synced (so every run re-sends), and calls `asyncio.get_event_loop().run_until_complete(...)` which raises on Python 3.13 in a thread with no loop.
- **Blocker:** Usage is recorded to a local ledger (62 unsynced rows in my test run: `unsynced count: 62`) and never reaches Stripe, so metered billing would invoice $0.
- **Fix:** Delete the app/tasks version. Schedule app/billing/webhooks.py:sync_usage_to_stripe from the lifespan as a periodic task, and cover it with a test that asserts mark_synced is called.
- **Verifier:** Both implementations confirmed unreachable. (1) grep for unsynced_usage|mark_synced across *.py hits only app/tenancy/store.py:464/478 (definitions), app/billing/webhooks.py:68/83 (inside sync_usage_to_stripe itself), and tests/test_billing.py:29/36/50. No scheduler/startup/CLI caller. (2) app/tasks/tasks.py:253 is a dramatiq actor, and grep for 'app.tasks|from app import tasks' across app/ scripts/ cli/ outside app/tasks/ returns NOTHING -- never imported by live code. I independently confirmed its three internal bugs by reading tasks.py:280-299: it posts payload[stripe_customer_id]=org_id (internal org id, not a cus_... id, which tenancy tracks separately via set_stripe_customer), sends timestamp=datetime.now().isoformat() where the Stripe meter_events API expects a unix integer, never marks rows synced, and ends with asyncio.get_event_loop().run_until_complete(_sync()) which raises on Python 3.13 in a loop-less thread.

### `PARTIAL` — Stripe webhook receiver (tenancy) — signature verification

- **Surface:** POST /webhooks/stripe (app/api/main.py:3086-3137), app/billing/webhooks.py:87-126
- **Severity:** high · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** Signature verification is REAL and fails closed. Executed against a live TestClient with STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET=whsec_test123: no signature → `401 {"detail":"Invalid Stripe signature."}`; stale timestamp (t-100000, correctly signed) → `401`; correct t+v1 → `200 {"status":"applied","org_id":"67cb3640...","plan":"team"}` and `store.get_org()` afterwards really shows `plan='team'` in SQLite. Uses `hmac.compare_digest` (app/billing/webhooks.py:110) and rejects an empty secret (line 96). BUT: (a) PLAN-NAME DRIFT — `_PLANS` (webhooks.py:17) accepts `starter_monthly`, `starter_yearly`, `pro_monthly`, while `PLAN_QUOTAS` (app/tenancy/models.py:45-49) only knows free/team/enterprise and `normalize_plan` maps everything else to "free". Verified live: sending plan=`pro_monthly` returned `{"status":"applied","plan":"pro_monthly"}` but the org row afterwards read `plan='free'` — a paying customer is silently DOWNGRADED and the webhook reports success. (b) NO EVENT DEDUP — unlike the PagerDuty and Slack handlers, which call `_claim_delivery(raw)` (app/api/main.py:3081, 3181), the Stripe handler does not; an out-of-order Stripe redelivery of an older `subscription.updated` will overwrite a newer plan. (c) UNKNOWN ORG SWALLOWED — `set_plan` is a bare `UPDATE ... WHERE id=?` (app/tenancy/store.py:145); posting org_id=`org_ghost` returned `{"status":"applied"}` with zero rows changed.
- **Blocker:** Signature checking is fine, but the plan vocabulary disagrees with the quota table, so a real Stripe price mapping to starter_monthly/pro_monthly silently drops the customer to the free tier while returning HTTP 200 to Stripe (so Stripe never retries).
- **Fix:** Make `_PLANS` exactly `PLAN_QUOTAS.keys()` and 400 on anything else; check the rowcount from set_plan and 404 on unknown org so Stripe retries; add `_claim_delivery(event['id'])` for idempotency plus a stored `last_event_created_at` per org for ordering.

### `PARTIAL` — Fleet-wide LLM spend cap

- **Surface:** app/spend.py; quota_gate 429 path (app/api/main.py:555-561); _record_spend (app/api/main.py:508-521)
- **Severity:** medium · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** Logic is real and unit-tested: `python3 -m pytest tests/test_spend.py tests/test_customers.py -q` → `26 passed`. `over_cap`/`alert_due` are pure functions (app/spend.py:24-31); RedisSpend does a real INCRBY with TTL (lines 76-85) so it is cross-replica. Wired into quota_gate at app/api/main.py:555-561 and recorded at :508. HOWEVER `copilot_global_token_cap` defaults to 0 (app/config.py:281) and `over_cap`/`_record_spend` both short-circuit on 0, so the guardrail is OFF unless an operator sets it. Also, without COPILOT_REDIS_URL it falls back to InMemorySpend (app/spend.py:96), a per-process counter — with N uvicorn workers the effective cap is N× the configured value.
- **Blocker:** Off by default, and silently N× too permissive on multi-worker deployments without Redis.
- **Fix:** Log a startup warning when the cap is set but Redis is not, and consider a non-zero default cap in production profiles.
- **Verifier:** Confirmed. over_cap/alert_due are pure (app/spend.py:24-31); RedisSpend.record does a real INCRBY + expire on a window-bucketed key (76-85). Wired at main.py:555-561 (429) and recorded at main.py:508-521. `pytest tests/test_spend.py tests/test_billing.py -q -p no:cacheprovider` -> 8 passed. copilot_global_token_cap defaults to 0 (app/config.py:281) and BOTH over_cap (spend.py:26 `cap > 0 and ...`) and _record_spend (main.py:509 early return) short-circuit on 0, so the guardrail is off unless configured. make_spend_tracker falls back to InMemorySpend (spend.py:96) without COPILOT_REDIS_URL, so N workers = N x the cap. ADDITIONALLY (auditor missed): RedisSpend.total returns 0 on any exception (72-74) and record returns 0 on exception (83-85), so a Redis outage silently turns the cap OFF rather than failing closed.

BUT the auditor missed a third investigation entrypoint that bypasses the gate: app/api/main.py:2861 _run_triggered_investigation calls session.ask(seed) at :2874 with no quota_gate on the /webhooks/pagerduty -> _enqueue_investigation path (nor on _slo_trigger). require_auth returns early for any /webhooks/ path (main.py:433-435), and tenant_context.set_tenant is called from only three sites (grep: main.py:461, 1795, 1878) -- none on that path -- so metering.record_investigation returns immediately at app/metering.py:27 and over_quota() returns False at :46. Alert-triggered investigations, the product's headline loop, are neither gated nor billed. PARTIAL, not WORKS.

### `PARTIAL` — Usage report / forecast / history analytics

- **Severity:** low · **Confidence:** high

It correctly declines when data is thin: `200 /billing/usage/forecast {"forecast_available":false,"reason":"Insufficient historical data"}`.
- **Blocker:** The math operates on the in-memory usage summaries that nothing populates (see the /billing/track finding), so in practice it always returns the 'insufficient data' branch or all-zeros.
- **Fix:** Point it at the tenancy usage ledger; keep the forecast math.
- **Verifier:** Read generate_usage_forecast (app/billing/usage_tracker.py:450-499) to its return: it computes real per-month growth ratios ((v[i]-v[i-1])/v[i-1]), averages them, and compounds last_value * (1+avg_growth)**i. Only `confidence: max(0.5, 1 - i*0.1)` is fabricated. Executed: with zero data /billing/usage/forecast -> {"forecast_available":false,"reason":"Insufficient historical data"} (the len(history)<3 branch). After a single POST /billing/track, /billing/usage/report returned genuine deltas {"ai_investigations":{"current":1,"previous":0,"change":1,"change_percent":100.0}}, proving the math is live rather than canned. But it reads the in-memory _usage_summaries dict that nothing in the product populates, so in practice it is always the insufficient-data / all-zeros branch.

### `WORKS` — Pricing plan catalog and comparison matrix _(verdict overturned from PARTIAL on verification)_

- **Surface:** GET /billing/plans, GET /billing/plans/comparison, GET /billing/upgrade-options
- **Severity:** low · **Confidence:** high

These are pure, deterministic, and correct. Two defects: (a) SHAPE DRIFT — `/billing/upgrade-options` returns the raw plan dict (`"monthly_price_cents":29900`) via `p.to_dict()` (app/billing/api.py:456) while frontend/src/types/billing.ts:186-194 declares `monthly_price: number`; the endpoint has zero .tsx callers so the mismatch is currently latent. (b) The catalog's plan names (starter/team/growth/business/scale) do not intersect the enforced tenancy plan names (free/team/enterprise) except for 'team'.

No mismatch, latent or otherwise. I also verified the two query-param names are aligned: billing_router.py:153 takes `plan` and billing.ts:98 sends `?plan=`; billing_router.py:202 takes `metric` and billing.ts:194 sends `?metric=`. The endpoints are correct and deterministic: /billing/plans?include_enterprise=true returns 6 tiers with correct arithmetic (team 299.0/15.05%, growth 699.0/15.02%, business 1499.0/15.01%, scale 2499.0/15.01% -- 2499*12=29988 vs 25488 = 15.006%), and /billing/plans/comparison returns a real 33-feature x 5-plan boolean matrix from FEATURE_TO_MIN_TIER. The surviving sub-point (b), that starter/team/growth/business/scale do not intersect the enforced free/team/enterprise vocabulary, is real but is an integration defect already captured by the checkout and webhook findings; the read-only catalog feature itself works as advertised.

**Verifier notes for this domain.** I regenerated all evidence myself rather than trusting the auditor's quoted output. Commands run: live TestClient probes of all 22 /billing/* routes (single-tenant, token-set, and multi-tenant with fresh SQLite tenant DBs); a real `pip download stripe==11.1.0` into /tmp/stripelib plus a hasattr() probe of the StripeClient protocol methods; a signed /webhooks/stripe HMAC exercise (missing/stale/forged/valid signatures); a full /signup -> PATCH /admin/plan privilege-escalation run; a quota_gate 402 run with 50 usage rows plus a deliberate duplicate event_key; `pytest tests/test_spend.py tests/test_billing.py -q -p no:cacheprovider` (8 passed); and greps for every claimed dead-code symbol.

OVERTURNED 2 of 20:
(1) "Pricing plan catalog" PARTIAL -> WORKS. The auditor's sub-defect (a) SHAPE DRIFT is FALSE. /billing/upgrade-options returns BOTH `monthly_price_cents: 29900` AND `monthly_price: 299.0` (verified live), so PricingPlan.to_dict() at app/billing/api.py:456 satisfies the UpgradeOption interface in frontend/src/types/billing.ts exactly. I also confirmed the query-param names are aligned: /billing/subscription/proration takes `plan` (app/api/billing_router.py:153) and frontend/src/api/billing.ts:98 sends `?plan=`; /billing/usage/limit takes `metric` (billing_router.py:202) and billing.ts:194 sends `?metric=`. The catalog math is correct: 6 tiers, annual_savings_percent 15.01-15.05% (scale 2499*12=29988 vs 25488 = 15.006%), and /billing/plans/comparison returns a real 33-feature x 5-plan matrix.
(2) "Usage quota enforcement" WORKS -> PARTIAL. The auditor only tested /chat and /chat/stream. There is a third investigation entrypoint they missed: app/api/main.py:2861 `_run_triggered_investigation`, reached via POST /webhooks/pagerduty -> _enqueue_investigation and via _slo_trigger. It calls `session.ask(seed)` at main.py:2874 with NO quota_gate on that path. Worse, `tenant_context.set_tenant` is called from only three places (grep: app/api/main.py:461, 1795, 1878) and require_auth returns early for any path starting with /webhooks/ (main.py:433-435), so no TenantConfig is ever on the contextvar there -- `metering.record_investigation` short-circuits at app/metering.py:27 and `metering.over_quota()` returns False at line 46. Webhook/SLO-triggered investigations are both ungated and unbilled. That is the product's headline loop.

MATERIAL THINGS THE AUDITOR MISSED:
- frontend/src/api/billing.ts:22 reads `import.meta.env.VITE_API_TOKEN` at BUILD time. Vite inlines this into the shipped bundle, so the shared COPILOT_API_TOKEN would be readable by every browser loading the billing page. BASE_URL also hard-defaults to http://localhost:8000.
- app/spend.py:83-85 RedisSpend.record swallows every exception and returns 0, and .total does the same at :72-74. Since quota_gate calls over_cap(total, cap), a Redis outage silently DISABLES the fleet spend cap (fails open) rather than failing closed.
- app/api/billing_router.py:55-62 caches `_BILLING_API` on first use with whatever `_get_stripe_service()` returned at that moment; if the first billing request arrives before Stripe init succeeds the process is stuck in demo mode permanently even with a valid key.
- The demo-checkout Subscription is written under the fixed all-zero UUID in single-tenant (verified: session_id `demo_session_00000000-0000-0000-0000-000000000000_scale`), so every user of a single-tenant deployment shares one subscription object -- one user's free upgrade upgrades everyone.
- app/customers/manager.py convert_trial accepts arbitrary money with zero verification: I posted `{"tier":"enterprise","mrr_cents":999900,"stripe_customer_id":"cus_totally_fake"}` and GET /customers/stats then reported `mrr_usd: 9999.0, paying_customers: 1, conversion_rate: 1.0`.
- The dead-code claims are stronger than stated: SubscriptionManager, app/billing/webhooks.py:sync_usage_to_stripe, and app/tasks/tasks.py:sync_usage_to_stripe each have call sites ONLY in tests/ (test_startup_features.py, test_billing.py, test_tasks.py). Nothing outside app/tasks/ imports app.tasks at all.
- Confirmed `stripe` appears ZERO times in pyproject.toml AND zero times in uv.lock -- not even transitively resolvable.


## Slack bot, slash commands, interactivity, ChatOps

**Summary.** The Slack domain is a demo shell wearing production clothing. Exactly one router is live (app/slack_bot/router.py, mounted at /api/slack), and it has ZERO request-signature verification — the HMAC check is written and then commented out at router.py:349-353, and `verify_slack_signature` defined at router.py:27 is never called by any live handler. I posted a forged `app_mention` event with no headers at all and the server accepted it and tried to POST to slack.com. Every slash command returns a template string or a hardcoded dict: `/status` returns a literal four-service dict of fake latencies (router.py:488-493), `/oncall` returns "Alice Smith / Bob Jones" (router.py:544-548) even though a real on-call engine is live at /oncall/*, and `/rca` acknowledges and then never investigates anything. The `@mention` investigation path is a `asyncio.sleep(3)` followed by a hardcoded "Database connection pool exhaustion" RCA (router.py:312-330). All button-driven modal flows are dead on arrival because the endpoint returns `{"response_action":"open_modal"}` in the HTTP body instead of calling `views.open` with the trigger_id — Slack ignores this entirely, and the only module with a real `views.open` (client.py) is unreachable. About 6,000 lines under app/slack_bot/ plus all of app/slack_advanced/ and app/chatops/ are dead code hanging off the unregistered app/slack_bot/api.py. The two things that genuinely work are the legacy /webhooks/slack/interactions approval receiver (proper HMAC + 5-min replay window + delivery dedupe) and POST /share/slack. A paying user could install this app and it would answer /help; anything that requires state or truth would lie to them.

**Wiring.** LIVE: app/slack_bot/router.py is the only Slack router registered — app/api/main.py:725-726 `include_router(slack_bot_router, prefix="/api")`, exposing POST /api/slack/events, /api/slack/interactivity, and /api/slack/commands/{rca,status,oncall,incident,warroom,postmortem,stakeholder,help}. Also live and defined directly in main.py: POST /webhooks/slack/interactions (main.py:3141) and POST /share/slack (main.py:2204). Live support modules actually imported by router.py: messages.py, blocks.py, interactions.py, war_room.py. Live support module for main.py: app/integrations/slack.py (signature verify + block kit) and app/integrations/slack_notify.py (real posting).

NOT WIRED (no entrypoint): app/slack_bot/api.py declares a SECOND `APIRouter(prefix="/slack")` (api.py:25) with /events, /commands, /interactions, /options, /oauth/callback, /install. It is imported only by app/slack_bot/__init__.py:67 and is never passed to include_router — confirmed by the live route dump: /api/slack/options, /api/slack/oauth/callback and /api/slack/install do not exist. Everything reachable only through it is therefore dead: client.py (763 lines, the only real Slack Web API wrapper with views.open/conversations.create), core.py (686), handlers.py (781), commands.py (1043), conversation.py (775), workflows.py (934). Entire packages app/slack_advanced/ (2,334 lines: action_flows, digests, investigation, natural_language, thread_summarizer) and app/chatops/ (1,539 lines: slack_bot, teams_bot, commands) have zero references outside themselves — verified by `grep -rn "slack_advanced|chatops" --include="*.py" .` returning only self-references.


### `BROKEN` — Slack request signature verification (events, slash commands, interactivity)

- **Surface:** POST /api/slack/events, /api/slack/commands/*, /api/slack/interactivity
- **Severity:** critical · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** app/slack_bot/router.py:27-41 defines verify_slack_signature (with a 5-minute window), but `grep -n "verify_slack_signature" app/slack_bot/router.py` shows it is only referenced inside a COMMENTED-OUT block at router.py:349-353:
    # timestamp = request.headers.get("X-Slack-Request-Timestamp", "")
    # signature = request.headers.get("X-Slack-Signature", "")
    # if not verify_slack_signature(...): raise HTTPException(401)
The 8 slash-command handlers and /interactivity never even reference it. Executed with TestClient, no headers whatsoever:
  POST /api/slack/events {'type':'event_callback','event':{'type':'app_mention',...}} -> server accepted it and issued an outbound POST to https://slack.com/api/chat.postMessage (traceback shows router.py:377 -> send_slack_message), returning 500 only because the network call timed out.
  POST /api/slack/commands/incident (no headers) -> 200
  POST /api/slack/interactivity (no headers) -> 200 with a full incident-acknowledged block payload.
No auth dependency guards these routes either. Also note SLACK_SIGNING_SECRET is snapshotted into SlackConfig at class-definition/import time (router.py:19-24), so it is not re-read per request.
- **Blocker:** Anyone on the internet who knows the URL can forge Slack events, slash commands and button clicks: post arbitrary in-channel messages as the bot, trigger outbound Slack API calls, acknowledge/resolve/escalate incidents, and create war rooms. There is no HMAC check, no timestamp replay window, and no event_id de-duplication.
- **Fix:** Uncomment and hoist the check into a FastAPI dependency applied to the whole router: read the raw body once, read X-Slack-Request-Timestamp / X-Slack-Signature, reject if |now - ts| > 300, compare hmac.new(secret, f'v0:{ts}:{body}'.encode(), sha256) with hmac.compare_digest, and 401 when SLACK_SIGNING_SECRET is unset. Read the secret from get_settings() per request, not at import. Reuse the already-correct app/integrations/slack.py:verify_signature instead of the duplicate. Add event_id de-dup like main.py's _claim_delivery.
- **Verifier:** Independently confirmed. `grep -rn verify_slack_signature app/` -> the only live-router reference is app/slack_bot/router.py:352, inside the commented block at :349-353. I read the handler bodies: none of the 8 slash commands, /events or /interactivity reads X-Slack-Signature. I POSTed all of them from TestClient with zero headers: every one returned 200. /api/slack/events with a forged app_mention returned 200 and (with httpx patched to capture) issued 5 outbound calls to slack.com. Also confirmed there is no auth middleware - the only middleware is LimitsMiddleware (main.py:279) doing request-id/body-cap/rate-limit. Extra: router.py:30 int(timestamp) would raise ValueError on an absent header even if the check were re-enabled.

### `BROKEN` — Interactive buttons that open modals (Escalate / Resolve / Snooze / Post update / Add responder / Change severity / global shortcuts)

- **Surface:** POST /api/slack/interactivity (block_actions + shortcut)
- **Severity:** critical · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** Every modal branch returns a JSON body instead of calling Slack's views.open. router.py:1040-1043, 1049-1052, 1058-1061, 1071-1074, 1141-1144, 1147-1150, 1252-1255, 1258-1261 all do `return {"response_action": "open_modal", "view": {...}}`. `response_action: open_modal` is not part of the Slack API — response_action only accepts errors/update/push/clear, and only on view_submission. For block_actions a JSON body is interpreted as a message replacement, so Slack will render nothing useful. The trigger_id is captured at router.py:1019 and passed into _get_status_update_modal / _get_severity_modal / _get_create_incident_modal etc. (router.py:1299, 1352, 1391, 1430, 1512) where it is accepted as a parameter and then never used in the body. `grep -rn "views.open" app/slack_bot/` returns exactly one hit — client.py:545 — and client.py is only imported by the unregistered api.py and by core.py, so no live code path can open a modal.
Executed: POST /api/slack/interactivity payload {'type':'block_actions','actions':[{'action_id':'escalate_INC-123'}]} -> 200 {"response_action":"open_modal","view":{..."callback_id":"escalate_submit_INC-123"...}} — the view JSON is dumped into the HTTP response and thrown away.
- **Blocker:** No modal ever appears in Slack. Escalate, Resolve, Snooze, Post Status Update, Add Responder, Change Severity, and both global shortcuts (create_incident, quick_investigate) are all no-ops from the user's perspective. Because the modals never open, the view_submission handlers at router.py:1163-1246 are unreachable in practice.
- **Fix:** Replace each `return {"response_action":"open_modal", "view": v}` with an actual call to Slack views.open using the trigger_id (client.py:545 already implements it: `await client.open_view(trigger_id, view)`), then return an empty 200 within Slack's 3-second budget. Wire app/slack_bot/client.py into router.py or move these handlers onto the api.py implementation and register that router instead.

### `BROKEN` — view_submission handling for modal forms

- **Surface:** POST /api/slack/interactivity (type=view_submission)
- **Severity:** high · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** router.py:1163-1246 handles only 5 callback_ids: feedback_modal, escalate_submit_*, resolve_submit_*, snooze_submit_*, status_update_*. But the modals defined in the same file declare callback_ids that have NO handler: create_incident_submit (router.py:1434), quick_investigate_submit (router.py:1516), add_responder_{id} (router.py:1356), severity_update_{id} (router.py:1395). Those fall through to the bare `return {"ok": True}` at router.py:1263.
Executed:
  view_submission create_incident_submit -> 200 {"ok":true}
  view_submission quick_investigate_submit -> 200 {"ok":true}
  view_submission add_responder_INC-1 -> 200 {"ok":true}
  view_submission severity_update_INC-1 -> 200 {"ok":true}
Also, resolve_submit_ reads the resolution summary into a throwaway `_` (router.py:1198) and never passes it to the handler, and the only response_url follow-up in the whole endpoint is the escalate branch (router.py:1191); _send_response (router.py:1290-1296) posts with no timeout and no error handling.
- **Blocker:** Even if the modals could be opened, submitting Create Incident, Quick Investigate, Add Responder, or Change Severity silently succeeds and discards the user's input. Resolve discards the resolution summary the user typed.
- **Fix:** Add handlers for the four missing callback_ids that call the real incident service; pass the resolution summary through to _handle_resolve as details['notes']; add timeout + try/except to _send_response and use it on all branches, not just escalate.
- **Verifier:** Reproduced all 8 submissions: create_incident_submit, quick_investigate_submit, add_responder_INC-1, severity_update_INC-1 all -> 200 {"ok":true} (fall through to router.py:1263), while feedback_modal / resolve_submit_ / snooze_submit_ / status_update_ -> {"response_action":"clear"}. Confirmed router.py:1198 reads the resolution summary into `_` and never passes it on. Extra bug the auditor missed: router.py:1191 does result.details.update({level,team,reason}) AFTER handle_action already built result.blocks, so the escalation details the user typed never reach the message posted to response_url.

### `BROKEN` — Auto-investigation of alerts posted in monitored channels

- **Surface:** POST /api/slack/events (event type=message)
- **Severity:** medium · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** router.py:400-438 regex-matches any message containing [alert], [critical], [incident], 'pagerduty', 'opsgenie', the siren emoji, 'error rate' or 'latency spike', then posts to Slack and schedules run_investigation. Two defects: (1) it shares the same faked run_investigation (router.py:312-330), so every alert gets the identical hardcoded 'payment-service v2.4.1 connection leak' RCA; (2) `text = event.get("text","").lower()` at router.py:407 is then re-matched with re.IGNORECASE and, more importantly, the send path 500s with no bot token (same traceback at router.py:377/85). Combined with the missing signature check, an unauthenticated caller can make the server emit outbound Slack posts by sending a fake message event.
- **Blocker:** Any human typing the phrase 'error rate' in a monitored channel triggers a bot post plus a canned fake root cause. Without a bot token the endpoint 500s and Slack retries, and there is no event_id dedupe so retries duplicate the noise.
- **Fix:** Gate the pattern list behind an explicit per-channel opt-in config, dedupe on event_id, wrap outbound sends in try/except so the webhook always returns 200 fast, and connect it to the real investigation engine.

### `STUB` — @mention AI investigation in channel ("@omnipotent why is checkout slow?")

- **Surface:** POST /api/slack/events (app_mention)
- **Severity:** critical · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** router.py:298-341 run_investigation is explicitly fake. router.py:312-314:
    # Simulated investigation - in production, this would call the real RCA engine
    import asyncio
    await asyncio.sleep(3)
and router.py:316-328 posts a fixed answer regardless of the query: root_cause="Database connection pool exhaustion due to connection leak in payment-service v2.4.1", confidence=87, evidence=["Error rate spiked 300% at 14:32 UTC", ...], recommendation="Rollback payment-service to v2.4.0". No orchestrator, no LLM, no telemetry query. The "View Full Report" button links to a literal placeholder URL https://omnipotent.example.com/investigation/123 (router.py:291).
The bot user id used to strip the mention is hardcoded: router.py:372 `bot_user_id = "U123456"  # Would be fetched from auth.test`, so a real mention `<@U0REAL>` is never stripped and the raw mention text becomes the "query".
Executed: POST /api/slack/events with an app_mention -> 500 {"error":"INTERNAL_ERROR"} because send_slack_message (router.py:71-93) unconditionally POSTs to slack.com with `Bearer ` (empty SLACK_BOT_TOKEN) and the ConnectTimeout propagates out of the handler.
- **Blocker:** With no bot token the endpoint 500s (Slack will mark the app unhealthy and retry, and there is no event_id dedupe so retries duplicate work). With a valid bot token it succeeds but posts an identical hardcoded 'database connection pool exhaustion' RCA for every question asked, with a fabricated 87% confidence.
- **Fix:** Replace the asyncio.sleep(3) + literal dict in run_investigation with a call into the real investigation orchestrator that main.py already uses (see main.py:2819 _post_to_slack / the /investigations path), pass the result through build_rca_result_blocks, fetch bot_user_id from auth.test at startup instead of "U123456", guard send_slack_message on a non-empty token and wrap it in try/except so the webhook always 200s, and de-dupe on event_id.

### `STUB` — /incident create|update|resolve|escalate|severity

- **Surface:** POST /api/slack/commands/incident
- **Severity:** critical · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** router.py:589-712. `create` mints an id with `incident_id = f"INC-{int(datetime.now(timezone.utc).timestamp()) % 100000}"` (router.py:615) — collides every ~27.7 hours — then only renders a block card via messages.get_message_builder().incident_card. There is no DB write, no repository, no service call. `update`, `resolve`, `escalate` and `severity` (router.py:636-691) are pure f-string echoes of the user's own text; they take no incident_id argument at all, so 'resolve' does not identify, let alone resolve, anything.
Executed end-to-end: POST /api/slack/commands/incident text='create PROBE_UNIQUE_TITLE' -> 200 with a rendered card, then GET /api/incidents -> 200 returning only pre-existing seed rows ("Checkout API 500 errors", ...) — the Slack-created incident is absent. Confirms zero persistence into the live incidents store registered at main.py:718.
- **Blocker:** An incident 'created' from Slack exists only as pixels in one message. It never appears in /api/incidents, the web UI, timelines, or metrics. `/incident resolve` prints a green checkmark while nothing is resolved — actively misleading during a real incident. The mod-100000 id will also collide with a prior incident roughly daily.
- **Fix:** Have each subcommand call the live incidents service used by the /api/incidents router (create/update/transition), use a real unique id (uuid or the DB sequence), and thread the incident_id through update/resolve/escalate/severity instead of echoing text.
- **Verifier:** Executed end-to-end myself: POST /api/slack/commands/incident text='create PROBE_UNIQUE_TITLE_XYZ' -> 200 rendered card; GET /api/incidents -> the probe title is absent (only seeded rows). resolve/escalate/update/severity are pure f-string echoes. Extra defect the auditor missed: text='severity INC-1 SEV1' renders '*Severity Updated* to INC-1 SEV1' - the incident id is swallowed into the severity string. Confirmed the id generator is `int(now.timestamp()) % 100000` (27.7h collision period).

### `STUB` — /status slash command — service health

- **Surface:** POST /api/slack/commands/status
- **Severity:** high · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** router.py:487-493 is literally commented `# Mock status data` followed by a hardcoded dict: {"api": 45ms/0.01%, "checkout": degraded 230ms/2.3%, "payment": 120ms/0.05%, "database": 12ms/0%}. No settings, no metrics client, no DB.
Executed: POST /api/slack/commands/status text='checkout' -> 200 {"blocks":[{..."*Checkout Service Status*\n:large_yellow_circle: Degraded\n• Latency: 230ms\n• Error Rate: 2.3%"}]}. Same values on every call. Any service name other than api/checkout/payment/database silently falls through to the 4-service overview rather than saying 'unknown service'.
- **Blocker:** Reports fabricated health numbers to an incident channel. During a real outage it will state that checkout is 'Degraded, 230ms' whether checkout is fine or on fire, and always claim api/payment/database are healthy.
- **Fix:** Query the real metrics/SLO source (the SLO router is live at /slo/*) or the observability integrations in app/integrations/, and return 'no data' when a service is unknown rather than falling through to the canned overview.

### `STUB` — /oncall slash command — who is on call

- **Surface:** POST /api/slack/commands/oncall
- **Severity:** high · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** router.py:543-548, commented `# Mock on-call data`, hardcoded: platform -> Alice Smith / Bob Jones, infra -> Charlie Brown / Diana Prince, security -> Eve Wilson / Frank Miller.
Executed: POST /api/slack/commands/oncall text='platform' -> 200 "*Platform On-Call*\n:bust_in_silhouette: Primary: Alice Smith\n:busts_in_silhouette: Secondary: Bob Jones".
This is doubly damning because a real on-call system IS live in the same app — the route dump shows GET /oncall/teams, GET /oncall/teams/{team_id}/current-oncall, POST /oncall/schedules, /oncall/schedules/{id}/generate-shifts, /oncall/schedules/{id}/gaps (registered at main.py:706). The slash command simply does not call it.
- **Blocker:** During an incident the bot names people who do not exist instead of paging the actual rotation, and any team not named platform/infra/security silently falls through to the fake overview.
- **Fix:** Replace the dict with a call into the live on-call service backing GET /oncall/teams/{team_id}/current-oncall, and return an explicit 'no schedule configured for <team>' for unknown teams.

### `STUB` — /warroom create and list

- **Surface:** POST /api/slack/commands/warroom, and the warroom_* / create_war_room_* buttons on /api/slack/interactivity
- **Severity:** high · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** Two divergent implementations. The `create` subcommand (router.py:728-760) only formats a message announcing a channel name — it never touches the war-room manager. The `list` subcommand (router.py:762-789) reads from WarRoomManager. The button path (router.py:1063-1067 -> interactions.py:418-462 -> war_room.py:140-179) does register a room.
Persistence is a module-level singleton dict: war_room.py:136-138 `self._war_rooms: dict[str, WarRoom] = {}`, `_incident_to_room`, `_channel_to_room`. No SQLAlchemy model, no migration.
No Slack channel is ever created: war_room.py:158 fabricates `channel_id=f"C{incident_id}"`. `grep -rn "conversations.create" app/slack_bot/` hits only client.py:350, which is dead code. war_room.py:_get_oncall_users returns the literal `["oncall_user"]`.
Executed: button warroom_INC-777 -> 200, then `/warroom list` -> "*Active War Rooms (1):* #inc-inc-777-incident-inc-777". Then `/warroom create MyRoom` -> 200 "War Room Created ... #inc-inc-30798-warroom", and `/warroom list` again still shows only 1 room — the slash-created room was never registered. Channel name also double-prefixes: 'inc-inc-777-...'.
- **Blocker:** No Slack channel is actually created, nobody is invited, and the registry dies on process restart and is not shared across workers/replicas. `/warroom create` and `/warroom list` disagree with each other. Users are told to join #inc-inc-777-... which does not exist.
- **Fix:** Make the slash `create` path call the same WarRoomManager.create_war_room the button uses; have create_war_room actually call Slack conversations.create + conversations.invite (client.py:350 already wraps it) and store the returned real channel_id; back _war_rooms with a DB table; fix the doubled 'inc-inc-' prefix in _generate_channel_name; replace the ['oncall_user'] placeholder with the live /oncall lookup.
- **Verifier:** Reproduced the divergence exactly: list -> 'No active war rooms.'; button warroom_INC-777 -> registers and list -> 'Active War Rooms (1): #inc-inc-777-incident-inc-777' (double 'inc-' prefix confirmed); then /warroom create MyRoom -> 'War Room Created ... #inc-inc-33923-warroom'; list again -> still 1 room. Read war_room.py:136-138 (three module-level dicts, no SQLAlchemy model), :158 `channel_id=f"C{incident_id}"` fabricated, :214-216 `_get_oncall_users` returns literal ['oncall_user']. No conversations.create anywhere in app/slack_bot/ except dead client.py:350.

### `STUB` — /rca slash command — start AI investigation

- **Severity:** high · **Confidence:** high

It returns an acknowledgement promising ":hourglass: Results will appear in a thread..." and then schedules nothing. No orchestrator import, no response_url follow-up, no thread post.

### `DEAD_CODE` — OAuth install flow / app distribution (/slack/install, /slack/oauth/callback)

- **Surface:** app/slack_bot/api.py:325 (oauth/callback), :375 (install) — NOT mounted
- **Severity:** medium · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** api.py:25 creates a second `APIRouter(prefix="/slack")` with @router.post('/events'), ('/commands'), ('/interactions'), ('/options') and @router.get('/oauth/callback'), ('/install'). `grep -rn "slack_bot.api" --include="*.py" app/` returns exactly one hit: app/slack_bot/__init__.py:67 `from app.slack_bot.api import router as slack_router` — an import, never an include_router. main.py's full include_router list (lines 698-768) registers slack_bot_router from router.py only. The live route dump contains no /api/slack/options, /api/slack/oauth/callback or /api/slack/install.
This is the painful part: api.py:35-60 contains the CORRECT signature verification as a FastAPI dependency (required headers, 5-minute window, HMAC, 500 when the secret is unconfigured) — the exact code the live router has commented out. api.py is also the only consumer of app/slack_bot/client.py, the only real Slack Web API wrapper (client.py:251 chat.postMessage, :350 conversations.create, :545 views.open).
- **Blocker:** There is no way to install the Slack app via OAuth — no multi-workspace support, single global SLACK_BOT_TOKEN only. And ~4,800 lines reachable only from api.py are dead: client.py (763), core.py (686), handlers.py (781), commands.py (1043), conversation.py (775), workflows.py (934) — verified each is imported only by api.py/core.py/handlers.py, i.e. only within the dead subtree.
- **Fix:** Pick one router. api.py is the better-built one (real signature dependency, real SlackClient, OAuth) — register it and delete router.py's duplicate handlers, or at minimum port api.py's verify_slack_signature dependency and SlackClient usage into router.py and delete api.py to stop the confusion.

### `PARTIAL` — Automated test coverage for Slack

- **Surface:** tests/integration/test_slack_integration.py
