But the assertions are theater: test_slack_events_message:46, test_slack_commands:61 and test_slack_interactions:86 all assert `response.status_code in [200, 404]` — a route that does not exist passes, and a route returning fabricated data passes. test_signature_verification:103-141 exercises SlackClient.verify_signature in app/slack_bot/client.py, which is DEAD code, not the live /api/slack router that has verification commented out — so the suite reports signature verification as tested while the shipped endpoint has none. test_create_war_room:211 asserts only on the returned in-memory dataclass fields, never that a Slack channel was created. There is no test anywhere asserting the content of /status, /oncall or /rca, which is why the hardcoded values survived. `ls tests/ | grep -i slack` shows only this one file for ~16,000 lines of Slack code.

- **Fix:** Replace `in [200, 404]` with exact status assertions; add a test that an UNSIGNED POST to /api/slack/events returns 401; assert /status and /oncall reflect injected fixture data rather than any fixed string; assert that /incident create results in a row visible from the incidents service.

They never reach the shipped router once. test_signature_verification (:103) exercises app/slack_bot/client.py, which is dead. But the auditor also MISSED real coverage: tests/test_webhooks.py:64-70 tests app/integrations/slack.verify_signature (good/bad/expired) and :93-104 tests that /webhooks/slack/interactions rejects a bad signature. So coverage exists for the legacy path and is genuinely absent for the /api/slack router.

- **Evidence:** router.py:917-1003 returns a well-formed static Block Kit help card. Executed: POST /api/slack/commands/help -> 200 with header ':robot_face: OmniPotent Help' and correctly rendered sections. It is a static string by design, which is fine — but it advertises capabilities that do not function: it documents '/incident resolve' (no persistence), '/rca <query> - Start AI investigation' (never runs), '/oncall [team]' (fake data), and 'Use buttons on incident cards for quick actions like Acknowledge, Escalate, Resolve, and Snooze' (Escalate/Resolve/Snooze never open their modals).
- **Blocker:** Works as a help card but documents six commands that are stubs, so it is the primary source of the false impression that the bot is functional.
- **Fix:** No code change needed for the card itself; fix the underlying commands or trim the card to what actually works.
- **Verifier:** Executed and dumped every block: valid Block Kit (header, sections, two real dividers - I checked, the empty-looking entries are {"type":"divider"}, not empty sections, so it will render). It advertises /incident create|update|resolve|escalate, /warroom create, /stakeholder, /rca, /status, /oncall, /postmortem generate and 'Use buttons on incident cards for ... Acknowledge, Escalate, Resolve, and Snooze' - every one of which I independently proved is a stub or a dead modal.

### `WORKS` — Approval buttons resuming a paused investigation (legacy receiver)

- **Surface:** POST /webhooks/slack/interactions
- **Severity:** low · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** app/api/main.py:3159-3189. Uses app/integrations/slack.py:15-26 verify_signature, which does a proper constant-time HMAC over b'v0:'+ts+b':'+raw with an explicit 300-second replay window, plus _claim_delivery de-duplication of retried Slack deliveries.
Executed with a real signature (SLACK_SIGNING_SECRET=testsecret):
  unsigned  -> 401 {"detail":"invalid or unconfigured Slack signature"}
  correctly signed, ts=now -> 200 {"text":"Approved — continuing the investigation."}
  replay of the identical signed body -> 200 {"text":"Already processed."} (dedupe fires)
  correctly signed with ts = now-100000 -> 401 (replay window enforced)
It maps action_id=='approve' + actions[0].value (the thread_id) into _resume_triggered, which resumes the real investigation and posts back via slack.post_message (main.py:2834). This is what the /api/slack router should have looked like.

### `WORKS` — Share investigation report to Slack from the web UI

- **Surface:** POST /share/slack (called from frontend/src/api.ts:302)
- **Severity:** low · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** main.py:2204-2233 -> app/integrations/slack_notify.py. is_slack_configured (slack_notify.py:18-21) checks slack_bot_token or slack_webhook_url; post_to_slack (slack_notify.py:145-186) does a genuine httpx POST to the webhook URL, falling back to chat.postMessage with the bot token, and returns the real ok/error from Slack.
Executed unconfigured: POST /share/slack -> 400 {"detail":"Slack is not configured. Set SLACK_WEBHOOK_URL or SLACK_BOT_TOKEN."} (honest failure, not a silent 200).
Executed with SLACK_WEBHOOK_URL set and httpx.AsyncClient.post patched to capture: -> 200 {"success":true,"message":"Report posted to Slack"} and the captured outbound request went to https://hooks.slack.com/services/T/B/X with real derived blocks: header 'Investigation Report', '*Query:* why slow', '*Root Cause* :white_check_mark:\npool exhaustion', '*Evidence:*\n• e1\n• e2'. The blocks are built from the actual report dict (slack_notify.py:24-100), not canned.

**Verifier notes for this domain.** I regenerated all evidence myself with TestClient probes (/tmp/probe1..13.py) plus direct source reads; I did not rely on the auditor's quoted output.

WIRING RE-VERIFIED: `grep -n include_router app/api/main.py` -> 18 routers; only line 726 `app.include_router(slack_bot_router, prefix="/api")` with `from app.slack_bot.router import router` (main.py:725). Live route dump confirms POST /api/slack/{events,interactivity,commands/rca,commands/status,commands/oncall,commands/incident,commands/warroom,commands/postmortem,commands/stakeholder,commands/help}, plus POST /share/slack and POST /webhooks/slack/interactions. No /api/slack/options|oauth/callback|install. `grep -rn 'verify_slack_signature|verify_signature' app/` shows app/slack_bot/router.py:352 is the ONLY reference in the live router and it is inside a comment.

NO AUTH LAYER: the only middleware is LimitsMiddleware (main.py:279-308) = request-id + body-size cap + per-IP POST rate limit. There is no auth middleware, so the "anyone can forge" conclusion holds; a per-IP rate limit is the sole (irrelevant) mitigation.

WHERE THE AUDITOR WAS WRONG:
1. The "500 on app_mention / auto-investigation" evidence is a SANDBOX ARTIFACT, not a product bug. With httpx patched, POST /api/slack/events (app_mention) returns 200 and emits 5 outbound calls (chat.postMessage, chat.update, reactions.add, chat.update, reactions.add). The auditor's 500 was just a ConnectTimeout because this box has no network. In production with a bad token, slack.com returns ok:false and the handler silently returns 200 -> a silent no-op, not a 500, so "Slack marks the app unhealthy and retries" is not established. I downgraded the auto-investigation claim from BROKEN to STUB for this reason (it executes end-to-end; the output is just canned).
2. tests/integration/test_slack_integration.py is even worse than described: it POSTs to /slack/events, /slack/commands and /slack/interactions — paths that DO NOT EXIST (live paths are /api/slack/... and /api/slack/interactivity, not /interactions). So all three "endpoint" tests get a hard 404 and pass on the `in [200, 404]` branch. They never touch the shipped router at all.
3. The auditor said only one Slack test file exists. tests/test_webhooks.py:64-104 genuinely tests app/integrations/slack.verify_signature (good sig, bad sig, expired ts) and /webhooks/slack/interactions signature enforcement. Real coverage exists — but only for the legacy path.

MATERIAL THINGS THE AUDITOR MISSED ENTIRELY:
A. app/mcp/servers/slack/server.py (566 lines) is a FIFTH Slack surface and it IS registered: app/mcp/client.py:22 `_SERVERS` includes "slack" and client.py:209-217 spawns `python -m app.mcp.servers.slack.server` over stdio with SLACK_BOT_TOKEN injected. It has real `httpx.post(f"https://slack.com/api/{method}")` (server.py:135-148) and real tools send_message, create_channel (:290), invite_to_channel (:445), create_incident_channel (:507), each with an `offline_demo` fallback. So the agent CAN actually create a Slack channel — the auditor's "no live code path creates a channel" is only true when scoped to app/slack_bot/. It does not rescue the war-room verdict (war_room.py never calls MCP), but it changes the domain map.
B. app/integrations/slack_bot.py (537 lines) is a FOURTH independent Slack bot (SlackBot, SlackBlockBuilder, verify_request at :312). It is eagerly imported by app/integrations/__init__.py:12 (so it loads on every boot) but is wired to no route; its only non-package consumer is tests/test_roadmap_features.py:46,444,458,480.
C. router.py:29-31 `if abs(time.time() - int(timestamp)) > 60*5` raises ValueError on a missing/empty X-Slack-Request-Timestamp header, so even if the commented block at :349-353 were restored it would 500 rather than 401 on malformed input.
D. Extra bug in the escalate view_submission path: router.py:1191 `result.details.update({'level','team','reason'})` mutates the result AFTER handler.handle_action already built result.blocks, so the escalation level/team/reason the user typed never appear in the message posted to response_url.
E. /stakeholder does not strip the subcommand: text='update all good' echoes the literal string 'update all good' into the broadcast body.
F. /incident severity takes no separate arg: text='severity INC-1 SEV1' renders ':chart_with_upwards_trend: *Severity Updated* to INC-1 SEV1' — the incident id is treated as part of the severity value.
G. /share/slack silently ignores the `channel` argument on the webhook path (frontend/src/api.ts:302 passes one), and if only SLACK_BOT_TOKEN is set with no channel, is_slack_configured() returns True while post_to_slack returns success:False -> a confusing 400.
H. /webhooks/slack/interactions returns "Approved — continuing the investigation." even when active_api_key() is empty, in which case nothing is dispatched (main.py:3187). I proved both branches.


## Inbound webhooks &amp; alert ingestion (PagerDuty, Datadog, Grafana, Alertmanager, GitHub, GitLab)

**Summary.** There are two completely independent, unrelated webhook stacks in this codebase and they behave very differently. The `/webhooks/pagerduty` route defined inline in `app/api/main.py` is the real one: it enforces PagerDuty's actual `v1=<hex>` HMAC scheme, parses the real v3 event shape, dedupes redeliveries, and enqueues a genuine LLM agent investigation — that path works. The `/api/webhooks/*` router (`app/webhooks/router.py`) is a parallel, much weaker implementation: it accepts unauthenticated POSTs from anyone, writes "incidents" only into an in-memory demo dict that is seeded with fake data and dies on restart, and its "auto-investigate" creates an empty Investigation record whose six agents sit at `idle`/progress 0 forever. The single biggest problem is that this second stack is the one a customer would wire Datadog/Grafana/Alertmanager into, and it 500s on the actual JSON those products send (Datadog's `tags` is a comma-separated string, not a dict) while its PagerDuty variant expects a PagerDuty v2 payload with a `sha256=` signature and therefore rejects or silently drops 100% of real PagerDuty v3 traffic. GitHub and GitLab webhooks verify signatures correctly but are pure echo endpoints — they return a JSON summary of the payload and do nothing else; the `auto_triage` field is a string label with no downstream effect. There is zero test coverage for `/api/webhooks/*` and `/webhooks/{github,gitlab}`; the only test that mentions a webhook path outside PagerDuty asserts `status_code in [200,202,400,401,403,404]` against a route that returns 404. A paying user could get alerts *in* today only via `/webhooks/pagerduty` with a configured secret and an LLM key; everything else is a facade.

**Wiring.** LIVE: `/webhooks/pagerduty`, `/webhooks/github`, `/webhooks/gitlab`, `/webhooks/stripe`, `/webhooks/slack/interactions` — all defined inline in app/api/main.py (lines 3030-3400). LIVE: `/api/webhooks/{pagerduty,alertmanager,datadog,grafana,generic}` — app/webhooks/router.py, registered at app/api/main.py:721-722 with prefix="/api". NOT WIRED: app/api/v1/webhooks.py defines a duplicate router (github/gitlab/pagerduty/stripe delegating back to main.py) that is never included — `[r.path for r in app.routes if r.path.startswith('/api/v1')]` returns `[]`. NOT WIRED: the entire app/connectors/ package (grafana.py, gitlab.py, prometheus.py, splunk.py, newrelic.py, servicenow.py, jenkins.py) — no importer outside the package except kubernetes.py via app/digital_twin/, which itself is not reachable from app/api/main.py. NOT WIRED: app/integrations_advanced/ (bidirectional.py, correlation.py, health.py, oauth.py, sdk.py) and app/github_app/ (pattern_matcher.py, reviewer_suggester.py, risk_analyzer.py) — `grep -rln` over app/ and tests/ finds importers only inside their own packages. NOT WIRED: app/database/repositories.py:130 IncidentRepository (Supabase-backed, real persistence) has zero callers anywhere in app/.


### `BROKEN` — Datadog alert ingestion (/api/webhooks/datadog)

- **Surface:** POST /api/webhooks/datadog (app/webhooks/router.py:155-194)
- **Severity:** critical · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** Posted a realistic Datadog monitor webhook body `{"alert_type":"error","title":"[Triggered] High CPU","body":"...","alert_id":"987","tags":"env:prod,service:api",...}` → `DATADOG 500 {"error":"INTERNAL_ERROR"}`. Server traceback: `File app/webhooks/router.py, line 180, in datadog_webhook / service=data.get("tags", {}).get("service", "unknown") / AttributeError: 'str' object has no attribute 'get'`. Datadog's `$TAGS` template variable renders as a comma-separated STRING (and users commonly template it as a JSON list); both crash. Confirmed both shapes: `tags:"env:prod"` → 500, `tags:["env:prod"]` → 500, `tags` absent → 200. There is also no signature/secret check on this endpoint at all — an unauthenticated POST creates an incident. Zero tests reference `/api/webhooks/datadog` (`grep -rln 'api/webhooks' tests/` returns nothing).
- **Blocker:** Line 180 assumes `tags` is a dict. Real Datadog payloads send a string or a list, so every real alert 500s and is lost. Datadog retries a few times and then drops the alert.
- **Fix:** Normalise tags before use: parse `"k:v,k:v"` / `["k:v"]` / `{}` into a dict, e.g. `tags = _normalize_tags(data.get('tags'))` then `tags.get('service','unknown')`. Add a regression test using a captured Datadog webhook body.
- **Verifier:** Reproduced: tags as comma-separated string -> 500; tags as list -> 500; tags absent -> 200; tags as dict -> 200. Read app/webhooks/router.py:180 `data.get("tags", {}).get("service", "unknown")` — assumes dict. Also unauthenticated: anonymous POST created incident f1f1df76-... with no header of any kind. AND worse than claimed: the DEFAULT Datadog payload template sends `event_type`, not `alert_type`, so {"event_type":"error",...} -> 200 {"status":"ignored"} and the alert is dropped silently with no provider retry. No tests reference the path.

### `BROKEN` — PagerDuty alert ingestion (/api/webhooks/pagerduty) — duplicate of the root route

- **Surface:** POST /api/webhooks/pagerduty (app/webhooks/router.py:45-97) vs POST /webhooks/pagerduty (app/api/main.py:3030)
- **Severity:** critical · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** The two routes are NOT duplicates — they are incompatible implementations of different PagerDuty API versions. (1) Payload shape: the /api route reads `data['messages'][*]['event'] == 'incident.triggered'` (v2/legacy). A real PagerDuty v3 body returns `PD-v3-api 200 {"status":"ok","incident_id":null,"message":"Processed 0 incidents"}` — silently dropped, 200 OK, nothing created. (2) Signature scheme: router.py:28-31 computes `f"sha256={hex}"`, but PagerDuty sends `v1=<hex>`. With PAGERDUTY_WEBHOOK_SECRET set and a correctly-signed real PD header: `REALPD->apiroute 401 {"detail":"Invalid signature"}`. (3) Auth bypass: router.py:55 is `if settings.pagerduty_webhook_secret and x_pagerduty_signature:` — omitting the header skips verification entirely. Verified: `PDapi-NOsig-header-omitted 200 {"status":"ok","incident_id":"2a7291ef-..."}` with the secret configured. So with a secret set, genuine PagerDuty traffic 401s while any anonymous attacker who omits the header gets incidents created.
- **Blocker:** Wrong payload version (v2 `messages` vs v3 `event`), wrong signature format (`sha256=` vs `v1=`), and signature verification is skipped when the header is absent. Net effect: real PagerDuty traffic never produces an incident on this route, and unsigned attacker traffic does.
- **Fix:** Delete this route and keep only /webhooks/pagerduty, or make it delegate to app.integrations.pagerduty.verify_signature/parse_incident and reject (401) whenever the secret is configured and the header is missing.

### `STUB` — Auto-investigate on ingested alert (copilot_auto_investigate)

- **Surface:** BackgroundTask auto_investigate (app/webhooks/router.py:34-42) → IncidentStore.start_investigation (app/incidents/store.py:380-408)
- **Severity:** critical · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** POSTed to /api/webhooks/generic, waited 2s, then read GET /api/incidents/{id}: `{"completed_at":null,"overall_confidence":0.0,"root_cause":null,"recommended_actions":[],"agents":{"knowledge":{"status":"idle","progress":0,"findings":0}, "reasoning":{...idle,0,0}, "action":..., "learning":..., "collaboration":..., "ambient":{...idle,0,0}}}`. Reading store.py:380-408: start_investigation constructs an Investigation with all six agents at `AgentStatus.IDLE`, three empty SafetyGates, appends a timeline entry that reads "6-agent parallel investigation initiated", and returns. No LLM call, no task spawn, no scheduler — nothing in the codebase ever advances these agents for a webhook-created incident. Contrast with the real path at app/api/main.py:2862 (`session.ask(seed)`) which the /api/webhooks/* router never touches.
- **Blocker:** The advertised 'alert arrives → AI investigates' loop does not exist for any of the five /api/webhooks providers. The UI will show an investigation stuck at 0% forever.
- **Fix:** Have auto_investigate call `app.api.main._enqueue_investigation({...})` (the path PagerDuty's root route uses) instead of the demo store's start_investigation.
- **Verifier:** Read app/incidents/store.py:380-408 to its `return investigation`: it builds an Investigation with all six agents at AgentStatus.IDLE and three SafetyGates, appends the timeline string '6-agent parallel investigation initiated', and returns. Nothing spawns a task. Confirmed the only advancing method, update_agent_status (store.py:413), has ZERO callers — `grep -rn 'update_agent_status' app/ tests/ --include=*.py` returns only its own definition. Ran it live: POST /api/webhooks/grafana, slept, GET /api/incidents/{id} -> all six agents ('idle', 0, []), completed_at=null, overall_confidence=0.0. The loop does not exist for any /api/webhooks provider.

### `STUB` — Incident persistence for ingested alerts

- **Surface:** app/incidents/store.py (module-level `_store` singleton, line 458-465)
- **Severity:** critical · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** File docstring line 1: `"""In-memory incident store for demo purposes."""`. `_store: IncidentStore | None = None` at line 458 with a lazy singleton getter; `__init__` calls `self._init_demo_data()` which hardcodes fake incidents including a canned root cause "Database connection pool exhaustion...", agent findings with confidences 0.92/0.89/0.85 and a fake Jira ticket "PLAT-1234" (store.py:29-120). Every webhook-created incident goes into `self._incidents[incident.id]` (store.py:299) — a process-local dict. It dies on restart, is not shared across uvicorn workers, and has no org_id/tenant field, so all tenants share one global list. Meanwhile app/database/repositories.py:130 `IncidentRepository.create(org_id, title, severity, ..., source, external_id)` is a real Supabase-backed implementation with an `incidents` table — `grep -rn 'IncidentRepository' app/` returns hits only inside repositories.py itself. Verified live: 6 incidents accumulated in one process across my probes, none of them namespaced.
- **Blocker:** Alerts ingested from any provider are written to an ephemeral, non-tenant-scoped, worker-local dict pre-seeded with demo data. Restart or a second worker and the data is gone/inconsistent.
- **Fix:** Point app/webhooks/router.py at IncidentRepository (it already accepts source/external_id/org_id, which is exactly what a webhook has) and resolve org_id from the webhook's shared secret.

### `STUB` — GitHub webhook receiver + auto-triage

- **Surface:** POST /webhooks/github (app/api/main.py:3206-3300)
- **Severity:** high · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** Signature verification is correct and constant-time (main.py:3190-3197, `sha256=` + hmac.compare_digest): with GITHUB_WEBHOOK_SECRET=ghsecret, a correctly-signed `issues` payload → 200, a tampered one → `GH-badsig 401 {"detail":"invalid GitHub webhook signature"}`. But the handler is a pure echo: read main.py:3225-3300 to the `return result` — it only reflects fields back. A real PR event returns `{"event":"pull_request","delivery_id":"d-1","processed":true,"action":"opened","pr_number":7,"pr_title":"Add x","pr_state":"open"}`. Nothing is persisted, no incident is created, no agent is triggered, and the advertised `auto_triage` values (`"large_pr_flagged"`, `"incident_detected"`, `"deployment_failure_detected"`, `"security_alert"`) are string literals set in the response dict with no downstream consumer — grep confirms no caller. It also fails OPEN: `github_webhook_secret` defaults to `""` (app/config.py:115) so with no secret configured the check at main.py:3214 is skipped and anyone can POST (`GH-pr-nosig 200`). Zero tests reference `/webhooks/github`. The related app/github_app/{risk_analyzer,pattern_matcher,reviewer_suggester}.py that would do the actual triage has no importer anywhere.
- **Blocker:** Parsed and dropped. The 'auto-triage' feature is a label in a JSON response, not behaviour.
- **Fix:** Route triage-worthy events (issues labelled bug/incident, failed deployment_status, security_advisory) into the same `_enqueue_investigation` path the PagerDuty webhook uses, and persist deliveries. Default to rejecting unsigned requests.

### `STUB` — GitLab webhook receiver + auto-triage

- **Surface:** POST /webhooks/gitlab (app/api/main.py:3311-3400)
- **Severity:** high · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** Token check works when configured: `GL-goodtok 200`, `GL-badtok 401 {"detail":"invalid GitLab webhook token"}`, `GL-notok 401`. Fails open with no secret (`_verify_gitlab_token` at main.py:3304-3308 has an explicit `return True  # No secret configured, allow`, and gitlab_webhook_secret defaults to ""). Handler body (main.py:3340-3400) is echo-only, same as GitHub — a failed pipeline returns `{"event":"pipeline","processed":true,"pipeline_id":31,"status":"failed","ref":"main"}` and nothing else happens. Also crashes on a plausible payload: `{"object_kind":"merge_request","object_attributes":null}` → 500 AttributeError (line 3358 `attrs.get` on None). Zero tests. The GitLabConnector in app/connectors/gitlab.py that could act on these events is unreferenced outside its own package.
- **Blocker:** Parsed and dropped; 500s when `object_attributes` is null; fails open when no secret is set.
- **Fix:** Same as GitHub: wire pipeline/deployment failures into _enqueue_investigation, use `(payload.get('object_attributes') or {})`, and require a token in production.

### `DEAD_CODE` — Duplicate /api/v1/webhooks/* router

- **Surface:** app/api/v1/webhooks.py (github/gitlab/pagerduty/stripe)
- **Severity:** medium · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** `python3 -c "from app.api.main import app; print([r.path for r in app.routes if r.path.startswith('/api/v1')])"` → `[]`. app/api/main.py:698-768 lists all 18 include_router calls; none is the v1 router. Only importer is app/api/v1/__init__.py:12 (self-referential). Worse, tests/integration/test_api_integration.py:79-98 POSTs to `/api/v1/webhooks/pagerduty` and asserts `response.status_code in [200, 202, 400, 401, 403, 404]` with the comment "Should not crash, regardless of signature validation" — a test that passes on a 404 for a route that does not exist. Classic test theater.
- **Blocker:** Route is not registered; the only test covering it passes on 404.
- **Fix:** Either register the v1 router or delete app/api/v1/webhooks.py and tighten the integration test to assert a concrete status.

### `DEAD_CODE` — Provider connectors (Grafana, GitLab, Prometheus, Splunk, NewRelic, ServiceNow, Jenkins)

- **Surface:** app/connectors/*.py
- **Severity:** medium · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** `grep -rn 'from app.connectors' app/ --include=*.py` returns hits only in app/connectors/__init__.py and app/digital_twin/infrastructure_clone.py (kubernetes only). app/digital_twin is itself not imported by app/api/main.py (`grep -rn 'digital_twin' app/api/main.py` → no matches). So GrafanaConnector, GitLabConnector, PrometheusConnector etc. have no entrypoint. Only tests/test_connectors_gitlab.py exercises one directly.
- **Blocker:** No router registration and no import from live code.
- **Fix:** Wire the connectors into the webhook handlers (e.g. enrich an incoming Grafana alert by querying the Grafana/Prometheus connector) or remove the package.
- **Verifier:** Verdict stands but the auditor's evidence is partly wrong. `grep -rn 'from app.connectors' app/ --include=*.py` -> only intra-package imports plus app/digital_twin/infrastructure_clone.py:280/317/378 (kubernetes only), and digital_twin is not imported by app/api/main.py. No connector is reachable from any registered route. CORRECTION to the auditor: there are FOUR connector test files (tests/test_connectors.py, test_connectors_gitlab.py, test_connectors_grafana.py, test_connectors_jenkins.py), not just the gitlab one — so these are unit-tested-but-unwired, not untested.

### `DEAD_CODE` — Advanced integrations (bidirectional sync, correlation, integration health, OAuth, SDK) and GitHub App analyzers

- **Surface:** app/integrations_advanced/*.py, app/github_app/*.py
- **Severity:** medium · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** `grep -rln 'integrations_advanced|github_app' app/ tests/` returns only the files inside those two packages themselves — no external importer, no test. `grep -rn 'APIRouter' app/integrations_advanced/*.py app/github_app/*.py` → no matches, so there is no router that could even be registered. These are exactly the modules that would implement the 'auto-triage' the GitHub/GitLab webhooks claim to perform.
- **Blocker:** No APIRouter, no importer, no CLI entrypoint.
- **Fix:** Either expose them (e.g. call risk_analyzer from the GitHub pull_request handler) or delete them so the repo stops advertising capabilities that cannot run.

### `PARTIAL` — Alertmanager alert ingestion (/api/webhooks/alertmanager)

- **Surface:** POST /api/webhooks/alertmanager (app/webhooks/router.py:100-152)
- **Severity:** high · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** A well-formed Alertmanager v4 batch works: `ALERTMANAGER 200 {"status":"ok","incident_id":"4549910e-...","message":"Processed 1 alerts"}`, and GET /api/incidents shows the object. But: (a) no auth of any kind — anonymous POST creates incidents; (b) no dedup — posting the identical alert (same `fingerprint`) 3× produced 3 distinct incident ids `['cdb66853-...','935a089e-...','c3bd2d49-...']`, so Alertmanager's normal repeat_interval re-sends will flood the incident list; (c) `status:"resolved"` batches are skipped entirely (router.py:121) → `RESOLVED {'status':'ok','incident_id':None,'message':'Processed 0 alerts'}`; incidents opened by a firing alert are never closed; (d) it 500s on Alertmanager-plausible variants: `{"alerts":null}` → 500 TypeError, `{"alerts":[{"status":"firing","labels":null,"annotations":null}]}` → 500 AttributeError, a top-level JSON array `[]` → 500. Zero tests cover this route.
- **Blocker:** Unauthenticated, no fingerprint dedup, ignores resolves, and null sub-objects cause 500s. Persistence is the in-memory demo store (see separate finding).
- **Fix:** Add a shared-secret/HMAC header check; key incidents by `alert.fingerprint` and update instead of re-creating; handle `status == 'resolved'` by resolving the matching incident; use `(alert.get('labels') or {})` everywhere and guard `data` not being a dict.
- **Verifier:** Reproduced. Valid v4 batch -> 200 with an incident id. No auth of any kind (no header check exists in router.py:100-152). No dedup: three identical POSTs of the same alert (same fingerprint) produced c49298a1/38b62339/7d80f2a2, and a single batch containing the same fingerprint twice produced "Processed 2 alerts"; router.py never reads `fingerprint`. status:resolved skipped at router.py:121 -> "Processed 0 alerts", so firing incidents are never closed. 500s on {"alerts":null}, on labels/annotations=null, and on a top-level JSON array.

### `PARTIAL` — Grafana alert ingestion (/api/webhooks/grafana)

- **Surface:** POST /api/webhooks/grafana (app/webhooks/router.py:197-244)
- **Severity:** high · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** Legacy Grafana payload works: `GRAFANA 200 {"status":"ok","incident_id":"1c65a74a-..."}` with severity read from `tags.severity`. Grafana 9+ unified-alerting payload also 200s but degrades badly — it creates exactly ONE incident for a batch of N alerts (the handler never looks at `data['alerts']`), and because unified alerting has no top-level `tags` object, service becomes `"unknown"` and severity defaults to MEDIUM regardless of the per-alert `severity` label. If Grafana's notification template omits the top-level `state` field the whole batch is dropped: `GRAF-unified-nostate 200 {"status":"ignored",...,"message":"Not alerting state"}`. Crashes on a string `tags`: `{"state":"alerting","tags":"a,b"}` → 500 AttributeError. No auth. No tests.
- **Blocker:** Only understands the deprecated legacy webhook shape; unified alerting collapses an N-alert batch into one mislabelled incident and loses severity/service.
- **Fix:** Branch on presence of `data['alerts']`: if present, reuse the alertmanager code path (Grafana unified alerting is Alertmanager-compatible); otherwise fall back to the legacy fields. Coerce `tags` to a dict first.
- **Verifier:** Reproduced, including the strongest form of the claim. Legacy payload with tags.severity=critical -> 200. Grafana 9+ unified payload WITH the top-level state field and 2 firing alerts -> 200 but exactly ONE incident, and GET /api/incidents/{id} returned severity="medium", service="unknown", tags=['grafana','unknown'] despite every alert carrying severity=critical and service labels — the handler never reads data['alerts'] (router.py:197-244). Unified payload without `state` -> {"status":"ignored","message":"Not alerting state"}, whole batch dropped. tags as a string -> 500. No auth, no tests.

### `PARTIAL` — Generic webhook ingestion (/api/webhooks/generic)

- **Surface:** POST /api/webhooks/generic (app/webhooks/router.py:247-305)
- **Severity:** medium · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** This is the only /api/webhooks route with real auth. With COPILOT_WEBHOOK_SECRET set: `GEN-nosecret 401 {"detail":"Invalid secret"}` / `GEN-secret 200 {"status":"ok","incident_id":"0adbdcf2-..."}`. Severity aliasing (p1..p4 → CRITICAL..LOW) works — verified via GET /api/incidents/{id}: `"severity":"critical"` for `severity:"p1"`. Note the comparison is a plain `!=` on the plaintext secret (router.py:257), not constant-time. Fragile inputs 500: `{"title":"x","tags":{"a":"b"}}` → 500 (line 285 calls `.append` on a dict), `{"tags":123}` → 500, a bare JSON string body `"juststring"` → 500 (line 265 calls `.get` on a str).
- **Blocker:** Any caller sending `tags` as an object/number, or a non-object JSON body, gets a 500. Incidents land only in the in-memory store.
- **Fix:** Validate the body with a Pydantic model instead of raw `.get()` chains; use `hmac.compare_digest` for the secret comparison.
- **Verifier:** Reproduced. With COPILOT_WEBHOOK_SECRET set: no header -> 401 {"detail":"Invalid secret"}; correct header -> 200. Severity aliasing verified end-to-end: severity:"p1" -> GET /api/incidents/{id} shows "severity":"critical". router.py:257 is a plain `!=` on the plaintext secret (not hmac.compare_digest) — the only /api/webhooks route with auth uses a timing-unsafe comparison. Crashes reproduced: tags={"a":"b"} -> 500, tags=123 -> 500 (router.py:285 .append), bare JSON string body -> 500 (router.py:265 .get on str).

### `PARTIAL` — Webhook abuse protection (rate limit / body cap / replay)

- **Surface:** LimitsMiddleware (app/api/main.py:279-307), _claim_delivery (app/api/main.py:3011-3027)
- **Severity:** medium · **Confidence:** medium
- **Evidence:** Real protections exist and apply to webhook POSTs: body-size cap via copilot_max_body_bytes (main.py:297-299) and per-client-IP rate limiting on all POSTs (main.py:300-306), plus SHA-256 body dedup with a 600s TTL and a 5000-entry bound for the root PagerDuty/Slack routes. However the dedup dict `_SEEN_DELIVERIES` is process-local (dies on restart, not shared across workers), and the dedup check on /webhooks/pagerduty sits *after* the `active_api_key()` gate (main.py:3077-3081) so it is only exercised when an LLM key is present — my duplicate POST returned `accepted_no_llm` twice. None of the /api/webhooks/* routes participate in dedup at all. Per-IP rate limiting is also a poor fit for webhook providers, which deliver from a small shared egress range and would be throttled collectively.
- **Blocker:** Idempotency is in-process only and bypassed on the no-LLM path; the /api/webhooks/* family has none.
- **Fix:** Move delivery dedup into shared storage keyed on the provider's delivery id (X-GitHub-Delivery, PagerDuty event id, Alertmanager fingerprint) and check it before any other gate; exempt authenticated webhook paths from the per-IP limiter.
- **Verifier:** Verified by execution, not by reading. With COPILOT_RATE_LIMIT_PER_MIN=3, six POSTs to /api/webhooks/datadog -> [200,200,200,429,429,429]; with COPILOT_MAX_BODY_BYTES=500 a 2KB body -> 413. Webhook paths are not in _OPEN_PATHS (main.py:230), so both apply. Limitations confirmed: _SEEN_DELIVERIES (main.py:3013) is a process-local dict; _claim_delivery is called AFTER the active_api_key() gate at main.py:3077-3081, so with no LLM key a duplicate delivery returns accepted_no_llm twice (I reproduced this) and no dedup happens; none of the /api/webhooks/* routes call _claim_delivery at all. Per-IP limiting also throttles a provider's shared egress range collectively.

### `WORKS` — PagerDuty webhook → auto-start AI investigation (/webhooks/pagerduty)

- **Surface:** POST /webhooks/pagerduty (app/api/main.py:3030-3083)
- **Severity:** low · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** Executed with TestClient. With PAGERDUTY_WEBHOOK_SECRET=pdsecret and a real PD v3 body signed as `v1=<hmac_sha256_hex>`: `PD-root-goodsig 200 {"status":"accepted_no_llm","incident":"PXYZ123"}`. Tampered signature: `PD-root-badsig 401 {"detail":"invalid or unconfigured PagerDuty webhook signature"}`. Signature logic is correct per PagerDuty docs (app/integrations/pagerduty.py:15-22 — comma-separated `v1=` digests, hmac.compare_digest). Parser handles the real v3 envelope (`event.event_type` / `event.data`, app/integrations/pagerduty.py:25-38). Fails closed: if no secret is configured, every request 401s (main.py:3064). Downstream is real, not simulated: _enqueue_investigation (main.py:2852) → _run_triggered_investigation (main.py:2862) builds a seed prompt and calls `session.ask(seed)` on a live agent session, then meters tokens and posts to Slack. Redelivery dedup via SHA-256 of the raw body with a 600s TTL (main.py:3011-3027). The `accepted_no_llm` status is correct behaviour here — no ANTHROPIC_API_KEY is set in this environment.
- **Verifier:** Re-ran independently. PAGERDUTY_WEBHOOK_SECRET=pdsecret: valid v1= HMAC -> 200 accepted_no_llm; tampered v1=deadbeef -> 401; header omitted -> 401 (fails closed, main.py:3064); comma-separated multi-digest header -> 200 (matches PD's documented format); incident.resolved -> {"status":"ignored","event":"incident.resolved"}. Re-ran with ANTHROPIC_API_KEY set: 1st POST -> {"status":"accepted"}, identical 2nd POST -> {"status":"duplicate"} (dedup real), and stderr shows a genuine agent session + MCP stdio subprocess starting, so _enqueue_investigation -> _run_triggered_investigation -> session.ask(seed) is a live path, not a stub. Caveats I confirmed but that do not break the claim: the result is delivered only to Slack, is never persisted, and never appears in /api/investigations; _run_triggered_investigation swallows all exceptions so the jobqueue retry/dead-letter never fires.

**Verifier notes for this domain.** I re-ran everything from scratch with TestClient (PYTHONPATH=. python3, probes in /tmp/probe1..7.py) rather than trusting the auditor's transcripts. Every one of the 14 verdicts held; none overturned. Two of the auditor's *evidence statements* are factually wrong even though the verdicts survive (see connectors / integrations_advanced below).

Independently regenerated key results:
- /webhooks/pagerduty: good v1 sig 200, tampered 401, missing header 401, comma-separated multi-digest header 200, incident.resolved -> {"status":"ignored"}. With ANTHROPIC_API_KEY set the response flips to {"status":"accepted"} and a second identical POST returns {"status":"duplicate"} — and the logs show a real agent session + MCP subprocess actually spinning up (langchain_mcp_adapters stack traces), so the downstream is genuinely live, not simulated.
- LimitsMiddleware verified by execution, not by reading: COPILOT_RATE_LIMIT_PER_MIN=3 -> [200,200,200,429,429,429] on /api/webhooks/datadog; COPILOT_MAX_BODY_BYTES=500 -> 413. Webhook paths are not in _OPEN_PATHS (app/api/main.py:230), so the caps do apply.

THINGS THE AUDITOR MISSED ENTIRELY (all material, all in this domain):

1. /api/investigations is permanently empty and never shows a PagerDuty-triggered investigation. app/api/main.py:2118 does `from app.api.graphql.schema import _investigations`. `grep -n '_investigations\[' app/api/graphql/schema.py` -> exactly one write, line 330, inside the GraphQL `startInvestigation` mutation — and strawberry-graphql is not installed (the app logs "GraphQL endpoint unavailable" at import). So there are THREE disconnected investigation/incident stores: app/incidents/store.py (webhook incidents), app/api/graphql/schema.py:_investigations (what the history endpoint reads), and the PagerDuty agent-session path (writes to neither). The one path the auditor called WORKS produces output that lands only in Slack; if slack_bot_token/slack_channel are unset, `slack.post_message` is skipped (app/api/main.py:2833-2835) and the result is unobservable anywhere in the product.

2. The job-queue retry/dead-letter for triggered investigations is dead. app/api/main.py:2844 `_handle_job` docstring says "Raises on failure so the worker retries", and app/jobqueue.py:89 `process_one` only retries/dead-letters when the handler raises. But `_run_triggered_investigation` (main.py:2871-2883) ends in `except Exception: log.exception(...)` — it swallows everything. A failed investigation is therefore never retried and never dead-lettered; the durability story in the module docstring is not real.

3. Datadog is broken in a second way the auditor didn't find: Datadog's DEFAULT webhook payload template uses `$EVENT_TYPE` -> key `event_type`, not `alert_type`. `POST /api/webhooks/datadog {"event_type":"error","title":"...","body":"...","id":"1"}` -> 200 {"status":"ignored","message":"Not an alert event"}. So the out-of-the-box Datadog integration silently drops 100% of alerts with a 200 (no retry at all), and only a hand-edited template reaches the `tags` AttributeError crash.

4. GitHub webhook has an additional 500 the auditor missed: `{"ref":"refs/heads/main","commits":null,"repository":null}` with X-GitHub-Event: push -> 500, `TypeError: object of type 'NoneType' has no len()` at app/api/main.py:3254.

5. Minor: `_verify_gitlab_token`'s `return True  # No secret configured, allow` (main.py:3306) is unreachable — the caller already guards with `if s.gitlab_webhook_secret and ...`. The fail-open is caused by the caller's guard, not by that line. Same fail-open shape at main.py:3229 for GitHub. Both secrets default to "" (app/config.py:115,117 — confirmed at runtime: gh secret='' gl secret='').

6. Test coverage nuance: `tests/test_webhooks.py` DOES exist and covers the root /webhooks/pagerduty + /webhooks/slack/interactions routes (6 passed, 4.84s). Zero tests still touch /api/webhooks/* or /webhooks/{github,gitlab}.


## Knowledge base, knowledge graph, GraphQL, search/embeddings

**Summary.** The knowledge base is a working CRUD surface over a process-local Python dict — there is no table, no migration, and no persistence of any kind, so every document a customer writes vanishes on restart and is invisible to other workers; it is also completely unauthenticated and un-tenanted, so any anonymous caller can read and delete every org's documents. "Semantic search" is a bag-of-words vector whose cosine similarity is mathematically meaningless (two documents with zero words in common score 0.866), so the search endpoint returns high-confidence false positives for queries that match nothing. The knowledge graph is the worst offender: node/edge writes are accepted and counted, but the two flagship read paths — `/dependencies/{id}`, `/blast-radius/{id}` — and `/timeline/{id}` are hardcoded to return empty results whenever Neo4j is absent, and Neo4j can never be present because the `neo4j` package is not installed AND `app/config.py` defines no neo4j settings at all, so URI/credentials are unconfigurable. GraphQL is doubly dead: strawberry-graphql isn't installed, and even if it were, `include_graphql_router()` is called after the parent router was already mounted, which FastAPI ignores. The one genuinely working piece of search in this domain is `app/incident_memory.py`'s BM25 ranking over a JSON corpus, which is wired into the live investigation graph. There are zero tests anywhere in tests/ for `app/knowledge_base` or `app/knowledge_graph`.

**Wiring.** LIVE: `app/knowledge_base/api_router.py` (mounted at /api/knowledge, app/api/main.py:734) and `app/knowledge_graph/api_router.py` (mounted at /api/knowledge-graph, app/api/main.py:747). All 10 KB routes and all 20 KG routes return 200 unauthenticated. `app/knowledge_graph/neo4j_backend.py` and `sync_engine.py` are live behind those routes. `app/incident_memory.py` + `app/vector_store.py` are live via app/graph/nodes.py:107 -> app/session.py -> the investigate endpoint, and via the MCP memory server.
NOT WIRED / NO ENTRYPOINT: `app/api/graphql/` (schema.py, types.py) — nothing in app/ imports it and `import app.api.graphql` raises ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'strawberry'. `app/knowledge_graph/graphql_schema.py` — only imported inside `_init_graphql()`, which ImportErrors; and its mount point is a no-op (see finding). `app/similar_search/` (8 modules, ~3400 lines, incl. embeddings.py with a real OpenAI path) — grep shows zero Python importers outside the package; the only "similar_search" hits are billing string constants. `app/learning/knowledge_graph.py` — imported only by tests/test_advanced_differentiators.py. `app/graph/` is the LangGraph agent graph (different concern), and IS live via app/session.py.


### `BROKEN` — Neo4j-backed graph persistence

- **Surface:** All 20 /api/knowledge-graph/* routes; app/knowledge_graph/neo4j_backend.py
- **Severity:** critical · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** `python3 -c 'import neo4j'` -> ModuleNotFoundError (no neo4j in the env). GET /api/knowledge-graph/stats -> {"connected":false,"mode":"in-memory",...}. Worse, the connection is unconfigurable: app/knowledge_graph/api_router.py:41-46 reads `getattr(settings, 'neo4j_uri', 'bolt://localhost:7687')` etc., and `grep -rn neo4j app/config.py` returns NOTHING. Verified: `NEO4J_URI=bolt://prod:7687 COPILOT_NEO4J_URI=bolt://prod:7687 python3 -c "from app.config import get_settings; print(getattr(get_settings(),'neo4j_uri','<<MISSING>>'))"` -> `<<MISSING -> default bolt://localhost:7687>>`. Password defaults to empty string.
- **Blocker:** Even after `pip install neo4j`, there is no settings field for neo4j_uri/username/password/database, so every deployment would try bolt://localhost:7687 with an empty password and fail, silently dropping back to the in-memory fallback (neo4j_backend.py:301-306 catches all exceptions and logs a warning). Graph data therefore lives in per-process dicts and dies on restart.
- **Fix:** Add neo4j_uri/username/password/database to app/config.py Settings, add the neo4j driver to dependencies, and surface connection failure (e.g. a degraded flag on /stats plus 503 on traversal routes) instead of silently returning empty graphs.

### `BROKEN` — Test coverage for the knowledge domain

- **Surface:** tests/
- **Severity:** high · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** `ls tests | grep -iE 'knowledge|graph|search|embed|rag'` -> no matches. `grep -rln 'knowledge_base|knowledge_graph|knowledge/documents|knowledge-graph|similar_search' tests/*.py tests/*/*.py` -> only test_advanced_differentiators.py (which tests app/learning/knowledge_graph, the dead one), test_billing_api.py / test_billing_usage_tracker.py (billing string constants) and two verification tests. Zero tests exercise app/knowledge_base/api_router.py, app/knowledge_base/rag.py, app/knowledge_graph/api_router.py, neo4j_backend.py or sync_engine.py — ~3700 lines of live, route-exposed code with no test at all.
- **Blocker:** None of the defects above (empty blast radius, resolved_at-on-open-incident, 200-on-not-found, junk similarity) would be caught by CI.
- **Fix:** Add a TestClient suite that builds the 3-node graph and asserts blast-radius affected_count == 2 with the correct impact scores, plus a KB test asserting a no-match query returns zero results.

### `BROKEN` — GraphQL API for the knowledge graph

- **Surface:** GET /api/knowledge-graph/graphql/status; intended /api/knowledge-graph/graphql; app/knowledge_graph/graphql_schema.py
- **Severity:** medium · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** GET /api/knowledge-graph/graphql/status -> 200 {"available":false,"message":"Install strawberry-graphql[fastapi] to enable GraphQL"}; POST and GET /api/knowledge-graph/graphql -> 404. `import strawberry` -> ModuleNotFoundError. Second, independent defect: app/api/main.py:747-748 does `app.include_router(knowledge_graph_router, prefix='/api')` and THEN `include_graphql_router(knowledge_graph_router)`, which calls `parent_router.include_router(gql, prefix='/graphql')` at app/knowledge_graph/api_router.py:506-510. FastAPI copies routes at include time, so mutating the sub-router afterwards is a no-op. Proved with a minimal repro: including a child router into an already-mounted parent produced routes ['/api/kg/x'] only — the late '/api/kg/graphql/late' never appeared.
- **Blocker:** Two stacked blockers: strawberry-graphql is not installed, and the mount ordering bug means installing it still would not expose the endpoint. The 680-line schema in graphql_schema.py (whose resolvers do map onto real backend methods) is therefore unreachable.
- **Fix:** Install strawberry-graphql[fastapi], and move `include_graphql_router(knowledge_graph_router)` to BEFORE `app.include_router(knowledge_graph_router, prefix='/api')` (or mount the GraphQLRouter directly on `app`). Note the resolvers call find_dependencies/find_blast_radius, so they inherit the empty-fallback bug above.

### `STUB` — Knowledge base document CRUD

- **Surface:** GET/POST /api/knowledge/documents, GET/PUT/DELETE /api/knowledge/documents/{id}; frontend/src/pages/KnowledgeBase.tsx
- **Severity:** critical · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** Ran TestClient: POST /api/knowledge/documents -> 200 {"id":"..."}; GET /api/knowledge/documents -> 200 with 5 seeded + created docs; GET /api/knowledge/stats -> {"total_documents":5,...}; DELETE -> 200. But storage is app/knowledge_base/api_router.py:22 `_kb: KnowledgeBase | None = None` -> app/knowledge_base/rag.py:102 `self._documents: dict[str, Document] = {}`. `grep -rn '__tablename__' app/database/*.py` lists organizations, api_keys, members, investigations, investigation_events, audit_logs, alert_rules, alert_events, runbook_executions, usage_records, teams, team_members, team_invitations — no documents/knowledge/embeddings table. No migration in app/database/migrations/versions either. Also no auth: every call above succeeded with no token, and app/api/main.py:680-693 adds only LimitsMiddleware/GZip/CORS — no auth middleware, and the router declares no Depends(...) for auth or org scoping.
- **Blocker:** Documents live in a module-global dict. They are lost on every process restart, are not shared between uvicorn/gunicorn workers (a doc created on worker 1 404s on worker 2), and are global across all organizations with no authentication — any anonymous caller can list, edit and DELETE every tenant's runbooks. The 5 'seeded' docs also reappear after being deleted, on the next restart.
- **Fix:** Add a `knowledge_documents` SQLAlchemy model + Alembic migration with an org_id FK, replace `kb._documents` with repository calls, and put the router behind the same auth/org dependency the teams/incidents routers use.

### `STUB` — Blast radius calculation

- **Surface:** GET /api/knowledge-graph/blast-radius/{service_id}
- **Severity:** critical · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** Built a real 3-node dependency chain through the API: POST /services svc-a(criticality .9), svc-b(.8), svc-c(.7) all 200; POST /relationships {svc-b -> svc-a, DEPENDS_ON} -> 200; {svc-c -> svc-b, DEPENDS_ON} -> 200. GET /api/knowledge-graph/stats confirms the writes landed: {"connected":false,"mode":"in-memory","node_counts":{"Service":3},"relationship_count":2}. Then GET /api/knowledge-graph/blast-radius/svc-a -> 200 {"source_service":"svc-a","affected_count":0,"total_impact_score":0.0,"affected_services":[]}. Correct answer is 2 affected (svc-b at distance 1, svc-c at distance 2, impact 0.8 + 0.35 = 1.15). Cause: app/knowledge_graph/neo4j_backend.py:734-740 — the non-Neo4j branch is a hardcoded empty literal; the real Cypher traversal at :704-733 is unreachable. The `_in_memory_rels` list built at :642 is written but never read by any traversal.
- **Blocker:** Neo4j is the only code path that computes anything. `import neo4j` -> ModuleNotFoundError on this machine, so connect() falls through app/knowledge_graph/neo4j_backend.py:294-299 and every traversal returns a canned zero. The endpoint 200s and looks alive.
- **Fix:** Implement the in-memory branch as a real BFS over `_in_memory_rels` (filter rel_type in DEPENDS_ON/CALLS, reverse direction, min distance, impact = criticality/distance), or make the endpoint return 503 when `_connected` is False instead of a fake empty answer.

### `STUB` — Knowledge base semantic search

- **Surface:** POST /api/knowledge/search
- **Severity:** high · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** app/knowledge_base/rag.py:129-145 `_generate_embedding` builds a 128-dim vector by taking `sorted(word_freq.items())[:128]` and writing freq at index i — the index is the alphabetical rank of the word, not a semantic dimension, so unrelated words collide on the same slot. Executed: `kb._cosine_similarity(kb._generate_embedding('zebra yak xylophone'), kb._generate_embedding('completely different words here'))` -> 0.8660254037844387 for two texts with ZERO shared vocabulary. Consequence, executed against the seeded corpus: `await kb.search('zzz nonexistent term')` -> [('kb-005', 0.388), ('kb-002', 0.342), ('kb-004', 0.339)] — three confident hits for a query that matches nothing. And `search('the')` -> kb-003 at 0.619. rag.py:198 blends this junk at 60% weight with a real (but crude) keyword-overlap score at 40%; rag.py:200 threshold 0.1 is far below the ~0.34 floor the junk term produces. No embedding is ever sent to an embedding model: the only real embedding code in the repo is app/vector_store.py (chromadb/faiss, `copilot_vector_store: str = 'none'` at app/config.py:397, and `import chromadb` -> ModuleNotFoundError) and app/similar_search/embeddings.py, neither of which the KB imports.
- **Blocker:** The 'semantic' half of the score is a positional bag-of-words artifact that is high for arbitrary unrelated text, so search returns irrelevant runbooks with plausible-looking relevance_score for essentially any query. Embeddings are computed but only into the same in-memory dict (rag.py:103) — never persisted, never model-generated.
- **Fix:** Drop `_generate_embedding`/`_cosine_similarity` and route the KB through the existing `app/vector_store.py` hybrid_search (or `app/incident_memory.bm25_rank`, which already works), persisting embeddings alongside the documents table.

### `STUB` — Service dependency graph query

- **Surface:** GET /api/knowledge-graph/dependencies/{service_id}
- **Severity:** high · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** Same graph as above. GET /api/knowledge-graph/dependencies/svc-a -> 200 {"service_id":"svc-a","direction":"both","max_depth":3,"dependencies":[]} despite relationship_count == 2. app/knowledge_graph/neo4j_backend.py:688-694 returns `"dependencies": []` unconditionally in the no-session branch.
- **Blocker:** Identical to blast-radius: the fallback branch is a hardcoded empty result, and Neo4j is unavailable.
- **Fix:** Traverse `_in_memory_rels` in the fallback branch honouring the `direction` and `depth` arguments.

### `STUB` — Service event timeline

- **Surface:** GET /api/knowledge-graph/timeline/{service_id}
- **Severity:** high · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** Created svc-a, then POST /deployments d1 (200), POST /alerts a1 for svc-a (200), POST /incidents i1 affecting svc-a (200); GET /stats shows node_counts {Service:1, Deployment:1, Incident:1, Change:1, Alert:1}. GET /api/knowledge-graph/timeline/svc-a -> 200 {"service_id":"svc-a","hours":24,"events":[],"count":0}. Source: app/knowledge_graph/neo4j_backend.py:850-851 `else: return []`.
- **Blocker:** Fallback returns a literal empty list; the nodes exist in `_in_memory_nodes` and are simply never scanned.
- **Fix:** In the fallback branch, scan `_in_memory_nodes` for Deployment/Alert/Incident entries referencing the service within the cutoff and build the same event list the Neo4j branch does.

### `DEAD_CODE` — Similar-incident search / embeddings engine (app/similar_search)

- **Surface:** app/similar_search/{search,embeddings,similarity,patterns,recommendations,knowledge_graph,models}.py — no route, no CLI
- **Severity:** medium · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** `grep -rn similar_search app tests frontend` outside the package yields only billing plumbing: app/billing/models.py:64 `SIMILAR_SEARCH = 'similar_search'`, usage_tracker counters, and frontend/src/types/billing.ts. No Python module imports app.similar_search. `grep -rn APIRouter app/similar_search/` -> no matches. Ironically this is the better implementation: app/similar_search/embeddings.py:98-111 has a genuine OpenAI text-embedding-3-small call and :113-132 a proper normalised hashing fallback — far more defensible than the KB's alphabetical bag-of-words. ~3400 lines total.
- **Blocker:** Nothing can reach it. Billing meters a 'similar_search' feature that has no implementation behind any endpoint.
- **Fix:** Either mount a router over SimilarIncidentSearch (and point the KB's /search at IncidentEmbedder instead of rag._generate_embedding), or delete the package and the billing feature key.
- **Verifier:** Re-grepped `similar_search` across app/, tests/ and frontend/src: every hit outside the package is a billing string or counter (app/billing/models.py:64 SIMILAR_SEARCH, usage_tracker.py:159 track_similar_search, feature_gate.py:202, billing/api.py:515, frontend/src/types/billing.ts:31). Zero Python modules import app.similar_search. `grep -rn APIRouter app/similar_search/` -> no matches, so it cannot be mounted anywhere. Also checked for dynamic entry points: not in any include_router, not in a lifespan handler, not referenced from app/mcp. Confirmed DEAD_CODE, and confirmed the irony - app/similar_search/embeddings.py has the only real embedding implementation in the KB/search area while the reachable one (rag.py) is the fake.

- **Severity:** low · **Confidence:** high

Nothing imports app.api.graphql. `python3 -c 'import app.api.graphql'` -> ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'strawberry'. No route in the 292 live routes touches it.
- **Blocker:** No entrypoint at all, and the package cannot even be imported.

### `DEAD_CODE` — Second in-memory incident knowledge graph (app/learning)

- **Surface:** app/learning/knowledge_graph.py (457 lines)
- **Severity:** low · **Confidence:** high

Storage is `self._nodes: dict[str, KnowledgeNode]` at line 115. This is the third parallel knowledge-graph implementation in the repo (with app/knowledge_graph and app/similar_search/knowledge_graph).
