### `PARTIAL` — Knowledge graph node CRUD (services, deployments, incidents, changes, alerts, relationships)

- **Surface:** POST /api/knowledge-graph/{services,deployments,incidents,changes,alerts,relationships}, GET /services, /services/{id}, /incidents/{id}, /deployments/recent, /stats
- **Severity:** high · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** Writes and reads round-trip within a process: POST /services svc-a -> 200; GET /services -> full record; POST /deployments d1 -> 200; GET /deployments/recent -> {"count":1} with the real record; GET /stats -> {"connected":false,"mode":"in-memory","node_counts":{...},"relationship_count":2}. Two concrete correctness bugs: (1) app/knowledge_graph/neo4j_backend.py:1065-1068 `_parse_datetime(None)` returns `datetime.now()`, and :1004-1006 feeds resolved_at/detected_at/acknowledged_at through it — so POST /incidents {status:'open'} reads back as {"status":"open","resolved_at":"2026-08-24T00:34:11.989398+00:00","detected_at":...,"acknowledged_at":...}, i.e. every open incident looks resolved and `ttr_seconds` (:166-169) computes ~0. Verified by running exactly that POST/GET pair. (2) GET /api/knowledge-graph/services/nope returns HTTP 200 with body {"error":"Service not found"} (api_router.py:185-186), not 404 — same for /incidents/{id}; clients cannot distinguish missing from present.
- **Blocker:** Data is process-local and non-durable (see the Neo4j finding), open incidents are reported as resolved with TTR 0, and not-found is signalled as 200.
- **Fix:** Make `_parse_datetime` return `None` for a `None` input (add a nullable variant for the optional timestamp fields), raise HTTPException(404) on missing nodes, and back the store with a real database.
- **Verifier:** Round-trip verified: POST /services svc-a -> full record echoed; GET /services/svc-a -> same; POST /deployments d1 -> 200 and GET /deployments/recent -> {'count':1} with the real record; /stats node_counts correct. Both bugs reproduced exactly. (1) POST /incidents {'id':'i1','title':'t','status':'open'} then GET /incidents/i1 -> {'status':'open','resolved_at':'2026-08-24T01:20:42.749617+00:00','detected_at':...,'acknowledged_at':...}. Root cause read in source: _parse_datetime (neo4j_backend.py:1065) starts `if value is None: return datetime.now(timezone.utc)`, and _dict_to_incident feeds resolved_at/detected_at/acknowledged_at through it, so an unresolved incident is stamped resolved at read time. (2) GET /services/nope -> HTTP 200 {'error':'Service not found'} and GET /incidents/nope -> HTTP 200 {'error':'Incident not found'} (api_router.py get_service: `if not service: return {'error': ...}`) - no 404. Additional note: POST /relationships requires source_id/target_id; the auditor's from_id/to_id payload returns 422.

### `PARTIAL` — Continuous sync from GitHub / CloudTrail / Prometheus / Kubernetes / Jaeger

- **Surface:** POST /api/knowledge-graph/sync/{configure,start,stop,once}, GET /api/knowledge-graph/sync/status
- **Severity:** medium · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** This is NOT a no-op task — the syncers make real HTTP calls. Executed: POST /sync/configure {source: prometheus, urls to 127.0.0.1:59999} -> {"status":"configured"}; POST /sync/once -> {"status":"completed","results":{"prometheus":0}} and the log shows a genuine network attempt: `ERROR omnipotent.knowledge_graph.sync :: Prometheus fetch error: All connection attempts failed`. Source confirms real API clients: app/knowledge_graph/sync_engine.py:188-206 (GitHub httpx client against api.github.com with a bearer token), :697 (Alertmanager /api/v2/alerts), :742 (Prometheus /api/v1/targets), plus CloudTrail (boto3) and Kubernetes syncers. But: POST /sync/once with no sources -> {"results":{}} and POST /sync/start with no sources -> {"status":"started"} then GET /sync/status -> {"running":true,"sources":{}} — 'running' with nothing to run. And after the connection failure above, status reported {"status":"idle","errors":0,"items_synced":0} because fetch_events swallows the exception at sync_engine.py:757-758 before sync_once's error accounting at :136-141 can see it.
- **Blocker:** Everything it syncs is written into the in-memory graph fallback, so it is lost on restart and invisible to the (broken) traversal queries. Source configuration is per-process and non-persistent — a restart silently drops all configured sources while /sync/status keeps claiming success. A completely unreachable source reports status 'idle' with errors=0, so operators cannot tell sync is dead.
- **Fix:** Persist source configs, let fetch_events propagate exceptions (or increment state.errors and set status='error'), and refuse /sync/start when `self.syncers` is empty.
- **Verifier:** Reproduced: POST /sync/configure {source:'prometheus', urls -> 127.0.0.1:59999} -> {'status':'configured'}; POST /sync/once -> {'status':'completed','results':{'prometheus':0}} and the log shows a genuine outbound attempt: 'ERROR omnipotent.knowledge_graph.sync :: Prometheus fetch error: All connection attempts failed'. So it is real HTTP, not a no-op. Confirmed the error-swallowing: PrometheusSyncer.fetch_events wraps the whole body in try/except and ends `except Exception as e: log.error(...)` then `return events, ''`, so sync_once's `except` at :136-141 (which would set status='error' and errors+=1) never fires - GET /sync/status after the total failure returned {'status':'idle','errors':0,'items_synced':0}. Also confirmed POST /sync/once with no sources -> {'results':{}} and POST /sync/start with no sources -> {'status':'started'} then status {'running':true,'sources':{}}. ADDITION the auditor missed: boto3 is NOT installed either, so the CloudTrail syncer (sync_engine.py:494) is equally unavailable, not a 'real API client' in this env. And everything synced lands in the in-memory fallback that the traversals never read.

### `PARTIAL` — Changes-before-incident correlation

- **Surface:** GET /api/knowledge-graph/changes-before-incident/{incident_id}
- **Severity:** medium · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** This one actually has a working in-memory implementation (app/knowledge_graph/neo4j_backend.py:774-783). Executed: POST /changes ch1 -> 200, sleep 0.2s, POST /incidents i9 -> 200, GET /changes-before-incident/i9 -> {"count":1, changes:[{"id":"ch1","change_type":"config",...}]}. Correct time-window filtering. However neither the fallback nor the Cypher at :758-766 actually filters by service overlap — the Cypher OPTIONAL MATCHes it and only sorts by it, the fallback ignores it entirely — so it returns every change in the org within the window.
- **Blocker:** No relevance filtering to the incident's affected services, and it depends on the same volatile in-memory node store (changes disappear on restart).
- **Fix:** Filter/rank fallback results by intersection of change.affected_resources with incident.affected_services, and back the store with a real datastore.

### `PARTIAL` — Knowledge base UI (article list / read / create / delete)

- **Surface:** frontend/src/pages/KnowledgeBase.tsx
- **Severity:** medium · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** The page calls only three endpoints: GET /api/knowledge/documents (line 463), POST /api/knowledge/documents (524), DELETE /api/knowledge/documents/{id} (546). It never calls GET /documents/{id}, /search, /tags or /stats. The article reader renders `article.content` straight from the LIST payload (lines 251, 135), but app/knowledge_base/rag.py:44 truncates list content to 500 chars + '...'. Proved: created a 900-char doc, GET /documents returned content length 503 ending 'XX...', while GET /documents/{id} returned 900. So any runbook longer than 500 characters is silently cut off mid-sentence in the reader — including 4 of the 5 seeded runbooks. Separately, app/api/main.py:691 sets `allow_methods=['GET','POST','OPTIONS']`, so the DELETE at line 546 is blocked by CORS preflight in any deployment where VITE_API_URL points at a different origin (line 31: `const API_BASE = import.meta.env.VITE_API_URL || ''`). The PUT /documents/{id} endpoint has no UI at all — there is no edit path. 'Starred' is localStorage-only (lines 497-510).
- **Blocker:** Article bodies are truncated to 500 chars in the reader; cross-origin deletes fail CORS preflight; no edit UI despite a working PUT endpoint; the /search endpoint the backend advertises is unused (UI search is the substring scan at api_router.py:220-222).
- **Fix:** Fetch GET /documents/{id} when opening an article; add DELETE and PUT to CORS allow_methods; wire an edit modal to PUT.

### `UNVERIFIABLE` — Vector store / hybrid RRF search backend

- **Surface:** app/vector_store.py, reached only from app/incident_memory.py:115,233
- **Severity:** low · **Confidence:** medium
- **Evidence:** Real implementation: OpenAI embedding backend at app/vector_store.py:104-140 (`client.embeddings.create`), chromadb backend at :231-240, RRF hybrid_search consumed by incident_memory. But `is_vector_enabled()` returned False in my run because app/config.py:397 `copilot_vector_store: str = 'none'`, and both `import chromadb` and `import faiss` raise ModuleNotFoundError here (openai IS installed). So the enabled path could not be exercised.
- **Blocker:** Needs `pip install chromadb` (or faiss) + COPILOT_VECTOR_STORE set + OPENAI_API_KEY to run at all. Disabled-by-default is a deliberate, honest fallback to BM25 — not a fake — but I could not execute the vector path.
- **Fix:** To verify: install chromadb, set copilot_vector_store=chromadb and OPENAI_API_KEY, then assert hybrid_search returns different (better) ordering than bm25_rank alone. Note this real machinery is NOT used by /api/knowledge/search, which is where a paying user would expect it.

### `WORKS` — BM25 similar-incident retrieval (institutional memory)

- **Surface:** app/incident_memory.py, consumed live by app/graph/nodes.py:107 (_prior_incidents_block) via app/session.py -> investigate endpoint; also app/mcp/servers/memory/server.py
- **Severity:** low · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** Executed `from app import incident_memory as m; m.load_corpus()` -> 7 records loaded from app/mcp/servers/memory/corpus.json + learned_incidents.json; `m.search('checkout 5xx errors after deploy', limit=3)` -> [5.854 INC-2026-0412 'checkout-svc 5xx spike after discount feature deploy', 2.163 INC-2025-1042 'gateway 5xx from a bad config rollout', 1.739 LEARNED-...]. Real BM25 with k1/b at incident_memory.py:70, correct discriminative ranking (top hit scores 2.7x the second). Persistence is a real JSON file on disk (learned_incidents.json, written at :191-198) and learn_from_report at :204 is called from the live graph at app/graph/nodes.py:1087. Optional vector augmentation at :115 gates on app/vector_store.is_vector_enabled(), which returned False here (copilot_vector_store defaults to 'none' at app/config.py:397; chromadb and faiss are both not installed).

**Verifier notes for this domain.** I re-ran everything from scratch with TestClient and direct module calls; I did not reuse any of the auditor's output.

WHAT THE AUDITOR MISSED (material):

1. **GET /api/investigations is a live 500 — and it is exactly the "dead" GraphQL package.** `/Users/ankitpandey/OmniPotent/OmniPotent/app/api/main.py:2118` inside `list_investigations` does `from app.api.graphql.schema import _investigations`. That triggers `app/api/graphql/__init__.py:11` -> `schema.py:12 import strawberry` -> ModuleNotFoundError. Verified: `GET /api/investigations` -> **500** `{"error":"INTERNAL_ERROR"}` with the ModuleNotFoundError in the traceback. This is called by the live UI at `/Users/ankitpandey/OmniPotent/OmniPotent/frontend/src/pages/History.tsx:72` (`fetch('/api/investigations?limit=50')`). So the History page is entirely broken in a base install, and `app/api/graphql` is NOT dead code — it is a live, always-failing dependency. This was introduced by the two most recent commits (4266677 / e5ec716).

2. **The "no auth" evidence is wrong.** `app.router.dependencies` is `[Depends(require_auth)]` (defined at `app/api/main.py:417`). It is off only because `COPILOT_API_TOKEN` is unset in this dev env. Verified: with `COPILOT_API_TOKEN=secret123`, `GET /api/knowledge/documents` -> 401 without a token, 401 with a wrong token, 200 with the right one; `GET /api/knowledge-graph/stats` -> 401. The auditor's "any anonymous caller can DELETE every tenant's runbooks" is false for a configured deployment.
   BUT the tenancy half survives and is worse than described: `grep -rn "tenant\|org_id" app/knowledge_base/ app/knowledge_graph/` returns **nothing**. In `copilot_multi_tenant` mode every authenticated tenant shares one global `_kb` dict and one global `_graph`, so it is a genuine cross-tenant data leak, not an anonymous-access one.

3. **The auditor's relationship payload was wrong.** `RelationshipInput` (api_router.py:143) is `source_id`/`target_id`, not `from_id`/`to_id`; their quoted payload 422s. I re-ran with the correct shape (rel writes -> 200, `relationship_count: 2`) and the blast-radius/dependencies results were still empty, so their conclusion holds — but their quoted evidence could not have produced the output they reported.

4. `strawberry-graphql`, `neo4j`, `chromadb`, `faiss-cpu` are declared *optional extras* in `pyproject.toml:88-102`, not missing deps. That does not rescue the KG traversals (the fallback branches are hardcoded literals regardless) but it does mean the neo4j "unconfigurable" finding is the real blocker, and it is confirmed: `grep -n "neo4j" app/config.py` returns nothing.

5. `boto3` is also not installed, so the CloudTrail syncer (`sync_engine.py:494`) is unavailable too — the auditor called it a "real API client" without checking.

6. KB search is worse than reported: `POST /api/knowledge/search {"query":"zzz nonexistent term"}` returned **all 6** documents (`total: 6`), top score 0.388, `matched_terms: []` on every one.

7. `POST /api/knowledge-graph/graphql` returns **405**, not 404 — the SPA catch-all GET route matches the path. Cosmetic, but it means a client sees "method not allowed" rather than "no such endpoint".

COMMANDS I RAN: full KB CRUD + search + stats + tags round trip; 3-node KG dependency chain with correct payloads then blast-radius/dependencies/timeline/changes-before-incident/incidents-get/not-found; sync configure/once/start/stop/status against 127.0.0.1:59999; a standalone FastAPI repro of the late `include_router` no-op (produced `['/api/kg/x']` only); `kb._cosine_similarity` on disjoint vocabularies (0.866); `incident_memory.load_corpus/search` on three queries; `vector_store.hybrid_search` with an injected fake store to force the vectors-enabled RRF branch; `pytest tests/test_vector_store.py tests/test_learning.py` (22 passed).


## Automation: Runbooks, Playbooks, Planning/DAG decomposition, Coordinator execution

**Summary.** The domain splits cleanly into one genuinely-working half and one theatrical half. The DAG orchestration primitives (app/planning/execution_engine.py and app/coordinator/dag_executor.py) are real: I built 3- and 4-node DAGs and verified topological execution order, parallel fan-out, output propagation to dependents, cycle rejection, retry, and an approval gate that actually blocks on an asyncio.Future until POST .../approve resolves it. The single biggest problem is the most safety-critical endpoint in the brief: POST /api/runbooks/{id}/execute executes nothing at all — it writes a dict to a module-level `_executions` map and returns `steps: []`; the `background_tasks` parameter it accepts is never used, and app/runbooks/executor.py (the only thing that could run steps) is never imported by the router. Approving that execution just flips a string to "running" and it stays there forever. Even if it were wired, that executor is 100% simulated (every step type returns an f-string like "Command executed: {action}"). Meanwhile the code that DOES contain real `subprocess.run(shell=True)` / `create_subprocess_shell` (app/runbook_builder/, app/runbook/) is completely unreferenced dead code, as are app/playbook_marketplace/, app/sandbox/, app/safety_gates/, app/tasks/ and app/coordinator/replanner.py. A paying user can list seeded runbooks and click Execute and get a green 200, but nothing happens, the execution list on the dashboard silently 404s due to route shadowing, and saving an edited runbook 405s. Nothing in this domain persists across a restart — there is a `runbook_executions` SQLAlchemy table and migration that no code writes to.

**Wiring.** LIVE (registered in app/api/main.py): app/runbooks/router.py at /api/runbooks (main.py:730), app/planning/api_router.py at /api/planning (main.py:752), app/coordinator/api_router.py at /api/coordinator (main.py:756). All 38 domain routes resolve. NOT WIRED (zero importers outside their own package, verified by grep -rn "app.<pkg>" --include=*.py app | grep -v "^app/<pkg>/"): app/runbook/ (its own __init__ only), app/runbook_builder/ (only tests/test_launch_features.py), app/playbook_marketplace/ (only its own __init__), app/sandbox/ (nothing at all; also `import kubernetes` fails - module not installed), app/safety_gates/ (only app/agents/parallel_integration.py, which itself has zero importers), app/tasks/ (dramatiq is installed but no app code imports app.tasks). Partially wired: app/runbooks/executor.py is NOT used by the HTTP router; it is only reachable through app/mcp/servers/runbooks/server.py, which is spawned by app/mcp/client.py:224 for CopilotSession, which is only constructed from app/cli.py:70 — i.e. CLI-only. app/coordinator/replanner.py DynamicReplanner is instantiated in api_router.py:80 and then never referenced by any endpoint; app/coordinator/decision_integration.py is imported only by app/coordinator/__init__.py. Frontend: only Dashboard.tsx and RunbookBuilder.tsx call this domain (/api/runbooks*); no UI calls /api/planning or /api/coordinator at all.


### `BROKEN` — List runbook executions (GET /api/runbooks/executions)

- **Surface:** GET /api/runbooks/executions; consumed by frontend/src/pages/Dashboard.tsx:465
- **Severity:** high · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** Route-ordering bug: `@router.get("/{runbook_id}")` is declared at app/runbooks/router.py:110 and `@router.get("/executions")` at router.py:237, so FastAPI matches the path param first. Ran: TestClient GET /api/runbooks/executions -> 404 {"detail":"Runbook not found"} (the literal detail string from get_runbook). Dashboard.tsx:479 guards with `if (executionsRes.value.ok)` so the UI silently shows an empty execution history forever.
- **Blocker:** The endpoint is unreachable; the only listing of runbook executions in the product always 404s.
- **Fix:** Move the `@router.get("/executions")` decorator/function above `@router.get("/{runbook_id}")` in app/runbooks/router.py (declaration order determines match order in FastAPI).
- **Verifier:** Route order confirmed: @router.get('/{runbook_id}') at app/runbooks/router.py:120, @router.get('/executions') at :238. Ran TestClient GET /api/runbooks/executions -> 404 {"detail":"Runbook not found"} (the literal string from get_runbook). Tried no auth variants — the router has no auth dependency, so a 401 misread is not possible. frontend/src/pages/Dashboard.tsx:478 guards with `executionsRes.value.ok`, so the UI shows an empty history forever.

### `STUB` — Execute a runbook (POST /api/runbooks/{id}/execute)

- **Surface:** POST /api/runbooks/{runbook_id}/execute; frontend/src/pages/Dashboard.tsx:532
- **Severity:** critical · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** app/runbooks/router.py:194-234 — the handler constructs a RunbookExecution, stores it in the module dict `_executions`, and returns. It accepts `background_tasks: BackgroundTasks` (router.py:198) and NEVER calls background_tasks.add_task. It never imports or calls RunbookExecutor; router.py:16 imports only app.runbooks.models. Ran: TestClient POST /api/runbooks/restart-service/execute {'service':'api','namespace':'prod'} -> 200 {"id":"exec-32d532f1",...,"status":"pending","completed_at":null,"steps":[]}. The seeded 'restart-service' runbook has 4 steps; zero StepExecutions were produced. No subprocess, no k8s call, no sandbox — nothing runs anywhere.
- **Blocker:** The execute endpoint is a record-creation stub. `steps` is always [] and the execution never leaves pending/running. The one component that could run steps (app/runbooks/executor.py) is not imported by the router.
- **Fix:** In execute_runbook, call `get_executor()` from app.runbooks.executor and `background_tasks.add_task(...)` the coroutine, sharing a single runbook store between router and executor (today the router has `_runbooks` and the executor has its own `self._runbooks`, so IDs created via POST /api/runbooks are invisible to the executor). Then replace the simulated step handlers with real ones.
- **Verifier:** Read app/runbooks/router.py:194-235 to its return: it builds a RunbookExecution, stores it in the module dict _executions, and returns. `background_tasks: BackgroundTasks` (line 198) is accepted and never used; the file imports only app.runbooks.models (line 16-24), never RunbookExecutor. Ran TestClient POST /api/runbooks/restart-service/execute {'service':'api','namespace':'prod'} -> 200 {'status':'pending','completed_at':None,'steps':[]}. The seeded restart-service runbook has steps but zero StepExecutions are produced.

### `STUB` — Runbook step execution engine (what actually runs a step)

- **Surface:** app/runbooks/executor.py, reachable only via `python -m app.mcp.servers.runbooks.server` spawned for CopilotSession (app/cli.py)
- **Severity:** critical · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** Every step type returns a canned string with no side effect: _exec_command (executor.py:130-133) `return True, f"Command executed: {step.action}"`; _exec_kubernetes (executor.py:135-176) returns f-strings such as "Initiated rollout restart for {deployment}" and "Deployment {deployment}: 3 replicas, Ready"; _exec_slack (executor.py:178-187) logs and returns "Posted to Slack: ..."; _exec_http (executor.py:200-205) returns "HTTP {method} {url}: 200 OK" without any httpx call. Only _exec_wait does real work (asyncio.sleep, capped at 5s). grep confirms no subprocess/httpx/kubernetes import anywhere in the file.
- **Blocker:** Steps are simulated. Because this executor IS wired into the MCP runbooks server the LLM agent uses, the agent is told remediations succeeded when nothing was done — a silent false-success in the incident-response loop.
- **Fix:** Implement _exec_command against a real sandbox/subprocess with an allowlist, _exec_kubernetes against the kubernetes client, and _exec_http against httpx; or make the demo-mode simulation explicit in the return payload (`"simulated": true`) so the agent and UI cannot mistake it for a real action.

### `STUB` — Runbook execution approve / reject gate

- **Surface:** POST /api/runbooks/executions/{id}/approve and /reject
- **Severity:** critical · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** app/runbooks/router.py:264-289 (approve) sets `exec_.status = StepStatus.RUNNING` and returns; nothing is resumed. reject (router.py:292-314) sets FAILED. Ran: POST /api/runbooks/restart-service/execute -> pending, then POST /api/runbooks/executions/exec-32d532f1/approve -> 200 {"status":"running","steps":[],"completed_at":null}. The execution remains 'running' with zero steps indefinitely. The executor's real approve() (app/runbooks/executor.py:290-341), which does resume from the paused step, is never invoked by the router because the router has no `_pending_approvals` concept at all.
- **Blocker:** Approval is cosmetic: approving does not start execution and rejecting does not cancel anything (there was nothing running to cancel).
- **Fix:** Route approve/reject into RunbookExecutor.approve/reject and have the router's execution store be the executor's store.
- **Verifier:** Read router.py:266-309: approve sets exec_.status = StepStatus.RUNNING and returns; reject sets FAILED + completed_at. Neither touches any executor. Ran: execute -> exec-4069417a pending; POST /api/runbooks/executions/exec-4069417a/approve -> 200 {'status':'running','steps':[],'completed_at':null}. The real resume logic (executor.py:294-347 approve(), which pops _pending_approvals and replays remaining steps) is unreachable from the router, which has no _pending_approvals at all.

### `STUB` — Goal decomposition (POST /api/planning/goals/decompose)

- **Surface:** POST /api/planning/goals/decompose
- **Severity:** high · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** No LLM is ever called. app/planning/api_router.py:53 constructs `GoalDecomposer(use_templates=True)` with no llm_client; decompose() at goal_decomposer.py:329 then always takes the `_apply_template` branch. Ran the same endpoint with wildly different descriptions ('AAA' vs 'BBB totally different', category=deployment) and got byte-identical sub-goals: ['Prepare Deployment','Pre-deployment Validation','Deploy to Staging','Test Staging','Deploy to Production','Post-deployment Monitoring'] — the goal text is not used at all. Only 3 of 8 GoalCategory values have templates (INCIDENT_RESPONSE, DEPLOYMENT, INVESTIGATION at goal_decomposer.py:124/182/240); category=security or migration falls through to _generate_basic_decomposition (goal_decomposer.py:472) which returns the fixed 4 steps Analyze/Plan/Execute/Verify. `confidence` is the literal 0.85 (goal_decomposer.py:356) and `reasoning` is an f-string; for security/migration it still reports 'using template approach' although no template was used.
- **Blocker:** Decomposition is a lookup table keyed on category alone. Output is independent of the user's goal text, and the LLM path (_decompose_with_llm, goal_decomposer.py:386) is unreachable from the API because llm_client is never passed.
- **Fix:** Pass a real llm_client into GoalDecomposer in api_router.get_goal_decomposer() and use templates as few-shot context rather than the answer; also fix the reasoning/confidence string to reflect the branch actually taken.
- **Verifier:** api_router.py:53 constructs GoalDecomposer(use_templates=True) with no llm_client, so decompose() (goal_decomposer.py:329) always takes _apply_template for templated categories and _decompose_with_llm->_generate_basic_decomposition otherwise (llm_client is None at :387-389). Ran the endpoint with 'AAA' vs 'BBB migrate postgres to mysql urgently', both category=deployment: the (name, description) tuples of all 6 sub-goals were IDENTICAL. GoalDecomposer.DECOMPOSITION_TEMPLATES has only 3 of the 8 GoalCategory values ['incident_response','deployment','investigation']. category=security returned the fixed 4 steps with confidence 0.85 and reasoning 'Decomposed into 4 sub-goals using template approach' although no template exists.

### `STUB` — Planning execution control: list / pause / cancel / checkpoint

- **Surface:** GET /api/planning/executions, POST /api/planning/executions/{id}/cancel, /pause, POST /api/planning/graphs/{id}/checkpoint
- **Severity:** medium · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** ExecutionEngine.execute() pops the context in a `finally` (app/planning/execution_engine.py:158-161), so _active_executions is empty except during a run; and no endpoint ever returns an execution_id, so a client can never learn the id to pass to cancel/pause. Ran: GET /api/planning/executions -> {"executions":[],"count":0}; POST /api/planning/executions/nope/cancel -> {"cancelled":false}; /pause -> {"paused":false} (200, no 404). POST /api/planning/graphs/cy/checkpoint -> 200 with a task-state dict, but TaskGraph.create_checkpoint just builds and returns that dict — nothing stores it, the engine is constructed with checkpoint_dir=None (api_router.py:41) so _create_checkpoint never persists, and there is no restore/resume endpoint (execute_from_checkpoint exists at execution_engine.py:163 but is unreachable).
- **Blocker:** There is no way to obtain an execution_id, so pause/cancel are unusable; checkpoints are generated and thrown away with no resume path.
- **Fix:** Return execution_id from /execute and keep a completed-executions registry; give ExecutionEngine a checkpoint_dir (or Redis) and expose a resume endpoint bound to execute_from_checkpoint.
- **Verifier:** execution_engine.py:155-161 pops _active_executions in a `finally`, so the map is empty except mid-run. No planning endpoint returns an execution_id — /execute returns {'status':'started','graph_id':...} and /execute/sync returns ExecutionResult.to_dict() whose key is 'plan_id' (the graph id), which cancel/pause do not accept. Ran: GET /api/planning/executions -> {'executions':[],'count':0}; POST /executions/nope/cancel -> {'cancelled':false}; /pause -> {'paused':false}, both 200 with no 404. POST /graphs/g1/checkpoint -> 200 with a task-state dict that is only built and returned (api_router.py:303-304); the engine is constructed with no checkpoint_dir (api_router.py:43) and there is no restore route (execute_from_checkpoint at execution_engine.py:163 is unreachable).

### `PARTIAL` — Synchronous DAG execution with dependency ordering (POST /api/planning/graphs/{id}/execute/sync)

- **Surface:** POST /api/planning/graphs/{id}/execute/sync and /execute (background)
- **Severity:** high · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** Ordering is REAL. I instrumented ExecutionEngine directly with recording handlers on a diamond DAG A -> {B,C} -> D and got trace [start A, end A, start B, start C, end B, end C, start D, end D], result {'status':'completed','completed_tasks':4}, and D's inputs contained dep_B/dep_C carrying B's and C's outputs which in turn carried dep_A — real output propagation (app/planning/execution_engine.py:339-345, 376-385). Over HTTP, POST /api/planning/graphs/g1/execute/sync -> 200 {"status":"completed","completed_tasks":3,"failed_tasks":0} and it really did run to completion before responding. BUT the registered tools are stubs: create_default_tools() (execution_engine.py:509-561) returns logs/metrics/traces/ci_cd/notification handlers that each return a fixed dict, e.g. {"status":"analyzed","log_entries":0,"patterns_found":[]}. Tasks with no executor hit _execute_default_handler (execution_engine.py:406) which returns {"status":"executed","tool_results":{},"inputs_received":[...]}. Executing the seeded incident graph gave all 8 tasks that echo payload in 0.0005s. Also: re-running an already-completed graph returns status 'completed' with completed_tasks=0 (verified), which is silently misleading.
- **Blocker:** The orchestration layer is sound but there is nothing real behind any tool, so a completed execution accomplishes nothing. Re-execution of a terminal graph reports success without doing anything.
- **Fix:** Register the real coordinator handlers (app/coordinator/handlers/*) or MCP tools into the planning ExecutionEngine instead of create_default_tools(); reset task states (or reject) on re-execute.

### `PARTIAL` — Coordinator DAG execution (POST /api/coordinator/dags/{id}/execute)

- **Surface:** POST /api/coordinator/dags/{dag_id}/execute
- **Severity:** high · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** Ran: POST /api/coordinator/dags/d1/execute with body {"dag_input":{...},"execute_input":{"context":{}}} -> 200 {"status":"started"}; logs showed 'Executing level 0 with 1 tasks' ... level 2, and GET /api/coordinator/executions then returned exec_f2124996 state=completed with all three task_executions completed and correct level ordering. Real level-by-level execution with a resource pool, retry policy and timeouts (dag_executor.py:597-680). Problems: (1) the {dag_id} path param is ignored — the DAG is rebuilt from dag_input (api_router.py:161-186), so you must POST the full DAG again; (2) the body requires two embedded models (dag_input + execute_input), which is undiscoverable and differs from every other endpoint; (3) `_active_executions[execution.id] = execution` happens only AFTER `await executor.execute(...)` (api_router.py:189-190), so while a DAG is running (or blocked on approval) GET /executions returns nothing and the execution is invisible; (4) _active_executions is a module dict — in-memory, per-process.
- **Blocker:** Executions are unobservable until they finish, dag_id is ignored, and nothing persists across restart.
- **Fix:** Register the DAGExecution in _active_executions before awaiting; store DAGs server-side so /dags/{id}/execute only needs the id; back the registry with Redis (DAGExecutor already supports a redis_client but api_router.py:53 passes None).
- **Verifier:** Ran POST /api/coordinator/dags/IGNORED/execute with {'dag_input':{...id:'d1'...},'execute_input':{'context':{}}} -> 200 {'status':'started','dag_id':'IGNORED'}. GET /api/coordinator/executions afterwards returned exec_61fe6fee with dag_id 'd1' (the path param is provably ignored; the DAG is rebuilt from dag_input at api_router.py:170-186) state=completed current_level=2, and task_executions t1/t2/t3 all completed with correct ordering — real level-by-level execution. Confirmed api_router.py:194-196: `_active_executions[execution.id] = execution` happens only after `await executor.execute(...)`, so a running or approval-blocked execution is invisible to GET /executions. _active_executions is a module dict (api_router.py:37). All three tasks reported duration_ms=0.

### `PARTIAL` — Coordinator human-approval gate on a DAG task

- **Surface:** POST /api/coordinator/executions/{execution_id}/tasks/{task_id}/approve; app/coordinator/dag_executor.py:762
- **Severity:** high · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** The gate is REAL. I drove DAGExecutor directly: DAG t1 -> t2(requires_approval=True). After 0.3s the trace showed only ['t1'] had run and ex._pending_approvals == ['t2'] — execution was genuinely blocked on an asyncio.Future (dag_executor.py:771-782). approve_task('t2', True) -> True, and the run then completed with both tasks 'completed'. A denial run (approve_task('u1', False)) left the handler unrun and marked the task 'skipped' (dag_executor.py:613-618). So approval both blocks and denies correctly. Weaknesses: the API's approve handler takes execution_id in the path but ignores it and keys only on task_id (api_router.py:266-268) — task ids collide across concurrent DAGs; there is no endpoint to discover which tasks are pending approval (_pending_approvals is not exposed and the execution is not in _active_executions while blocked); on timeout (task.approval_timeout_minutes) it silently denies; and a denied task leaves the overall execution state 'completed' rather than blocked/failed.
- **Blocker:** The gate works but is undiscoverable and unsafe under concurrency: no 'pending approvals' endpoint, execution_id ignored so approving task 'restart_pods' approves it in every running DAG.
- **Fix:** Key _pending_approvals on (execution_id, task_id), register executions before awaiting, and add GET /api/coordinator/approvals.
- **Verifier:** Drove DAGExecutor directly with two-arg handlers: DAG t1 -> t2(requires_approval=True). After 0.4s: trace=['t1'], ex._pending_approvals=['t2'], task.done()==False — genuinely blocked on the asyncio.Future created at dag_executor.py:771-772. approve_task('t2', True) -> True and the run finished with both tasks 'completed'. A denial run (approve_task('u1', False)) left the handler unrun, task 'skipped', but overall ExecutionState.COMPLETED. Confirmed api_router.py:264-268: the handler takes execution_id in the path and calls executor.approve_task(task_id, ...) which keys only on task_id (dag_executor.py:785-790) — collides across concurrent DAGs. _request_approval returns False on asyncio.TimeoutError (dag_executor.py:781-782), i.e. silent deny. No endpoint exposes _pending_approvals.

### `PARTIAL` — Coordinator investigation & remediation handlers (collect_logs, collect_metrics, scale_deployment, restart_pods, rollback_deployment, ...)

- **Surface:** GET /api/coordinator/handlers; used by /api/coordinator/dags/{id}/execute and /investigations
- **Severity:** high · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** GET /api/coordinator/handlers -> 200 with 12 handlers registered (collect_logs, collect_metrics, check_deployments, correlate_events, generate_hypotheses, scale_deployment, restart_pods, rollback_deployment, update_config, verify_resolution, decision_gate, execute_remediation). The handler code contains genuine Loki/Elasticsearch/Prometheus/k8s clients (app/coordinator/handlers/investigation.py:93-176). BUT api_router.get_executor() hardwires k8s_client=None, knowledge_graph=None, llm_client=None and reads `getattr(settings, "prometheus_url", None)` / `"loki_url"` (api_router.py:60-73) — and I verified those attributes DO NOT EXIST on Settings (`[k for k in Settings.model_fields if 'prom'/'loki'/'elastic' in k]` -> only kube_config_path, kube_namespace). So the getattr always yields None and the real branches are unreachable no matter what you configure. Result: LogsHandler falls to _simulate_logs (investigation.py:178-185) which `return []`, and every remediation handler returns `result["simulated"] = True` (remediation.py:60,157,282,453). Confirmed empirically: all coordinator tasks completed in duration_ms=0 with empty payloads.
- **Blocker:** The handlers can never be configured through the live API — Settings has no prometheus_url/loki_url field and the router passes None for the k8s/LLM clients. Every investigation returns empty data and every remediation is a no-op that reports success.
- **Fix:** Add prometheus_url/loki_url/elasticsearch_url to app/config.Settings and wire a real k8s client + llm client into create_investigation_handlers/create_remediation_handlers in app/coordinator/api_router.py:60.

### `PARTIAL` — Coordinator incident investigation workflow (POST /api/coordinator/investigations)

- **Surface:** POST /api/coordinator/investigations
- **Severity:** medium · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** Ran: POST with {incident_id:'inc1', affected_services:['api'], severity:'P3'} -> 200 with an 8-task DAG and levels [[collect_logs,collect_metrics,check_deployments],[correlate],[hypothesize],[decision_gate],[execute_fix],[verify_fix]]. It really executed all 6 levels in order and reached state=completed. Confirmed the literal blocking sleep: remediation.py:526 wait_seconds default 60, :536 `await asyncio.sleep(wait_seconds)` preceded by the log line 'Waiting %d seconds before verification'. Confirmed dag_executor.py:957 `requires_approval=severity in ('P1','P2')` on execute_fix, which will block the background task with no way to discover the pending approval (execution not registered until it finishes, no pending-approvals endpoint). All data is empty per the handlers finding.

### `PARTIAL` — Coordinator DAG creation, validation and critical path (POST /api/coordinator/dags) _(verdict overturned from WORKS on verification)_

- **Surface:** POST /api/coordinator/dags
- **Severity:** low · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** Ran: POST /api/coordinator/dags with t1, t2(dep t1), t3(dep t1,t2) -> 200 {"execution_levels":[["t1"],["t2"],["t3"]],"critical_path":[["t1","t2","t3"],180]}. dag.validate() is enforced and returns 400 on invalid DAGs (app/coordinator/api_router.py:148-150). Level computation and longest-path/critical-path are real graph algorithms in app/coordinator/dag_executor.py. Note this endpoint is stateless — it validates and returns, it does not store the DAG (hence /dags/{id}/execute requires you to resend the whole DAG body).
- **Verifier:** The happy path is real (POST with t1,t2(dep t1),t3(dep t1,t2) -> execution_levels [['t1'],['t2'],['t3']], critical_path [['t1','t2','t3'],180]). But I broke it two ways with raise_server_exceptions=False: (a) a task whose depends_on names a nonexistent task -> HTTP 500, not 400, because TaskDAG.add_task raises ValueError at dag_executor.py:209 and api_router.py:150 does not catch it; (b) a perfectly VALID DAG listed in non-topological order (task 'a' depends_on 'b' with 'b' second in the array) -> also HTTP 500, so the endpoint rejects legal input based purely on JSON array ordering. Both return the misleading body {"error":"INTERNAL_ERROR","message":"The requested resource was not found."}. Furthermore the auditor's 'validate() returns 400' path is unreachable: since add_task requires deps to pre-exist, any DAG built by create_dag has no cycle, no missing dep, and always has a root, so validate() always returns [] and the 400 branch at api_router.py:148-150 is dead.

### `PARTIAL` — Coordinator resource pool (GET /api/coordinator/resources)

- **Surface:** GET /api/coordinator/resources
- **Severity:** low · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** Ran: GET /api/coordinator/resources -> 200 {"total":{"cpu_cores":8.0,"memory_mb":16384,"gpus":0,"network_bandwidth_mbps":1000},"available":{...},"used":{"cpu_cores":0.0,...},"allocations":0}. The pool is a real semaphore-style allocator that DAGExecutor acquires/releases around task execution (dag_executor.py:~595 `await self.resource_pool.release(resource_lock)`). However the capacity numbers are hardcoded literals in app/coordinator/api_router.py:45-49 (cpu_cores=8, memory_mb=16384, gpus=0), not measured from the host or configurable, and the pool is per-process.
- **Blocker:** Reported capacity is a hardcoded constant unrelated to the actual machine, so 'available resources' is decorative.
- **Fix:** Read capacity from settings/psutil and share the pool via Redis if you intend it to gate real concurrency.
- **Verifier:** Confirmed hardcoded literals at api_router.py:46-50 (cpu_cores=8, memory_mb=16384, gpus=0); pool is a module global. Verdict stands but it is worse than claimed: in a fresh process GET /api/coordinator/resources is the FIRST coordinator call and returns HTTP 200 {'error':'Resource pool not initialized'} because /resources has no Depends(get_executor) (api_router.py:281-289) and _resource_pool is only assigned inside get_executor(). I reproduced this exactly: RESOURCES FIRST -> {'error': ...}; then after GET /handlers, RESOURCES AFTER -> the full 8-core/16384MB payload.

**Verifier notes for this domain.** I re-ran everything from scratch with TestClient against app.api.main:app and by driving ExecutionEngine/DAGExecutor directly. 21 of 22 verdicts hold. One overturned (coordinator DAG creation: WORKS -> PARTIAL). Things the auditor got wrong or missed:

1) OVERTURNED — POST /api/coordinator/dags is not WORKS. `TaskDAG.add_task` (app/coordinator/dag_executor.py:207-210) raises a bare ValueError if a dependency is not already in `self.tasks`, and app/coordinator/api_router.py:150 does not catch it. Verified with raise_server_exceptions=False:
   - missing dep -> HTTP 500 {"error":"INTERNAL_ERROR","message":"The requested resource was not found."}
   - a perfectly VALID DAG whose tasks are listed in non-topological order (task 'a' depends_on 'b', 'b' listed second) -> also HTTP 500. So the endpoint is JSON-array-order-sensitive and 500s on a legal DAG, with a misleading error body.
   Additionally the auditor's "dag.validate() is enforced and returns 400 on invalid DAGs" is unreachable dead code from this route: because add_task requires every dep to pre-exist, a DAG built through create_dag can never contain a cycle, never contain a missing dep, and its first task always has no deps, so validate() always returns []. The 400 branch (api_router.py:148-150) can never fire.

2) WIRING CLAIM WRONG (material, makes the STUB worse, not better) — the auditor said app/runbooks/executor.py is "CLI-only", reachable only from app/cli.py. It is not. app/mcp/client.py:22-27 lists "runbooks" in _SERVERS and :224-228 spawns `python -m app.mcp.servers.runbooks.server`, which calls `from app.runbooks import get_executor` (server.py:46/84/120/184/218/257/305/325) and exposes execute_runbook/approve_execution as agent tools. CopilotSession is constructed by app/api/main.py:963 inside `_get_session`, which is called from the LIVE HTTP routes POST /chat (main.py:1596), POST /approve (1687), POST /chat/stream (1800), POST /approve/stream (1882) and POST /remediate (2996). So the 100%-simulated executor IS in the live production agent path, not CLI-only.

3) MISSED — the RunbookBuilder Save button can never work at all, not just for edits. frontend/src/pages/RunbookBuilder.tsx:442 mints new ids as `rb-${Date.now()}`, and :530 computes `isNew = runbook.id.startsWith("rb-") && !runbook.id.includes("-")`, which is a contradiction — any id starting with "rb-" necessarily contains "-", so isNew is ALWAYS false and the method is ALWAYS PUT. I confirmed PUT and PATCH /api/runbooks/{id} both return 405. Creating a runbook from the UI is impossible too, and :541 `if (res.ok)` swallows it silently.

4) MISSED — GET /api/coordinator/resources returns HTTP 200 with body {"error": "Resource pool not initialized"} if it is the first coordinator endpoint hit in a fresh process, because `_resource_pool` is only assigned inside `get_executor()` (api_router.py:39-50) and /resources has no Depends(get_executor) (api_router.py:281-289). Verified in a clean interpreter. Only after hitting /handlers or /dags does it return the hardcoded 8-core/16GB numbers.

5) MISSED — app/coordinator/dag_executor.py:759 does `eval(condition, {"__builtins__": {}}, {})` on a task condition string after `${var}` interpolation of execution context values. Not reachable from the API today (TaskNodeInput has no `condition` field), but it is a live eval in the branch-condition path used by create_investigation_dag-style DAGs.

6) EVIDENCE CORRECTIONS (verdicts unchanged) — the auditor claimed `grep -rn playbook_marketplace --include=*.py .` outside the package "returns nothing at all"; it actually hits tests/test_moat_builders.py:28,31. Likewise app/runbook/ is imported by tests/test_competitive_parity.py:190, not "no test". Both are still DEAD_CODE for the product (no route, no CLI, no live importer), but the stated grep results are wrong.

Confirmed independently: route shadowing 404 on GET /api/runbooks/executions (literal {"detail":"Runbook not found"}); execute returns steps:[] and never runs anything; approve flips status to "running" forever; goal decomposition is byte-identical name/description output for different goal text under category=deployment (only 3 of 8 GoalCategory values have templates; security/migration fall to Analyze/Plan/Execute/Verify with hardcoded confidence 0.85 and a reasoning string that still says "using template approach"); planning DAG ordering/fan-out/output-propagation is genuinely real (diamond trace [start A, end A, start B, start C, end B, end C, start D, end D], D saw dep_B+dep_C); re-running a completed graph returns completed_tasks=0 with status "completed"; GET /api/planning/executions is always empty because execute() pops _active_executions in a finally; coordinator approval gate genuinely blocks on an asyncio.Future (pending=['t2'], task.done()==False after 0.4s) and resumes on approve_task; denial leaves the task 'skipped' but the overall execution 'completed'; Settings has no prometheus_url/loki_url/elasticsearch_url field (only kube_config_path, kube_namespace, copilot_prompt_cache match); `import kubernetes` -> ModuleNotFoundError; asyncio.get_event_loop() in a non-main thread on this Python 3.13.7 raises RuntimeError, so app/tasks/tasks.py:392 would break in a dramatiq worker; no /api/playbooks, /api/marketplace, /api/sandbox or /api/safety route exists in the live route table; the only dynamic import in app/ is app/llm.py:55 (LLM provider), so there is no hidden auto-registration that could rescue any DEAD_CODE verdict.


## SLO tracking, error budgets, status page, subscribers

**Summary.** The domain has two live routers (app/slo/api_router.py, app/status_page/api_router.py) plus GET /metrics/slo, and the plumbing is real — CRUD, incident/maintenance lifecycle, component-status propagation and webhook fan-out all executed successfully under TestClient. But the load-bearing thing a paying SRE buys — the error-budget and burn-rate numbers — is mathematically wrong in both directions: error budget consumption is computed as max(0, target - observed_SLI) instead of (1 - observed_SLI), so any service meeting its target always reports "100% budget remaining, healthy", while the multi-window burn rate divides by an extra time-fraction factor and reports 360x / 60x (severity "critical", is_alerting=true) for a service running at 99.5% against a 99% target whose true burn rate is 0.5x. Second-biggest problem: nothing is persisted. There is no SLO or status-page table in migrations/versions/001_initial_schema.py; everything lives in module-level dicts (app/slo/tracker.py:151-152, app/status_page/store.py:255-258), verified by creating an SLO in one process and getting an empty list in a fresh one. Also broken: POST /api/status/components 500s on every call (reserved LogRecord key "name"), the "public" status page returns 401 whenever COPILOT_API_TOKEN is set while the React page sends no Authorization header, email subscribers can never be verified (the verification email is a TODO) so they never receive a single notification, and uptime_30d/90d is always the literal 100.0 because record_uptime() has zero callers. A large surrounding surface (app/reports/, app/scheduled_reports/, app/status_page/{sla,widgets,updates,components,incidents}.py, app/observability/slo.py, app/reliability/) has no entrypoint at all. Today a user can run an incident/maintenance status page in a single-process, no-auth deployment; they cannot trust any SLO number and will lose all data on restart.

**Wiring.** LIVE: app/slo/api_router.py -> app/api/main.py:763-764 (GET/POST /api/slos, GET/PATCH/DELETE /api/slos/{id}, GET/POST /api/slos/{id}/sli, GET /api/slos/{id}/budget, GET /api/slos/{id}/burn-rate). app/status_page/api_router.py -> app/api/main.py:767-768 (GET /api/status, components/incidents/maintenances CRUD, POST /api/status/subscribe, GET /api/status/subscribe/verify, DELETE /api/status/subscribe/{id}); it uses only status_page/{models,store,notifications}.py. GET /metrics/slo -> app/api/main.py:1977 backed by app/slo_metrics.py, fed by app/api/main.py:531 _record_slo. app/slo_poller.py IS started from the lifespan (app/api/main.py:386-398) but only when COPILOT_SLO_POLLER=true (app/config.py:347 default False); its burn_fn is app/mcp/servers/datadog/server.compute_burn_rate. Frontend: frontend/src/pages/StatusPage.tsx and frontend/src/pages/SLOTracker.tsx are both mounted in frontend/src/App.tsx:133,157.
NO ENTRYPOINT: app/reports/generator.py (690 lines, imported only by its own __init__ and tests); app/scheduled_reports/scheduler.py (673 lines, same); app/status_page/sla.py, widgets.py, updates.py, components.py, incidents.py (~1870 lines, imported only by app/status_page/__init__.py, never called by the router); app/observability/slo.py SLOTracker (a third parallel SLO implementation, no callers); app/reliability/dead_letter_queue.py (app/jobqueue.py has its own DLQ). app/metrics_source.py is wired to GET /metrics but is a JSON-file reader and is NOT connected to SLOs at all.


### `BROKEN` — Error budget calculation (GET /api/slos/{id}/budget)

- **Surface:** GET /api/slos/{slo_id}/budget -> app/slo/tracker.py:calculate_error_budget
- **Severity:** critical · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** app/slo/tracker.py:346 `consumed = max(0, slo.target - current_sli)`. Correct SRE math: consumed = error_rate = 1 - observed_SLI; remaining_% = (allowed_error - error_rate)/allowed_error.

Ran (TestClient): create SLO target=0.99 window=rolling_30d, POST sli good=99500 total=100000 (SLI=0.995, error rate=0.005, allowed error=0.01).
HAND-DERIVED: consumed=0.005 (50% of budget), remaining=0.005, remaining_percent=50.0, burn=0.5x.
API RETURNED: {"total_budget":0.01,"consumed":0.0,"remaining":0.01,"remaining_percent":100.0,"burn_rate":0.0,"status":"healthy"}.
Second case target=0.99, SLI=0.98 (error 0.02 = 200% of budget): hand-derived consumed=0.02, remaining_percent=-100. API returned consumed=0.01, remaining_percent=0.0, burn_rate=1.0 (should be 2.0).
Also app/slo/tracker.py:352-353: window_start is defined as now - window_duration, so window_elapsed always == window_total; expected_consumption collapses to total_budget and `burn_rate` degenerates into "fraction of budget consumed", not a burn rate.
Same bug repeated in the report path, app/slo/tracker.py:505 `error_budget_consumed = max(0,(target-achieved)/(1-target))`.
- **Blocker:** Budget consumption is measured as the shortfall below target instead of the actual error rate, so every SLO that is meeting its target reports exactly 0 consumed / 100% remaining / healthy no matter how much of the budget it has actually burned. A team at 99.5% against a 99% target has spent half its month's budget and the API says it has spent none.
- **Fix:** In calculate_error_budget: consumed = 1 - current_sli; remaining = total_budget - consumed (allow negative or clamp only for display); remaining_percent = remaining/total_budget*100; burn_rate = consumed/total_budget for the full window. Apply the same correction at tracker.py:505 ((1-achieved)/(1-target)). Add a unit test asserting target=0.99 + SLI=0.995 -> remaining_percent==50.
- **Verifier:** Read app/slo/tracker.py:346 `consumed = max(0, slo.target - current_sli)` and ran it. target=0.99, good=99500/100000 (SLI=0.995, true error rate 0.005 = 50% of a 0.01 budget) -> API returned consumed=0.0, remaining_percent=100.0, burn_rate=0.0, status='healthy'. target=0.999, SLI=0.9999 -> also consumed=0.0/100%. target=0.99, SLI=0.98 (200% of budget) -> consumed clamps at 0.01, remaining_percent=0.0, burn_rate=1.0 (true 2.0). tracker.py:352 sets window_start = now - window_duration so window_elapsed always equals window_total and burn_rate collapses to 'fraction consumed'. Same bug at tracker.py:505. Confirmed.

### `BROKEN` — Multi-window burn-rate alerting (GET /api/slos/{id}/burn-rate)

- **Surface:** GET /api/slos/{slo_id}/burn-rate -> app/slo/tracker.py:calculate_burn_rate
- **Severity:** critical · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** app/slo/tracker.py:411-416: `time_fraction = window/slo_window; expected_error = allowed_error * time_fraction; return error_rate / expected_error`. Google SRE burn rate is error_rate / allowed_error with NO time scaling — the 14.4 threshold already encodes "2% of budget in 1h of a 30d window".

Ran (TestClient) target=0.99 (rolling_30d), SLI=0.995: API returned {"short_window_burn_rate":360.0,"long_window_burn_rate":60.0,"is_alerting":true,"severity":"critical","message":"Critical: 2% of error budget consumed in 1 hour (burn rate: 360.0x)"}. Hand-derived correct value: 0.005/0.01 = 0.5x, not alerting.
360 = 0.005 / (0.01 * (1h/720h)) — an exact 720x inflation for the 1h window and 120x for the 6h window.
Second case target=0.999, SLI=0.9999 (10% of budget used, obviously healthy): API returned short=72.0, long=12.0, severity="high", is_alerting=true. Correct burn rate 0.1x.
Only a perfect 100% SLI produces severity "none" (verified: target=0.99, good=total -> short=0.0, long=0.0, sev=none).
- **Blocker:** Every window-scaled burn rate is inflated by slo_window/window (720x for 1h on a 30d SLO), so the fixed 14.4/6/3 thresholds fire for essentially any non-zero error rate. A service at 99.99% against a 99.9% target pages as severity 'high'. The endpoint is an unconditional false-alarm generator.
- **Fix:** Delete the time_fraction scaling: `return error_rate / allowed_error`. Keep the 14.4/6/3 thresholds (they are defined against that unscaled ratio). Add tests: (target 0.999, err 0.0001) -> 0.1x/no alert; (target 0.999, err 0.0144) -> 14.4x/critical.
- **Verifier:** tracker.py:411-416 divides by `allowed_error * (window/slo_window)`, an extra 1/time_fraction factor absent from Google SRE burn rate. Ran it: target=0.99 SLI=0.995 -> short=360.0, long=60.0, is_alerting=true, severity='critical' (true 0.5x, not alerting). target=0.999 SLI=0.9999 (10% of budget, healthy) -> short=71.99, long=11.99, severity='high', is_alerting=true. 360 = 0.005/(0.01*(1/720)) — exact 720x inflation. Confirmed.

### `BROKEN` — Status page component CRUD (create component)

- **Surface:** POST /api/status/components (also PATCH/DELETE)
- **Severity:** high · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** Ran POST /api/status/components {"name":"Payments","description":"pay","group":"Core"} -> 500 {"error":"INTERNAL_ERROR"}. Traceback: app/status_page/store.py:301 `log.info("component created", extra={"component_id": component_id, "name": name})` -> logging/__init__.py makeRecord -> KeyError: "Attempt to overwrite 'name' in LogRecord".
'name' is a reserved LogRecord attribute, so this raises on every call regardless of logging config. Only this one line in app/status_page/ uses a reserved extra key (verified by grep).
PATCH /api/status/components/api {"status":"degraded"} -> 200 and DELETE work; only creation is dead. GET /components works because the four default components are inserted directly in _setup_default_components (store.py:261-271) without logging.
- **Blocker:** Users cannot add any component to their status page — every creation attempt is a 500. They are stuck with the four hardcoded defaults (API, Dashboard, ...).
- **Fix:** Rename the key: extra={"component_id": component_id, "component_name": name}. One-line fix; add a test that POSTs a component and asserts 201.
- **Verifier:** Reproduced: POST /api/status/components {'name':'Payments'} -> 500, traceback ends at app/status_page/store.py:301 log.info(..., extra={'name': name}) -> logging/__init__.py:1637 KeyError "Attempt to overwrite 'name' in LogRecord". Unconditional, config-independent. Material correction to the blocker text: store.py:300 inserts the component into self._components BEFORE the crashing log, so it is a PARTIAL WRITE — my subsequent GET /api/status/components listed the orphan 'comp_dcfbe7b7a744' (alongside 5, not 4, defaults) while the client received a 500 with no id. PATCH and DELETE work. Verdict stands.

### `BROKEN` — Status page email subscriptions (subscribe + verify)

- **Surface:** POST /api/status/subscribe (email), GET /api/status/subscribe/verify
- **Severity:** high · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** POST /api/status/subscribe {"email":"a@b.com"} -> 201 {"verified":false,"message":"Subscription created. Please check your email to verify."} — but app/status_page/api_router.py:331 is literally `# TODO: Send verification email/webhook` followed by :332 `# For now, auto-verify webhooks and mark emails as pending`. No send_email call exists anywhere in the subscribe path.
The verification token is generated at app/status_page/store.py:601 (secrets.token_urlsafe(32)), stored in the in-memory Subscriber, and never returned in SubscribeResponse (confirmed: response body contains no token). GET /api/status/subscribe/verify?id=...&token=bogus -> 400 'Invalid verification link'; there is no way for the subscriber to ever learn the real token.
Consequence chain: app/status_page/notifications.py:132-133 `if not subscriber.verified: continue` — unverified subscribers are skipped for every notification.
Note the email transport itself is real and would work (app/notifications/email.py:243 send_email with SMTP/SendGrid/SES providers) — it is simply never invoked here.
- **Blocker:** An email subscriber is stored as a row and nothing else: no verification mail is sent, the token is unobtainable, verified stays false forever, and notify_subscribers skips unverified subscribers — so email subscribers receive zero incident notifications, ever.
- **Fix:** In subscribe_to_updates, after create_subscriber, call app.notifications.email.send_email with a link to /api/status/subscribe/verify?id={id}&token={token}; return 202 on send failure. ~15 lines using the existing provider.
- **Verifier:** POST /api/status/subscribe {'email':'a@b.com'} -> 201 {'verified':false,'message':'Subscription created. Please check your email to verify.'}. Read api_router.py:331-332: literally '# TODO: Send verification email/webhook' then '# For now, auto-verify webhooks and mark emails as pending'. No send_email in the subscribe path. `grep -rn verification_token app/` -> generated at store.py:601, stored at models.py:237, checked at store.py:633, and never returned by SubscribeResponse (confirmed the 201 body has no token field). GET /subscribe/verify (api_router.py:350-361) is the only consumer and 400s without it. Chain closes at notifications.py:132-133 `if not subscriber.verified: continue`. Confirmed.

### `STUB` — Component uptime percentages (uptime_30d / uptime_90d)

- **Surface:** GET /api/status and GET /api/status/components -> uptime_30d, uptime_90d
- **Severity:** medium · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** GET /api/status/components returns "uptime_30d":100.0,"uptime_90d":100.0 for every component. app/status_page/store.py:672-681 defines record_uptime, but `grep -rn 'record_uptime' app/ tests/ frontend/src` matches ONLY the abstract declaration (store.py:225) and the implementation (store.py:672) — zero callers.
With no records, calculate_uptime returns the literal 100.0 (store.py:698, :707, :713 'Assume 100% if no records'). Incident create/resolve changes component.status but never appends an uptime record, so uptime never moves even during a logged outage.
- **Blocker:** The uptime numbers on the status page are a hardcoded 100.0%, not a measurement — including during and after a real recorded outage.
- **Fix:** Emit record_uptime on every component status transition (in create_incident/resolve_incident/update_component and maintenance start/end), computing the duration in the previous state; or derive uptime from the incident timeline directly.
- **Verifier:** Read calculate_uptime to its returns (store.py:687-716): returns the literal 100.0 at three separate no-records branches. `grep -rn record_uptime app/ tests/ frontend/src` matches ONLY the ABC declaration (store.py:225) and the implementation (store.py:672) — zero callers, so uptime_records is permanently empty. Empirically: all components returned uptime_30d/90d = 100.0, and after I created a major incident on 'api' and resolved it, GET /api/status/components/api still showed uptime_30d=100.0. Confirmed.

### `DEAD_CODE` — Scheduled reports (SLO status / incident summary reports on a schedule)

- **Surface:** app/scheduled_reports/scheduler.py — no route, no scheduler
- **Severity:** medium · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** `grep -rn 'get_due_reports|run_scheduled_report|ReportScheduler' app/` outside app/scheduled_reports/ returns nothing; the only importers are app/scheduled_reports/__init__.py and tests/test_advanced_features.py:406-483. No APIRouter in app/scheduled_reports/ (grep for APIRouter -> no match), and the lifespan (app/api/main.py:367-405) starts only the job worker and the SLO poller — nothing calls get_due_reports (scheduler.py:617).
Even if it were wired, the content is canned: app/scheduled_reports/scheduler.py:349-430 returns literal dicts — INCIDENT_SUMMARY {'total_incidents':12,'resolved':10,'mttr_minutes':45}, SLO_STATUS {'total_slos':8,'meeting_target':6} with fake rows like {'name':'API Availability','actual':'99.95%'}, ALERT_SUMMARY {'total_alerts':150,'noise_ratio':'70%'}. None of it reads the SLO tracker or the incident store.
- **Blocker:** Nothing schedules it, no endpoint exposes it, and its report bodies are hardcoded demo numbers rather than queries against real data.
- **Fix:** Either delete it, or (1) register a router, (2) add a lifespan task that polls get_due_reports() on an interval, and (3) replace _generate_report_content with real queries into the SLO tracker / incident store.

- **Surface:** app/status_page/sla.py, widgets.py, updates.py, components.py, incidents.py

### `PARTIAL` — SLO CRUD (create/list/get/update/delete SLOs)

- **Surface:** GET|POST /api/slos, GET|PATCH|DELETE /api/slos/{slo_id}
- **Severity:** high · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** Executed all verbs via TestClient: POST /api/slos -> 201 with id slo_8752d21dd35f2e12; GET/PATCH/DELETE all behave; service/team filters work; unknown sli_type/window correctly 400.
BUT storage is module-global dicts: app/slo/tracker.py:151-152 `_SLOS: dict[str,SLO] = {}` / `_SLI_DATA: dict[str,list[SLI]] = defaultdict(list)`. Proved volatility: process A created 1 SLO then listed 1; a freshly started process listed 0 ('count in proc1: 1' / 'count in fresh proc: 0').
No DB backing: `grep -in 'slo' migrations/versions/001_initial_schema.py` returns nothing; app/database/migrations/001_initial_schema.sql has no SLO table.
Tenancy is a query parameter, not identity: app/slo/api_router.py:141 `org_id: str = Query("default")` on every route — any caller can read/write any org's SLOs by changing ?org_id=.
- **Blocker:** All SLOs and SLI history are lost on restart and are not shared between uvicorn workers (two workers = two different SLO lists), and org isolation is client-controlled via a query string.
- **Fix:** Add slo/sli tables + an Alembic revision, swap the module dicts for a repository behind SLOTracker, and derive org_id from the authenticated principal (tenant_context) instead of Query().

### `PARTIAL` — SLI ingestion / metrics source

- **Surface:** POST /api/slos/{slo_id}/sli, GET /api/slos/{slo_id}/sli, app/metrics_source.py
- **Severity:** high · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** POST /api/slos/{id}/sli -> 201 {"value":0.995,"good_count":99500,"total_count":100000}; GET returns the stored points sorted; retention pruning at app/slo/tracker.py:280-284 works (2x window).
There is NO automated feed: `grep -rn 'record_sli' app/` outside tracker.py only matches app/slo/__init__.py re-exports — nothing in the app calls it. No poller, no scraper, no cron writes SLIs.
app/metrics_source.py reads runtime.logs_path()/metrics.json + app.log from disk (its own docstring: 'This is the "file" connector. To go live, swap read_all/error_summary for a Prometheus/Loki/Datadog client'). It is wired only to GET /metrics (app/api/main.py:1957) and is never used by the SLO code.
- **Blocker:** SLIs are 100% manually POSTed. There is no Prometheus/Datadog/Loki integration behind the SLO tracker, so unless the customer writes their own exporter every SLO sits at status "no_data".
- **Fix:** Add a periodic collector that queries a real metrics backend (app/mcp/servers/datadog/server.get_metric already speaks Datadog; a Prometheus range-query client would be ~50 lines) and calls record_sli per SLO on an interval, plus an slo.query field describing the good/total queries.
- **Verifier:** POST /api/slos/{id}/sli -> 201 with value 0.995; GET returns stored points. `grep -rn record_sli app/` outside tracker.py hits only app/slo/api_router.py:231/239 (the HTTP route itself) and app/slo/__init__.py re-exports — no poller, scraper or cron. `grep -rn metrics_source app/` returns only main.py:49 (import) and main.py:1957 (GET /metrics), never SLO code. With no manual POSTs every SLO returns status='no_data' (tracker.py:337-349). Confirmed.
