BUT with COPILOT_API_TOKEN=secret123: GET /api/status -> 401 {"detail":"Missing or invalid API token."} (same for /components, /incidents and POST /subscribe). /api/status is not in app/api/main.py:230 _OPEN_PATHS.
frontend/src/pages/StatusPage.tsx:111 does a bare `fetch('/api/status')` with no Authorization header, and :139 the same for /api/status/subscribe.
- **Blocker:** A status page whose whole purpose is public, unauthenticated reachability is behind the shared bearer token in any deployment that sets one; the shipped React page sends no token, so it shows 'Unable to load status' as soon as auth is enabled.
- **Fix:** Add '/api/status' and its GET subpaths (+ POST /api/status/subscribe, GET /api/status/subscribe/verify) to _OPEN_PATHS / an unauthenticated prefix list, keeping the write endpoints authenticated.
- **Verifier:** Unauthenticated GET /api/status -> 200 with real aggregation (verified overall_status flipped to partial_outage on incident create and back to operational on resolve). With COPILOT_API_TOKEN=secret123 I got 401 {'detail':'Missing or invalid API token.'} on /api/status, /api/status/components, /api/status/incidents and POST /api/status/subscribe; only /metrics/slo returned 200, matching main.py:230 _OPEN_PATHS which omits /api/status. frontend/src/pages/StatusPage.tsx:111 and :139 use bare fetch — and notably bypass the existing authHeaders helper at frontend/src/api.ts:10. Confirmed.

### `PARTIAL` — Proactive SLO burn poller (auto-open investigations)

- **Surface:** app/slo_poller.py, started in app/api/main.py lifespan when COPILOT_SLO_POLLER=true
- **Severity:** medium · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** Genuinely wired: app/api/main.py:385-398 constructs SLOPoller(services_fn=_dd.list_services, burn_fn=_dd.compute_burn_rate, trigger_fn=_slo_trigger, interval/cooldown from settings) and asyncio.create_task(poller.run()); cancelled on shutdown at main.py:400-405. Gated by app/config.py:347 `copilot_slo_poller: bool = False` (off by default).
The math it consumes is the CORRECT one: app/mcp/servers/datadog/server.py:342-372 `_burn_rate` uses burn = error_rate / (1 - slo) with 14.4/6 thresholds — i.e. the Datadog MCP path is right while the /api/slos path (above) is wrong. Two contradictory implementations.
`python3 -m pytest tests/test_slo_poller.py -q -p no:cacheprovider` -> passes (cooldown/dedup/tick logic tested with injected fns).
- **Blocker:** Off by default, and its data source is app/mcp/servers/datadog/server.py which runs in OFFLINE fixture mode (bundled demo JSON) unless real Datadog credentials are set — so with the flag on and no DD creds it evaluates burn rates over demo fixtures. It also has no connection whatsoever to the /api/slos SLOs a user creates in the UI.
- **Fix:** Point the poller at the user-defined SLOs (tracker.calculate_burn_rate, once fixed) rather than only Datadog services, and surface the enable flag + poller status in the UI so a user can tell whether it is running.

### `PARTIAL` — Status incident lifecycle + component status propagation

- **Surface:** POST /api/status/incidents, PATCH /api/status/incidents/{id}, GET /api/status/incidents
- **Severity:** medium · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** Full lifecycle executed: POST incident (severity major, affected ['api']) -> 201 is_active=true; component 'api' auto-set to partial_outage and /api/status overall_status became partial_outage; PATCH status=identified -> 200 with an appended update; PATCH status=resolved -> 200 with resolved_at set, component back to operational, summary shows active 0 / recent 1. Real logic in app/status_page/store.py:364-486.
Storage is in-memory (store.py:255-258 `self._incidents = {}`), global singleton at store.py:766-772; lost on restart, not shared across workers. No status-page tables in migrations/versions/001_initial_schema.py.
Nit: every affected component is forced to PARTIAL_OUTAGE (store.py:392-395) regardless of incident severity, so a 'critical' incident never shows major_outage.
- **Blocker:** Incidents live only in one process's memory — a restart or a second uvicorn worker makes the public status page disagree with itself and lose incident history.
- **Fix:** Back InMemoryStatusStore with a DB implementation of the existing StatusStore ABC (the abstraction is already there) + migration; map incident severity to component status.
- **Verifier:** Ran full lifecycle: POST incident (major, affected ['api']) -> 201 is_active=true, component 'api' -> partial_outage, /api/status overall_status -> partial_outage; PATCH status=resolved -> 200 with resolved_at, component back to operational, summary back to operational. Real logic. Storage is store.py:255-258 in-memory dicts behind the store.py:766-772 global singleton; a fresh process listed 0 incidents. Confirmed the severity nit myself: a 'critical' incident on 'dashboard' still set the component to partial_outage, never major_outage. Confirmed.

### `PARTIAL` — Scheduled maintenance windows

- **Surface:** POST/GET/PATCH/DELETE /api/status/maintenances
- **Severity:** medium · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** Executed: POST maintenance (scheduled_start/end, affected ['api']) -> 201 status=scheduled is_upcoming=true; PATCH status=in_progress -> 200 and GET /api/status/components/api showed status='maintenance'; PATCH status=completed -> 200 and the component reverted to 'operational'; DELETE -> 204. Real transitions at app/status_page/store.py:555-590.
- **Blocker:** Same in-memory-only persistence as incidents; also nothing auto-starts/auto-completes a window at its scheduled time (transitions are manual PATCHes only — no scheduler references the maintenance store).
- **Fix:** Persist to DB and add a small background task in the lifespan that promotes scheduled -> in_progress -> completed based on scheduled_start/scheduled_end.
- **Verifier:** Ran: POST -> 201 status='scheduled'; PATCH in_progress -> 200 and GET component 'database' showed status='maintenance'; PATCH completed -> 200 and component reverted to 'operational'; DELETE -> 204. Transitions are real but purely manual — no scheduler anywhere references the maintenance store, and the same in-memory singleton loses everything on restart. Confirmed.

### `PARTIAL` — Status page webhook subscriptions + incident notification fan-out

- **Surface:** POST /api/status/subscribe (webhook_url), notification dispatch on incident create/update/resolve
- **Severity:** medium · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** POST /api/status/subscribe {"webhook_url":"https://example.com/hook"} -> 201 verified=true, target masked as 'https://example.com/***'.
Delivery proven: patched app.status_page.notifications.httpx.AsyncClient with a fake, then POST /api/status/incidents -> exactly 1 webhook call to https://example.com/hook with body event_type='incident_created', title='Outage', and header X-Status-Signature='t=1787531818,v1=9e8a...'. Real HMAC-SHA256 over '{timestamp}.{body}', retry with exponential backoff on 5xx/timeout, no retry on 4xx (app/status_page/notifications.py:203-256).
Two defects: (1) the docstring at api_router.py:279-281 promises 'a verification POST' for webhooks; there is none — the URL is auto-trusted (api_router.py:334), so anyone can register an arbitrary URL as a fan-out target (SSRF/abuse). (2) notifications.py:152-163 zips `subscribers` against `results` from a FILTERED task list; verified by running notify_subscribers with [unverified-email, verified-webhook-that-fails] -> returned {'success': [], 'failed': ['unverified1']}: the skipped subscriber is reported as failed and the one that actually failed is not reported at all.
- **Blocker:** No webhook ownership verification, and the delivery success/failure accounting is misaligned whenever any subscriber is filtered out, so the delivery logs are wrong.
- **Fix:** Send a signed verification POST and require an echo/challenge before verified=True; and build the task list as a list of (subscriber, coro) pairs so results zip against the filtered subscribers.
- **Verifier:** Patched app.status_page.notifications.httpx with a fake client and POSTed an incident: exactly 1 delivery to https://example.com/hook with body event_type='incident_created' and header X-Status-Signature='t=1787534872,v1=9d79e598...'. Real HMAC-SHA256 over f'{timestamp}.{body}', retry+backoff on 5xx/timeout, no retry on 4xx (notifications.py:205-260). Webhook auto-verified with no ownership check (api_router.py:334) despite the docstring promising a verification POST. Reproduced the accounting bug directly: notify_subscribers([unverified email 'unverified1', verified webhook 'hook2' stubbed to return False]) -> {'success': [], 'failed': ['unverified1']} — notifications.py:152 zips the unfiltered `subscribers` list against `results` from the filtered task list. Confirmed.

### `PARTIAL` — Frontend SLO Tracker page

- **Surface:** frontend/src/pages/SLOTracker.tsx (mounted via frontend/src/App.tsx:157)
- **Severity:** medium · **Confidence:** medium
- **Evidence:** Fetches the real API at SLOTracker.tsx:378 (/api/slos) and :387 (/budget) and polls every 30s. But: (a) SLOTracker.tsx:415 fabricates the displayed current SLI as `current = target*100 - burn_rate*0.1` — pure arithmetic invention, because ErrorBudgetResponse (app/slo/api_router.py:76-88) does not expose current_sli/good_count/total_count even though the dataclass computes them; (b) :386 hardcodes a 43.2-minute budget default and rescales remaining by 43.2/(1-target); (c) when /api/slos is empty it falls through to SLOTracker.tsx:428-489 which INVENTS SLOs from incident/alert counts: `uptime = 100 - incidentCount*0.1`, `budgetUsed = incidentCount*5`, `budget_burn_rate = 0.5 + incidentCount*0.3`, target hardcoded 99.9. node_modules is not installed so the page was not rendered; verdict from source.
- **Blocker:** A user with no SLOs configured sees a dashboard full of invented availability and burn-rate numbers that look like measurements, and even with real SLOs the 'current' SLI column is derived arithmetic rather than the measured value.
- **Fix:** Add current_sli/good_count/total_count to ErrorBudgetResponse and render those; delete the incident-derived fallback and show an empty state instead.

### `PARTIAL` — Agent SLO Prometheus metrics (GET /metrics/slo) _(verdict overturned from WORKS on verification)_

- **Surface:** GET /metrics/slo -> app/slo_metrics.py
- **Severity:** low · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** Ran: recorded metrics().record(3.5,1200,True,False) and record(45.0,800,False,True), then GET /metrics/slo -> 200 text/plain with copilot_investigations_total 2, success 1, abstained 1, failed 1, copilot_tokens_total 2000, and a correct cumulative histogram (le="5" 1, le="60" 2, le="+Inf" 2, _sum 48.5, _count 2). Real thread-locked accounting in app/slo_metrics.py:38-60, no fakery.
Fed from real traffic at app/api/main.py:531-542 `_record_slo` after each investigation. Deliberately open without auth (app/api/main.py:230 _OPEN_PATHS) — verified 200 even with COPILOT_API_TOKEN set.
`python3 -m pytest tests/test_slo_metrics.py -q -p no:cacheprovider` passes.
- **Verifier:** OVERTURNED. The accounting in app/slo_metrics.py:31-60 is real (thread-locked, correct cumulative histogram) and the endpoint is genuinely open (200 with COPILOT_API_TOKEN=secret123, since /metrics/slo IS in _OPEN_PATHS at main.py:230). But the FEED is far narrower than 'fed from real traffic': grep shows _record_slo has exactly ONE call site, main.py:1613, inside POST /chat, guarded by `elif result.status == "completed"`. So (a) POST /chat/stream (main.py:1816), which is what the shipped UI calls (frontend/src/api.ts:89), records nothing; (b) the PagerDuty path _run_triggered_investigation (main.py:2874) meters/spends but never records SLO metrics; (c) failures never reach _record_slo at all, so copilot_investigations_failed_total is structurally always 0 — and success is `bool(report) or bool(result.final_text)`, so success ~= total. The headline reliability numbers are unmeasurable. Counters are also a per-process in-memory singleton (no persistence, divergent across uvicorn workers).

**Verifier notes for this domain.** I re-ran everything from scratch under TestClient rather than trusting quoted output.

REPRODUCED EXACTLY: budget for target=0.99 / SLI=0.995 -> {"consumed":0.0,"remaining_percent":100.0,"burn_rate":0.0,"status":"healthy"}; burn-rate -> {"short":360.0,"long":60.0,"severity":"critical"}; target=0.999/SLI=0.9999 -> short=72.0, long=12.0, severity "high"; POST /api/status/components -> 500 KeyError "Attempt to overwrite 'name' in LogRecord"; uptime 100.0 for every component including after a full create->resolve incident cycle; cross-process SLO count 1 -> 0; 401 on /api/status, /api/status/components, /api/status/incidents, /api/status/subscribe with COPILOT_API_TOKEN set; notify_subscribers([unverified-email, failing-verified-webhook]) -> {'success': [], 'failed': ['unverified1']}; webhook fan-out delivered 1 signed POST with X-Status-Signature t=...,v1=...; datadog list_services() -> ['checkout-svc','inventory-svc'] (offline demo fixtures).

ONE OVERTURN: GET /metrics/slo is NOT "WORKS". The accounting code is real, but the feed is far narrower than claimed. app/api/main.py:1613 is the ONLY _record_slo call site (grep confirms), it sits inside POST /chat, and it is guarded by `elif result.status == "completed"`. Consequences: (a) POST /chat/stream (app/api/main.py:1816) — the endpoint the shipped frontend actually uses, frontend/src/api.ts:89 — never records anything; (b) the PagerDuty-triggered path _run_triggered_investigation (app/api/main.py:2874) meters and spends but never records SLO metrics; (c) because only status=="completed" records, and success is `bool(report) or bool(result.final_text)`, copilot_investigations_failed_total is structurally always 0 from live traffic — the single most important agent-SLO signal is a hardcoded zero. Also the counters are a per-process in-memory singleton, so a multi-worker deployment serves a different worker's counters on each scrape.

THINGS THE AUDITOR MISSED:
1. The /metrics/slo feed gap above.
2. TEST THEATER, precisely located: there is NO test file for either live router. `ls tests/ | grep -i 'slo|status_page'` returns only test_slo_metrics.py, test_slo_poller.py, test_status_page_widgets.py — the last of which exercises only the DEAD widgets module. The one error-budget test (tests/test_advanced_features.py:213-235) records good=995/total=1000 against target=0.99 and asserts ONLY `budget.total_budget == approx(0.01)`. It never asserts `consumed`, which is the broken field. That is why the bug ships green.
3. POST /api/status/components is a PARTIAL WRITE, not a clean failure: app/status_page/store.py:300 inserts into self._components BEFORE the crashing log at :301. I confirmed GET /api/status/components afterwards lists a real 'comp_dcfbe7b7a744'. So the client gets a 500 with no id while an orphaned, un-addressable component is silently added to the public status page. The auditor's blocker text ("users cannot add any component") understates this.
4. Billing SELLS the dead scheduled-reports feature: app/billing/plans.py:75/99/131/166 set scheduled_reports=True on paid tiers and plans.py:349 labels it "Scheduled Reports" in the customer-facing feature map, while app/scheduled_reports/ has no APIRouter and no non-test importer.
5. Frontend bypasses an EXISTING auth helper: frontend/src/api.ts:10 has `authHeaders` that attaches `Bearer ${TOKEN}`, but StatusPage.tsx:111/139 and SLOTracker.tsx:378/387/508 all use bare `fetch`. This is an easy fix the auditor framed as an architectural gap.
6. There are 5 default components (api, dashboard, database, authentication, notifications), not 4.
7. SLO org isolation is genuinely enforced server-side (orgB GET/DELETE of orgA's SLO both -> 404, verified); the defect is purely that org_id is a client-supplied `Query("default")`, so anyone can just pass ?org_id=orgA. Worth stating precisely.
8. The frontend SLI fabrication is worse than described: since the backend returns burn_rate=0.0 for ANY SLO meeting target, `current = target*100 - burn_rate*0.1` renders exactly the target. A service at 99.5% against a 99% target displays "current 99.00%" — the target masquerading as the measurement.


## Model router, cost estimation, budgets, LLM spend tracking

**Summary.** This domain is split into two halves that never meet. The half that is actually load-bearing — per-run token budget, fleet-wide token cap, per-tenant metering, real token accounting from provider `usage_metadata`, and a 2-regex fast/main model triage in `app/routing.py` — is small, real, and I verified it enforces (I reproduced a 429 from `quota_gate` after exceeding a configured cap). The half that is marketed as the product's "adaptive model router with cost optimization" — the entire `/api/model-router/*` surface — is a live, unauthenticated-in-dev API whose output nothing consumes: `get_llm()` is never called with an explicit model anywhere in the repo, so no routing decision ever changes which model runs. Its pricing table is worse than stale: it contains only 2024-era models (claude-3-5-*, claude-3-opus, gpt-4o), lists gpt-4o at 2x its real price, and returns **$0.00 cost for every model the product actually calls** (`claude-opus-4-8`, `claude-haiku-4-5`) because they aren't in the table — I recorded 480,000 tokens of real-model usage and the analytics endpoint reported `total_cost: 0.0`. Budgets in that subsystem are report-only and stored in a module-level dict that dies on restart. On top of that, four of the five other cost/router packages (`app/cost_optimizer`, `app/cost_optimization`, `app/cost_tracking`, `app/llm_advanced`, plus `app/router` and `app/analytics/cost_attribution`) — roughly 7,300 lines — are never imported by the live app. A paying user could call these endpoints and get plausible JSON back, but would be billed by their LLM provider for amounts this system reports as zero.

**Wiring.** LIVE: `app/model_router/api_router.py` is the only cost/routing router registered (`app/api/main.py:742`, mounted at `/api`), exposing all 13 `/api/model-router/*` routes; all returned 200 unauthenticated (the shared-bearer `require_auth` is open when `COPILOT_API_TOKEN` is unset). Also live and real: `app/spend.py` via `_get_spend()`/`quota_gate` (`app/api/main.py:500-560`, wired as a `Depends` on the two investigation routes at main.py:1552 and 1763, plus `app/api/websocket.py:391`), `app/metering.py` via `/usage` (main.py:2025) and `quota_gate`, `app/routing.py:use_fast_model` via `app/graph/nodes.py:191`, and `app/cache.py:get_cache().stats()` via `/cache/stats` (main.py:2060). NO ENTRYPOINT: `python3 -c "from app.api.main import app; ..."` then inspecting `sys.modules` shows only `app.model_router.*` loaded from this domain — `app/cost_optimizer/` (3,479 LOC), `app/cost_optimization/` (495), `app/cost_tracking/` (1,346, tests only), `app/llm_advanced/` (928, tests only), `app/router/` (1,054, tests only), and `app/analytics/cost_attribution.py` are never imported by live code. The React frontend contains zero references to `model-router` (grep over `frontend/src` returned nothing), so no UI surfaces any of it.


### `BROKEN` — Cost estimation for a model request

- **Surface:** POST /api/model-router/estimate-cost, POST /api/model-router/record-usage, GET /api/model-router/stats
- **Severity:** critical · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** app/model_router/cost_optimizer.py:139-146 — on an unknown model_id `estimate_cost` returns `estimated_cost=0.0` with no error; cost_optimizer.py:196-200 — `record_usage` sets `cost = 0.0` for unknown models. The catalog (DEFAULT_COSTS, cost_optimizer.py:76-105) contains only claude-3-5-sonnet-latest, claude-3-5-haiku-latest, claude-3-opus-latest, gpt-4o, gpt-4o-mini. But app/llm.py:31-37 sets the anthropic defaults to `claude-opus-4-8` / `claude-haiku-4-5` — neither is in the table. Executed via TestClient: POST /api/model-router/estimate-cost {'model_id':'claude-opus-4-8','input_tokens':100000,'output_tokens':20000} -> 200 {'estimated_cost': 0.0, 'with_caching': 0.0, 'with_batching': 0.0}. Same for claude-haiku-4-5, claude-sonnet-5, claude-opus-5. I then POSTed record-usage for all four (480,000 tokens total) and GET /api/model-router/stats returned cost_analytics total_cost 0.0 / total_tokens 480000. Real cost for that traffic at Opus 4.8 rates ($5/$25 per MTok) is roughly $1,000.
- **Blocker:** The pricing table has no entry for any model the product actually runs, and both estimate_cost and record_usage silently fall back to $0.00 instead of raising or flagging unknown-model. Cost is therefore under-reported by 100% for all real traffic.
- **Fix:** Return an explicit error/`"priced": false` flag instead of 0.0 for unknown model ids, and add the current model ids to DEFAULT_COSTS: claude-opus-5 / claude-opus-4-8 at 0.005/0.025 per 1k, claude-sonnet-5 at 0.003/0.015, claude-haiku-4-5 at 0.001/0.005. Better: derive the table from the same source as app/llm.py:_DEFAULTS so they cannot drift.

### `BROKEN` — SLA min_quality floor in routing

- **Surface:** POST /api/model-router/route (min_quality field)
- **Severity:** high · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** app/model_router/adaptive_router.py:213 compares ModelTier enums by their STRING values: `if sla.min_quality.value > target_tier.value: target_tier = sla.min_quality`. The tier strings sort alphabetically as balanced < fast < premium < quality, which is not the capability order. Reproduced directly: router.route(P0 query, ENTERPRISE, no SLA) -> claude-3-opus-latest (target premium); same query with SLARequirements(min_quality=ModelTier.QUALITY) -> gpt-4o (target quality). Python confirms `'quality' > 'premium'` is True. Also `'balanced' > 'fast'` is False, so the API's DEFAULT min_quality='balanced' (api_router.py:59) can never raise a FAST route to BALANCED — the floor is a silent no-op in the most common case.
- **Blocker:** A parameter documented as a minimum quality floor instead DOWNGRADES a critical P0 route from Opus to gpt-4o, and is inert for its own default value.
- **Fix:** Compare by index into the existing `tier_order` list (already defined at adaptive_router.py:222) instead of by `.value` string, e.g. `if tier_order.index(sla.min_quality) > tier_order.index(target_tier)`.

### `STUB` — Budget set / check in the model-router

- **Surface:** POST /api/model-router/budget/set, GET /api/model-router/budget/check
- **Severity:** high · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** Executed: POST /budget/set {'customer_id':'acme','monthly_budget':0.001} -> 200; POST /record-usage gpt-4o 1000+1000 for acme -> cost 0.02; GET /budget/check?customer_id=acme -> 200 {'can_proceed': false, 'reasons': ['Customer budget exceeded ($-0.0190 remaining)']}. The arithmetic is correct — but `can_proceed:false` blocks nothing. `grep -rn 'check_budget' app/ | grep -v app/model_router/ | grep -v app/cost_optimizer/` finds no caller; the LLM path (app/graph/nodes.py, app/llm.py) never consults it. Storage is `self._customer_budgets: dict` and `self._usage_history: list` on a module-global singleton (app/model_router/cost_optimizer.py:118-121, api_router.py:28-31 `_cost_optimizer: CostOptimizer | None = None`) — no SQLAlchemy model, nothing in app/database/models.py, no migration; every budget and every recorded dollar is lost on restart and invisible to other workers. Additionally budget_limit_daily/budget_limit_monthly are never settable through any endpoint, so daily_remaining/monthly_remaining are permanently null (confirmed in the response above). And customer_id is a free-text client-supplied string with no tenant binding, so any authenticated caller can read or overwrite any other customer's budget.
- **Blocker:** Report-only, in-memory, per-process, and un-scoped. Exceeding a budget does not block an LLM call; restarting the process wipes all budgets and spend.
- **Fix:** Persist UsageRecord and customer budgets to the existing SQLAlchemy layer with a migration, derive customer_id from the authenticated tenant rather than the request body, and call check_budget from the investigation entrypoint alongside the existing quota_gate so exceeding a budget actually raises 402/429.

### `STUB` — Usage tracking & analytics (daily/monthly/stats)

- **Surface:** POST /api/model-router/record-usage, GET /api/model-router/usage/daily, /usage/monthly, /stats
- **Severity:** high · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** Endpoints return correctly-shaped aggregates: GET /usage/monthly?customer_id=acme -> 200 {'total_cost':0.02,'total_requests':1,'total_input_tokens':1000,'total_output_tokens':1000,'by_model':{'gpt-4o':{...}}}, and GET /stats returns routing + 7-day cost analytics. But the entire store is `self._usage_history: list[UsageRecord]` on a process-global CostOptimizer (app/model_router/cost_optimizer.py:119, api_router.py:30/47) — no DB table, no migration, no cross-worker sharing, purged to 30 days by an in-process list comprehension (cost_optimizer.py:359-366). Nothing in the LLM path ever calls record_usage: `grep -rn 'record_usage' app/ | grep -v app/model_router/|grep -v app/cost_optimizer/` finds only unrelated tenant-store code. So the only rows that ever exist are ones a client POSTs by hand, and as shown above they price at $0 for every real model. GET /stats on a fresh process returns total_routed 0 / total_cost 0.0.
- **Blocker:** In-memory only, never populated by the actual LLM calls, and prices real models at zero. It is a scratchpad with an HTTP interface, not spend tracking.
- **Fix:** Emit a usage record from app/graph/nodes.py:_log_usage into a persisted table (an LLMUsage model + Alembic migration), and have these endpoints read that table instead of the process-local list.

### `DEAD_CODE` — Response caching layer

- **Surface:** app/cache.py, GET /cache/stats
- **Severity:** medium · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** app/cache.py implements a full MemoryCache (TTL+LRU) and RedisCache with `@cached` (line 310) and `@cached_async` (line 355) decorators. `grep -rn 'from app.cache import\|cache.cached' app/ tests/` returns exactly two hits in app/ — app/api/main.py:2066 `from app.cache import get_cache` for the stats endpoint — and two in tests/test_cache.py. The decorators are applied to nothing in production code. Executed GET /cache/stats -> 200 {'backend':'memory','enabled':true,'size':0,'max_size':1000,'hits':0,'misses':0,'hit_rate':0.0,'evictions':0}. Separately, the semantic/LLM cache in app/llm_advanced/semantic_cache.py (416 LOC) is imported only by tests/test_roadmap_features.py.
- **Blocker:** No call site caches anything, so no request or LLM call is ever served from cache. /cache/stats reports enabled:true with permanently zero hits, which reads as a working cache with a 0% hit rate rather than an unused module.
- **Fix:** Either decorate the expensive read paths (usage aggregates, model catalog, integration fetches) with @cached_async, or drop /cache/stats so it stops implying a live cache.

### `DEAD_CODE` — Alternate cost/router packages (cost_optimizer, cost_optimization, cost_tracking, llm_advanced, router, analytics/cost_attribution)

- **Surface:** none — no router registration, no import from live code, no CLI
- **Severity:** medium · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** `python3 -c "from app.api.main import app; import sys; print([m for m in sys.modules if 'cost' in m or 'model_router' in m or m.startswith('app.router') or m.startswith('app.analytics')])"` prints only ['app.model_router', 'app.model_router.adaptive_router', 'app.model_router.api_router', 'app.model_router.complexity_analyzer', 'app.model_router.cost_optimizer'] — nothing else in the domain is loaded. External-import grep: app/cost_optimizer/ (3,479 LOC across budget_manager, gpu_tracker, model_router, roi_tracker, token_optimizer) and app/cost_optimization/ (495 LOC) have ZERO references anywhere in the repo including tests; app/cost_tracking/ (1,346 LOC) and app/llm_advanced/ (928 LOC) and app/router/ (1,054 LOC) are imported only by tests/test_moat_builders.py, tests/test_roadmap_features.py, tests/test_router.py. app/analytics/cost_attribution.py is reachable only via app/analytics/__init__.py, which nothing live imports. All five packages import cleanly (verified), so this is unreachable code, not broken code. Notable irony: the dead app/cost_optimizer/token_optimizer.py:200-224 has real CREATE TABLE token_usage DDL and Redis counters — actual persistence the LIVE path lacks — and the dead app/llm_advanced/model_router.py has more accurate prices than the live table.
- **Blocker:** ~7,300 lines of cost/routing code with no entrypoint. tests/test_router.py (45 passed) and tests/test_moat_builders.py give these packages a green test signal while nothing in the product can reach them — test theater at the package level.
- **Fix:** Delete four of the five, or pick the best implementation (cost_optimizer/token_optimizer has the DB schema, llm_advanced/model_router has the correct pricing) and consolidate it behind the one registered router.
- **Verifier:** Regenerated every number. After `from app.api.main import app`, sys.modules filtered for cost/model_router/app.router/app.analytics/app.llm_advanced contains ONLY ['app.model_router', 'app.model_router.adaptive_router', 'app.model_router.api_router', 'app.model_router.complexity_analyzer', 'app.model_router.cost_optimizer'] — nothing else in the domain loads. Per-package external-reference grep (excluding each package's own tree): app/cost_optimizer/ 3479 LOC, ZERO references anywhere including tests; app/cost_optimization/ 495 LOC, ZERO references; app/cost_tracking/ 1346 LOC, only tests/test_moat_builders.py:70-80; app/llm_advanced/ 928 LOC, only tests/test_roadmap_features.py:27,42; app/router/ 1054 LOC, only tests/test_router.py:4-13; app/analytics/cost_attribution.py reachable only via app/analytics/__init__.py:35 (nothing live imports it) plus tests/test_roadmap_features.py:33. app/eval is also not loaded (sys.modules filter for 'app.eval' -> []). ~7,300 LOC with no entrypoint, green-tested. Confirmed the irony the auditor flagged: the dead app/llm_advanced/model_router.py:78-100 carries more accurate prices than the live table.

### `PARTIAL` — Model catalog & pricing table accuracy

- **Surface:** GET /api/model-router/models, GET /api/model-router/tiers, POST /api/model-router/recommend
- **Severity:** high · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** GET /api/model-router/models -> 200, lists exactly 5 models, all from 2024. Prices vs. reality: gpt-4o listed at 0.005/0.015 per 1k (cost_optimizer.py:95-99) but the real price has been $2.50/$10 per MTok (0.0025/0.01) since Aug 2024 — 2x overstated on input. claude-3-5-haiku-latest listed at 0.001/0.005 but its actual price is $0.80/$4 per MTok (0.0008/0.004) — the launch price, never updated. Proof this repo already knows the right numbers: the DEAD app/llm_advanced/model_router.py:82-95 has gpt-4o at 0.0025/0.01 and claude-3-5-haiku at 0.0008/0.004. Anthropic cached-input is modelled as a flat 0.1x read (cost_optimizer.py:24) with no 1.25x cache-WRITE cost, so `with_caching` always understates. Latency/quality_score values (adaptive_router.py:96-137, e.g. quality_score 0.85, avg_latency_ms 1500) are invented constants with no measurement behind them — nothing in the repo ever updates them. POST /recommend for use_case=coding/budget=quality -> 200 {'recommended_model':'claude-3-opus-latest'}, a hardcoded lookup table (adaptive_router.py:472-495) recommending a legacy model the product cannot call.
- **Blocker:** Catalog is a hand-written 2024 snapshot with at least two materially wrong prices, invented quality/latency scores, and no entry for any model in current use.
- **Fix:** Replace the static dict with current pricing, add the 4.x/5 model ids, and either measure quality_score/avg_latency_ms from recorded usage or drop those fields from the response so callers don't treat them as data.

### `PARTIAL` — Adaptive routing decision changes which model runs

- **Surface:** POST /api/model-router/route (and app/llm.py get_llm)
- **Severity:** high · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** POST /api/model-router/route works and returns a real computed decision: {'query':'production outage P0 customers affected, root cause?','incident_severity':'p0'} -> 200 model_id=gpt-4o, target_tier=quality, complexity level=critical. But nothing consumes it. `grep -rn 'get_llm(' app/` shows every call site is `get_llm()` or `get_llm(fast=True)` (app/graph/nodes.py:124,167-178,313,900,1099; app/agents/base.py:92) — the `model` parameter of app/llm.py:64 is never passed by any caller. app/llm.py contains zero references to model_router. `grep -rn 'AdaptiveModelRouter\|get_router()' app/ | grep -v app/model_router/` returns nothing. The routing decision is also not persisted anywhere a worker could read it: `_router` is a module-global (app/model_router/api_router.py:28-31).
- **Blocker:** The router is an advisory HTTP endpoint with no consumer. Its decision never reaches app/llm.py, so the actual model used at runtime is unaffected by anything this API returns. No frontend calls it either.
- **Fix:** Either wire it: have graph/nodes.py call the router once per run and pass `get_llm(model=decision.model_id)`; or delete the endpoint and keep app/routing.py as the honest routing story. Wiring requires first fixing the catalog so the router can return models the provider layer can actually build.

### `PARTIAL` — Per-run token budget kill switch

- **Surface:** COPILOT_MAX_TOKENS_PER_RUN -> app/graph/nodes.py:_over_token_budget
- **Severity:** medium · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** app/graph/nodes.py:66-70 `_over_token_budget(tokens_used, settings)` compares real accumulated provider tokens against settings.copilot_max_tokens_per_run; when over, nodes.py:186-215 flips the agent to the no-tools LLM with a 'summarize now' instruction, which genuinely terminates the agent<->tools loop. Also checked at nodes.py:320 and 1184. Real, not a no-op. However app/config.py:276 defaults `copilot_max_tokens_per_run: int = 0`, and line 70 returns False when cap is 0 — so out of the box there is NO per-run ceiling.
- **Blocker:** Off by default (cap=0). It is a real enforcement mechanism only once an operator sets the env var.
- **Fix:** Ship a sane non-zero default (e.g. 200k) so a runaway loop cannot bill unbounded on a fresh install.

### `PARTIAL` — Fleet-wide LLM spend cap (hard block)

- **Surface:** app/spend.py + quota_gate dependency on POST investigation routes (app/api/main.py:1552, 1763) and app/api/websocket.py:391
- **Severity:** medium · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** Executed end-to-end: set COPILOT_GLOBAL_TOKEN_CAP=1000, cleared the settings cache, called `await main._record_spend(1500)` then `await main.quota_gate()` -> raised HTTPException 429 'Fleet-wide token budget reached — investigations are paused.' plus a WARNING 'fleet spend at 1500/1000 tokens (>= 80% of cap)'. This is genuine enforcement, not reporting. app/spend.py:24-29 pure cap/alert math; InMemorySpend (per-process) and RedisSpend (INCRBY on a window-bucketed key with TTL, shared across replicas) at spend.py:32-86; selected by make_spend_tracker on COPILOT_REDIS_URL. tests/test_spend.py: 5 passed. Caveat: app/config.py:281 defaults copilot_global_token_cap=0 (off), and without Redis the counter is per-process so an N-worker deployment effectively gets an Nx cap.
- **Blocker:** Disabled by default, and the in-memory backend does not share state across workers (documented at spend.py:11-13). Needs COPILOT_GLOBAL_TOKEN_CAP + COPILOT_REDIS_URL to be a real fleet guardrail.
- **Fix:** Nothing structural; document that the cap requires Redis in multi-worker deployments, and consider defaulting the cap to non-zero.

### `PARTIAL` — Per-tenant usage metering & plan quota

- **Surface:** app/metering.py, GET /usage, quota_gate (402)
- **Severity:** medium · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** app/metering.py:22-45 records conclusive investigations + tokens to the tenant store with an idempotency key; :48-56 over_quota() checks month-to-date investigations against the plan quota; app/api/main.py:549-552 raises HTTP 402 when over. This is real enforcement against a persisted ledger (tenant store), not a counter in a dict. Executed: GET /usage -> 200 {'multi_tenant': false, 'detail': 'usage quotas apply in multi-tenant mode only'}. app/metering.py:26-28 returns early when no tenant is on the contextvar.
- **Blocker:** Entirely inert in single-tenant mode, which is the default configuration I ran. I could not exercise the enforcing path without a configured tenant store, so the quota math itself is verified only by reading (models.quota / within_quota).
- **Fix:** None for the design; to verify, add a test that sets tenant_context and asserts the 402. Note the token figure it records is investigation tokens, not dollars — there is no dollar-denominated tenant budget anywhere.

### `WORKS` — Query complexity analysis

- **Surface:** POST /api/model-router/analyze-complexity
- **Severity:** low · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** app/model_router/complexity_analyzer.py:161-260 — a deterministic, pre-compiled-regex heuristic, not random and not a canned value. It scores 5 components (semantic/context/reasoning/domain/stakes) via pattern hits with fixed increments, takes a weighted sum (0.15/0.2/0.25/0.15/0.25), and applies a stakes override at _score_to_level (line 316). Executed: 'show pods' -> level=trivial score=0.1; 'production outage P0 customers affected, root cause?' with incident_severity=p0 -> level=critical score=0.275, detected_patterns listing the matched high_stakes/complex_reasoning regexes. No random.*, no hardcoded return. Caveat, not a defect but worth stating plainly: this is regex keyword matching plus a weighted sum — there is no classifier or model behind it, and estimated_tokens is `len(query.split()) * 2 * 3` (line 371-383), a word-count approximation, not a tokenizer.

### `WORKS` — Runtime fast/main model triage (the routing that actually happens)

- **Surface:** app/routing.py -> app/graph/nodes.py:191 (agent_node), COPILOT_MODEL_ROUTING
- **Severity:** low · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** app/routing.py:38-51 `classify_difficulty` / `use_fast_model` — two regexes, complex-wins-ties, unknown->complex. Consumed for real at app/graph/nodes.py:191: `fast = routing.use_fast_model(_last_user_text(state), settings.copilot_model_routing)` then `tools_llm = llm_tools_fast if fast else llm_tools` (nodes.py:192-193), which are `get_llm(fast=True)` vs `get_llm()` (nodes.py:175-178) resolving to claude-haiku-4-5 vs claude-opus-4-8 (app/llm.py:32). Enabled by default (app/config.py:343 copilot_model_routing=True). Executed: 'which services are emitting logs?' -> simple/fast=True; 'why is checkout failing?' -> complex/fast=False; 'summarize the incident' -> complex (correctly biased away from under-powering). tests/test_routing.py passes (45 passed with tests/test_router.py).

### `WORKS` — Real token accounting

- **Surface:** app/graph/nodes.py:_log_usage, AgentState.tokens_used
- **Severity:** low · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** app/graph/nodes.py:38-63 reads `resp.usage_metadata` from the LangChain response and returns `total_tokens` — provider-reported, not estimated from character count. It also logs cache_read/cache_creation so a silently non-caching prefix is visible. Returns 0 (not a guess) when a provider omits usage_metadata. Same pattern at app/agents/base.py:313. These totals feed the per-run budget (nodes.py:186), the fleet spend counter, and metering. Contrast with the dead/advisory path, where ComplexityAnalyzer._estimate_tokens uses word count * 6.

**Verifier notes for this domain.** WHAT I RE-RAN (all regenerated, not taken from the auditor):
1. TestClient against app.api.main: /api/model-router/{estimate-cost,record-usage,stats,models,tiers,recommend,budget/set,budget/check,usage/daily,usage/monthly}, /cache/stats, /usage.
2. Direct AdaptiveModelRouter.route() and ComplexityAnalyzer.analyze() calls with hand-built SLARequirements/CustomerTier.
3. asyncio harness for main._record_spend + main.quota_gate with COPILOT_GLOBAL_TOKEN_CAP=1000 -> reproduced HTTPException 429.
4. A REAL multi-tenant metering run the auditor said they could not do (see below).
5. Restart simulation: two separate python3 processes hitting /usage/monthly + /budget/check for the same customer_id.
6. sys.modules inspection after importing the app; LOC counts and external-import greps for all six alternate packages.
7. pytest tests/test_routing.py tests/test_spend.py -q -p no:cacheprovider -> 12 passed; -k "token_budget or over_token or max_tokens_per_run" -> 2 passed.

THINGS THE AUDITOR MISSED (all reproduced):

A. /Users/ankitpandey/OmniPotent/OmniPotent/app/model_router/complexity_analyzer.py:316-320 — STAKES BOUNDARY OFF-BY-ONE. `_score_to_level` uses `if stakes > 0.5: CRITICAL` and `if stakes > 0.3: ...`, but analyze() adds EXACTLY 0.5 for p0/sev0/critical and EXACTLY 0.3 for p1/sev1/major (lines 219-225). So a declared incident with no lucky keyword hit never trips its own branch. Executed: analyze("restart the checkout service", incident_severity="p0") -> level=medium, stakes=0.5; same with "p1" -> level=trivial, stakes=0.3. Routed end-to-end: router.route("restart the checkout service", ENTERPRISE, incident_severity="p0") -> claude-3-5-sonnet-latest, target_tier=balanced. An explicitly declared P0 gets the balanced model and a declared P1 gets the FAST model. This is a second, independent routing-correctness bug on top of the min_quality string-compare bug.

B. Same file: the score-only HIGH threshold (>=0.7, line 322) is effectively unreachable. An engineered query maxing reasoning_depth=1.0, semantic=0.85, context=0.75, domain=0.4 with zero stakes scored only 0.588 -> MEDIUM. HIGH/CRITICAL are reachable in practice only via the stakes path, which has bug (A).

C. /Users/ankitpandey/OmniPotent/OmniPotent/app/model_router/cost_optimizer.py:95-104 — gpt-4o and gpt-4o-mini leave `cached_input_cost_per_1k` at its 0.0 default, so `with_caching` (line 168-169) treats OpenAI cached input as FREE. Executed: gpt-4o 100k in / 20k out -> {'estimated_cost': 0.8, 'with_caching': 0.3} — 0.3 is the output cost alone. Real OpenAI cached input is 50% of input, not 0%. Distinct from the Anthropic cache-write gap the auditor noted.

D. api_router.py:71-76 — `use_cache` and `use_batch` are accepted on POST /estimate-cost and then never read; `estimate_cost` ignores both parameters (they appear only in the signature, cost_optimizer.py:135-136) and always returns all three figures. Dead request fields.

E. POST /api/model-router/recommend silently accepts an unknown use_case. Executed {'use_case':'bogus','budget':'quality'} -> 200 recommended_model=claude-3-opus-latest. No 400/422.

F. AUTH: the auditor's blanket "all returned 200 unauthenticated" is incomplete. With COPILOT_API_TOKEN=secret123 set, GET /api/model-router/models and POST /api/model-router/budget/set both return 401. The real defect is narrower and worse: under a single SHARED bearer token, `customer_id` is free-text from the request body with no tenant binding, so any authenticated caller can read or overwrite any customer's budget (I overwrote acme's 0.001 budget with 999999 and flipped can_proceed from false to true).

G. A THIRD, mutually inconsistent pricing table exists at app/eval/metrics.py:134-140 (`MODEL_PRICING`, per-MTok, gpt-4o at $5/$15 — also stale). app/eval is NOT loaded by the live app (sys.modules check returned []). So the repo carries three disagreeing price tables (model_router/cost_optimizer.py, llm_advanced/model_router.py, eval/metrics.py) and the only live one is the least accurate.

H. app/metering.py:62 — `remaining = (limit - used) if limit >= 0 else -1`, but -1 is also the documented "unlimited" sentinel (line 67 comment). At exactly 51 used / 50 quota, GET /usage returns investigations_remaining: -1, indistinguishable from an unlimited plan. Reproduced in the run below.

I. LIVE PATH TRACKS TOKENS ONLY, NEVER DOLLARS. app/graph/nodes.py:_log_usage, AgentState.tokens_used, app/spend.py and app/metering.py all deal exclusively in token counts. No live endpoint converts tokens to a dollar figure. The only dollar arithmetic in the repo lives in the disconnected model_router / dead packages. So there is no correct spend-in-dollars number anywhere a user can reach.

METERING RUN THE AUDITOR COULD NOT DO: pointed COPILOT_TENANT_DB at a tmp sqlite file, called store.setup() + create_org(plan="free"), set the tenant contextvar, then recorded 51 investigations. Results: usage_summary before {'investigations_used': 0, 'investigations_quota': 50, 'investigations_remaining': 50}; after 51 {'investigations_used': 51, 'tokens_used': 51000, 'warning': True}; metering.over_quota() -> True; main.quota_gate() -> HTTPException 402 "Monthly investigation quota reached for your plan". Idempotency also verified: re-recording with idem="run-0" left the count at 51. The quota math and 402 enforcement are REAL and now execution-verified, not read-verified.

CONFIRMED VERBATIM: the min_quality string-compare bug (P0/ENTERPRISE no-SLA -> claude-3-opus-latest, same query with min_quality=QUALITY -> gpt-4o; 'quality'>'premium' is True, 'balanced'>'fast' is False), the $0.00 fallback for claude-opus-4-8/claude-haiku-4-5, the restart wipe (two processes, monthly total 0.02 -> 0.0), zero AdaptiveModelRouter consumers, zero @cached/@cached_async applications, and the exact dead-package LOC (cost_optimizer 3479, cost_optimization 495, cost_tracking 1346, llm_advanced 928, router 1054) with the exact external-reference sets.


## Verification, event intelligence, decision engine, policy, safety gates, guardrails

**Summary.** This domain splits cleanly into two halves. The half that is actually wired into the live agent loop — `app/policy.py` (graduated approve/notify/allow tool gate + evidence-confidence gate) and `app/guardrails.py` (prompt-injection provenance boxing) — is real, pure, deterministic, unit-tested, and genuinely invoked before every tool call via `app/graph/edges.py:29` and `app/graph/builder.py:91`. That is the load-bearing safety layer and it works. The half that is exposed as REST (`/api/verification/*`, `/api/event-intelligence/*`) is an island: it 200s on every call but has zero configured evidence sources, zero persistence, zero auth, and zero callers anywhere else in the codebase. The single biggest problem is that `/api/verification/claims/verify` can never return `verified`: the `EvidenceCollector` is constructed with `prometheus_url=getattr(settings,"prometheus_url",None)` but no such field exists on `Settings`, and `k8s_client`/`knowledge_graph`/`redis_client` are hardcoded `None` (app/verification/api_router.py:38-44), so evidence is always `[]` and every claim collapses to `insufficient_data` or a false `refuted`/`hallucination_detected`. Second worst: `/api/verification/actions/check` returns `is_safe: true, safety_score: 1.0` for `drop_database` and `terraform_destroy` because unrecognized action types fall through to a generic checker backed by a fabricated `{"replicas": 2}` state. The much larger `decision_engine`, `policy_engine`, `safety_gates`, `risk`, `validation`, and `governance` packages (~9k lines) are dead — `app/api/v1/__init__.py` is never included by `app/api/main.py`, producing 0 live `/v1/*` routes. A paying user could use the copilot's approval gate today; they could not use any of the REST verification/policy surface for anything real.

**Wiring.** LIVE (registered in app/api/main.py:738 and :760, both unauthenticated): /api/verification/{claims/verify, claims/verify-batch, hypotheses/verify, actions/check, actions/{id}/validate, outcomes, calibration, evidence-sources, cache} and /api/event-intelligence/{noise/check, severity/predict, features/record-metric, features/record-alert, features/{service}/{metric}, deployments/start|end|{service}, maintenance/add|{service}, stats}. Confirmed 200 without any auth header, unlike /api/teams which 401s.

LIVE VIA THE AGENT GRAPH (not HTTP-addressable but on the hot path): app/policy.py -> app/graph/edges.py:29 (route_after_agent -> "approval"), app/session.py:140 (_pending_gate -> policy.confidence_gate), app/mcp/client.py:19; app/guardrails.py -> app/graph/builder.py:91 (guarded ToolNode wraps every ToolMessage).

DEAD / NO ENTRYPOINT: app/api/v1/decision.py and app/api/v1/policy.py — `app/api/v1/__init__.py` builds a /v1 router but nothing imports app.api.v1; `python3 -c "from app.api.main import app; print(len([r for r in app.routes if r.path.startswith('/v1')]))"` -> 0. app/safety_gates/* — only importer is app/agents/parallel_integration.py, which has zero importers itself (grep returned nothing outside the file). app/decision_engine/* and app/policy_engine/* — reachable only via app/coordinator/decision_integration.py (DecisionAwareDAGExecutor), but app/coordinator/api_router.py constructs a plain `DAGExecutor` (line 53), never the decision-aware one. app/risk/*, app/validation/*, app/governance.py — grep for `from app.risk|from app.validation|governance` outside their own packages returns zero hits in app/.


### `BROKEN` — Verify a claim (POST /api/verification/claims/verify)

- **Surface:** POST /api/verification/claims/verify, /hypotheses/verify, /claims/verify-batch
- **Severity:** critical · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** The verification pipeline is real code (app/verification/evidence_collector.py issues genuine httpx calls to Prometheus /api/v1/query, Loki /loki/api/v1/query_range, ES /_search) but it is wired to sources that can never exist. app/verification/api_router.py:38-44 builds the singleton agent with `prometheus_url=getattr(settings,"prometheus_url",None)`, `loki_url=getattr(...)`, `elasticsearch_url=getattr(...)`, `k8s_client=None`, `knowledge_graph=None`, `redis_client=None`. Those three settings DO NOT EXIST: `python3 -c "from app.config import Settings; print([f for f in Settings.model_fields if 'url' in f])"` -> ['traces_api_url','deploys_api_url','argocd_server_url','slack_webhook_url','teams_webhook_url','jira_base_url','frontend_url','backend_url','status_page_url','docs_url','supabase_jwks_url','supabase_url','supabase_database_url','copilot_redis_url','copilot_prompt_cache','copilot_database_url'] — no prometheus/loki/elasticsearch. getattr therefore always yields None, `_collect_metric_evidence` returns [] at evidence_collector.py:159, `_collect_git_evidence` is an outright stub (`return evidence` with nothing added, evidence_collector.py:~390), so collect_evidence() always returns []. Executed live: POST {'text':'The database is down','service_id':'svc'} -> 200 {"status":"insufficient_data","confidence":0.2,"reasoning":"No direct evidence found..."}. POST {'text':'Error rate rose to 42 percent'} -> 200 {"status":"refuted","confidence":0.1,"hallucination_detected":true,"hallucination_type":"fabricated_fact"}. POST with cited_values {'error_rate':0.42} -> also refuted/fabricated_fact. Because verification_agent.py:170-186 requires len(supporting)>0 to reach VERIFIED and supporting is derived from evidence, the VERIFIED branch is structurally unreachable in the shipped configuration.
- **Blocker:** Settings has no prometheus_url/loki_url/elasticsearch_url field, and k8s/knowledge-graph/redis are hardcoded None, so evidence is always empty. Worse than a no-op: an empty evidence list makes the hallucination detector flag any numeric claim as a FABRICATED_FACT, so the endpoint actively returns FALSE 'refuted' verdicts on true statements.
- **Fix:** Add prometheus_url/loki_url/elasticsearch_url to app/config.Settings (or read the existing traces_api_url/deploys_api_url), pass the real k8s client and knowledge graph into create_verification_agent, and short-circuit HallucinationDetector._check_fact_grounding to return detected=False when `not evidence` (it currently cannot distinguish 'contradicted' from 'nothing to check against').
- **Verifier:** Independently confirmed. Settings has 185 fields, none matching prometheus/loki/elasticsearch, and model_config sets extra="ignore" (app/config.py:23) so no env var can create them — getattr always yields None. _collect_git_evidence (evidence_collector.py:394-406) is a hard stub: `if not self.github_token or not service_id: return evidence` followed immediately by `return evidence`, so it returns [] even when a token IS configured. create_verification_agent has exactly one call site (api_router.py:37) with k8s_client/knowledge_graph/redis_client hardcoded None; no lifespan hook reconfigures it. Live: {'text':'The database is down'} -> insufficient_data/0.2; {'text':'Error rate rose to 42 percent','cited_values':{'error_rate':0.42}} -> refuted, hallucination_detected=true, type=fabricated_fact; {'text':'CPU is high','cited_sources':['prometheus']} -> refuted, type=wrong_attribution. verification_agent.py:186 requires len(supporting)>0 and supporting derives from evidence, so VERIFIED is structurally unreachable. Actively worse than a no-op: it emits false 'refuted' verdicts on true statements.

### `BROKEN` — Pre-action safety check (POST /api/verification/actions/check)

- **Surface:** POST /api/verification/actions/check
- **Severity:** critical · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** Two compounding defects. (1) app/verification/consistency_checker.py:93-100 dispatches on an EXACT action_type string in {scale, restart, rollback, config_update, delete}; anything else falls to `_check_generic_action`. (2) `_capture_state` at consistency_checker.py:357-358 does `if not self.k8s: return {"replicas": 2, "available_replicas": 2, "updating": False}` — a fabricated state — and the router hardcodes k8s_client=None (api_router.py:41). So the generic checker sees a valid state, sets preconditions_met=True, `_calculate_safety_score` returns 1.0, and is_safe=True. Executed live against the running app: action_type='drop_database' -> {'is_safe': True, 'safety_score': 1.0, 'is_reversible': True, 'failed_preconditions': [], 'identified_risks': []}; action_type='terraform_destroy' -> identical; action_type='delete_deployment' target='prod-db' -> is_safe=True score=1.0. Only the literal strings work: action_type='delete' -> is_safe=False score=0.5 failed=['Delete actions require explicit confirmation']; action_type='scale' params={'replicas':0} -> is_safe=False. Also note 'scale' to 50 replicas from the fake baseline of 2 -> is_safe=True score=1.0 despite the risk being listed, because `_calculate_safety_score` subtracts only 0.1 per risk and then adds back 0.1 for is_reversible and 0.05 for a rollback_plan.
- **Blocker:** Unknown/compound action types silently bypass every destructive-action rule, and with no k8s client the checker validates against invented cluster state rather than failing closed.
- **Fix:** Make the dispatch fail closed: any action_type not in the checkers map should append a failed_precondition ('unrecognized action type — manual review required') instead of routing to the generic checker; and make `_capture_state` return None (not a fake dict) when self.k8s is None so preconditions_met becomes False. Additionally match on a normalized verb prefix (delete_*, destroy_*, drop_*) rather than exact equality.
- **Verifier:** Independently confirmed and reproduced. consistency_checker.py:93-100 dispatches on an exact-string dict of 5 keys; everything else hits _check_generic_action. _capture_state (consistency_checker.py:357-358) returns the fabricated {'replicas':2,'available_replicas':2,'updating':False} when self.k8s is None, which the router guarantees (api_router.py:41). Live results I ran: drop_database -> is_safe True, score 1.0, risks []; terraform_destroy -> is_safe True, score 1.0; delete_deployment on 'prod-db' -> is_safe True, score 1.0. Only literal strings gate: delete -> is_safe False score 0.5; scale replicas=0 -> is_safe False score 0.75. And scale to 50 replicas from the invented baseline of 2 -> is_safe True, score 1.0 while simultaneously reporting the risk 'Large scale-up from 2 to 50 may cause resource issues'. Fails open on unknown action types and validates against invented cluster state.

### `BROKEN` — Record verification outcome (POST /api/verification/outcomes)

- **Surface:** POST /api/verification/outcomes
- **Severity:** high · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** Cache-key mismatch makes the write a guaranteed no-op. verification_agent.py:156 stores results under `cache_key = f"{claim.id}:{claim.text[:50]}"`, but verification_agent.py:425 looks up `self._verification_cache.get(claim_id)` with the bare id, then only calls `self.calibration.record_outcome(...)` inside `if cached:`. Executed the full live flow: POST /claims/verify -> claim_id 'ebdb6665'; POST /outcomes {'claim_id':'ebdb6665','was_correct':true} -> 200 {"status":"recorded"}; GET /calibration -> {"brier_score":null,"ece":null} and sum of bin samples == 0. The endpoint reports success while recording nothing. This is masked by test theater: tests/test_verification_agent.py:592-598 seeds the cache with the composite key `'claim-1:Test claim text that is lon'` and then passes that exact composite string as the claim_id — a value no API caller can ever produce. 88 tests pass (`python3 -m pytest tests/test_verification_agent.py tests/test_safety_gates.py tests/test_policy.py tests/test_guardrails.py -q -p no:cacheprovider` -> 88 passed) without catching this.
- **Blocker:** Lookup key != storage key, so `cached` is always None and record_outcome silently returns. The endpoint's 200 is a lie.
- **Fix:** Index `_verification_cache` by `claim.id` alone (or keep a secondary id->result map) and return 404 when the claim_id is unknown instead of a false 'recorded'. Rewrite the test to go through verify_claim() and then record_verification_outcome(result.claim_id, ...).
- **Verifier:** Independently confirmed. Storage key is `f"{claim.id}:{claim.text[:50]}"` (verification_agent.py:156, written at :222); lookup is `self._verification_cache.get(claim_id)` on the bare id (verification_agent.py:425), and record_outcome only fires inside `if cached:`. I ran the full live flow myself: POST /claims/verify -> claim_id 'c0f79419'; POST /outcomes {'claim_id':'c0f79419','was_correct':true} -> 200 {'status':'recorded','claim_id':'c0f79419','was_correct':True}; GET /calibration -> brier None, ece None, sum of all bin samples == 0. The 200 is a lie. I also read tests/test_verification_agent.py:585-605 and confirmed the test seeds AND queries the composite key 'claim-1:Test claim text that is lon' — a value the API can never generate since it mints claim.id as uuid4()[:8]. Pure test theater.

### `BROKEN` — Auth / tenant isolation on the verification and event-intelligence APIs

- **Surface:** All 21 routes under /api/verification/* and /api/event-intelligence/*
- **Severity:** high · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** Neither router declares any auth dependency. app/verification/api_router.py:26 `APIRouter(prefix='/verification', tags=['verification'])` and app/event_intelligence/api_router.py:27 likewise — no `dependencies=[Depends(...)]`, and no per-route auth. Probed with an unauthenticated TestClient: GET /api/verification/evidence-sources -> 200, GET /api/event-intelligence/stats -> 200, POST /api/verification/actions/check -> 200, DELETE /api/verification/cache -> reachable; by contrast GET /api/teams -> 401. There is also no organization_id/tenant parameter anywhere in these routers, so the module-global FeatureStore, NoiseReductionEngine and VerificationAgent singletons pool every tenant's alerts, deployments, maintenance windows and dedup fingerprints into one shared namespace — one org's service name collides with another's.
- **Blocker:** Unauthenticated write endpoints (DELETE /cache, POST /deployments/start, POST /maintenance/add) on a shared cross-tenant in-memory store.
- **Fix:** Add the same auth dependency the teams router uses at the APIRouter level for both routers, and key the FeatureStore/NoiseReductionEngine state by organization_id instead of using process-global singletons.
- **Verifier:** Confirmed independently. Neither router declares auth: `grep -n dependencies` on both api_router.py files returns nothing, and no per-route Depends on any auth function. I also checked for a global catch — `grep -n 'add_middleware|@app.middleware' app/api/main.py` shows only LimitsMiddleware, GZipMiddleware and CORS; there is no auth middleware that could cover them in production. Live unauthenticated probes: GET /api/verification/evidence-sources 200, GET /api/event-intelligence/stats 200, DELETE /api/verification/cache 200, POST /api/event-intelligence/deployments/start 200, versus GET /api/teams 401. Registration confirmed at app/api/main.py:738 and :760, both with prefix='/api' and no dependencies argument. No organization_id/tenant parameter exists in either router, so the module-global FeatureStore/NoiseReductionEngine/VerificationAgent singletons pool all tenants into one namespace — and since the noise fingerprint is only service:metric:source, two tenants with a service named 'checkout' will dedup-suppress each other's alerts.

### `STUB` — Sandbox verification gate

- **Surface:** app/safety_gates/sandbox_gate.py (SandboxGate, gate 3 of the orchestrator)
- **Severity:** critical · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** The default executor is pure theater and its 'pass' result is what unlocks AUTO_APPROVED autonomous remediation. sandbox_gate.py:317 -> `self.executor = executor or LocalSandboxExecutor()`. LocalSandboxExecutor (lines 101-190): create_sandbox() does `tempfile.mkdtemp()` and stores a dict; apply_change() does `await asyncio.sleep(0.1)  # Simulate work` then `return {'success': True}`; run_tests() does `await asyncio.sleep(0.2)` then `return SandboxTestResult(passed=5, failed=0, total=5, failures=[])` with the literal comment '# Default: all tests pass'; detect_side_effects() returns []; verify_rollback() sleeps 0.1s and returns {'success': True}. The KubernetesSandboxExecutor is no better — line 227 carries the comment '# In production: use kubernetes client to create namespace / For now, simulate', run_tests() returns a hardcoded 10/10 pass, apply_change() is `asyncio.sleep(0.5)`. Nothing is ever actually executed, deployed, or tested. Because orchestrator.py:104-121 only runs the sandbox when confidence and policy pass, and this executor can only return pass, the sandbox gate is a guaranteed rubber stamp that produces a '10/10 tests passed in sandbox' claim to a human approver.
- **Blocker:** Both sandbox executors return canned success after a sleep; no isolation, no deployment, no tests are ever run.
- **Fix:** Do not ship this as a gate. Until a real executor exists, make SandboxGate default to enabled=False and have the orchestrator treat a missing sandbox as 'not verified' (escalate) rather than 'passed'. A real LocalSandboxExecutor would at minimum shell out to the runbook's dry-run command and parse its exit code.

### `STUB` — Calibration statistics (GET /api/verification/calibration)

- **Surface:** GET /api/verification/calibration
- **Severity:** high · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** The statistics themselves are genuine: app/verification/confidence_calibrator.py implements real binned Brier score (calculate_brier_score, weighted sum of (mean_confidence - accuracy)^2), real ECE (calculate_ece, sample-weighted |accuracy - mean_confidence|), plus working PlattScaling gradient-descent and IsotonicCalibration pool-adjacent-violators implementations. But there is never any data to compute over. State lives in `self._bins` on a module-global `_agent` (api_router.py:27), redis_client is hardcoded None (api_router.py:44) so `_save_calibration_data`/`_load_calibration_data` short-circuit, and the only writer (record_outcome) is unreachable per the bug above. GET /api/verification/calibration returns {"brier_score":null,"ece":null,"reliability_diagram":{"bins":[{"confidence":0.05,"accuracy":0.05,"samples":0}, ...]}} — every bin has samples=0 and CalibrationBin.accuracy falls back to the bin midpoint, so the reliability diagram renders as a perfect 45-degree line regardless of reality. PlattScaling and IsotonicCalibration have no call sites anywhere (grep for 'PlattScaling|IsotonicCalibration' outside the module: no hits). No SQLAlchemy model or migration exists (grep __tablename__ in app/database/*.py: organizations, api_keys, members, investigations, investigation_events, audit_logs, alert_rules, alert_events, runbook_executions, usage_records, teams, team_members, team_invitations — nothing calibration-related).
- **Blocker:** Correct statistics over a permanently empty in-memory bin set; also dies on restart and is not shared across workers. The zero-sample fallback fabricates a perfect calibration curve.
- **Fix:** Persist bins to Redis (pass copilot_redis_url through create_verification_agent) or a real table, fix the record_outcome key bug, and return `accuracy: null` for zero-sample bins instead of the bin midpoint so the diagram does not lie.
- **Verifier:** Confirmed by reading to the return statements. calculate_brier_score (confidence_calibrator.py:222-242) is a genuine sample-weighted sum of (mean_confidence-accuracy)^2 that `continue`s past every bin with predictions==0, so with zero data it returns None. CalibrationBin.accuracy (line 29-33) falls back to the bin midpoint (lower+upper)/2 when predictions==0, and mean_confidence is the same midpoint — so the reliability diagram renders a perfect 45-degree line (accuracy==confidence in every bin) with samples=0 everywhere. I reproduced that exact output live. `grep -rn 'PlattScaling|IsotonicCalibration' app/ tests/` excluding the module itself returns nothing (exit 1) — both classes are dead. redis_client is hardcoded None so the persist/load paths never run, and the only writer (record_outcome) is unreachable per the bug above. `grep -rn __tablename__ app/database/*.py` lists 14 tables, none calibration-related. Correct math over a permanently empty bin set.

### `STUB` — Post-action validation (POST /api/verification/actions/{id}/validate)

- **Surface:** POST /api/verification/actions/{action_id}/validate
- **Severity:** medium · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** app/verification/api_router.py:177-186 — the handler takes an `agent` dependency and ignores it entirely, returning a literal `{"action_id": action_id, "validation": "pending", "message": "Post-action validation requires stored action check state"}`. The real implementation exists (DataConsistencyChecker.post_action_validate at consistency_checker.py:110-131, which polls `_capture_state` against `expected_post_state` until a deadline) and VerificationAgent.validate_action_result wraps it, but neither is reachable from HTTP because ActionSafetyCheck objects from /actions/check are never persisted anywhere.
- **Blocker:** Handler body is a canned dict; the ActionSafetyCheck produced by /actions/check is discarded, so there is no state to validate against.
- **Fix:** Store ActionSafetyCheck by action_id (the same store used for /actions/check) and have this handler call agent.validate_action_result(check, timeout_seconds). Note the underlying implementation would also be a no-op today since `_capture_state` returns fake data without a k8s client.
- **Verifier:** Confirmed by reading the handler and executing it. app/verification/api_router.py:177-186 takes both `timeout_seconds` and an `agent: VerificationAgent = Depends(get_agent)` and uses neither — the entire body is a literal dict return. Live: POST /api/verification/actions/abc123/validate -> {'action_id':'abc123','validation':'pending','message':'Post-action validation requires stored action check state'}. The real implementation does exist (DataConsistencyChecker.post_action_validate, consistency_checker.py:112-131, polls _capture_state against expected_post_state until a deadline) but is unreachable over HTTP because /actions/check discards the ActionSafetyCheck it builds — and even if reachable it would compare against the same fabricated {'replicas':2} state.

### `STUB` — Feature store (/api/event-intelligence/features/*, deployments/*, maintenance/*)

- **Surface:** POST /features/record-metric, POST /features/record-alert, GET /features/{service}/{metric}, POST /deployments/start|end, GET /deployments/{service}, POST /maintenance/add, GET /maintenance/{service}
- **Severity:** medium · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** The computation is real (Python `statistics` mean/stdev over recorded points, app/event_intelligence/feature_store.py:1-140 plus rolling-window cleanup), and it round-trips correctly: POSTed 5 metric values [1.0,1.2,0.9,5.0,1.1] to /features/record-metric then GET /features/checkout/error_rate -> {'hourly_avg':1.84,'hourly_stddev':1.7700282483621554,'daily_avg':1.84,...,'vector':[1.84,1.77...]} — genuine arithmetic, correct 16-element ML vector. Deployment and maintenance flags also round-trip (POST /deployments/start -> GET /deployments/checkout -> {'in_progress':true}; a maintenance window spanning now -> {'in_maintenance':true}) and DO feed the noise engine (api_router.py:118-119 passes deployment_in_progress / maintenance_window_active into NoiseSignal). But there is NO persistence: FeatureStore.__init__ (feature_store.py:93-107) holds `_metric_history`, `_alert_history`, `_service_alerts`, `_deployments`, `_maintenance_windows` as plain defaultdicts on a module-global singleton (api_router.py:47-51 constructs FeatureStore() with redis_client=None). The Redis branches in record_metric are all guarded by `if self.redis:` and never execute. No SQLAlchemy model and no migration exist for any of this (grep __tablename__ across app/database/ shows only org/investigation/alert/runbook/usage/team tables). Everything is lost on restart and is invisible to sibling gunicorn workers. Also: no auth — GET /api/event-intelligence/stats returns 200 with no credentials while /api/teams returns 401.
- **Blocker:** 'Persistence' is a module-level dict on a process-global singleton; nothing survives a restart and nothing is shared across workers, so the rolling statistics are meaningless in any multi-worker deployment.
- **Fix:** Pass the existing Redis client (settings.copilot_redis_url) into FeatureStore() in get_feature_store(), which activates the already-written zadd/expire code paths, or add a metric_samples/alert_events table. Also add the auth dependency used by the teams router.
- **Verifier:** Confirmed. The arithmetic is genuine — I POSTed [1.0,1.2,0.9,5.0,1.1] and GET /features/checkout/error_rate returned hourly_avg 1.84, hourly_stddev 1.7700282483621554 (correct sample stdev) and a well-formed 16-element vector. Deployment/maintenance flags round-trip and DO reach the noise engine (api_router.py:118-119 sets deployment_in_progress/maintenance_window_active on NoiseSignal). But every Redis path is behind `if self.redis:` (feature_store.py:131, 174) and get_feature_store() constructs FeatureStore() with no redis (api_router.py:47-51), so _metric_history/_alert_history/_service_alerts/_deployments/_maintenance_windows are plain defaultdicts on a module-global singleton. `grep -rn __tablename__ app/database/*.py` -> 14 tables, none for features/deployments/maintenance; no migration either. Also confirmed the computed statistics are NEVER handed to the noise engine — /noise/check reads only is_deployment_in_progress and is_in_maintenance, never get_features. 'Persistence' is a process-local dict.

Not in app/agents/__init__.py. 0 routes. Schema drift confirmed at confidence_gate.py:158 ('confidence_score') and :167 ('specialist_results'); hardcoded historical success dict confirmed at :244-252 under the comment 'In production, this would query past remediations. For now, use reasonable defaults.'

### `DEAD_CODE` — Policy engine (OPA / Kyverno / change windows / entry criteria)

- **Surface:** app/policy_engine/*, exposed only via app/api/v1/policy.py which is never mounted
- **Severity:** high · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** `python3 -c "from app.api.main import app; print(len([r for r in app.routes if r.path.startswith('/v1')]))"` -> 0. app/api/v1/__init__.py builds a /v1 APIRouter including policy.router, but `grep -rn 'api.v1' app/` outside app/api/v1/ returns nothing — main.py never includes it. The other importer, app/coordinator/decision_integration.py:39, is only exported from app/coordinator/__init__.py; the live coordinator router (app/coordinator/api_router.py:53) instantiates a plain `DAGExecutor`, not DecisionAwareDAGExecutor. Separately, the engine FAILS OPEN despite claiming otherwise: engine.py:187 sets `self.deny_on_error = True  # Fail closed`, but with no backends injected every `_evaluate_*` returns None early (`if not self.opa: return None` at :406, `if not self.kyverno: return None` at :423, `if not self.windows: return None` at :443). Executed: `PolicyEngine().evaluate('delete', {'type':'database','environment':'production','is_reversible':False,'risk_score':0.99}, {'is_change_freeze':True})` -> {'decision':'allow','violations':[],'requires_approval':False,'opa_allowed':None,'kyverno_allowed':None,'window_allowed':None,'criteria_passed':None}. A production database delete during a declared change freeze at risk 0.99 is ALLOWED. app/api/v1/policy.py:51 uses `create_policy_engine()` — if that factory also defaults the clients to None, mounting the router would ship this fail-open behavior. The embedded (no-OPA-server) rego-equivalent logic in opa_client.py:381-500 is real and reasonable, but PolicyEngine never uses OPAClient unless one is explicitly injected.
- **Blocker:** Not mounted anywhere (0 live /v1 routes), and if it were, a PolicyEngine with no configured backends returns 'allow' for everything while advertising deny_on_error=True.
- **Fix:** If keeping it: `app.include_router(v1_router)` in main.py, default create_policy_engine() to inject OPAClient() (which has the embedded evaluator), and make evaluate() return DENY/REQUIRE_APPROVAL when zero backends produced a result instead of falling through to ALLOW.

The other importer (app/coordinator/decision_integration.py:39) is unreachable because app/coordinator/api_router.py:53 instantiates a plain DAGExecutor (grep for DecisionAware in that file: no hits). Fail-open reproduced twice: PolicyEngine().evaluate('delete', {'type':'database','environment':'production','is_reversible':False,'risk_score':0.99}, {'is_change_freeze':True}) -> {'decision':'allow','violations':[],'requires_approval':False,'opa_allowed':None,...}. I additionally checked what the auditor only speculated about: create_policy_engine() has signature (opa_client=None, kyverno_client=None, change_window_manager=None, criteria_evaluator=None, redis_client=None) and I confirmed e2.opa/kyverno/windows/criteria are all None and it returns the SAME 'allow'. So mounting app/api/v1/policy.py:51 would in fact ship the fail-open, despite engine.py:187 `self.deny_on_error = True  # Fail closed`.
