### `UNVERIFIABLE` — Auth callback

- **Surface:** frontend/src/pages/AuthCallback.tsx (App.tsx:37-40 routes path /auth/callback)
- **Severity:** medium · **Confidence:** medium
- **Evidence:** AuthCallback.tsx makes no backend call at all — it only does `initSupabase()` then `supabase.auth.getSession()` (lines 28-36). If Supabase is unconfigured it bails immediately. App.tsx:37-40 does catch `window.location.pathname === '/auth/callback'`, and the backend has both `GET /auth/callback` and `GET /auth/{provider}/callback`, but since the SPA is mounted at `/` via StaticFiles (app/api/main.py:3732) a browser hitting /auth/callback will be served by the *backend* route, not the SPA, when the two are same-origin — the two callback handlers collide on the same path. I could not verify the runtime outcome without a real Supabase project or OAuth provider.
- **Blocker:** Requires a live Supabase project or a configured OAuth provider to exercise. Potential path collision between the backend GET /auth/callback route and the SPA route of the same name in same-origin deployments.
- **Fix:** Verify against a real Supabase project; if the backend OAuth flow is the intended one, move the SPA callback to a distinct path (e.g. /app/auth/callback) to avoid shadowing.

**Verifier notes for this domain.** METHOD: I re-dumped all 292 routes from `app.api.main`, re-probed ~45 endpoints with TestClient(raise_server_exceptions=False), read every page/component named in the claims to its return statement, and independently reproduced the two root-cause tracebacks. I did NOT rely on any quoted output from the auditor.

VERDICT TALLY: 20 of 24 claims CONFIRMED as stated. 4 overturned (Team BROKEN→STUB, Login PARTIAL→BROKEN, AuthCallback UNVERIFIABLE→BROKEN, and the deep-link claim's supporting evidence is wrong even though its verdict stands).

THINGS THE AUDITOR GOT WRONG:
1. AuthCallback "path collision" is REFUTED. app/api/main.py:776-789 `supabase_auth_callback` deliberately returns `FileResponse(frontend/dist/index.html)`. My probe: `GET /auth/callback -> 200 text/html <!doctype html>`. There is no collision — it is intentional design. The auditor called this UNVERIFIABLE on a theory that is provably false.
2. "The backend's SPA mount (StaticFiles html=True) would happily serve index.html for such paths, so the server side is ready" is FALSE. My probe: `GET /dashboard -> 404 application/json {"detail":"Not Found"}`. StaticFiles with html=True only serves index.html for directory paths, not arbitrary SPA routes. Deep links are worse than claimed: broken on BOTH the client (no router) and the server (404, not index.html).
3. `POST /api/chat` returns 405 (not 404) — the StaticFiles mount matches the path and rejects the method. Immaterial to the verdict.
4. `/metrics` is not "real per-service time series". app/metrics_source.py:20-58 reads a static `metrics.json` fixture off disk under `runtime.logs_path()`. It is a demo file, not telemetry.

THINGS THE AUDITOR MISSED ENTIRELY (material):
A. /oncall/* is DOUBLY dead, not singly. Even if the `@asynccontextmanager` bug at app/database/engine.py:116 were fixed, the default DB URL is `sqlite+aiosqlite:///data/copilot.db` (app/database/engine.py:52-54) and `/Users/ankitpandey/OmniPotent/OmniPotent/data` DOES NOT EXIST. I ran the fixed form directly: `async with get_session()` -> `sqlalchemy.exc.OperationalError: (sqlite3.OperationalError) unable to open database file`. Furthermore `Base.metadata.create_all` (app/database/engine.py:150) is NEVER called from app/api/main.py — grep for init_database in main.py returns nothing, so no tables are ever created at startup. Fixing the decorator alone would turn 500-TypeError into 500-OperationalError.
B. API_BASE is INCONSISTENT across the frontend and the shipped build proves it. `frontend/src/api.ts:5`, `api/billing.ts:22`, `hooks/useWebSocket.ts:14`, `pages/ApiDocs.tsx:21`, `landing/Navbar.tsx:14`, `landing/Documentation.tsx:190` all use `?? "http://localhost:8000"`, while ALL 15 page components use `|| ""` (same-origin). `grep -c localhost:8000 frontend/dist/assets/index-C0XddFiH.js` = 6 — the committed production bundle hardcodes `http://localhost:8000`. So in the same-origin FastAPI-serves-dist deployment, the Console, WebSocket, and the entire Billing page point at localhost:8000 while every other page points at the current origin. That makes the CORS/credentials finding bite even in "production", and on an HTTPS host these become mixed-content blocked outright.
C. SLOTracker fabricates MORE than the auditor said. `budget_total: 43.2` is a hardcoded literal at SLOTracker.tsx (the `total: 43.2` in the default object AND `total: 43.2` in the mapped object), independent of the SLO's window or target, and `budget.remaining` is rescaled by `budgetData.remaining * 43.2 / (1 - target)`. The initial default is `{remaining: 43.2, total: 43.2, burn_rate: 0.5, status: "healthy"}` — if the budget fetch fails, the UI shows a healthy 43.2h budget with a 0.5 burn rate that came from nowhere.
D. ServiceMap edges are 100% synthesized, not merely "not fetched". ServiceMap.tsx:568-604 picks a node whose name contains gateway/ingress/frontend (else nodes[0]) and star-connects it to everything, then cross-joins every service to every db/cache node. The `/api/knowledge-graph/dependencies/{service_id}` route that holds real edge data is live and never called.
E. RunbookBuilder can only ever edit `runbooks[0]`. RunbookBuilder.tsx:462-467 fetches the list and unconditionally loads `runbooks[0].id`. There is no runbook picker at all, so even a working PUT would only ever save the first runbook.
F. Postmortem tags all hardcoded template strings with `aiGenerated: true` (Postmortem.tsx:363, 370), so the UI actively labels canned text as AI output.
G. Confirmed the auditor's negative claim: `grep -rn dependency_overrides app/` returns nothing, and no test in tests/ issues an HTTP request to any `/oncall` path (grep for quoted "/oncall" in tests/ is empty) — so the 500s are not a test-harness artifact and are wholly uncovered.


## Frontend build health: install, typecheck, lint, unit tests, e2e

**Summary.** The frontend toolchain itself is genuinely healthy: `npm ci` installs cleanly (553 packages, exit 0), `tsc -b --force` reports zero type errors, `eslint .` reports zero errors (8 warnings), and `vite build` produces a real bundle in ~5.6s. The app actually renders — I drove Chromium against a live `npm run dev` on :5173 and the landing page mounted with real content. The single biggest problem is that the test layer is theater: there is exactly ONE unit test file (src/hooks/useConfig.test.ts, 2 pure-function assertions) covering 0.46% of statements across ~90 components/pages, and because vitest.config.ts hard-enables coverage with 70% thresholds, `npm test` exits 1 on every run — CI hides this with `continue-on-error: true`. The Playwright suite in frontend/e2e is orphaned (referenced by no workflow), its webServer config points at port 3000 and runs a non-existent `npm run dev` script from the e2e dir, and 9 of its 13 tests fail on strict-mode locator violations even when pointed at a working server. A paying user can be served the built app (the backend mounts frontend/dist), but a developer doing a plain `npm run dev` with no .env gets a half-broken app: 19 of 24 pages default API_BASE to "" and there is no Vite proxy, so their fetches hit the Vite dev server and get index.html back instead of JSON.

**Wiring.** The built SPA is live: app/api/main.py:3730-3733 mounts frontend/dist via StaticFiles(html=True) at "/", and the server logged "serving built frontend from /Users/ankitpandey/OmniPotent/OmniPotent/frontend/dist" on import. Runtime config is served by GET /frontend-config (app/api/main.py:1188, open path per main.py:230) which returned 200 {"supabase_url":"","supabase_anon_key":"","api_url":""}. Two API-base conventions coexist in src: src/api.ts:5, src/api/billing.ts:22, src/hooks/useWebSocket.ts:14 use `?? "http://localhost:8000"`, while 19 page modules (src/pages/Dashboard.tsx:4, Incidents.tsx:24, OnCall.tsx:19, KnowledgeBase.tsx:31, ...) use `|| ""`. Routing is state-based in src/App.tsx (no react-router), so every page module is imported and reachable from the single bundle. CI wiring: .github/workflows/ci.yml:114-171 runs the frontend lint/typecheck/test/build job; .github/workflows/e2e.yml:56-67 runs only tests/e2e/specs/api.spec.ts. NOTHING in the repo references frontend/e2e — `grep -rln "frontend/e2e"` returned no matches — so that entire Playwright suite has no entrypoint.


### `BROKEN` — Unit tests (vitest)

- **Surface:** `npm test` (vitest run), vitest.config.ts, src/hooks/useConfig.test.ts
- **Severity:** high · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** `npm test` output: "✓ src/hooks/useConfig.test.ts (2 tests) 4ms — Test Files 1 passed (1), Tests 2 passed (2)" but the process EXITS 1 (`npm test >/dev/null 2>&1; echo $?` → 1) because vitest.config.ts:20 sets coverage.enabled=true with thresholds lines/functions/statements 70, branches 60, and the run ends with: "ERROR: Coverage for lines (0.46%) does not meet global threshold (70%)" / functions 5.79% / statements 0.46% / branches 23.3%. `find src e2e -name '*.test.*' -o -name '*.spec.*'` returns exactly ONE unit test file: src/hooks/useConfig.test.ts, which only asserts two pure string formatters (providerLabel/modelShort). Coverage table shows 0% for every one of the 19 pages, all ~60 components, src/api.ts (15.7%), src/api/billing.ts, src/utils/exportReport.ts, and 8 of 9 hooks — including useCopilot.ts (388 lines) and useWebSocket.ts (369 lines). A full jsdom harness exists (src/test/setup.ts mocks matchMedia/ResizeObserver/IntersectionObserver, @testing-library/react installed) and is completely unused.
- **Blocker:** `npm test` always exits 1 because coverage is hard-enabled with 70% thresholds against a suite that covers 0.46% of the code. There is effectively no unit-test safety net: 2 assertions on 2 pure functions guard a ~30k-line React app.
- **Fix:** Two-part: (1) move coverage thresholds behind `npm run test:coverage` (set coverage.enabled=false in vitest.config.ts, since the `test:coverage` script already passes --coverage) so `npm test` reflects test results, and (2) drop thresholds to the honest current number and ratchet up, while adding render smoke tests for the load-bearing surfaces (src/hooks/useCopilot.ts, src/api.ts fetch wrappers, src/pages/Dashboard.tsx, src/pages/Billing.tsx).
- **Verifier:** Tried to prove it works and could not. `npm test >/dev/null 2>&1; echo $?` -> 1, deterministically, because vitest.config.ts hard-sets coverage.enabled=true with thresholds 70/70/60/70 and the run ends in four ERROR lines (lines 0.46%, functions 5.79%, statements 0.46%, branches 23.3%). `find src e2e -name '*.test.*' -o -name '*.spec.*'` returns exactly one unit test: src/hooks/useConfig.test.ts. Coverage table shows 0% for all 19 pages and for useCopilot.ts (388 lines), useWebSocket.ts (369), supabase.ts, exportReport.ts. The 2 tests pass but the command is a failing gate.

### `BROKEN` — Playwright e2e suite (frontend/e2e)

- **Surface:** frontend/e2e/playwright.config.ts + e2e/tests/{landing,console,responsive,api-health}.spec.ts
- **Severity:** high · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** I installed it for real (`npm install` in frontend/e2e + `npx playwright install chromium`, 94.7 MiB downloaded) and ran it. Default config run: `npx playwright test --project=chromium` → "[WebServer] npm error Missing script: \"dev\"" then "Error: Process from config.webServer was not able to start. Exit code: 1" — frontend/e2e/playwright.config.ts:32 runs `npm run dev` with cwd = e2e/, but e2e/package.json has no dev script; and its baseURL/webServer url is http://localhost:3000 while Vite serves 5173 (vite.config.js). With a hand-written override config pointing at a live `npm run dev` on :5173, the result was **9 failed / 4 passed (22.0s)**. Failures are locator bugs, not app bugs: landing.spec.ts:8 `locator('text=OmniPotent')` → "strict mode violation: resolved to 8 elements"; landing.spec.ts:16 and console.spec.ts:10 `getByRole('button',{name:/launch|start|get started|try/i})` → "resolved to 11 elements". The 3 api-health.spec.ts failures were only because no backend was up — after I started `python3 -m uvicorn app.api.main:app --port 8000` (healthz=200) they passed: "3 passed (807ms)". Critically, `grep -rln 'frontend/e2e'` matches NOTHING in the repo: .github/workflows/e2e.yml:56-67 uses working-directory tests/e2e and runs only specs/api.spec.ts (with continue-on-error: true). So this suite has never run in CI.
- **Blocker:** Cannot start: webServer command `npm run dev` does not exist in e2e/package.json and the port is 3000 vs Vite's 5173. Once started, 9/13 tests fail on Playwright strict-mode locator violations. And the suite is wired into no workflow, so nobody would notice.
- **Fix:** In frontend/e2e/playwright.config.ts set `webServer.command: 'npm run dev --prefix ..'` and baseURL/url to http://localhost:5173 (or add `server.port: 3000` to vite.config.js). Then make locators unique — use `.first()` or add data-testid to the nav Launch Console button and the hero heading. Finally add a CI job that runs this suite, or delete it in favour of tests/e2e.
- **Verifier:** Reproduced both halves. Default config: `npx playwright test --project=chromium` -> '[WebServer] npm error Missing script: "dev"' then 'Process from config.webServer was not able to start' — e2e/playwright.config.ts:32-37 runs `npm run dev` from e2e/ whose package.json has no dev script, and targets :3000 while vite.config.js serves :5173. With my own temp override config against a live dev server AND a live backend on :8011, the result was 6 failed / 7 passed — the auditor's 9 failed/4 passed minus the 3 api-health tests that only failed for lack of a backend, so our numbers agree. Failures are Playwright strict-mode violations (getByRole button /launch|start|get started|try/i resolved to 11 elements). `grep -rln 'frontend/e2e'` outside node_modules returns nothing; e2e.yml uses working-directory tests/e2e.

### `BROKEN` — Runtime API base URL resolution (dev)

- **Surface:** src/api.ts:5, src/api/billing.ts:22, src/hooks/useWebSocket.ts:14 vs 19 modules in src/pages/*.tsx; vite.config.js
- **Severity:** high · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** There is no frontend/.env (`ls .env .env.local` → No such file), only .env.example. Two conflicting defaults exist: src/api.ts:5 `import.meta.env.VITE_API_URL ?? "http://localhost:8000"` vs src/pages/Dashboard.tsx:4, Incidents.tsx:24, OnCall.tsx:19, Analytics.tsx:8, Login.tsx:31, ServiceMap.tsx:21, KnowledgeBase.tsx:31 and 12 more using `import.meta.env.VITE_API_URL || ""` (relative). vite.config.js defines NO server.proxy. I started the real dev server (`npm run dev`, VITE ready on :5173) and curled a relative path: `curl -i http://localhost:5173/api/investigations` → "HTTP/1.1 200 OK, Content-Type: text/html" (Vite's SPA index.html), not JSON. So on a clean `npm run dev`, every page using the `|| ""` default fetches HTML and its res.json() throws. In production this is masked because app/api/main.py:3732 serves dist/ from the same origin as the API — I confirmed the backend logs "serving built frontend from .../frontend/dist".
- **Blocker:** With no .env and no Vite dev proxy, 19 of 24 page modules resolve API_BASE to "" and hit the Vite dev server, which returns index.html for /api/* — the pages fail to parse JSON. Only the src/api.ts / billing.ts / useWebSocket.ts paths (which default to http://localhost:8000) reach the backend, and those then need CORS.
- **Fix:** Add `server: { proxy: { '/api': 'http://localhost:8000', '/frontend-config': 'http://localhost:8000' } }` to vite.config.js (and delete the stale compiled vite.config.js/.d.ts so vite.config.ts is authoritative), or standardise every module on the `?? 'http://localhost:8000'` default and ship a checked-in .env.development.
- **Verifier:** Reproduced live. No frontend/.env or .env.local (only .env.example). vite.config.js has no server.proxy. I started `npm run dev` (VITE 5.4.21 ready on :5173) and curled five paths: /api/investigations, /api/incidents, /oncall/alerts, /frontend-config, /healthz — every one returned code=200 ctype=text/html with Vite's index.html body, so res.json() throws. Two corrections to the auditor: the `|| ""` default appears in 13 modules (12 pages + OnboardingWizard.tsx), not 19 of 24; and they missed frontend/src/pages/History.tsx:72 which hardcodes fetch('/api/investigations?limit=50') with no base at all, so it is broken in dev even when VITE_API_URL is set.

- **Severity:** medium · **Confidence:** high

Its baseURL is http://localhost:8000 and its webServer boots uvicorn directly (tests/e2e/playwright.config.ts:44). .github/workflows/e2e.yml:64-70 runs ONLY `npx playwright test specs/api.spec.ts --project=chromium` and sets `continue-on-error: true` — so auth.spec.ts and investigation.spec.ts (the actual user-flow specs, with page objects) never execute in CI, and even the api spec's failures cannot fail the build. I did not install this suite; the frontend/e2e api-health equivalents passed against the same live backend.

But auth.spec.ts + investigation.spec.ts gave 16 failed / 3 passed: they assert toHaveTitle(/DevOps Copilot/) (actual title 'OmniPotent'), toHaveURL(/login|auth/) after goto('/dashboard') (which 404s), and target [data-testid=...] selectors when grep shows ZERO data-testid attributes anywhere in frontend/src. So they are not merely unwired — they are unrunnable dead specs. Confirmed e2e.yml:64-70 runs only api.spec.ts with continue-on-error: true.

But ci.yml:139-140 wraps the vitest step in `continue-on-error: true`, and the follow-up "Check coverage thresholds" step (ci.yml:142-150) only emits `::warning::Frontend coverage is below 70%` — it never exits non-zero. Codecov upload is also continue-on-error. So the frontend job is green today even though `npm test` exits 1 locally with 0.46% line coverage. Lint/typecheck/build steps are real gates and currently pass (exit 0 each, verified).

The auditor UNDERSTATED the follow-up: the 'Check coverage thresholds' step gates on `[ -f coverage/coverage-summary.json ]`, but vitest's reporter list is ['text','json','html','lcov'] which emits coverage-final.json, not coverage-summary.json. I confirmed after a real run that frontend/coverage/coverage-summary.json does not exist — so the step is a total no-op and not even the ::warning:: is ever printed.

### `PARTIAL` — Supabase runtime config / auth bootstrap

- **Surface:** src/lib/supabase.ts + GET /frontend-config (app/api/main.py:1188)
- **Severity:** medium · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** src/lib/supabase.ts:11-12 reads VITE_SUPABASE_URL / VITE_SUPABASE_ANON_KEY at build time; if absent it falls back to `fetch('/frontend-config')` (supabase.ts:33). I probed the backend via TestClient: GET /frontend-config → 200 {"supabase_url":"","supabase_anon_key":"","api_url":""} and GET /auth/providers → 200 []. So with no env configured, createClient() returns null (supabase.ts:52-54) and isSupabaseConfigured() is false — auth silently disabled, which is a designed degrade, not a crash. Worse in dev: `curl -i http://localhost:5173/frontend-config` returns Content-Type text/html (Vite index.html), so res.ok is true but res.json() throws and is swallowed by the catch at supabase.ts:41 → console.warn only. Frontend .gitignore correctly excludes .env so no keys are committed.
- **Blocker:** Supabase keys are absent everywhere (env, .env file, and /frontend-config returns empty strings), so auth is off by default. In dev the runtime-config fallback additionally cannot work at all because /frontend-config isn't proxied and returns HTML.
- **Fix:** Populate SUPABASE_URL/SUPABASE_ANON_KEY on the backend so /frontend-config returns real values, and proxy /frontend-config in vite.config.js. Also check `res.headers.get('content-type')?.includes('json')` before res.json() in src/lib/supabase.ts:35 so a mis-proxied HTML response is a loud error, not a silent warn.
- **Verifier:** Verified against a real uvicorn on :8000, not TestClient: GET /frontend-config -> 200 {"supabase_url":"","supabase_anon_key":"","api_url":""}; GET /auth/providers -> 200 []. Read src/lib/supabase.ts to its returns: fetchRuntimeConfig only assigns when config.supabase_url is truthy, so with empty strings createClient() hits `if (!supabaseUrl || !supabaseAnonKey) return null` — a designed degrade, not a crash. Also confirmed the dev-mode hole myself: curl http://localhost:5173/frontend-config returns 200 text/html, so res.ok passes and res.json() throws into the catch at supabase.ts:41 (console.warn only).

**Verifier notes for this domain.** I regenerated every command from scratch rather than trusting quoted output. Commands run: deleted frontend/node_modules and re-ran `npm ci` (exit 0, 420 top-level entries; `npm audit --json` metadata = {moderate:3, high:4, critical:2, total:9} — matches auditor exactly); deleted tsconfig.tsbuildinfo + tsconfig.node.tsbuildinfo then `npm run typecheck` (exit 0, zero output); `npm run lint` (exit 0, 8 warnings, and via `-f json` confirmed 108 files linted = all 90 src .ts/.tsx + the 5 frontend/e2e files); `rm -rf dist && npm run build` (exit 0, identical hashes index-BK60gDTb.css / index-BS5rIA_P.js / index-C0XddFiH.js 1.80 MB); `npm test` (exit 1, 4 coverage ERROR lines at 0.46%/5.79%/0.46%/23.3%); started a real `npm run dev` on :5173 and curled /api/investigations, /api/incidents, /oncall/alerts, /frontend-config, /healthz — all returned 200 text/html; ran frontend/e2e with its own config (webServer death: `npm error Missing script: "dev"`) and then with a temp override config against live :5173 + live backend; installed and ran tests/e2e (which the auditor never did).

MATERIAL THINGS THE AUDITOR MISSED:

1. The CI "Check coverage thresholds" step (.github/workflows/ci.yml:142-150) is not merely non-blocking — it never executes at all. It gates on `[ -f coverage/coverage-summary.json ]`, but vitest.config.ts:15 sets `reporter: ['text','json','html','lcov']` and 'json' emits coverage-final.json, not coverage-summary.json (that needs 'json-summary'). I confirmed after a real run: /Users/ankitpandey/OmniPotent/OmniPotent/frontend/coverage/ contains coverage-final.json and lcov.info but NO coverage-summary.json. So not even the `::warning::` the auditor cited is ever emitted.

2. The production server has NO SPA fallback. app/api/main.py:3732 uses `StaticFiles(directory=dist, html=True)`, which only serves html for directory paths. Against the live backend I got: `/` 200 html, `/auth/callback` 200 html (that one is a real APIRoute, verified via app.routes), but `/dashboard` 404 {"detail":"Not Found"}, `/login` 404, `/billing` 404, `/settings/billing` 404 (all application/json). This is not hypothetical: app/billing/stripe_integration.py:183-186 defaults `success_url` to `{frontend_url}/settings/billing?success=true` and `cancel_url` to `.../settings/billing?canceled=true`, so a user completing Stripe checkout is redirected to a hard 404 JSON page. App.tsx's `params.has('success')` billing handler can therefore never fire on the default URL.

3. tests/e2e/specs/auth.spec.ts and investigation.spec.ts are not just "not run in CI" — they are written against a different application and cannot ever pass. I installed and ran them against the live backend serving the built dist: 16 failed / 3 passed (1.9m). Concrete failures: `expect(page).toHaveURL(/login|auth/)` got "http://localhost:8000/dashboard"; `expect(page).toHaveTitle(/DevOps Copilot/)` got "OmniPotent". They also target `[data-testid="logout-button"]`, `[data-testid="login-button"]` etc. — and `grep -rn "data-testid" frontend/src` returns ZERO matches in the entire frontend. Meanwhile the one spec CI does run, specs/api.spec.ts, genuinely passes 3/3 in 1.2s.

4. The auditor's module count for the API-base defect is inflated. `grep -rn VITE_API_URL frontend/src` shows only 13 modules using `|| ""` (12 pages + src/components/OnboardingWizard.tsx), not "19 of 24 page modules". Offsetting that, the auditor missed a 14th case that is worse: frontend/src/pages/History.tsx:72 hardcodes `fetch('/api/investigations?limit=50')` with no API_BASE at all, so it is relative even if VITE_API_URL is set.

5. vite.config.js and vite.config.ts are byte-identical today, so the "silently ignored edits" smell is real but currently inert. tsconfig.json `include: ["src"]` means the e2e specs are never typechecked (eslint does lint them, though).

Cleanup: I restored everything — node_modules reinstalled via npm ci, dist rebuilt, temp Playwright override config and all test-results/playwright-report artifacts deleted, background uvicorn/vite processes killed. `git status --short` is clean.


## Reachability census: which of ~145 app/ packages are live vs orphaned

**Summary.** I computed the real transitive import closure from every shipped entrypoint (uvicorn app.api.main:app per Dockerfile CMD / render.yaml, the 22 MCP stdio subprocesses spawned by app/mcp/client.py, app/cli.py, app/setup.py, app/tasks/worker.py) using both runtime sys.modules inspection and a full AST import graph over all 636 .py files. Ground truth: 271 of 636 modules and 110,679 of 238,756 LOC are reachable (46.4%). 128,077 LOC (53.6%) is unreachable from any entrypoint, and 76 of the 145 top-level packages under app/ have zero reachable module. The single biggest problem is that the codebase is structured as ~50 duplicated "advanced" feature packages (rca/rca_advanced, postmortem/postmortem_advanced, predictive x3, cost x3, analytics x2, sso x2, security x3) that were written, unit-tested, and then never wired to a router - 901 of 2,610 tests (35%) import at least one package that is dead in production. A user can genuinely use the ~60 live packages behind the 18 registered routers (incidents, runbooks, oncall, billing, status page, SLOs, knowledge base, Slack bot, MCP tool fleet all respond 200), but every feature the README-style package names promise - RCA, postmortems, dedup, analytics, SSO, RBAC, compliance, cost optimization - is 404. Two concrete production breakages fell out of the census: the entire app/api/v1 namespace (70 routes) is never registered, and /api/investigations - which the frontend calls - returns 500 because it imports the strawberry-dependent GraphQL module.

**Wiring.** LIVE entrypoints: exactly one ASGI app (app/api/main.py, 3,733 LOC, 293 routes) with 18 include_router calls at main.py:698-768 (websocket, billing, oncall, auth, teams, incidents, webhooks, slack_bot, runbooks, knowledge_base, event_intelligence, model_router, knowledge_graph, planning, coordinator, verification, slo, status_page). Most surface area is defined inline in main.py itself, not in routers. Secondary live entrypoints: 22 MCP servers spawned as `python -m app.mcp.servers.<name>.server` subprocesses (app/mcp/client.py:22-28, 52-226); console scripts `copilot`=app.cli:main and `copilot-setup`=app.setup:main (pyproject.toml [project.scripts]). NO ENTRYPOINT: app/api/v1/* (9 modules, 70 routes, never include_router'd - all 404); app/api/graphql (imported at main.py:2118 but strawberry not installed -> 500); 5 MCP server dirs (jira, linear, logs_metrics, newrelic, opsgenie) absent from the _SERVERS tuple; app/tasks/* (worker.py says "python -m app.tasks.worker" but no Dockerfile CMD, docker-compose service, or render.yaml service launches it - `'app.tasks' in sys.modules` is False after importing app.api.main); and 76 entire top-level packages (115,537 LOC) with zero reachable module.


### `BROKEN` — GraphQL API and /api/investigations history endpoint

- **Surface:** GET /graphql, GET /api/investigations; app/api/graphql/{__init__,schema,types}.py
- **Severity:** high · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** `python3 -c 'import strawberry'` -> ModuleNotFoundError. Import of app.api.main logs `strawberry-graphql not installed, GraphQL endpoint unavailable`. TestClient: GET /graphql -> 404. GET /api/investigations -> 500 {"error":"INTERNAL_ERROR"} with traceback ending `File app/api/main.py, line 2118, in list_investigations: from app.api.graphql.schema import _investigations` -> `app/api/graphql/__init__.py:11` -> `app/api/graphql/schema.py:12: import strawberry` -> ModuleNotFoundError. The frontend calls /api/investigations (frontend/src). This is the newest commit in the repo (4266677 'feat: add /api/investigations endpoint for history page' + e5ec716 'fix: add /api prefix'). Second-order defect: even with strawberry installed, `_investigations` is a module-level dict at app/api/graphql/schema.py:44, only written by the GraphQL mutation at schema.py:330 - which is unmounted - so the endpoint would return an empty list forever and would not survive a restart or be shared across uvicorn workers.
- **Blocker:** Live, frontend-consumed route imports an optional dependency that is not installed; the backing store is an in-memory dict with no reachable writer.
- **Fix:** Import `app.api.graphql.schema` lazily behind try/except or move `_investigations` out of the GraphQL module; better, back investigations with the SQLAlchemy models in app/database/ so the history page shows real data. Add strawberry-graphql to pyproject if GraphQL is meant to ship.
- **Verifier:** Reproduced exactly. TestClient(raise_server_exceptions=False).get('/api/investigations') -> 500 {"error":"INTERNAL_ERROR"} with traceback app/api/main.py:2118 `from app.api.graphql.schema import _investigations` -> app/api/graphql/__init__.py:11 -> app/api/graphql/schema.py:12 `import strawberry` -> ModuleNotFoundError. GET /graphql -> 404. Second-order defect confirmed by grep: _investigations is `dict[str, dict] = {}` at schema.py:44, read at :79/:93/:342 and written ONLY at :330 inside the unmounted GraphQL mutation - so even with strawberry installed it returns [] forever. frontend/src calls '/api/investigations' (confirmed in my own frontend grep).

### `DEAD_CODE` — v1 REST API namespace (policy, decision, digital-twin, chat, config, admin, webhooks, health)

- **Surface:** app/api/v1/*.py - 9 modules, 2,367 LOC, 70 route decorators
- **Severity:** critical · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** AST scan found 8 modules defining APIRouter routes that are outside the reachable closure, totalling 70 routes: app/api/v1/policy.py (22 routes, prefix=/policy, 786 LOC), app/api/v1/digital_twin.py (20 routes, prefix=/digital-twin, 785 LOC), app/api/v1/config.py (6), app/api/v1/decision.py (6, 552 LOC), app/api/v1/admin.py (5), app/api/v1/chat.py (4), app/api/v1/webhooks.py (4), app/api/v1/health.py (3). `grep -rn 'api.v1' app/ | grep -v '^app/api/v1/'` returns nothing outside the package - main.py never imports it. TestClient probes: GET /api/v1/health -> 404, /v1/health -> 404, /api/v1/policy/policies -> 404, /v1/policy/policies -> 404, /api/v1/digital-twin/twins -> 404, /api/v1/decision/decisions -> 404, /v1/chat -> 404. Note app/api/versioning.py (245 LOC) is also unreachable. The frontend calls /api/v1/query (grep -rhoE '/api/[A-Za-z0-9_/-]+' frontend/src) which is also 404.
- **Blocker:** app/api/main.py has no `from app.api.v1 import router` / `include_router`, so 70 endpoints - including the entire policy engine and digital twin surfaces - are unroutable.
- **Fix:** Add `from app.api import v1; app.include_router(v1.router, prefix='/api')` in main.py near line 768, then fix the resulting prefix collisions (/health already exists as /healthz).
- **Verifier:** Imported the 8 v1 modules directly and summed router.routes: 22+20+6+6+5+4+4+3 = exactly 70 routes with prefixes /policy, /digital-twin, /config, /decision, /admin, /chat, /webhooks and none for health. `grep -rn 'api\.v1' app tests` outside app/api/v1/ returns NOTHING - not even a test imports it. TestClient probes for /api/v1/health, /v1/health, /api/v1/policy/policies, /api/v1/digital-twin/twins, /api/v1/decision/decisions, /api/v1/chat all 404. app/api/versioning.py also in my dead set. Sub-claim corrected: the frontend's /api/v1/query is `${prometheusUrl}/api/v1/query` (ServiceMap.tsx:388), i.e. Prometheus, not this namespace.

### `DEAD_CODE` — Security hardening (headers, MFA, zero-trust, WebSocket auth, request signing, secret rotation, session binding)

- **Surface:** app/security/ (12 modules, 4,786 LOC) + app/security_audit/ (540) + app/security_scanner/ (555)
- **Severity:** critical · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** All 12 app/security modules are outside the reachable closure; `grep -rn 'from app\.security' app/ | grep -v '^app/security'` returns only app/security_audit and app/security_scanner (themselves dead) - no live importer. Proof of consequence: `TestClient(app).get('/healthz').headers` = {'content-type','x-request-id','vary','content-encoding'} - no Content-Security-Policy, no Strict-Transport-Security, no X-Frame-Options, no X-Content-Type-Options, i.e. app/security/headers.py is never installed as middleware. main.py only registers LimitsMiddleware, GZipMiddleware and CORS (app/api/main.py:682-688). app/security/websocket_auth.py, mfa.py, zero_trust.py, request_signing.py, session_binding.py, secret_rotation.py, api_auth.py, input_validation.py all unreachable. tests/test_advanced_security.py (37 tests) and tests/test_security.py (26 tests) exercise this package - 63 passing tests that describe security the deployed app does not have.
- **Blocker:** Nothing in app/api/main.py imports app.security; the middleware, MFA and WS-auth implementations exist but are never installed.
- **Fix:** At minimum register app/security/headers.py as middleware in main.py and wire app/security/websocket_auth.py into the /ws route; delete the rest or wire it deliberately.
- **Verifier:** app/security (12 mods, 4,786 LOC), security_audit (540), security_scanner (555) all in my fully-dead set. `grep -rn 'app\.security'` across app/ and tests/ returns ONLY tests/test_security.py and tests/test_advanced_security.py - no app-code importer at all. `'app.security' in sys.modules` after importing app.api.main -> False. Consequence re-proven: GET /healthz response headers = {content-type, x-request-id, vary, content-encoding} and app.user_middleware has exactly 3 entries - no CSP, HSTS, X-Frame-Options or X-Content-Type-Options anywhere.

### `DEAD_CODE` — RBAC / roles and permissions

- **Surface:** app/rbac/ - 4 modules, 1,010 LOC
- **Severity:** high · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** Zero reachable modules. `grep -rn 'from app\.rbac\|import app\.rbac' app/` returns nothing outside app/rbac/ itself. Reachable only from tests/. Live authz is instead the ad-hoc `Authorization required` check seen on GET /api/teams -> 401 {"detail":"Authorization required"}, which comes from main.py, not from app/rbac.

- **Verifier:** app/rbac (4 mods, 1,010 LOC) is in my fully-dead set. `grep -rn 'app\.rbac' app tests` returns no hit inside app/ outside app/rbac itself. `'app.rbac' in sys.modules` after importing app.api.main -> False. GET /api/teams -> 401 {"detail":"Authorization required"} confirms the live gate is the inline check, not the RBAC engine.

Proof it is not loaded by the API: `python3 -c "import sys, app.api.main; print('app.tasks' in sys.modules)"` -> False. app/tasks/tasks.py is the only live-ish importer of app/alerting and app/runbooks/executor from a background context.

- **Verifier:** Confirmed and strengthened. Dockerfile:54 CMD is the single `uvicorn app.api.main:app` line; grep for dramatiq/worker/app.tasks across Dockerfile, docker-compose.yml, render.yaml and start.sh returns NOTHING. `'app.tasks' in sys.modules` after importing app.api.main -> False. Beyond the auditor: I grepped for dramatiq `.send(` across app/ and there is NO producer either - nothing enqueues, so the blocker's phrasing (features 'silently never execute') understates it; both ends are dead. Importing app.tasks.tasks also logs 'No Redis URL configured, using stub broker (tasks won't persist)'.

### `PARTIAL` — Backend reachability census (whole-repo totals)

- **Surface:** app/ - 636 modules, 238,756 LOC, 145 top-level packages
- **Severity:** critical · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** Method: (1) runtime closure - `python3 -c "import sys, app.api.main, app.cli, app.setup; print(len([m for m in sys.modules if m.startswith('app')]))"` -> 183 modules for main alone, 189 across all three entrypoints. (2) full AST import graph over all 636 files (imports resolved incl. function-local/lazy imports), transitively closed from app.api.main + app.cli + app.setup + app/tasks/* + the 22 `python -m app.mcp.servers.X.server` subprocess entrypoints. RESULT: REACHABLE 271 modules / 110,679 LOC = 46.4%. UNREACHABLE 365 modules / 128,077 LOC = 53.6%, of which 157 mods / 60,113 LOC are reachable only from tests/ and 208 mods / 67,964 LOC are reachable from nothing at all. Top-level package classification: LIVE (all modules reachable) 61 pkgs / 81,358 LOC; PARTIAL_LIVE (some dead submodules) 8 pkgs / 41,861 LOC; ORPHAN (no prod, no test) 26 pkgs / 51,339 LOC; TEST_ONLY 50 pkgs / 64,198 LOC. 76 of 145 top-level packages have ZERO reachable module (115,537 LOC). All 76 dead packages import cleanly (`importlib.import_module` succeeded for every one) - they are not broken, just never wired. app/api/main.py exposes 293 routes; `grep -c include_router app/api/main.py` = 18.
- **Blocker:** 53.6% of the backend (128,077 LOC) cannot be executed by any shipped process. The headline orphan number is 115,537 LOC across 76 whole packages.
- **Fix:** Delete or archive the 76 zero-reachability packages (this alone removes 115k LOC and ~900 tests that assert nothing about the running product), then wire the small number of clusters you actually intend to ship one router at a time.
- **Verifier:** Rebuilt independently: AST closure over all 636 files (full-AST walk for lazy imports + ancestor-package closure) from main/cli/setup/tasks/22 MCP servers gives 304 modules / 114,001 LOC reachable = 47.7%, and 75 fully-dead top-level packages / 112,260 LOC. Auditor said 271 / 110,679 / 46.4% and 76 / 115,537. Directionally correct; their module count undercounts package __init__ ancestors and their dead-LOC overcounts because app/connectors (3,277 LOC) was wrongly classified dead. Verified runtime sys.modules (185 modules) is a strict subset of my closure, and importlib.import_module succeeded for all 75 dead packages (0 failures). 18 include_router at main.py:698-768, 293 routes, single FastAPI app, no dynamic router discovery - all confirmed.

Route distribution: /api 162, /oncall 44, /billing 22, /auth 8, /admin 8, /customers 6, /webhooks 5. 61 top-level packages are fully live (81,358 LOC), including slack_bot (11,030), oncall (9,959), billing (4,408), coordinator (4,281), status_page (4,009), digital_twin (3,947), knowledge_graph (3,628), policy_engine (3,424), verification (2,720), resilience (2,705), graph (2,452), planning (2,359), decision_engine (2,277).

Only oncall is genuinely persistent. **Verifier notes for this domain.** I rebuilt the census from scratch rather than trusting the auditor's numbers: my own AST import graph over all 636 app/*.py files (walking the FULL AST so function-local/lazy imports are captured, plus ancestor-package closure, which the auditor appears to have omitted), transitively closed from app.api.main + app.cli + app.setup + app/tasks/* + the 22 `python -m app.mcp.servers.<n>.server` entrypoints. I cross-validated it against runtime `sys.modules` (185 app modules after importing main+cli+setup) and confirmed the runtime set is a strict SUBSET of my AST closure (runtime-only difference = 0), so my closure is a sound over-approximation: anything I call dead is definitively dead.

MY GROUND TRUTH (regenerated, differs from the auditor's):
- 636 modules, 238,756 LOC, 145 top-level entries under app/ (matches).
- REACHABLE 304 modules / 114,001 LOC = 47.7% (auditor: 271 / 110,679 / 46.4%).
- FULLY DEAD top-level packages: 75 / 112,260 LOC (auditor: 76 / 115,537).
- All 75 dead packages import cleanly: I ran importlib.import_module over every module in them -> 0 failures. Confirmed.
- app/api/main.py: exactly 18 include_router (main.py:698-768), 293 routes, only one FastAPI() app in app/ (the other is a test app in app/middleware/error_handler.py). No importlib/pkgutil dynamic router discovery anywhere. So the "no entrypoint" DEAD_CODE verdicts cannot be rescued by hidden wiring.

THREE CONCRETE FACTUAL ERRORS I FOUND IN THE AUDIT (all verified by command):
1. app/connectors is NOT dead. The auditor wrote "app/connectors is imported only by app/digital_twin/infrastructure_clone.py - but that module is itself unreachable". False. app/digital_twin/__init__.py:11 imports infrastructure_clone at module scope, app/coordinator/__init__.py:26 imports app/coordinator/decision_integration.py which imports app.digital_twin at module scope, and coordinator's router IS registered (main.py:756). Runtime proof: after `import app.api.main`, `'app.digital_twin.infrastructure_clone' in sys.modules` is True. Also app/policy_engine/criteria.py:705 imports app.connectors.prometheus, and policy_engine is live. So 3,277 LOC of the claimed 11,950-LOC dead cluster is import-reachable. (Functionally the connector call sites are still never executed - the only instantiators of InfrastructureCloner/ChaosEngine/etc. are app/api/v1/digital_twin.py, which is unrouted - so the conclusion survives but the evidence is wrong and the census LOC is wrong.)
2. app/mcp/http_client.py DOES have an importer: app/mcp/servers/github/server.py:77 and :109, and github IS in _SERVERS. And "29/60 mcp modules reachable" is wrong - I get 50/60; the only dead mcp modules are exactly the 5 unspawned server dirs (jira, linear, logs_metrics, newrelic, opsgenie) = 2,571 LOC.
3. "The frontend calls /api/v1/query" is wrong. frontend/src/pages/ServiceMap.tsx:388 calls `${prometheusUrl}/api/v1/query` - that is Prometheus's own query API, not app/api/v1. (ServiceMap.tsx:455 calling `${API_BASE}/api/kubernetes/services` IS real and IS a 404, but it is wrapped in try/catch with a "fallback" data source, so it degrades silently rather than breaking.)

The test-theater numbers were also inflated by exactly the connectors error: I count 2,566 test functions (not 2,610), 769 in files importing at least one dead package (not 901), and 494 in 19 files importing ONLY dead packages (not 626 in 22). The 626-494 = 132 gap is precisely test_connectors_jenkins.py (48) + test_connectors_gitlab.py (45) + test_connectors_grafana.py (39), which the auditor misclassified. Also "app/cloud, app/kubernetes, app/chaos, app/deployment, app/dependency_graph have zero test importers" is wrong for kubernetes (2 test files) and chaos (1).

WHAT THE AUDITOR MISSED ENTIRELY:
- 11 fully-dead packages worth ~13,300 LOC never mentioned in any claim: continuous_learning (2,594), eval (1,925), learning (1,873), safety_gates (1,607), risk (1,481), log_intelligence (1,393), sandbox (751), reliability (604), timeline (580), change_intelligence (513), ai_fix (970).
- The biggest thing the audit understates: the "WORKS" live surface is overwhelmingly DEMO DATA IN MODULE-LEVEL DICTS, not a working product. app/incidents/store.py line 1 literally says '"""In-memory incident store for demo purposes."""' and `_init_demo_data()` (store.py:30) seeds the 3 incidents the auditor cited as "real records" - the UUIDs differ every process (auditor got f59e46b8..., I got 17cdf810... then a3c236df...), proving zero persistence and zero cross-worker sharing. app/api/billing_router.py:33 hardcodes `InMemoryBillingStore()` with no DB alternative. app/status_page/store.py:772 hardcodes `InMemoryStatusStore()`. app/runbooks/router.py:30 says "# In-memory storage (would be DB in production)". app/waitlist/manager.py:110 is `_waitlist_store: dict[str, WaitlistEntry] = {}`. The ONLY genuinely persistent live surface is oncall (SQLAlchemy via app/database/engine.py, sqlite+aiosqlite:///data/copilot.db default). Under the Dockerfile's single-process uvicorn this "works", but it is a demo, and the audit's WORKS verdict does not say so.
- frontend/src/pages/Team.tsx:65 fetches `/api/team/members`, which does not exist (the registered route is /api/teams). It falls back to showing only the logged-in user, so the Team page can never show a real team.
- app/tasks is dead on BOTH ends, not just the consumer end. I grepped for dramatiq `.send(` across app/ - there is no producer either, so the auditor's blocker ("features that enqueue work silently never execute") overstates it: nothing enqueues. Also app/tasks/broker.py falls back to StubBroker ("No Redis URL configured, using stub broker (tasks won't persist)").

Things I re-ran and fully confirmed: 70 v1 routes (policy 22, digital_twin 20, config 6, decision 6, admin 5, chat 4, webhooks 4, health 3) with zero importers in app/ OR tests/ and all probes 404; /api/investigations -> 500 with the exact ModuleNotFoundError traceback through app/api/main.py:2118 -> app/api/graphql/__init__.py:11 -> schema.py:12; /healthz headers = {content-type, x-request-id, vary, content-encoding} with only 3 middleware (no CSP/HSTS/X-Frame-Options); app/analytics_advanced/dashboards.py:302 `_generate_placeholder_data` returning `{"placeholder": True}` at :353; app/postmortem_advanced/action_items.py:197 emitting `"    pass  # TODO: Implement test"`; 0 test importers for postmortem_advanced, deduplication, similar_search, alert_correlation, predictive_advanced, predictive_scaling, cost_optimizer, analytics_advanced, sso_advanced, chatops, slack_advanced, cloud, deployment, dependency_graph.


## Test suite reality: does 2600 passing tests mean anything?

**Summary.** The 2600 tests are real, they run, and they pass — I executed ~1,166 of them across 52 files and got 0 failures. But they are almost entirely offline unit tests of internal helper functions: only 108 of 2,614 test functions (4.1%) issue an HTTP request through the FastAPI app, and only 31 of 288 live routes (10.8%) are referenced by any test at all. The single biggest problem is that this coverage shape makes the suite blind to whole-feature outages: I probed all 288 live routes with TestClient and 44 of them (22 GET + 22 non-GET) return HTTP 500 right now, including the entire /oncall/* surface (~60 routes, killed by a real code bug — `get_session` is an `@asynccontextmanager` at app/database/engine.py:116 but is used 40 times as `Depends(get_async_session)` in app/oncall/api_router.py) and `/api/investigations` (added in HEAD commit, imports the uninstalled `strawberry` package). Zero tests catch any of it. There is no top-level tests/conftest.py, no shared DB fixture, all 19 integration tests skip without DATABASE_URL, and only 8 of 127 test files touch a real database (SQLite only — the async SQLAlchemy path where the oncall bug lives has zero test coverage). CI's enforced gate is `--cov-fail-under=38` while the PR comment template advertises "Coverage threshold: 80%". A paying user hitting on-call scheduling, billing cancellation, or investigation history today would get a 500 on a green build.

**Wiring.** Live surface is 288 APIRoutes from 18 include_router calls in app/api/main.py plus ~120 routes defined inline in main.py itself. Of the 31 routes any test references, essentially all are the inline main.py routes (/chat, /approve, /feedback, /healthz, /readyz, /config, /me, /usage, /audit, /admin/*, /github/*, /webhooks/*, /signup, /metrics) plus /scim/v2/Users. Every one of the 18 registered sub-routers is untested at the HTTP layer: /oncall/* (0 test references, 60+ routes), /api/knowledge-graph/* (21), /api/planning/* (20), /api/status/* (17), /api/teams/* (13), /api/model-router/* (13), /api/event-intelligence/* (11), /api/slack/* (10), /api/slos/* (9), /api/verification/* (9), /api/incidents/* (9), /api/knowledge/* (8), /api/runbooks/* (8), /api/coordinator/* (8), /billing/* (14), /auth/* (6), /customers/* (3). Test-side: tests/integration/* and tests/contract/* are wired to CI but skip 19/32 tests locally (no DATABASE_URL/REDIS_URL). tests/e2e (Playwright, 3 specs), tests/load (k6/locust), tests/chaos, tests/mutation have no local runnable path (no node_modules, no k6, mutation runs `|| true` weekly). evals/run_redteam.py and evals/run_golden.py are wired into ci.yml lines 103-107; evals/run_evals.py and evals/run_benchmark.py have no CI entrypoint.


### `BROKEN` — Test suite detects broken HTTP endpoints

- **Surface:** All 288 live APIRoutes in app/api/main.py
- **Severity:** critical · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** I probed every live route with TestClient(app, raise_server_exceptions=False). GET probe (128 routes): 200x73, 400x5, 401x7, 404x17, 422x4, **500x22**. Non-GET probe (160 routes, json={}): 200x27, 400x2, 401x6, 403x2, 404x21, 422x65, 429x15, **500x22**. That is 44 routes returning 5xx on a green build. The 22 GET 500s are all /oncall/* plus /api/investigations; the 22 non-GET 500s are /oncall/* plus /billing/subscription/cancel, /billing/subscription/reactivate, /billing/webhooks/stripe. Grep of tests for these paths: `grep -rn '"/oncall' tests --include='*.py' | wc -l` -> 0. Zero of the 2600 tests reference any of them.
- **Blocker:** Only 108 of 2,614 test functions (4.1%) make an HTTP call through the app, and they cover 31 of 288 routes (10.8%). The suite structurally cannot see a route-level outage.
- **Fix:** Add a single parametrized smoke test that enumerates `app.routes` and asserts no route returns >=500 for a well-formed request (the exact loop I ran, ~20 lines). That one test would have failed on 44 routes and on the HEAD commit.

### `BROKEN` — On-call scheduling API (largest untested surface)

- **Surface:** /oncall/* — 60+ routes from app/oncall/api_router.py
- **Severity:** critical · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** Every DB-backed /oncall route 500s. Traceback root cause: `TypeError: '_AsyncGeneratorContextManager' object is not an async iterator`. app/database/engine.py:116 decorates `get_session` with `@asynccontextmanager`; app/oncall/api_router.py:23 imports it as `get_async_session` and uses it as a FastAPI dependency `db=Depends(get_async_session)` in 40 places (`grep -rc 'Depends(get_async_session)' app` -> app/oncall/api_router.py:40). FastAPI receives a context-manager object and tries to iterate it. This fails regardless of DB config. Coverage run confirms the service layer is untouched: app/oncall/schedule_service.py 10%, ai_oncall_service.py 8%, escalation_service.py 10%, rotations.py 22%, schedules.py 26%, api_router.py 37%. `grep -rl 'schedule_service\|ai_oncall_service\|escalation_service' tests` returns nothing — no test file imports them at all.
- **Blocker:** A dependency-injection type error that no test can reach, in a subsystem with 0 HTTP tests and 8-10% line coverage on its core services.
- **Fix:** Change app/database/engine.py to expose an undecorated `async def get_session_dep()` generator for `Depends()` and keep the `@asynccontextmanager` version under a different name; then add HTTP tests for /oncall/teams and /oncall/schedules.

### `BROKEN` — Route/API-contract coverage of the test suite

- **Surface:** tests/ vs app.api.main routes
- **Severity:** critical · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** Extracted every path literal from tests (AST + regex over `.get/.post/.put/.patch/.delete/.request/.websocket_connect` calls, plus a generous pass over ANY '/...' string literal in tests/): 82 distinct path literals. Matched against 288 live (method, path) pairs with wildcard normalization of {params}: **31 covered (10.8%), 257 uncovered**. Uncovered by prefix: /api/knowledge-graph 21, /api/planning 20, /api/status 17, /oncall/schedules 15, /api/teams 13, /api/model-router 13, /api/event-intelligence 11, /api/slack 10, /api/slos 9, /api/verification 9, /api/incidents 9, /api/knowledge 8, /api/runbooks 8, /api/coordinator 8, /billing/usage 6, /oncall/teams 6, /billing/subscription 5, /api/webhooks 5, /auth/{provider} 3, /customers/* 3. Every route from all 18 include_router() calls is uncovered; the only covered routes are the ones defined inline in app/api/main.py.
- **Blocker:** 18 of 18 registered routers have zero HTTP-level tests.
- **Fix:** Per-router smoke tests: for each include_router, one authenticated happy-path request per route. ~290 tests would replace the marginal value of ~1,500 existing unit tests.
- **Verifier:** Recomputed from scratch: my AST extractor found 35 distinct '/...' path literals passed to .get/.post/.put/.patch/.delete/.request/.websocket_connect in tests/. Matched against the 288 live (method,path) pairs with {param}->[^/]+ regex normalization: 31 covered = 10.8%, identical to the claim. The 31 covered routes are all inline-in-main.py handlers (/chat, /approve, /feedback, /healthz, /readyz, /config, /me, /usage, /audit, /admin/*, /github/*, /webhooks/*, /signup, /metrics, /model/configure, /remediate, /cache/stats, /frontend-config) plus POST /scim/v2/Users and GET /auth/providers. Zero routes from any of the 18 include_router calls. Sharp detail: POST /webhooks/stripe (inline) is tested but POST /billing/webhooks/stripe (router) is not — and the latter is one of the 44 500s.

- **Severity:** high · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** `c.get('/api/investigations')` -> 500 {"error":"INTERNAL_ERROR"}. Traceback: app/api/main.py:2118 `from app.api.graphql.schema import _investigations` -> app/api/graphql/schema.py:12 `import strawberry` -> `python3 -c "import strawberry"` -> ModuleNotFoundError. Also note app/api/graphql/schema.py:44 `_investigations: dict[str, dict] = {}` — the 'persistence' behind history is a module-level dict. Git log shows commits 4266677 'feat: add /api/investigations endpoint for history page' and e5ec716 'fix: add /api prefix to investigations endpoint'. No test references the path (it appears in my uncovered-route list).
- **Blocker:** Feature shipped in the two most recent commits with zero tests; it has never returned a 200.
- **Fix:** Move `_investigations` out of the strawberry-importing module into a plain store, or guard the import. Add one HTTP test.

### `BROKEN` — Test fixtures / shared conftest / real database

- **Surface:** tests/conftest.py (absent), tests/integration/conftest.py
- **Severity:** high · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** `ls tests/conftest.py` -> No such file. There is exactly one conftest in the whole tree (`find . -name conftest.py` -> ./tests/integration/conftest.py). No shared TestClient fixture, no dependency_overrides anywhere, no auth fixture, no DB fixture for unit tests. tests/integration/conftest.py:20 and :29 call `pytest.skip('DATABASE_URL not set')` / `('REDIS_URL not set')` — running `pytest tests/integration tests/contract -rs` gives '13 passed, 19 skipped'; all 17 real integration tests skip. Real-DB usage across the whole suite: `grep -rl 'sqlite\|:memory:' tests --include='*.py' | wc -l` -> 8 of 127 files, and all 8 use the file-based SQLite TenantStore. Zero tests exercise the async SQLAlchemy engine in app/database/engine.py — which is precisely where the bug that 500s all of /oncall lives.
- **Blocker:** No unit-level DB fixture at all; the only real-DB tests are opt-in and skip silently by default.
- **Fix:** Add tests/conftest.py with an in-process SQLite (aiosqlite + StaticPool) engine bound via app.dependency_overrides, plus a `client` fixture. Make integration tests xfail-strict rather than skip when the env is expected.
- **Verifier:** `find . -name conftest.py -not -path '*/node_modules/*'` returns exactly one file: tests/integration/conftest.py. No tests/conftest.py. grep 'dependency_overrides' across app/ and tests/ -> zero hits, so there is no TestClient/DB/auth override fixture anywhere. Ran `pytest tests/integration tests/contract -rs` myself: 13 passed, 19 skipped, with 17 skips reading 'DATABASE_URL not set' / 'REDIS_URL not set' (conftest.py:20, :29) and 2 reading 'Requires pact-python and running server'. Real-DB files: 8 of 127 (test_billing, test_governance, test_database, test_scaling, test_tenancy_api, test_checkpointer, test_tenancy, test_scim), all file-based SQLite. tests/test_database.py only tests _get_database_url and DatabaseHealth with patched settings — it never opens a session. Confirmed `async with get_session()` appears nowhere in tests/ or app/ outside its own docstring.

- **Severity:** high · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** `find frontend/src -name '*.test.*' -o -name '*.spec.*'` -> exactly one file: frontend/src/hooks/useConfig.test.ts. `grep -c 'it(\|test(' frontend/src/hooks/useConfig.test.ts` -> 2. `find frontend/src -name '*.tsx' -o -name '*.ts' | wc -l` -> 90 source files. So 2 test cases for 90 files, and CI runs them with continue-on-error: true (ci.yml:140). Could not execute (frontend/node_modules not installed, per environment).
- **Blocker:** Effectively no frontend test coverage; the '2600 tests' figure is entirely backend and says nothing about the UI a user actually sees.

- **Severity:** high · **Confidence:** high

All 11 tests patch the exact method under test. tests/test_e2e_investigation.py:31 `with patch.object(CopilotSession, 'ask', new_callable=AsyncMock) as mock_ask:` then line 16-24 builds `mock_result.report = {...'root_cause': 'Null pointer in discount calculation'...}` and line 36-39 asserts `result.report is not None`, `'root_cause' in result.report`, `result.report['confidence'] in ['high','medium','low']`. It asserts on the dict the test itself wrote. 13 patch.object calls across 11 test functions (lines 31, 67, 68, 103, 104, 141, 171, 201, 233, 260, 283, 307, 326) — every one targets CopilotSession.ask / .resume / .ask_stream.
- **Blocker:** The subject under test is replaced by a MagicMock in 11/11 tests; the file proves nothing about the investigation pipeline.

- **Severity:** low · **Confidence:** high

Wrong: load-tests.yml triggers on push-to-main AND pull_request (paths app/**, tests/load/**), not workflow_dispatch-only, and line 165-166 is an ENFORCED `::error::` + `exit 1` when error rate > 1% (only P95 at :161-162 is a warning) — that is a real gate. ### `DEAD_CODE` — Tests of unreachable / no-surface modules

- **Surface:** 33 test files, 855 tests
- **Severity:** medium · **Confidence:** medium
- **Evidence:** Built a static import graph over all 636 app/*.py modules (AST, includes function-level lazy imports) rooted at app.api.main, app.cli, app.setup, app.tasks: 190 reachable, 446 unreachable. Test files whose ONLY app imports are unreachable modules: 33 files, **855 of 2,572 tests (33.2%)**. Largest: test_timeline_collector 54, test_connectors_jenkins 48, test_status_page_widgets 46, test_connectors_gitlab 45, test_moat_builders 44, test_growth_enablers 40, test_connectors_grafana 39, test_router 38, test_advanced_security 37, test_secrets_advanced 35, test_vpc_connector 34, test_advanced_differentiators 34, test_digital_twin 32, test_policy_engine 27, test_security 26, test_competitive_parity 25, test_monitoring 24. Spot-check caveat: a few (e.g. app.status_page.widgets) ARE import-reachable via a package __init__ but still have no HTTP surface — `grep -i widget /tmp/routes.txt` returns 0 routes, so those 46 tests still guard code no user can invoke. Conversely app.customers is lazily imported inside main.py handlers and is correctly counted reachable.
- **Blocker:** A third of the suite guards code with no entrypoint. It inflates the 2600 number and the coverage denominator without protecting any user-facing behavior.
- **Fix:** Either wire the modules (register the routers) or delete both the modules and their tests. Track 'tests per live route' as the real metric instead of raw test count.

### `PARTIAL` — OAuth / authentication test coverage

- **Surface:** tests/test_auth_endpoints.py (66 tests), /auth/* routes
- **Severity:** high · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** 66 tests titled 'Comprehensive tests for OAuth2 authentication endpoints', but none goes through HTTP (the file does not appear in my HTTP-call detection over tests). They import handler internals directly (tests/test_auth_endpoints.py:20-40: `_extract_token`, `_get_or_create_org_for_user`) and the module-level stores `_OAUTH_ACCOUNTS`, `_OAUTH_STATES`, `_USER_ACCOUNTS`. Those are in-memory dicts: app/auth/oauth.py:109 `_OAUTH_STATES: dict[str, tuple[str, datetime]] = {}`, :322 `_OAUTH_ACCOUNTS: dict[str, OAuthAccount] = {}`, :323 `_USER_ACCOUNTS: dict[str, list[str]] = {}`. Route coverage: only GET /auth/providers is referenced by a test; /auth/{provider}, /auth/callback, /auth/me, /auth/accounts, /auth/logout are all in the 257 uncovered list.
- **Blocker:** The tests validate a process-local dict, so they would pass unchanged even if the OAuth routes were unreachable or the session cookie was never set. They also cannot detect that account state does not survive a restart or a second worker.
- **Fix:** Add HTTP tests over /auth/callback with a stubbed provider token endpoint, and back the three dicts with the existing SQLAlchemy models.

### `PARTIAL` — Billing test coverage (Stripe paths)

- **Surface:** tests/test_billing_api.py (38 tests), /billing/*
- **Severity:** high · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** POST /billing/subscription/cancel and /reactivate both return 500. Traceback: app/api/billing_router.py:141 -> app/billing/api.py:356 -> `ValueError: Stripe not configured` (unhandled). The test suite ENSHRINES this: tests/test_billing_api.py:437 `test_cancel_subscription_no_stripe` asserts `cancel_subscription` raises, and :445 `test_reactivate_subscription_no_stripe` the same. Both pass. Neither goes through HTTP, so neither sees the 500. Coverage from the API test batch: app/billing/stripe_integration.py 25%, app/billing/webhooks.py 23%, app/billing/subscriptions.py 31%, app/billing/store.py 44%. `grep -rl 'billing.webhooks' tests` -> nothing. Note HEAD-1 commits 7f67be7/3bcd690 added a demo-checkout fallback and 'updated billing tests for demo checkout behavior' — checkout got a fallback, cancel/reactivate did not, and no test noticed.
- **Blocker:** Unit tests assert the raise; no HTTP test asserts the user-visible status code, so an unhandled ValueError reaches the user as a 500 instead of a 402/503.
- **Fix:** Add HTTP-level tests for the 14 /billing routes asserting explicit status codes; catch ValueError in billing_router and return 503 with a clear message.
- **Verifier:** Reproduced the 500 with a raising TestClient: POST /billing/subscription/cancel -> app/api/billing_router.py:141 -> app/billing/api.py:356 `raise ValueError("Stripe not configured")`, unhandled, surfaced as 500. Read the test that covers it: tests/test_billing_api.py:437 test_cancel_subscription_no_stripe does `with pytest.raises(ValueError, match="Stripe not configured"): await api_no_stripe.cancel_subscription(org_id)` and :445 the same for reactivate. Both pass. So the suite asserts the raise at the service layer and has no HTTP test asserting the user-visible status, exactly as claimed. The asymmetry is real: HEAD-1 commits 7f67be7/3bcd690 added a demo-checkout fallback to checkout only; cancel/reactivate/webhooks still 500 and no test noticed.

### `PARTIAL` — Connector tests (Datadog/PagerDuty/K8s/Sentry/GitHub/traces) _(verdict overturned from STUB on verification)_

- **Surface:** tests/test_connectors.py (30 tests), app/mcp/servers/*
- **Severity:** medium · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** Every connector test either asserts on hardcoded demo fixtures or skips when a real credential is present. tests/test_connectors.py:17 `test_datadog_offline_error_summary_finds_checkout_bug` calls `dd._offline_error_summary('checkout-svc')` (app/mcp/servers/datadog/server.py:107, which just counts lines from a bundled sample log file under LOGS_DATA_PATH) and asserts `'applyDiscount' in s['breakdown'][0]['message']` — asserting the fixture equals the fixture. 14 tests skip when the real token IS set: `pytest.skip('PAGERDUTY_API_TOKEN is set — offline fixtures inactive')` at :51, KUBE_CONFIG_PATH at :61/:68/:77/:86, SENTRY_API_TOKEN at :97/:105, GITHUB_TOKEN at :117/:124, TRACES_API_URL at :191/:202/:221, DEPLOYS_API_URL at :248/:256. The httpx live-API branches (e.g. datadog server.py:272-297) have zero test coverage by construction.
- **Blocker:** The test design guarantees the real integration path is never exercised — configuring a real credential makes the tests skip rather than test.
- **Fix:** Use respx/responses to record and replay the real Datadog/PagerDuty/Sentry HTTP shapes so the live branches are covered offline.
- **Verifier:** OVERTURNED to PARTIAL. I read the implementations to their return statements. tests/test_connectors.py:26 test_cluster_logs_collapses_lines_into_templates feeds three hand-written log lines to dd._mine_templates and asserts real behaviour (2 distinct templates, ERROR template count==2, '<NUM>' masking) — that is a genuine algorithm test with no fixture round-trip. app/mcp/servers/datadog/server.py:107 _offline_error_summary really parses and Counter-aggregates app/mcp/servers/logs_metrics/sample_data/app.log; the test asserts the aggregation found the right dominant message. So 'asserting the fixture equals the fixture' is unfair. What IS true and damning: 16 pytest.skip guards (lines 51, 61, 68, 77, 86, 97, 105, 117, 124, 146, 191, 202, 221, 240, 248, 256) all of the form 'skip if the real credential IS set' — configuring a real token makes the test disappear rather than run — and the httpx live branches (e.g. datadog server.py:270-292 get_error_summary) have zero coverage by construction. Real offline logic tested, real integration path structurally untestable = PARTIAL.

### `PARTIAL` — CI coverage gate

- **Surface:** .github/workflows/ci.yml
- **Severity:** medium · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** .github/workflows/ci.yml:83 `--cov-fail-under=38` — the enforced backend gate is 38%. .github/workflows/ci.yml:200 posts a PR comment reading 'Coverage threshold: 80%' and :205 'Coverage threshold: 70%' (frontend) — neither number is enforced anywhere. Frontend vitest at ci.yml:139-140 has `continue-on-error: true`, and the 'Check coverage thresholds' step at :148 only emits `::warning::Frontend coverage is below 70% threshold`. Mutation testing (.github/workflows/quality-gates.yml:94-97) runs `./tests/mutation/run_mutation_tests.sh || true` and only on `if: github.event.schedule == '0 0 * * 0'`. My measured coverage on the user-facing modules from the API test batch: TOTAL 41% over app.api.main + app.billing + app.oncall (app/api/main.py 51%, app/oncall/api_router.py 37%).
- **Blocker:** The gate is set below what the suite already achieves, so it can never fail; the advertised 80% is a string in a comment template.
- **Fix:** Raise --cov-fail-under to the current measured value and ratchet; remove continue-on-error from the frontend test step; make the PR comment read the real coverage.xml number.

### `PARTIAL` — Vacuous / low-signal test measurement

- **Surface:** tests/ (AST analysis across 2,614 test functions)
- **Severity:** medium · **Confidence:** high
- **Evidence:** Ran AST analysis over all 127 test files. Classic theater is actually LOW: 62/2,614 (2.4%) assert only a status code (13/35 in test_integration_api.py, 12/19 in test_tenancy_api.py, 10/25 in test_api.py, 8/66 in test_auth_endpoints.py); 42 tests have zero assertions and no pytest.raises (e.g. tests/test_billing_store.py:80 test_create_usage_record, tests/test_tasks.py:798 test_start_worker_configures_broker, tests/test_audit.py:875 test_archive_segment_uploads_to_s3, tests/test_agents_orchestrator.py:172 test_process_routes_to_initial); only 3 tests assert solely on mock call records (tests/test_audit.py:586, tests/test_verification_agent.py:355 and :396); 23 tests (0.9%) are tautological enum checks (tests/test_teams_models.py:22 `assert TeamRole.OWNER.value == 'owner'`, tests/test_audit.py:1204, tests/test_alerting_engine.py:322). Only 413/2,614 (15.8%) use mocks at all. Skips: 24 pytest.skip() calls, 14 importorskip, 3 skipif, 2 mark.skip; 0 xfail.
- **Blocker:** The tests are individually well-written unit tests. The problem is not assertion quality, it is subject choice: 96% never touch the HTTP layer and 33% target code with no entrypoint.
- **Fix:** Fill in the 42 assertion-less tests, and redirect new-test effort from internal helpers to the 257 uncovered routes.
- **Verifier:** I wrote my own AST classifier over all 127 test files and reproduced the numbers essentially exactly: 2614 test functions total; 42 with zero asserts and no pytest.raises (my sample: tests/test_billing_store.py:80 test_create_usage_record, test_resilience_advanced.py:429, test_observability_advanced.py:439/446, test_verification_agent.py:586/607, test_observability.py:11); 62 whose every assert mentions only status_code; 4 whose asserts are only mock call records. So classic assertion theater really is ~2.4% and the individual tests are competently written. The auditor's framing is right: the defect is subject choice, not assertion quality — 96.3% of tests never touch the HTTP layer (my count: 97/2614 make an HTTP call) and ~22% target modules with no entrypoint.

### `PARTIAL` — RCA benchmark harness

- **Severity:** low · **Confidence:** high

But n=4 cases total, and what it validates is that a null model and an evidence-spam model both score 0 — i.e. it checks the scorer isn't trivially gameable, not that the product is good. No CI entrypoint.
- **Blocker:** 4 benchmark cases and no model actually under evaluation in the default run; it is a scorer self-test.
- **Fix:** Expand evals/benchmark_cases.yaml and run the real agent against it (needs the API key path from run_evals).
